# INV-03-02 — Code generation and semantics components for the captive runtime

The architecture is fixed: **host code generation with execution inside the captive VM and
an EL1 shim**, as established in Tracks 00/01. This investigation selects and
adapts components inside that design. Upstream QEMU, FEX and Box64 runtimes are
not competing end-to-end architectures. Guest semantics, code generation and
resident execution interfaces are separate component decisions. Guarded EL1 execution is now selected by the independently accepted
[INV-03-01 claim15](INV-03-01.md) contract; actual adapter conformance remains open.

## Current summary

- **Build-first direction, October25:** FEX is the first integration target;
  develop its adapter, the provisional ARM64 ABI and resident VM together, using
  actual emitted fragments early and hand-written blocks as controls. Revisit
  final generator selection against the running implementation.
  Extended FEX, FEX+TCG and TCG-only remain candidates; claim20's hybrid preference
  and Track03 closure stay withdrawn. Full translator adaptation grows through
  scoped executable increments rather than requiring a final selection first.
- **Superseded selection:** claims46/49/53's TCG/QEMU plus Bochs policy is
  withdrawn as a current choice. Their source and interface findings remain
  candidate evidence. Claim52's inventory does not justify a preference or
  postponing performance discrimination until product implementation.
- **Numerical evidence:** claims47–49/53 identify QEMU numerical limitations,
  corrections and Bochs full80/status-capable APIs. Suitable APIs do not establish
  numerical conformance or cost. Claim59 selects bounded cheaper inherited paths
  with strict fallback and lazy C1; the earlier full80 mismatch is a disclosed
  numerical limit. Workload tolerances and full-adapter cost thresholds remain
  synthesis inputs, not a revived unconditional full80 requirement.
- **Reusable state/recovery contracts:** claims43–45 cover scoped FX, XSAVE and
  legacy state images;36–37 cover entry and repair/completion/fault continuations;
  48 covers staged FP effects;50 covers event delivery/return;54 covers gather
  progress and ENTER temporary state. Their independently reviewed scope and
  candidate-specific premises remain in the findings thread. Rebind or recheck
  them when a candidate changes those premises. Claims56–59 and INV03-01 claim20
  supply accepted candidate bindings; final questions2/5 selection is reopened.
- **Retained semantic unknowns:**51's exact MOV-SS/ICEBP DR6 composition,
  55's Intel ordinary-RAM ENTER fault-prefix rule and45's protected14-byte FOP
  field remain unresolved. The existing recovery owner can represent either
  ENTER policy. Further work must identify a product-compatibility or candidate
  decision it changes; these details do not automatically block comparison.
- **AMD/KVM observations:** one retained exploratory invocation completed six
  valid method/control cases. The earlier ENTER frame store survived the later
  final-page fault; the delayed watchpoint bit was absent from the MOV-SS/ICEBP
  Linux report. These are observations of that AMD/KVM/Linux path, not native
  Intel behavior. Native Intel/Haswell acquisition is not the next dependency.
- **Actual emitted-code observations:** all three generators now built and ran
  unchanged extracted bodies in the captive M4 VM, with software and hardware-TSO
  lowering compared in one paired run. All288 admission and756 long output checks
  passed. FEX's live cached-state bodies cost less in this exploratory matrix;
  ordinary bare accesses sit near the loop floor. This does not select a generator
  or establish performance of complete adapters.
- **Boundary sensitivity:** the subsequent612 admission and1260 longer output
  checks pass. FEX remains cheaper with a preserved-root helper; synthetic dense
  canonical/clobber/reload atK=1 reverses that ordering, whileK>=4 retains it.
  Actual ABI requirements and frequencies matter; this is no generator selection.
- **Limits:** complete guard/state adaptation, dispatch/linking,
  compilation/reuse, required FP/status and recovery-root adaptation, and event rates can change
  selection. No stock boot, general SMP result, full adapter conformance or
  end-to-end performance is established. Numerical differences remain an explicit
  cost/compatibility tradeoff.
- **Queued recovery:** INV-03-01 claim19 independently replaces universal ordinary
  instruction polling with retained PAUSED fault records, bounded exits and precise
  cold completion. Existing admitted-route/partial-effect obligations remain; actual
  field/site recipes still require implementation under claims56–59. Special
  debug/event boundaries are retained.
- **Consolidated comparison:** all168 admission and294 longer outputs pass for
  branches, native scalar FP and actual QEMU FP/division helpers. Exact clear-status
  inputs keep QEMU on its software path; FEX's MXCSR read/restore masks drop sticky
  flags. These results do not establish a same-contract FP ranking. The source
  adaptation comparison identifies REP progress and fault-root liveness work.
- **Current accepted construction and evidence:** claims56/57/58/59 independently
  establish bounded native recovery, status-preserving scalar FP, concrete service
  composition and lazy C1. The fresh guarded primary and independent replica each
  pass204 admission and294 timing outputs. FEX wins the tested branches, guarded
  add/sub and mul/div, and restricted DIV; TCG wins SQRT. Full adapter costs and
  workload superiority are not measured.
- **Next action:** bind actual FEX fragments and an early live translation-request
  loop to the Rust resident ABI under the [implementation plan](../../implementation.md).
  Retain the C++ compiler; actual generated ARM64 code/metadata executes in the VM,
  not FEX itself. Existing exports use Linux ARM64; native macOS integration remains
  unestablished and a development worker must disclose its topology and cost. The source comparison and fault-gap
  audit are complete within their scopes. No new comparison campaign is implied.

## Evidence-history reading rule

The current summary and charter inventory direct new work. Detailed claims and
reviews below retain their original evidence, selection language and chronology;
claims46/49/53's former component choices and pass-local next actions are historical.
Consult them for a named finding, not to reconstruct current direction. Technical
acceptance remains scoped to its recorded premises; this correction adds no
runtime evidence or whole-architecture acceptance.

## Charter coverage and evidence inventory

The numbers below refer to the [Track 03 design questions](../tracks/03-architecture-options.md),
not new question IDs. INV-03-01 owns the combined architecture and CPU profile.

| Charter question | Contribution here | Shared requirement versus candidate-specific evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2 — Generator, semantics, helpers | Claims 01–07, 11, 13, 20 | Compare codegen interfaces; semantics/FP sources may be selected independently. Finite matched costs are accepted; claim20 owns the component inference. |
| 3 — Execution/state split | Claims 02–03, 09, 14–17, 20 | Resident addresses, authoritative state and explicit exits are shared requirements; QEMU state layouts, helper chains and exceptional landing derivations are conditional adapters. |
| 5 — Precise faults/recovery | Claims 02, 08–10, 12, 14–19 | Preserve guest state/partial effects and distinguish native repair from guest faults. TCG spill/metadata/poll proofs must be re-derived for another generator. |
| 4 and 6 — Memory/ordering | Claims 08, 10, 15–17, 20 | Exact-once access/result ownership is shared. Extracted QEMU walker paths and selected native lowering are component-specific; INV-03-04 owns memory/coherence composition. |
| 7 and 8 — Code lifetime/events | Claims 10, 12, 14–19 | Pins, legal event boundaries and pending debug ownership are shared. TCG chaining and CPU-loop traces do not transfer unchanged; INV-03-04/03 own lifetime and controller protocols. |
| 1, 10 and 12 — CPU/startup/containment | Claims 01, 03, 06, 14–17, 20 supply dependencies | Full CPU exposure, startup, isolation and resource policy remain in INV-03-01/03/04. Application decoding or helper availability is not acceptance of those behaviors. |

**Portable requirements:** guest semantics, authoritative state, checked guest
accesses, precise effects/recovery, resident helper reachability, event eligibility
and code/reference lifetime apply to every generator. The named three-outcome
interface and nonnesting resident-service protocol are proposed realizations,
not mandatory encodings or calls on the common fast path. Their composition
premises remain conditional.

**Component-specific evidence/proofs:** claims 01, 04–07 identify upstream
facilities and limits. Claims 08–12 and 14–18 trace particular QEMU frontend,
TCG, helper or walker paths; 11/13 diagnose a particular FP implementation.
Their source facts remain useful, but their preservation arguments must be
remapped or re-derived if the responsible component changes. Selecting FEX
codegen does not automatically select or reject a QEMU walker or FP helper.
Claim 19 combines architectural debug rules with a still-unresolved fusion case.
Claim 20 compares interfaces and identifies missing evidence; it accepts neither
ranking nor extraction feasibility.

**Cleanup disposition:** the earlier inference that QEMU system coverage made
its generator the preferred choice, the relative Box64 integration-size judgment,
and LLVM deprioritization lacked the required captive-runtime comparison. Those
selection inferences are withdrawn below; their cited source observations and
claim IDs remain. Findings reviews retain their original scope and chronology;
reviewed source facts or conditional adapters do not by themselves establish
a generator selection or acceptance of the combined architecture. Later scoped
choices retain the acceptance and limits stated in the current summary. No source audit or runtime result was added by this cleanup.

## Assumptions and breadth

The [Track 03 charter](../tracks/03-architecture-options.md) requires system semantics, precise exceptions, recoverable flags/FP/vector state, startup32, SMP, and interaction with memory/code lifetime and containment. AVX2 is a ceiling; exposing AVX/XSAVE requires their whole state contract. Nothing here removes baseline SSE2 or x87 behavior by hiding AVX. Real mode remains excluded; the required 32-bit protected/compatibility-mode transition remains included. User-mode 32-bit decoding is not evidence for that transition.

The [candidate notes](../notes/architecture-candidates.md) seeded alternatives only. In particular, neither “direct memory removes the system adaptation” nor “helper-heavy code forces translation inside the VM” is accepted. System checks still include CPL, segmentation, control registers, exception priority, MMIO and instruction partial effects. A host code generator can emit calls to guest-resident helpers when targets and ABI are explicitly relocated; that is an unimplemented adaptation, not an intrinsic prohibition.

Current blockers are owned here for translator/recovery/FP and in INV-03-01 for their combined architectural consequence. Memory/atomicity, code lifetime, guest isolation, event delivery and device writers are coupled obligations there. Performance estimates belong to Q-04 questions; the effort stop rule remains Q-06-06. Retained unknowns include end-to-end cost, Apple feature-specific emitter opportunities, M2 differences and workload sensitivity. No performance claim follows from Linux-user application compatibility or upstream marketing.

## Source identity and method

This bounded source-only exploration inspected upstream repository trees and the files cited below. Public GitHub API commit identities were resolved, then source retrieved at immutable revisions:

| Project | Inspected revision | Revision timestamp (UTC) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| QEMU | `c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294` | 2026-09-19 20:41:53 |
| FEX | `48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825` | 2026-09-20 00:24:34 |
| Box64/Box32 | `454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647` | 2026-09-20 03:13:17 |

UTC September 20 remains September 19 locally. These are inspected commits, not claims about latest releases. LLVM's official ORCv2 and Statepoints documentation was read as served on the audit date; no LLVM backend implementation was audited and no version-specific feasibility claim is made. Early web inspection of QEMU v10.0.0 and Box64 v0.3.8 was superseded by the pinned current source for findings below. Failed FEX tag URL lookups yielded no evidence. Read-only downloads under `/tmp/natemu-translator-audit` were convenience copies, not experiment runs or required retained artifacts; immutable upstream citations identify the evidence.

The breadth audit followed frontend mode checks → memory IR → helper calls → recovery metadata → FP/state restoration, and sought contrary evidence in configuration controls, explicit source TODOs and user-mode limitations. It did not enumerate all helpers or instructions. That omission is a material limit, not evidence that uninspected paths are correct.

## Supporting contracts and comparison

### CLM-03-02-01 — QEMU system-mode semantics are an inspected component source

**Source-backed:** QEMU's system frontend tracks CPL, IOPL, mode/control flags, segment bases and memory index. Its `CONFIG_USER_ONLY` branches replace several of these with constants and stub system helpers. `i386_tr_insn_start` records guest PC and condition-code operation. [Pinned `target/i386/tcg/translate.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/translate.c), symbols `DisasContext`, `CPL`, `CODE64`, `STUB_HELPER`, `i386_tr_insn_start`.

**Candidate-only inference, not an endorsed generator selection:** QEMU's system frontend is a source of relevant semantics, with greater system coverage than its user-only configuration. This does not establish that importing that frontend or selecting TCG is the best fit for the already-fixed captive runtime. It already represents the modes needed for startup32. Preserve the CPU-state/feature dependencies in the block key and invalidate or redispatch when they change. That is an adaptation direction, not proof that a specific modern Linux startup instruction stream is supported after porting.

**Source-backed:** `seg_helper.c` supplies protected-mode and long-mode interrupt/return/descriptor machinery, with CPU-state and memory-access dependencies. [Pinned segmentation helpers](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/seg_helper.c).

**Inferred:** extracting these helpers requires preserving their memory access and exception contracts; a C function over `CPUX86State` is not automatically freestanding EL1 code. The source dependency graph must determine which helpers become in-VM runtime, shim services or explicit host requests.

### CLM-03-02-02 — Recovery is a producer/runtime contract, not just a PC map

**Source-backed:** QEMU `cpu_restore_state_from_tb` decodes instruction metadata then delegates to the target's `restore_state_to_opc`; `cpu_restore_state` requires a PC in the generated code buffer and explicitly documents helper calls without `GETPC()` as a case it cannot resolve. [Pinned `accel/tcg/translate-all.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/translate-all.c). TCG helper-call documentation describes global-state reads/writes and the flags controlling them. [Pinned TCG operation contract](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/docs/devel/tcg-ops.rst), “Helpers”.

**Inferred:** preserving only the guest RIP/condition-code selector does not prove recovery of arbitrary optimized live vector temporaries, split instructions or host/shim helper faults. The port must retain the relationship between memory spills, helper annotations, fault sites and architectural commit points. Either reconstruct all necessary state at a synchronous fault or constrain lowering so authoritative state is committed at the applicable boundary. For asynchronous events, an architectural boundary reached by a poll is a distinct option from reconstructing arbitrary native-PC state. Polling must still cover bounded long operations and direct-link cycles. Neither option has been established here.

### CLM-03-02-03 — Host generation does not imply host execution of helpers

**Source-backed:** the AArch64 emitter's `tcg_out_call_int` emits an immediate BL when in range, otherwise materializes a target address and BLRs to it. [Pinned AArch64 emitter](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/aarch64/tcg-target.c.inc). Those addresses are runtime addresses, so unchanged host pointers are unsuitable for execution inside a VM.

**Inferred:** separate compilation/optimization on the host from execution of generated code and helpers inside the VM. Reuse pure arithmetic/SoftFloat helpers in the chosen guest execution context; route privileged work through a checked shim ABI. A helper executed at EL1 must not treat an unchecked guest operand as a private pointer. Host-backed services require explicit marshalling, ownership and commit points rather than cross-domain C calls. Literal pools, helper targets, dispatcher exits, CPU-state bases, return-PC recovery and branch ranges all require relocation or an agreed fixed layout. This is the concrete outstanding adaptation, not a requirement to move the translator itself into the VM.

### CLM-03-02-04 — QEMU FP inheritance includes known behavior differences

**Source-backed:** `fpu_helper.c` uses `floatx80`/SoftFloat and translates x87 precision/rounding control. `cpu_init_fp_statuses` still marks the selected SSE two-NaN propagation rule as incorrect against the referenced x86 rule. The same inspected revision now sets FTZ detection after rounding; the earlier v10.0.0 before-rounding TODO must not be carried forward as a current defect. [Pinned FP helpers](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c), `update_fp_status`, `cpu_init_fp_statuses`.

**Inferred:** “inherit QEMU FP” is a precise implementation direction but not “bit-exact x86 FP.” Resolve actual exposed instruction behavior and workload requirements, including NaNs, denormals, rounding modes, exception masks/status and save/restore. The known difference must be accepted as an explicit compatibility policy or corrected through the inherited implementation and independently validated; this audit does neither. A reduced ISA ceiling alone cannot remove SSE2's required behavior. Hardware x86/specification cases should independently arbitrate disputed results; QEMU-versus-a-QEMU-derived-port is useful for regression but is not an independent oracle.

### CLM-03-02-05 — Inspected FEX codegen, state and FP mechanisms

**Source-backed:** FEX describes itself as an x86/x86-64 Linux application emulator; its source structure separates core generation from Linux emulation. [Readme](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/Readme.md), [source outline](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/docs/SourceOutline.md). `JIT.cpp` emits packed native-PC/guest-RIP correspondence in its code tail. [Pinned JIT](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/JIT.cpp). Linux signal-frame code reconstructs XMM/YMM and x87-related state and handles both x64 and ia32 frames. [Pinned frame management](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/Source/Tools/LinuxEmulation/LinuxSyscalls/SignalDelegator/GuestFramesManagement.cpp).

**Source-backed contrary evidence:** in `SetupFrame_x64`, the same frame-management source comments that guest faults cannot reconstruct state to a real guest RIP. This comment and the JIT PC map must be read together: PC identification is not proof of complete architectural-state recovery. Its exact affected fault paths require further tracing; this is not a blanket claim that all FEX faults are imprecise.

**Inferred:** existing signal reconstruction is valuable evidence of machinery, not proof that all instruction partial states can be recovered into an EL1 x86 exception model. Privileged semantics, shadow faults, physical-page code identity and system instruction transitions still need a defined implementation and independent audit. Combining FEX with QEMU helpers also requires reconciling two CPU-state/flags/FP representations; it is not a drop-in helper library substitution.

**Source-backed:** FEX's `X87ReducedPrecision` option defaults false; its 64-bit reduced mode explicitly sacrifices accuracy. [Pinned configuration](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Config/Config.json.in). `F80Fallbacks.h` maps x87 rounding/precision into SoftFloat; its `HandleX87Exception` records invalid-operation status but does not in that function propagate every x87 exception flag. [Pinned F80 fallback](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Interpreter/Fallbacks/F80Fallbacks.h).

**Inferred:** do not reject FEX by claiming it always uses binary64 x87; that is contradicted by the audited source. Conversely, extended precision availability does not establish full x87 exception behavior. Trace other paths before making a global absence claim. FEX's configurable vector/memcpy TSO policies also require re-evaluation under Apple TSO: that control does not make multi-instruction accesses atomic.

### CLM-03-02-06 — Box64/Box32 has useful recovery machinery but application-oriented defaults

**Source-backed:** Box64 describes library-call wrapping and directs 32-bit binaries to Box86/Box32. [Pinned README](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/README.md). Its options document fast NaN/rounding, CALL/RET flag assumptions, selectable memory ordering, x87 precision modes and removable recovery metadata. `NOARCH=0` retains x87/MMX/SSE/AVX tracking. [Pinned usage](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/docs/USAGE.md), `FASTNAN`, `FASTROUND`, `SAFEFLAGS`, `STRONGMEM`, `X87DOUBLE`, `X87_NO80BITS`, `NOARCH`. Signal code calls `adjustregs` and handles saved vector/x87 state. [Pinned signals](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/libtools/signals.c).

**Inferred:** conservative settings are necessary inputs to investigation, not proof of correctness. User library wrapping and a 32-bit application path do not provide protected-mode kernel state transitions or system exception delivery. The earlier claim of a larger integration surface than QEMU is withdrawn: no matched captive-runtime interface inventory established that comparison. Box64 remains unranked; application defaults do not establish either adaptation difficulty or suitability. Its interpreter/cosimulation can catch internal regressions but shared ancestry prevents treating it alone as an independent semantic oracle.

### CLM-03-02-07 — LLVM or a new minimal translator does not eliminate semantic work

**Source-backed:** LLVM ORCv2 provides JIT compilation/linking and resource management; Statepoints provide compiler cooperation for garbage-collection safepoints. [ORCv2](https://llvm.org/docs/ORCv2.html), [Statepoints](https://llvm.org/docs/Statepoints.html).

**Inferred:** those contracts do not define x86 decoding, privileged state, instruction fault priority, partial writes or exact x87 semantics. A custom frontend must supply them and ensure optimization cannot move architectural effects across required boundaries. The inspected ORCv2/Statepoints contracts do not determine the cost of supplying those semantics or an adaptation ranking. The earlier relative-effort inference and deprioritization are withdrawn. No LLVM compilation-latency, generated-code performance or full-scope feasibility conclusion is established.

## Provisional codegen tradeoff and falsifiers

**September 20 reaffirmation of the existing owner boundary:** host translation plus captive in-VM shims is the enclosing architecture; QEMU TCG, FEX and Box64 are codegen choices within it, not alternative end-to-end solutions. Earlier QEMU-first audit ordering records where source investigation began; it is not a selection criterion. Semantic reference coverage, emitter suitability and runtime fit are separate decisions. The comparison below supersedes any implication that system-mode QEMU can be fully translated into the new execution model without replacing its coupled runtime assumptions. Tracks 00/01 already fix this enclosing direction and leave codegen choice later; the reminder corrects this investigation's weighting drift, not a new architecture decision or evidence finding.

| Code-generation/component option inside the fixed architecture | Reusable evidence/components | Required adaptation/interface | Decision consequence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Adapted host TCG generator | Decode/state/helper semantics and recovery mechanisms as references | Replace softMMU, host loop/exits, exclusion and helper access assumptions; establish resident shim ABI and recovery | Audited candidate, not a preferred bundle solely because system coverage exists |
| FEX-derived host generator | FEX AArch64 generation; optional selected semantics/helpers assessed separately | Explicit IR/state bridge, privileged semantics, system memory/fault/atomicity, recovery and resident shim ABI | Material codegen alternative; selected semantics/helper reuse is a separate component decision |
| Box64/Box32-derived generator | Dynarec, interpreter, application recovery | State/helper/memory/recovery interfaces and required guest behavior independent of application defaults | Unranked; extraction inventory is less complete, not evidence of worse suitability |
| LLVM/new generator | Generic optimizing backend or new emitter | Selected semantics frontend plus exact recovery/commit interface; generic JIT facilities alone do not supply these | Unranked; inspected documentation does not establish comparative adaptation cost |
| Interpreter execution fallback within the same runtime | Existing interpreter if appropriately reusable | State bridge and identical kernel/userspace memory, FP, atomics, event semantics | Retained fallback; kernel-time fraction is workload-dependent and unmeasured |

**Hypotheses, not results:** removing software-TLB lookup from suitable ordinary-memory paths might reduce steady-state cost, while shadow faults, host translation requests, spills, helper transitions and synchronization might erase the benefit. QEMU system coverage does not establish semantic portability or reduced adaptation effort once its memory, helper, synchronization and recovery contracts change. FEX composition likewise needs an explicit bridge rather than an assumed reuse advantage. Do not rank performance without matched-profile, matched-workload measurements qualified under Tracks 04/05.

For any generator, reject a proposed adaptation that cannot satisfy the required state/effect/recovery interface. Compare adaptation and execution cost only under the same guest profile and captive-runtime contract; the present source inventory does not establish a cost winner. Falsify any proposed recovery scheme on a missing state component, wrong exception/partial effect, MMIO replay or stale code/metadata lifetime.

**Historical work order:** the original helper-family audit plan led to Supplements A–K. It is completed or superseded as recorded there, not an additional current next action. The current summary owns the sole next bounded decision action.
