## Evidence limits and review provenance

No generator/guest runtime finding, benchmark, timing qualification or implementation-conformance acceptance is asserted. The failed existing-service exploration and static preparation checks are explicitly separated from decision evidence. Source presence establishes mechanisms and dependencies; it does not prove complete ISA coverage or their composition after adaptation. Track 01's handwritten-block recovery findings cannot establish translator-produced flags/vector recovery. The architecture record must keep those uncertainties visible.

Self-check: citations name immutable upstream commits for audited implementation claims; older-source contradictions were rechecked against current pinned source; assumptions and inference are distinguished from source facts. Authored changes now include this record and the disposable `experiments/track03/codegen-checkpoint` artifacts in claims 30–31. The linked findings review records the current scoped dispositions listed in the summary, including static-preparation acceptance for exploratory claim 31 in Return 21; it does not accept component selection, emitted maps or portability of candidate-specific proofs.


## Supplement A — bounded helper/recovery source audit (scoped findings accepted)

**Historical pass record:** the status, stop and next-action wording within this supplement describe that pass. Later supplements and reviews may supersede them; only the Current summary above states the current conclusion and work order.

Authorized source-only next step, September 19, 2026, author `/root/translator`.
Budget: at most 60 minutes of source inspection; no executable experiment or
implementation. Question: can the QEMU system frontend keep its precise-state
contract when compilation stays on the host and generated code/pure helpers run
in the VM, with privileged helpers reached through a shim gateway? Proposed
invariant: every fault/exit point identifies the correct x86 instruction and
recovers all architecturally committed state without replaying committed effects;
helper pointers and memory accesses obey the execution-domain ABI. Trace scalar
load, deferred flags, vector memory, x87, REP partial progress and startup32,
using an inventory of reachable helper families as a scope check. Stop at the
first unsatisfied invariant with a source counterexample and smallest remedy,
or submit a scoped argument; do not claim full dependency closure from samples.


### CLM-03-02-08 — first counterexample: replacing guest-memory IR with ordinary loads loses the recovery contract

**Source-backed:** at the pinned QEMU revision, `include/tcg/tcg-opc.h` marks
`qemu_ld`, `qemu_st` and their two-result variants with
`TCG_OPF_SIDE_EFFECTS | TCG_OPF_CALL_CLOBBER`. Ordinary `ld`/`st` do not carry
those flags. `tcg/tcg.c` explicitly synchronizes global state before an op with
`TCG_OPF_SIDE_EFFECTS`, because that op may trigger an exception; liveness also
uses the distinction. `target/i386/tcg/tcg-cpu.c::x86_restore_state_to_opc`
restores instruction PC and deferred condition-code selector, not arbitrary
GPR/vector native-register contents.

Direct sources: [operation flags](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/include/tcg/tcg-opc.h),
[global synchronization and liveness](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/tcg.c),
[x86 recovery callback](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/tcg-cpu.c).

**Inferred counterexample schema:** consider `mov rax,1; mov rdx,[rbx]; mov
rax,2` with `[rbx]` faulting and RDX subsequently live. At the fault, x86 RAX
must be 1. Replacing the guest-memory operation with ordinary TCG `ld` removes
the intervening architectural-state synchronization obligation; the first RAX
assignment can be dead across the later overwrite, or remain only in a native
register. The x86 restore callback has no general register-location map that
repairs that omission. A PC-only map cannot supply the missing value. This is a
source-derived failure of the proposed transformation's preservation argument,
not an observed emitted-code counterexample or a claim that unchanged QEMU
has this bug. It applies to **replacing guest-memory IR with ordinary memory
IR**, not to retaining `qemu_ld/st` semantics and changing their backend lowering.

**Smallest remedy / revised direction:** retain QEMU guest-memory IR, side-effect
and helper flags, `MemOp` alignment/atomicity/endianness information, and
instruction-start metadata. Adapt the backend memory expansion to use the
selected shadow-mapped direct fast path while retaining a fault/slow-path ABI.
Do not express faultable guest accesses as non-faultable CPU-state loads. Keep
upstream synchronization first; spill removal is a later change requiring a
replacement recovery proof and its own cost evidence. The remedy removes this
specific source-level loss but is not proof of the resulting AArch64 access
sequence, split-access semantics or asynchronous event delivery.

The audit **stopped its preservation claim at this first unsatisfied invariant**,
as budgeted. It did not use the remaining budget to declare whole-helper closure.
The initial blanket “delete the software MMU” transformation is narrowed to
“preserve guest-memory semantics and change the backend's execution path.”

### Helper family inventory and sampled paths

This is an adaptation inventory derived from the pinned
[`target/i386/helper.h`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/helper.h)
and the source paths below, **not** an exhaustive reachable-helper list. Exact
reachability depends on the unresolved CPU profile. Proposed domains and remedies
are hypotheses pending review.

| Family / sampled path | Source contract found | Proposed execution and unresolved work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Scalar memory and deferred flags | `gen_op_ld_v` emits `qemu_ld`; GPR/deferred operands are globals; `i386_tr_insn_start` plus `x86_restore_state_to_opc` restores PC/CC selector | Generated code in VM; preserve global synchronization and use recovery metadata keyed to VM execution addresses. The first counterexample above applies if the IR is weakened. |
| Pure condition-code/integer helpers | `cc_compute_*` have no-global/no-side-effect annotations; divide helpers mutate state and may raise exceptions | Relocatable in-VM arithmetic runtime for pure calls; faulting divide needs the exception gateway, not the pure-call classification. Audit call annotations individually. |
| SIMD memory/state | `gen_load_sse` selects load widths. `gen_ldy_env_A0` performs both 128-bit reads into temporaries before storing either half of YMM to CPU state; `gen_sty_env_A0` instead emits two stores. Alignment and `MO_ATOM_*` requirements are explicit | Retain this ordering and state layout; do not commit the low destination half before a high-half load fault. Do not infer whole-instruction store atomicity or MMIO replay safety from the load pattern. Native asynchronous interruption needs boundary deferral or full temporary-state handling. |
| x87 data and arithmetic | `helper_fldt_ST0` calls `access_prepare` for 10 bytes before updating stack/top/tag; `helper_fstt_ST0` also calls it. `access_prepare_mmu` probes both pages and records `mmu_idx`/return PC; actual accesses have RAM and slow paths | SoftFloat arithmetic can remain inherited in-VM code, but memory-touching x87 helpers are not pure arithmetic. Their checked guest-memory API must preserve privilege/MMIO and remapping behavior. Never replace it with an unchecked EL1 cast to guest address. |
| REP/string partial progress | `gen_movs` loads then stores before changing RSI/RDI. `do_gen_rep` commits count and condition-code state per completed iteration, uses `CC_OP_DYNAMIC`, and bounds an internal loop with `REP_MAX` before redispatching the same instruction | Preserve partial progress and RF/interrupt-shadow behavior; use an event-aware dispatcher even on reentry/direct links. The source's bound is not a chosen latency guarantee. |
| Segmentation/control/startup32 | Mode checks select 32/64-bit addressing; far control-transfer and control-register helpers retain descriptor/state logic | Privileged shim gateway plus checked guest-memory accessor; changing CR0/CR3/CR4 and segment state must reselect compatible blocks/mappings before continuing. Actual inherited Ubuntu/Debian trampoline paths still need this complete trace. |
| Extended-state save/restore | `fxsave`, `xsave`, `xsaveopt`, restore families are explicit helper entry points | Enable only with coherent CPUID/control/state format. Destination guest memory, partial effects and guest fault priority require separate access audit; no closure claimed. |
| I/O, clocks, interrupts, halt, exception delivery | Port-I/O, RDTSC/MSR, HLT and exception helper families are distinct | Validated shim/host service requests with commit identity. No host pointer calls from VM; no return through the host runtime's exception stack. |
| Atomics, optional virtualization/debug/features | Source families exist but feature profile controls reachability | Must inventory enabled atomics and privileged paths; hardware TSO does not replace width/alignment atomicity contracts. Disabled optional instructions must still reject correctly. |

Sampled sources: [frontend memory, flags, REP and vector expansions](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/translate.c),
[`emit.c.inc::gen_load_sse`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc),
[x87 helper access order](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c),
[`access.c::access_prepare_mmu` and accessors](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/access.c).

### CLM-03-02-09 — exception exits need an in-VM continuation contract

**Source-backed:** `raise_interrupt2` sets exception information and invokes
`cpu_loop_exit_restore`; `cpu_loop_exit` ultimately invokes `siglongjmp` on the
CPU runtime's jump context. Triple-fault handling also calls system reset
machinery. [Exception helpers](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/excp_helper.c),
[CPU loop exit](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/cpu-exec-common.c).

**Inferred:** retaining helper source is insufficient if it expects a host C
runtime jump environment while executing in the VM. Provide an in-VM exceptional
continuation that saves the same exception data, resolves the caller's execution
PC/CC metadata, abandons the helper frame and transfers to shim dispatch/delivery.
Helper nesting must retain the originating translated instruction identity;
PC-relative block metadata must use the correct code address domain. This may
reuse a guest-local runtime or replace the exit boundary; it must never jump to a
host stack address. No claim that `siglongjmp` itself cannot be implemented in a
VM is made. The missing item is a concrete valid continuation, not the function
name.

### Supplement disposition and bounded next action

**Result:** a specific unsafe transformation is identified and a narrower
TCG-specific lowering remedy proposed. The earlier QEMU-first selection inference is withdrawn; the source counterexample remains. No generator relocation implementation, FP
compatibility resolution, complete helper closure or architectural acceptance
results. The audit used source only, no guest execution; six families were
sampled but the first failed invariant terminated any claim of preservation.

Historical next action (superseded): independent review of CLM-03-02-08/09 and the revised lowering/domain
requirements, bounded to 45 minutes, stop with accept/return. Then one bounded
source protocol for the revised direct-access fault ABI must address register
synchronization, helper exceptional continuation and shadow-only repair versus
architectural guest fault; these are the remaining selection blockers. A later
experiment needs its own budget and reviewed method. Do not mark this supplement
accepted merely because its proposed remedy sounds plausible.


## Supplement B — revised direct-access and helper ABI (sampled source argument accepted; composition blocked)

**Historical pass record:** the status, stop and next-action wording within this supplement describe that pass. Later supplements and reviews may supersede them; only the Current summary above states the current conclusion and work order.

After Supplement A's first-counterexample stop, a further bounded source pass
was authorized within the original 60-minute aggregate budget to examine its
smallest remedy. No patch, build or experimental run. This pass considers the
combined record's guarded-EL1 candidate: generated code and native helpers run
at EL1; x86 CPL remains software state; a private virtual range is unavailable
to every guest-derived memory/control operand. EL0 translated execution with
EL1 gates remains an alternative. The choice of privilege does not change TCG's
state-synchronization requirements.

### Proposed access/exception ABI

1. **Compile:** retain system-mode frontend state, `qemu_ld/st` IR and all
   relevant operation/helper annotations. Compile from validated guest bytes and
   a matching CPU-state key on the host. Relocate native pointers/code metadata
   into the VM execution address domain; reject unknown relocation kinds.
2. **Emit access:** let unchanged TCG liveness/register allocation synchronize
   architectural globals before the guest-memory op. Its backend expansion first
   checks mode-specific guest address formation, segmentation/canonicality where
   applicable, private-range exclusion and applicable operation
   requirements, then issues an ordinary shadow-mapped RAM access only for the
   approved fast case. A guard failure uses an explicit guest-exception/slow
   service; it must not occur in a non-faultable ordinary `ld` IR operation.
   This placement keeps the guard inside the same exception-capable operation
   whose globals are synchronized.
3. **Capture native fault:** the shim saves the complete native continuation
   needed to resume the interrupted AArch64 instruction, including live GPRs,
   SIMD/FP registers and controls, native status, SP and PC, before using them as
   temporaries. An internal shadow repair preserves that native state and retries
   the faulting native instruction. It does not reconstruct x86 state or reissue
   an already committed earlier operation.
4. **Deliver guest fault:** when the access authority selects a real x86 fault,
   abandon the native block/helper continuation. Recover PC/CC selector with the
   original instruction metadata while retaining synchronized architectural
   state and instruction-specific partial progress. Use a VM-local exceptional
   continuation, never the host CPU loop's jump environment. A helper reports its
   originating translated-call PC and phase explicitly if its native return PC
   alone cannot identify that guest instruction.
5. **Events:** native interrupts may capture/resume internal execution but do not
   inject x86 asynchronous events mid-instruction or halfway through helper state
   changes. Record pending events and inject at a declared committed dispatcher
   or bounded string-iteration boundary. Direct links and wait/park paths must
   participate in the same event/rendezvous protocol. Deferral requires bounded
   progress or a separately justified internal safe point; it is not permission
   for uninterruptible infinite helpers.
6. **Helpers:** pure runtime operations use only typed private pointers/constants.
   Every guest-derived address, including descriptor/TSS/stack/x87/state-image
   memory, goes through the checked access authority. `access_prepare`/accessor
   calls preserve MMU index, width, fault origin and RAM/MMIO distinction; an EL1
   C cast is not a substitute. Helpers may not write guest RAM through private
   host aliases outside the all-writer coherence protocol. Helper control flow
   uses relocated trusted targets; x86 indirect targets return to checked guest
   dispatch, never become native branch addresses directly.

These clauses are **a proposed interface**, not established implementation
properties. In particular, a helper parameter being stored in `CPUX86State`
does not classify it as trusted. The operation partition must trace every use
that becomes an address or native control target; a guest-controlled segment
base remains guest-derived. Private-memory guards are an additional requirement
of the guarded-EL1 candidate and have not been proved complete here.

### CLM-03-02-10 — limited preservation argument for the sampled scalar/vector path

**Inferred from the pinned contracts in Supplement A:** retaining `qemu_ld`
through liveness and register allocation retains the synchronization obligation
that the rejected IR replacement lost. An inserted backend range guard is after
that synchronization and before the guest access. Thus the sampled scalar
`mov rax,1; mov rdx,[rbx]` can recover its pre-load GPR state from the canonical
CPU-state store and its PC/CC selector from metadata, provided the backend has
not committed RDX before the access succeeds. The unchanged frontend satisfies
that abstract ordering; the eventual native lowering must preserve it.

For the sampled YMM load, the source deliberately stores neither half to the
architectural destination until both memory reads succeed. If the second read
faults, a shadow repair must resume with the first temporary half preserved in
the saved native continuation; an architectural guest fault discards both
uncommitted temporaries. No vector-register reconstruction into architectural
YMM is needed for those particular uncommitted halves. This does **not** justify
eagerly saving the first half to CPU state, nor restarting the whole instruction
if its earlier read was side-effecting MMIO. Mixed RAM/MMIO, splitting and
replay must use an explicit slow-path partial-effect contract before this
argument covers those cases.

This is a source argument for retaining two upstream ordering invariants, not
proof that the new backend, fault handler or helper ABI exists or handles every
instruction. Scalar boundary-crossing accesses, locked operations, feature-wide
FP/XSAVE, gather-like partial completion and faults during x86 exception delivery
remain outside this sampled argument. The guarded-EL1 address partition and
INV-03-04's admission/park interactions remain selection blockers. A concrete
composition counterexample is a YMM second-load shadow fault with a live first
half temporary: if repair waits for the global coordinator, while the coordinator
waits for canonical park from this vCPU, both wait indefinitely. Full native
context capture is not canonical park. The design needs either a retained-state
quiescence phase with lifetime pins and a defined resume/abort service, or a drain
repair path that runs before canonical-park completion. Until that protocol is
reviewed, this access ABI cannot claim composition with global rendezvous.

**Historical next action (superseded):** independent findings review of this interface and its limited
argument, together with Supplement A, bounded to 45 minutes; stop with a scoped
accept/return and concrete counterexample if any. Then resolve one actual
slow-access/partial-effect protocol against the all-writer/rendezvous design,
without extending the claim to uninspected helpers.


## Supplement C — concrete inherited SSE NaN behavior (scoped finding accepted)

**Historical pass record:** the status, stop and next-action wording within this supplement describe that pass. Later supplements and reviews may supersede them; only the Current summary above states the current conclusion and work order.

### CLM-03-02-11 — source-derived ADDSD payload mismatch, not merely a TODO

**Source-backed path, same pinned QEMU revision:** `decode-new.c.inc` maps
opcode `0x58` to `VADD` with legacy F2/F3/66/no-prefix forms;
`emit.c.inc::FP_SSE(VADD, add)` and `gen_fp_sse` select `gen_helper_addsd` for
F2. `ops_sse.h::SSE_HELPER_S(add, FPU_ADD)` implements that helper using
`float64_add(v->ZMM_D(0), s->ZMM_D(0), &env->sse_status)`. Neither that helper nor
`update_mxcsr_status` overrides the two-NaN propagation rule initialized by
`cpu_init_fp_statuses` to `float_2nan_prop_x87`.

In SoftFloat, `float64_add` → `float64_addsub` → `float64_gen2` rejects NaNs from
its hardware fast path using `f64_is_zon2`, then takes `soft_f64_addsub` →
`parts64_addsub` → `parts64_pick_nan`. The selected x87 rule compares fractions
and returns the larger QNaN when both inputs are quiet NaNs. This establishes
that native host NaN selection does not override the sampled path.

Direct source anchors: [decode table](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/decode-new.c.inc),
[generator dispatch](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc),
[scalar helper expansion](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/ops_sse.h),
[status initialization/update](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c),
[SoftFloat dispatch](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/fpu/softfloat.c),
[NaN selector](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/fpu/softfloat-parts.c.inc).

**Source-backed specification:** Intel SDM Volume 1, **253665-078US, December
2022**, §4.8.3.5 Table 4-7 specifies the first source operand for SSE arithmetic
on two QNaNs, versus the larger significand for x87. The footnote identifies the
legacy SSE first source as generally the operand that becomes the destination.
[Official Intel Volume 1 PDF](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/671436/253665-sdm-vol-1.pdf).
This is the inspected revision; it is not mislabeled revision 092. No contrary
newer specification was inspected. The rule is stronger than merely producing
some quiet NaN.

**Inferred exact case:** with ordinary initialized SSE status/MXCSR (invalid
operation masked), execute legacy `ADDSD xmm0,xmm1` where low bits of XMM0 are
`0x7ff8000000000001` and low bits of XMM1 are `0x7ff8000000000002`. Both are
positive quiet binary64 NaNs. The inspected helper/SoftFloat path selects
`0x7ff8000000000002`; Table 4-7 selects `0x7ff8000000000001`. Upper XMM state is
not the disputed behavior. No runtime test or hardware observation was made;
this is an independently reviewable source derivation for one instruction/input
pair. It does not establish all FP differences or an unmasked-exception defect.

**Proposed reconciliation:** retain inherited SoftFloat arithmetic and select its
existing first-NaN rule for SSE status at the integration boundary, subject to an
independent audit of affected instruction families and tests in the later
implementation. This would adapt existing FP machinery, not introduce a custom
floating-point engine. It must not change x87's separate rule, FMA's operand-order
contract or instruction-specific min/max selection. Until that change's required
scope is established, selection remains provisional. If the project instead
wants QEMU-compatible NaN payload behavior as its declared guest contract, that
is an explicit compatibility decision; this document does not silently waive
Intel's rule or ask the owner to approve an unspecified FP deviation.

The concrete next technical question is whether selecting the existing SSE rule
is sufficient for all affected enabled helpers or whether other instruction
paths need distinct rules. That is smaller than treating all inherited FP as
unknown, but it does not eliminate the separate rounding, exception and
save/restore obligations.


## Supplement D — direct-link event boundary (scoped finding accepted)

**Historical pass record:** the status, stop and next-action wording within this supplement describe that pass. Later supplements and reviews may supersede them; only the Current summary above states the current conclusion and work order.

### CLM-03-02-12 — block-entry polling must restore the entry PC deliberately

Source-only inspection in the same bounded follow-up, no implementation.
**Source-backed:** TCG `goto_tb` and `exit_tb` carry `BB_EXIT|BB_END`; TCG
liveness and `tcg_reg_alloc_bb_end` require architectural globals in memory at
that edge. But x86 `gen_jmp_rel` deliberately emits `tcg_gen_goto_tb` before the
fallthrough `cpu_eip = new_eip` for a non-`CF_PCREL` block. A patched direct link
skips that store. `x86_cpu_synchronize_from_tb` specifically repairs EIP from the
block's PC/CS base unless `CF_PCREL` is enabled; in that mode the frontend updates
EIP before the edge. The general global-store invariant does not mean the stored
EIP already denotes the block now being entered.

[Edge construction and `gen_eob`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/translate.c),
[TCG edge flags](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/include/tcg/tcg-opc.h),
[global liveness/register allocation](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/tcg.c),
[`x86_cpu_synchronize_from_tb`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/tcg-cpu.c).

**Inferred minimal direction:** a target-entry event/rendezvous poll reached by
all direct and indirect links can inspect committed globals, but its slow path
must materialize the target block's architectural PC using the preserved entry
identity/mode/CS base, or inherit equivalent PC-relative handling. The poll must
precede any new x86 instruction effect and must not be bypassed by direct-link
patching. Native interrupt arrival merely sets pending state; actual x86 event
delivery occurs through that slow path after eligibility checks. Applying the
existing target-state synchronization concept is smaller than attempting
arbitrary mid-instruction asynchronous deoptimization.

`gen_eob` also handles interrupt-shadow clearing/setting, RF and single-step
logic; it avoids immediate chained lookup when clearing an inherited interrupt
shadow so interrupts get a chance. A new poll must preserve those eligibility
rules and the feature/mode guards. REP's bounded redispatch must reach the poll;
long helpers still need bounded progress or their own reviewed safe points.

This establishes why a naive poll reading only `env.eip` can report the wrong
boundary under chaining. It does not establish the actual poll implementation,
atomic pending-state protocol, all helper safepoints, or rendezvous closure.
No exact maximum event latency is claimed. The prior YMM/native-continuation
versus canonical-park deadlock remains unresolved until the combined protocol
handles retained-state quiescence or drain repair.


## Supplement E — affected SSE NaN-policy families (source audit complete; reviewed)

**Historical pass record:** the status, stop and next-action wording within this supplement describe that pass. Later supplements and reviews may supersede them; only the Current summary above states the current conclusion and work order.

Source-only follow-up authorized by parent research executor, September 19:
maximum 60 minutes, no implementation or execution. Trace the existing
`float_2nan_prop_ab` remedy against arithmetic/helper families reachable from the
conditional Haswell-v2-derived profile. Inputs are the same pinned QEMU sources
and primary Intel instruction contracts. Stop on a contrary special-case or
operand-order path, or a reviewable limited family argument; do not claim that
changing a setting establishes complete FP correctness. Include comparisons,
min/max, horizontal/dot-product operations, unary/conversions and FMA as negative
controls on the scope of the two-operand NaN rule.


### CLM-03-02-13 — A narrow SSE selector change has a bounded family argument, with dot-product latitude

**Accepted scoped source finding and conditional inference in findings-review Return 4.** The
pass reached its limited source-argument stop condition without patching,
compiling or executing QEMU. Inspection covered the pinned target/i386 top-level
C/headers and target/i386/tcg C/headers/includes, followed `sse_status` consumers,
and traced the relevant SoftFloat selectors. This establishes the following
family partition for the existing helper implementation; it does not prove every
decoder path, enabled instruction or FP exception contract.

| Family | Inspected helper behavior | Effect of changing only `sse_status` two-NaN rule to `float_2nan_prop_ab` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ADD/SUB/MUL/DIV, scalar and packed | `SSE_HELPER_S/P` supplies `v` then `s` to float32/64 arithmetic, independently per lane. NaNs take the SoftFloat path rather than the finite host fast path. | Picks the first NaN argument instead of the x87 payload-size rule. Includes the concrete ADDSD discrepancy in claim 11; ordinary non-NaN arithmetic is unchanged by this selector. |
| ADDSUB and HADD/HSUB | ADDSUB supplies `v[i]`, `s[i]`; horizontal helpers supply lower-index lane then adjacent lane, separately within each source and lane group. | Changes two-NaN selection at these binary operations without changing operand grouping or arithmetic order. The emitted source ordering remains a separate requirement for any port. |
| DPPS/DPPD | Enabled products use `v[i] * s[i]`, disabled products become zero; DPPS sums `(p0+p1)+(p2+p3)`, DPPD sums its two products; output mask then selects sum or zero. | Changes selection within products and intermediate sums. A chosen deterministic horizontal result must not be advertised as matching a particular Haswell's NaN payload. |
| MIN/MAX and comparisons | `FPU_MIN/MAX` use comparison and choose the second argument when unordered; CMP/COMI/UCOMI use comparison routines. | Their payload choice is not supplied by the generic two-NaN picker; preserve these special paths. No comparison exception-correctness claim follows. |
| SQRT, rounding and conversions; reciprocal/reciprocal-square-root | Unary/conversion paths have only one NaN input; reciprocal operations divide a constant by the input or its square root. | No competing pair of NaN payloads is introduced. This does not validate approximations, rounding, denormals or conversion exceptions. |
| FMA | The shared FMA helpers call `float32/64_muladd`; its NaN branch uses `pick_nan_muladd`, the separate three-NaN rule and infinity-times-zero policy. | Leave `float_3nan_prop_abc`, special infinity/zero policy and frontend operand permutations unchanged. This is independence from the proposed two-NaN setting, not a fresh proof of FMA semantics. |
| x87 and 3DNow! | Separate `fp_status` and `mmx_status` instances receive their own rule. | Do not change either instance; this proposal supplies no 3DNow! support or x87 correction. |

Source anchors: [SSE helper families, including `FPU_ADD`, `FPU_MIN`,
`SSE_HELPER_HPS`, `helper_dpps`, `helper_dppd`, FMA
helpers](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/ops_sse.h);
[`cpu_init_fp_statuses` and MXCSR status
updates](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c);
[`pick_nan`, `pick_nan_muladd`, arithmetic
paths](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/fpu/softfloat-parts.c.inc);
[finite fast-path guards and float32/64
entrypoints](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/fpu/softfloat.c);
[`FMA_SSE` operand
permutations](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc).

The two-NaN picker raises invalid when either argument is signaling before
selecting the payload; `float_2nan_prop_ab` selects `a` when it is NaN, otherwise
`b`, and the common return path quiets a selected signaling NaN. The change thus
also affects mixed signaling/quiet pairs, not just the two quiet payloads in
claim 11. It must not be approximated as “prefer signaling NaNs”: that is the
different `float_2nan_prop_s_ab` rule. Existing reset/MXCSR handling inspected
here does not override the chosen two-NaN rule later.

The specification boundary matters. The previously inspected Intel Volume 1,
253665-078US (December 2022), Table 4-7 supplies the SSE first-source NaN rule.
Intel Volume 2A, **253666-082US (December 2023)**, DPPD/DPPS descriptions on
printed pages 3-336–3-339 refer to that rule but expressly leave horizontal NaN
priority and positioning implementation-dependent. Their pseudocode specifies
masked products and subsequent summation. Consequently a conforming deterministic
choice does not establish bit-identical behavior to physical Haswell for these
horizontal cases. [Intel Volume 2A inspected
revision](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf).
These are identified inspected revisions, not assertions about the latest SDM.

**Provisional consequence:** retain the existing helper structure and propose
changing only the `sse_status` initialization to its already implemented
`float_2nan_prop_ab` rule. The audit found no need to replace SoftFloat or alter
x87/FMA/min-max policy to repair the sampled ADDSD discrepancy. This is a smaller
candidate than treating every inherited FP difference as an owner waiver. It is
not a patch approval, broad FP compatibility acceptance, or permission to claim
all Haswell payload results. An independent reviewer should check the family
partition, mixed-NaN rule and horizontal limitation before accepting the source
argument. Later implementation validation needs independent expected results
for both operand orders, quiet/signaling combinations, scalar/vector forms and
the excluded special families; comparing only with unmodified QEMU would retain
the known discrepancy. No such tests were performed or authorized here.


## Supplement F — VM-local exceptional continuation ABI (source audit complete; reviewed)

**Historical pass record:** the status, stop and next-action wording within this supplement describe that pass. Later supplements and reviews may supersede them; only the Current summary above states the current conclusion and work order.

Parent-authorized follow-up, September 19: maximum 45 minutes of source work,
no implementation, compilation or guest execution. Seek the smallest replacement
for the host CPU-loop nonlocal exit that preserves scalar/helper guest state and
nested retained native continuations. Trace the same pinned QEMU producer and
helper contracts against INV-03-04's retained-state admission constraints. Stop
on the first unsatisfied invariant with counterexample and smallest remedy, or
on a reviewable sampled argument; do not infer complete helper-family closure.


### CLM-03-02-14 — A permanent VM-local exit landing is sufficient for sampled fault paths only with separate origin and retained-native records

**Accepted scoped conditional ABI argument in findings-review Return 5.** The smallest candidate does not import
host `sigjmp_buf` layout or unwind each C helper. Use a permanent VM-local,
nonreturning exit landing with a dedicated per-vCPU dispatch stack and explicit
exception record. A tiny native entry resets execution to that stack, supplies
the private runtime/CPU-state pointer, and enters the permanent dispatcher as a
fresh nonreturning invocation. It never returns through discarded helper frames
or resumes an abandoned C activation. This is a proposed native ABI, not code
that exists in the inspected source or proof of complete helper porting.

The source makes three separate obligations visible:

1. `cpu_loop_exit_restore` restores instruction state from the supplied translated
   return PC before `cpu_loop_exit`. `cpu_loop_exit` resets `can_do_io` and plugin
   helper state before jumping. The outer `cpu_exec_longjmp_cleanup` separately
   releases translation page locks and the BQL. Replacing only `siglongjmp` would
   omit these responsibilities. A VM runtime must explicitly account for each
   enabled cleanup effect; host compilation/page locks cannot be inherited by
   a VM-local exit. Disabled facilities need an explicit configuration contract.
2. `cpu_restore_state` accepts a generated-code PC and explicitly rejects a
   helper-native PC not represented by GETPC. Preserve an originating translated
   call-site token through nested helper/accessor calls; the native fault PC and
   immediate C return address are not substitutes. The token must identify the
   pinned block generation and the matching recovery offset in the VM address
   domain. Failed resolution must stop the runtime rather than silently deliver
   an exception using stale guest PC/flags.
3. The current x86 exception helper records exception index, error code,
   software-interrupt classification and next EIP, including contributory/double
   exception handling, then exits. Page-walk failure separately records CR2 before
   raising. The replacement must preserve the relevant payload and existing
   fault/trap/instruction-progress classification. Actual exception delivery is
   a subsequent operation; its memory faults must not be confused with nested
   native faults inside the original instruction.

Sources: [exit and restore
entrypoints](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/cpu-exec-common.c),
[CPU-loop cleanup](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/cpu-exec.c),
[generated-code recovery
lookup](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/translate-all.c),
[`raise_interrupt2` and exception
classification](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/excp_helper.c),
[system page-fault
exit](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/excp_helper.c).

**Proposed record lifetime, not a new generic unwinder:** one architectural
operation origin contains block/generation, recovery offset, access intent and
its permitted partial-progress phase. Independently, each resumable native trap
frame contains the full native snapshot and the runtime/mapping/code references
needed to resume that exact frame. A helper's nested C calls preserve the same
architectural origin unless beginning a separately classified operation; a
nested native trap needs its own frame or a proved nonnesting rule. Internal
shadow repair resumes the retained native frame. A real guest fault copies its
payload into permanent per-vCPU storage, recovers required guest state while
metadata is pinned, marks the whole abandoned operation's native frames
nonresumable, and transfers to the permanent landing. Only after no CPU, helper
return, trap return or callback can refer to them may their pins be released.
No return-to-freed-code window may exist between publishing canonical park and
releasing references.

The exit transition itself must run on resident private runtime/control memory,
with native interrupt nesting controlled until the new frame/stack state is
published. This controls native delivery, not x86 IF. An interrupt recorded there
remains pending for the normal architectural boundary. It does not authorize
recursively entering the memory coordinator or dropping an outstanding drain
service obligation. INV-03-04's closed-admission protocol still owns service
completion and publication of canonical park.

**Sampled traces:**

- For the previously inspected scalar `qemu_ld` path, retained IR annotations
  synchronize architectural globals before the access. A shadow-only miss keeps
  the native destination and temporaries untouched through save/repair/retry;
  a true x86 fault uses that operation's pinned metadata, preserves the old guest
  destination and transfers with its fault payload. The landing is independent
  of whether the failing machine instruction was inline or inside an accessor.
- `helper_divl_EAX` reads EDX:EAX and checks zero divisor and quotient overflow
  before writing either destination register. Its default helper declaration
  does not opt out of global-state synchronization. The #DE branch supplies
  GETPC and never returns; replacing its final exit boundary with the proposed
  landing preserves this sampled precommit register state, subject to the origin
  and cleanup requirements above. It does not establish all divide modes.
- Non-null `helper_lldt` reads the descriptor's first two words (and the third
  under LMA) before committing the LDT cache and selector. A fault at its second
  descriptor read therefore has first-word temporary state on the native helper
  stack/registers but no committed replacement LDT. Shadow repair must retain
  that temporary and resume the second read; an architectural fault may abandon
  it and retain the old LDT. Its `cpu_ldl_kernel_ra` calls explicitly carry GETPC.
  The same guest-origin token must survive any additional VM accessor layers.
  This examines the shown descriptor-read fault phase, not every segmentation
  instruction, MMIO descriptor behavior or exception-priority interaction.

[Integer divide
helper](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/int_helper.c),
[helper declarations](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/helper.h),
[`helper_lldt`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/seg_helper.c).

**First unsatisfied invariant / stop:** a single mutable per-vCPU “fault context”
is insufficient. Counterexample: LLDT retains its first descriptor word while
its second read takes a shadow fault; a nested native interrupt or another fault
inside repair overwrites that same snapshot/origin slot. Returning from repair
then restores the wrong PC/SP/temporaries, or an exception decoder receives the
accessor's native PC instead of the translated origin. The old LDT remaining
unchanged does not make this a canonical park. Smallest remedy: explicit separate
architectural-origin storage and a native-frame stack with retained references,
or a proved resident/nonnesting repair regime that makes a single native frame
sufficient. This proposal chooses neither without the runtime operation/cleanup
partition. Frame capacity, overflow behavior and every potentially faulting or
blocking operation inside the exit/repair path remain open. No universal helper
cleanup ledger, nested-fault safety or guarded-EL1 isolation is accepted.

The pass stops with the sampled landing ABI and this concrete nesting/cleanup
closure obligation. No owner compatibility waiver, runtime experiment or broader
helper enumeration is needed to state it. Independent review must precede
acceptance; a later bounded pass can enumerate the permanent repair/exit path's
memory, lock and callback operations to decide whether nonnesting is provable or
stacked retained frames are required.


## Supplement G — resident nonnesting repair/exit regime (protocol audit complete; reviewed)

**Historical pass record:** the status, stop and next-action wording within this supplement describe that pass. Later supplements and reviews may supersede them; only the Current summary above states the current conclusion and work order.

Parent-authorized September 19 follow-up: maximum 30 minutes of source/protocol
work, no runtime or implementation. Enumerate operations actually required by
the sampled fault paths and retained-state coordinator; decide whether permanent
private stacks/control data, checked stable RAM aliases and preallocated metadata
permit a single retained native frame without recursive guest helper execution.
Stop on the first operation that necessarily requires nested resumable faults,
or a reviewable conditional regime. Preserve ordinary guest instructions and
fault semantics; runtime corruption is not a substitute for an expected guest
fault. Compare stacked frames only where an identified operation needs them.


### CLM-03-02-15 — Conditional nonnesting repair boundary and QEMU walker slow-path adaptation

**Proposed direction; scoped source and conditional sampled regime accepted in findings-review Return 6:** use one retained translated/
helper-native continuation per vCPU for this repair boundary, rather than a
recursively growing stack of guest-operation continuations. Keep a separate,
fixed transient native-IRQ save area for resident service code. A native IRQ
while a guest continuation is retained must only record pending work and restore
that resident service; it must not replace the retained guest snapshot or execute
an x86 helper. Mask native IRQ delivery during frame, mapping and exit publication
critical sections, with the IRQ stub itself nonnesting; do not hold native IRQs
masked across coordinator waits or host I/O. This is not a claim that all native
asynchronous exceptions are maskable or recoverable. Unexpected runtime faults
are containment failures, distinct from ordinary architecturally required guest
faults handled as explicit results.

The two fixed roles matter: “one retained continuation” does not mean one shared
register-save slot for both guest execution and an interrupt of its repair code.
The IRQ scratch frame returns only to resident service, never to an older guest
frame. Once that service restores the guest continuation and returns atomically,
a later guest access fault can reuse the vacated retained slot. Sequential faults
within one x86 instruction therefore require neither recursive handlers nor
restricting which valid guest instruction may execute.

#### Concrete resident operation partition

| Required operation | Proposed nonfaulting/resumable implementation boundary | Ordering and ownership requirement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Capture/access classification | Permanently mapped private vector code, per-vCPU control stack, retained snapshot and origin record; no guest-shadow dereference | Publish all native state before declaring PAUSED; preserve origin and helper temporaries separately from the IRQ scratch area. |
| Guest page walk / descriptor or operand backing lookup needed for repair | Checked GPA-to-RAM resolver through stable runtime-only RAM aliases, preserving physical width, device routing, x86 access type and modes | Alias ranges exclude runtime/private IPA and are pinned for the current authority; bounds/permissions failures return a guest result or explicit device request, not a native fault. Guest linear shadow mappings are never used to read their own page tables. |
| Page-table accessed/dirty updates | Atomic RAM update through the same checked alias, or serialized device transaction for non-RAM backing | These are guest-RAM writers even when performed by the walker. They participate in tracked-source invalidation and retain the same coordinator transaction; never acquire a second exclusive epoch. |
| Shadow table creation / repair | Private resident page-table pool and preallocated request/metadata reserve, then prescribed translation maintenance | Reserve exhaustion pauses for host refill while retaining the frame; no allocator that can reenter guest execution, no lock held while waiting for another participant's canonical park. Mapping visibility/maintenance protocol remains separately required. |
| Non-RAM read/write or unavailable backing | Explicit service request carrying access identity, width, value and completion state; host/coordinator supplies the result | Preserve specified device behavior and prior effects, including reads with side effects. Service request IDs/phase prevent duplicate effects across pause/resume. Do not invent #PF or kill a valid instruction because the target is MMIO. |
| Wait for coordinator / service reply | Resident wait state with the retained record immutable, IRQs enabled outside publication, optional host exit | Completion resumes this service state, not a new guest instruction; no recursive helper call or second memory-coordinator entry. |
| Decode recovery metadata / create guest fault | Resident immutable pinned metadata and private CPU-state fields, explicit fault payload, then permanent landing | Recover PC/CC before releasing code pins, retain permitted partial guest effects, complete/transfer service ownership before canonical park. No generic C cleanup is inferred. |
| Finish access service | Consume the explicit `RETRY_ACCESS`, `ACCESS_COMPLETED` or `GUEST_FAULT` result before atomically leaving the repair regime | `RETRY_ACCESS` restores the frame and retries only the still-unperformed access. `ACCESS_COMPLETED` restores declared result/status updates and branches to the emitter/accessor expansion's recorded success continuation without repeating the access. `GUEST_FAULT` takes the canonical exceptional landing and never a success continuation. Retain temporaries and routing references until the selected transition completes. A subsequent access fault begins a new service turn. |

“Resident” here means the VM runtime mappings/backing and control storage are
stable and accessible throughout the service, not that host OS scheduling or
physical page-in can never delay it. Guest RAM aliases cannot bypass the all-
writer protocol: reads require its admitted access authority, writes also require
its mutation/invalidation rules, and remap/reclamation waits for retained
references. Host requests carry checked identifiers/data, not guest-selected
native function pointers. No promise about end-to-end latency follows.

#### Source counterexample and minimal required adaptation

The pinned QEMU walker already distinguishes `TranslateResult` from
`TranslateFault`; many architectural page-walk failures return false and fill
an error record. `x86_cpu_tlb_fill` is the later boundary that updates CR2 and
raises. Thus fault classification need not itself invoke the nonlocal CPU exit.
`PTETranslate` also separates a direct `haddr` path from slow accesses. These
are useful extraction seams, not a freestanding VM walker as-is.

The actual contrary operation is `ptw_setl_slow`: it leaves cpu_exec, calls
`start_exclusive`, reads and conditionally writes the PTE through `_mmuidx_ra`
accessors with return address zero, ends exclusion and reenters cpu_exec.
Unchanged reuse while INV-03-04 already owns DRAIN_SERVICE can wait on or reenter
exclusion and those accessors can enter another fault path. Merely pinning the
control stack does not remove that recursion/deadlock. The source itself also
questions the exact x86 MMIO RMW behavior; this pass does not settle that semantic
question. [Pinned page-walk implementation, `PTETranslate`, `ptw_ldl/q`,
`ptw_setl_slow`, `mmu_translate`, `x86_cpu_tlb_fill`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/excp_helper.c).

**Smallest remedy:** replace these walker memory-access/exclusion boundaries,
not the whole decoder or guest instruction family. RAM PTE reads and A/D updates
use the checked resident alias and current transaction authority. Non-RAM cases
use an explicit read/conditional-write service under that same transaction,
returning success, retry or the correctly classified fault/result; they must not
call `start_exclusive` or longjmp recursively. The owner of the original native
frame remains paused through any host wait. Preserve successful earlier PTE
updates and device effects rather than restarting the complete walk blindly.
A non-RAM semantic uncertainty remains explicit; introducing stacked native
frames would not answer it.

For LLDT's second-read example, the original helper frame retains the first
word while the walker/service resolves the second access. An IRQ of the walker
uses only the fixed scratch frame. A missing guest mapping produces the original
instruction's guest fault; an internal shadow miss is repaired and returns to
the saved helper instruction only with `RETRY_ACCESS`. If service already
performed that read, `ACCESS_COMPLETED` supplies its result and resumes the
recorded success continuation, preserving the first word without rereading the
second. The expansion must publish that continuation/result layout before
execution; deriving it from an arbitrary native PC is not sufficient. This handles the earlier snapshot-overwrite
counterexample without forbidding descriptor instructions or converting expected
guest page faults into runtime failures. For scalar direct accesses the same
classification/repair boundary applies; divide's explicit #DE needs only the
resident fault-record/landing portion, not a page walk.

These three results are the same contract as INV-03-01's direct-fault ABI. A
coordinator-performed tracked store or fallback load must not return
`RETRY_ACCESS`; doing so can duplicate a committed side effect. This correction
was requested by the parent integrator before independent acceptance of claim 15.

**Stop/disposition:** the bounded pass identifies a concrete conditional regime
and one required walker slow-path replacement. Prefer it over generic nested
helper exception stacks for the sampled paths. It is not yet complete runtime
cleanup closure or a proof of nonnesting: every invoked service/accessor must
actually satisfy the table, and the QEMU slow walker cannot be linked unchanged.
Keep the current instruction/REP boundary requirements and existing coordinator
protocol. Next source work should enumerate the extracted walker/accessor calls,
including non-RAM PTE behavior and A/D writes to tracked code, against this
partition; stop on an operation lacking explicit result/ownership semantics.
There was no implementation or execution, and no architecture acceptance.

**Historical pass record:** the status, stop and next-action wording within this supplement describe that pass. Later supplements and reviews may supersede them; only the Current summary above states the current conclusion and work order.

Parent-authorized September 19: 45-minute source-only pass, no compilation or

translation/exclusion, fault and cleanup exits. Inspect missing dependencies
before classifying them; the initial convenience tree lacks accel/tcg/cputlb.c
and associated accessor definitions, so their absence supplies no negative

### CLM-03-02-16 — Walker extraction needs explicit routing, writer preparation and non-RAM completion gateways

**Scoped direct-gateway inventory and conditional extraction boundaries accepted in findings-review Return 7; proposed interfaces still require closure.** The full
`system/excp_helper.c` translation path was inspected, including all paging-mode
branches and its direct gateways. Additional pinned files obtained and inspected:
`accel/tcg/cputlb.c`, `ldst_common.c.inc`, `ldst_atomicity.c.inc`, `tb-maint.c`,
`watchpoint.c`; `include/accel/tcg/{cpu-ldst,probe}.h`, `include/exec/{cputlb,tlb-flags}.h`;
and `system/{memory,physmem}.c`. One guessed `include/exec/memory_ldst.h.inc`
path returned 404; the tree identified `include/system/memory_ldst.h.inc`, which
was retrieved. No absence claim rests on that failed lookup.

The inventory is complete for direct walker effects and the identified gateway
categories below, not a claim to have recursively audited every QEMU memory-region
callback, debugger/plugin, IOMMU or device. Those are explicit retained host
interfaces or feature-guarded exclusions whose final profile/machine must agree.
No native frame-stack growth is required merely by the walk's finite page levels;
upstream reentry into generic softmmu/exclusion is the boundary to replace.

| Reachable boundary | Source behavior | Required extraction contract / disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `get_physical_address` mode/entry selection | Physical index bypass; normal 32-bit address handling; PG/NPT selection; long-mode canonicality check; A20 mask on output | Preserve mode-specific address/fault ordering and output permissions/page size. Nonpaging protected startup is required even though one source comment calls no paging “real mode.” Do not replace every address check with long-mode canonicality. |
| `mmu_translate` local walk | Legacy, PAE, long mode, optional LA57; large-page/reserved/NX/user/write/SMEP/SMAP/key checks; per-level restart and full restart on final update race | Keep arithmetic/check order and explicit translation/error records. Profile-disabled branches require feature/control-register guards; they are not deleted simply because the initial guest is 64-bit. Retain prior successful A/D updates on a later fault. |
| `ptw_translate` via `probe_access_full_mmu` | Probes PTE backing with STORE intent, size zero, nonfault=true; obtains writable haddr or slow route; may fill TLB and invoke dirty-code handling | Replace with checked route resolution plus explicit writer preparation. STORE probe does not mean every lookup commits an A/D write; no guest page fault may be fabricated merely because backing is readonly/ROMD/device. |
| `ptw_ldl/q` fast RAM alias | Little-endian 32/64-bit direct read through haddr | Bounded stable backing capability with address/width/routing generation and admitted read authority, never unchecked GPA-to-private-pointer casting. Preserve native memory-order requirements. |
| `ptw_ldl/q` slow read | `_mmuidx_ra` creates LE unaligned MemOp; `cpu_ldl/q_mmu` → `do_ld4/8_mmu` → lookup and RAM/device route | Explicit read result/value and transaction identity. Preserve original guest origin separately from accessor native PC; do not recursively fault through guest linear shadows. |
| `ptw_setl` fast A/D writer | Low-32-bit endian-adjusted compare-exchange only if new A/D bits needed; compare failure selects source restart | Checked conditional-update operation under current authority; participates in code invalidation for **every** writable alias. Return compare success/failure separately from guest fault or pending service. Preserve already completed upper-level updates. |
| `ptw_setl_slow` | CPU execution exit/start plus `start_exclusive`/`end_exclusive`; reread then conditional write using ra=0 | Replace exclusion with the existing coordinator transaction and preserve read/compare/write phases. Host service must not recursively acquire admission or replay a completed device read. Its x86 MMIO-RMW TODO remains an inherited semantic qualification. |
| Physical route installation | `tlb_set_page_full` translates address space; separates RAM, readonly RAM, ROMD and MMIO; uses RCU-owned sections, TLB locks and raw host addends | Keep QEMU address-space/device ownership in the host; transfer checked route IDs or validated alias capabilities, never host pointers to EL1. Readonly writes may be discarded; ROMD reads and writes have different routes. Topology changes require retained routing references. |
| Dirty/source tracking | `probe_access_full_mmu` calls `notdirty_write` even for size zero (uses size one); invalidates translated source and updates dirty flags before returning the alias | Explicit `prepare_pte_update` must cover source tracking before an actual A/D mutation. Conservative preparation before knowing whether bits change is permitted, but cannot drop current-operation pins or falsely claim canonical park. Reuse coordinator source generations rather than VM-calling host TB locks. |
| Generic accessor special paths | Lookup may resize TLB, align-check, split at page boundary, check watchpoints and dirty tracking; reads/writes may split into device-supported widths | No retained raw pointer into a resizable host TLB. PTE addresses are naturally aligned to their width and fit their containing 4-KiB page; generic descriptor/operand gateways still need ordered split-access semantics. Guest debug behavior cannot be silently disabled as a host-plugin configuration choice. |
| MMIO preparation/dispatch | `io_prepare` records mem_io_pc and can trigger I/O recompilation; BQL protects memory-region calls; dispatch applies access validity, width adjustment, endianness, aliases and transaction results | Explicit host request/result boundary retains these device routing semantics and effect order. Icount/replay/plugin facilities need explicit configuration treatment; no BQL or C callback pointer crosses into the shim. `ACCESS_COMPLETED` uses the predeclared success continuation. |
| Successful walk publication | `x86_cpu_tlb_fill` calls `tlb_set_page_with_attrs`; output has permissions and source page-size information | Publish shadow/translation result only after required A/D effects and route validation. Shadow maintenance and invalidation ownership remain the reviewed memory protocol, not an implicit host TLB call. |
| Fault/probe exit | Probe failure stores error_code and returns; normal failure records CR2 or intercept information then raises with originating retaddr | Produce explicit translation fault versus runtime/service status. Canonicality #GP preserves CR2; #PF keeps its fault address/error bits. Only the architectural boundary takes the permanent guest-fault landing. |
| NPT/intercept branches | Recursive nested translation, stage2 error records, VMCB writes and `cpu_vmexit` | Guard by the final CPU virtualization contract. The Intel Haswell-derived candidate does not thereby acquire SVM/NPT support. If enabled later, these writes/exits require their own all-writer/result contracts; their source presence is not support. |

Direct source anchors: [walker and all its
branches](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/excp_helper.c),
[accessor signatures/MemOps](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/include/accel/tcg/cpu-ldst.h),
[common accessor wrappers](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/ldst_common.c.inc),
[lookup/probe/dirty/MMIO gateways](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/cputlb.c),
[source invalidation](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/tb-maint.c),
[watchpoint exits](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/watchpoint.c),
[physical address-space/IOMMU routing](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/system/physmem.c),
[memory-region dispatch and defaults](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/system/memory.c).

#### New concrete finding: even a nonfaulting PTE probe has side effects

`ptw_translate`'s zero-size probe can reach `notdirty_write`, which invokes
`tb_invalidate_phys_range_fast`, page collection locks and dirty-state changes.
The probe passes return address zero; the precise-SMC current-TB recognition in
`tb-maint.c` requires a nonzero retaddr. Consequently the resident replacement
cannot treat “nonfault probe” as pure permission lookup or assume that upstream
probe automatically supplies this port's current-instruction stop behavior.
A page that is both translated source and a guest page table is the concrete
counterexample: exposing its private writable alias without the explicit writer
protocol permits A/D modification outside source invalidation; importing the
upstream probe instead imports host locking/lifetime behavior and still does
not establish the port's forced next-boundary exit.

Smallest remedy is the table's route-plus-writer preparation boundary, using the
same coordinator transaction and retained origin. A/D writes that change bytes
invalidate future source entries and force the recorded boundary protocol;
current code/metadata remains pinned until its operation finishes. No extra
architectural instruction or recursive memory epoch is required. This is a
semantic interface requirement, not a claim that the implementation exists.

#### Non-RAM results are not synonymous with guest page faults

The inspected generic memory dispatch returns a MemTxResult and a value;
unassigned reads return zero with MEMTX_DECODE_ERROR, while unassigned writes
have no device effect. `io_failed` raises only through an installed target
transaction-failure callback when configured; the inspected `x86_tcg_ops`
initializer supplies no such callback. Therefore a replacement that maps every
failed host bus transaction to x86 #PF would not preserve this inherited path.
The PTE value can instead subsequently cause the walk's ordinary not-present
fault. [x86 operation hooks](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/tcg-cpu.c).

**Proposed retained contract:** host QEMU memory-region dispatch owns mapped
MMIO, ROMD, readonly and unassigned transaction responses; the resident walker
consumes their defined value/status without inventing a page-fault conversion.
Requests carry read versus conditional-A/D-update phase, width, physical route,
current transaction identity and completed effects. The guest architectural
walk produces #PF/#GP only from its corresponding checks. The existing QEMU
MMIO-PTE RMW question is not resolved by returning a generic failure code, and
the chosen machine must expose an explicit policy for that inherited behavior.
This keeps valid device-backed accesses serviceable rather than excluding them
from the guest contract.

**Stop and next decision:** the declared direct gateway inventory is complete;
transitive device semantics remain an explicit host boundary. The decisive open
semantic interfaces are now finite: (1) exact PTE-route/result and conditional
update contract, including non-RAM and readonly behavior, (2) A/D all-writer
preparation plus forced-boundary ownership, (3) guest debug/watchpoint versus
optional host tooling behavior, and (4) resident cleanup/retained resource bounds.
The next design step is to review and freeze those contracts with INV-03-01/04
and the selected machine, not to add another isolated helper example. This
inventory does not require a full implementation before architecture selection;
later emitter/runtime validation must demonstrate conformance to the accepted
contracts. No implementation, execution, complete helper coverage or architecture
acceptance is claimed.


## Supplement I — finite walker/result/debug/cleanup contracts (complete at stated stop; reviewed)

**Historical pass record:** the status, stop and next-action wording within this supplement describe that pass. Later supplements and reviews may supersede them; only the Current summary above states the current conclusion and work order.

September 19, parent-directed continuation within the ongoing research goal:
45-minute source/protocol budget, no runtime. Define the interfaces left by
claim16 rather than add another sampled helper. INV-03-02 owns walker outcomes,
access phases and debug/cleanup separation; concurrent INV-03-04 owns admission,
source mutation and mapping retirement. Stop at a reviewable combined contract
or one precise unsupported semantic case. Distinguish design closure from later
implementation conformance; no further authorization is needed for this bounded
source work under the active goal.


### CLM-03-02-17 — Proposed finite PTE transaction and cleanup contract

**Conditional finite contract accepted in findings-review Return 8, with INV-03-04's exclusive-entry baseline predicate; source-grounded in claim16.**
Use one serial walker suboperation within the retained guest operation. This is
an internal service interface, distinct from the outer `RETRY_ACCESS`,
`ACCESS_COMPLETED`, `GUEST_FAULT` instruction-access outcomes. INV-03-04 owns
admission and mutation ordering; it references this result vocabulary instead
of defining a competing walker API.

A request records operation/lifetime identity, walker level/restart phase,
access class, GPA, width/endianness, guest access intent and the resolved routing
generation. Conditional updates additionally record expected low 32 bits and
the A/D mask. The retained walker locals include already read entries and
accumulated permission bits; a service pause is not an instruction refetch or
walk restart. There is at most one outstanding serial suboperation for this
walker; IDs cannot be reused while a prior reply/callback can still refer to
them. Completed effects are folded into current phase, not an unbounded journal.

| Result | Meaning and legal next transition |
| --- | --- |
| `PENDING` | No result is available. Retain phase, snapshot and any published-request route reference; pause without holding host topology/BQL/page locks. Reenter the same service transaction when granted. |
| `READ_DONE(value, bus_status)` | Consume the read result exactly once and apply the next original walker check. A bus error is not automatically a guest fault; defined host dispatch value/status remains available. |
| `UPDATE_DONE(compare_matched, write_disposition, bus_status)` | Consume conditional-update outcome exactly once. Compare mismatch takes the source's named level/full restart; match proceeds as the source does, including readonly/discard behavior. `write_disposition` is one of `NO_WRITE`, `RAM_CHANGED`, `RAM_UNCHANGED`, `DEVICE_WRITE_OR_DISCARD`. |
| `WALK_FAULT(payload)` | The x86 walker has selected architectural vector/error/address under its checks; preserve earlier effects and take the outer guest-fault path. A service transport failure cannot manufacture this result. |

The statuses distinguish four easily conflated cases: failed compare, matched
compare with no changed RAM bytes, actual RAM mutation, and a device/readonly
write whose meaning belongs to its route. For mapped MMIO, even a write that
leaves a register value equal can have side effects; it must not be labeled
`RAM_UNCHANGED`. A device response cannot assert absence of backend DMA/RAM
mutation; those effects independently use INV-03-04's backend-writer contract.
An invariant or host lifecycle failure is a contained runtime/VM failure, not a
synthetic x86 page fault or silent access completion.

**Routing and physical result policy:** retain host QEMU memory-region dispatch
for MMIO/ROMD/readonly/unassigned semantics, including split access width and
endianness. Treat its transaction result/value as such; preserve the inspected
x86 path's lack of a generic transaction-failure-to-#PF conversion. The logical
walker can derive not-present #PF from a returned zero PTE. For non-RAM A/D
updates, retain the existing read/compare/conditional-write sequence under the
current coordinator authority; this specifies the virtual implementation's
inherited policy, not a new assertion of hardware x86 MMIO RMW equivalence.
The source's MMIO-RMW qualification remains in the compatibility record. No
valid MMIO access is converted into a fatal private-runtime fault.

Before a suboperation is published/admitted, a stale prepared route may be
resolved again under current authority. After publication it retains its route
until completion or acknowledged cancellation; a late reply cannot be applied
to a newly selected route. A route reference is an ownership reference, **not**
a host topology mutex held while waiting. Finish the admitted phase against its
retained view; only the next unperformed phase resolves anew. An algorithmic
restart after a compare race issues new reads with new serial request identity;
that intentional reread differs from duplicating a completed device effect just
because scheduling paused. No raw resizable `CPUTLBEntryFull *` survives a host
service transition.

**A/D mutation seam:** `RAM_CHANGED` is an ordinary all-writer RAM mutation,
including page-table/code aliases. Writer preparation, compare-and-change,
source/shadow generation updates and result publication share the existing
transaction authority. Before its own mutation, the walker compares saved dependency versions with
the exclusive-entry baseline and retains all preexisting mismatches. It may
fold its known A/D generation delta only for baseline-matched dependencies whose
address, permission and routing meaning remain unchanged, under INV-03-04's
exact predicate. A matched leaf compare cannot erase a stale parent-read
dependency. This prevents self-induced restart without accepting unrelated
intervening changes; those follow the original race/revalidation rules. `NO_WRITE` and unchanged/failed compare do not falsely claim a changed
source byte. Exact admission/promotion, forced-boundary and retained-mapping
rules are owned by the concurrent INV-03-04 composition, not newly accepted by
this record alone.

#### Finite cleanup and publication obligations

| Resource/effect | Owner across `PENDING` | Terminal handling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Original code/helper frame and recovery metadata | Retained guest operation; independent of serial route request | Release only after success/fault continuation has no possible native return or recovery reference. A canonical CPU-state snapshot alone does not release it. |
| Current route/backing and host request slot | Published suboperation; immutable identity and completion ownership | Completion/cancellation is acknowledged before slot reuse; release the route once no reply/replay or pending access can use it. Guest code pin may remain longer. |
| Walker stack/phase and IRQ scratch | Per-vCPU resident runtime; separate fixed roles | Resume same phase or discard through permanent landing; never overwrite guest snapshot with IRQ state. Sequential accesses reuse completed slots. |
| Host BQL, topology lock, page/TLB lock | Host service segment only | No lock is retained in a paused shim frame or transferred across the ABI. Release before publishing a reply/wait that needs another owner. |
| Writer preparation/service authority | Existing INV-03-04 transaction | Complete effects/result or explicitly transfer unfinished phase before ending authority; no second `start_exclusive`, no reverse cleanup of committed A/D/device effects. |
| Pending guest exception/debug state | Private architectural event record | Preserve required payload and phase until legal delivery; completed instruction state can retire native references separately. |
| Plugin/replay/icount/host debugger hooks | Explicit runtime configuration | Proposed initial port does not import these optional host facilities into EL1; omit their callbacks/cleanup together. Guest DR/TF/RF behavior remains mandatory and separate. |

The optional-tooling choice is an execution-runtime configuration, not removal
of x86 guest debug facilities. Guest-facing instructions and status still require
the separate event semantics below. VM execution enters with private I/O/service
eligibility controlled by this ABI, so QEMU's `can_do_io`/I/O-recompile host-loop
bookkeeping is not left half enabled. Host compilation owns its own locks and
cannot lend them to a VM nonlocal exit.

This defines who owns every cleanup category reached by the declared walker
inventory. It does not establish a maximum byte budget for arbitrary helpers or
a safe implementation of the native stack switch; those conformance/resource
bounds remain explicit elsewhere. Sources and exact pinned identities are those
of claim16, plus the prior exit/cleanup source in claims09/14. A complete PTE
service has no remaining unspecified success-versus-retry-versus-bus-fault
conversion in this proposed interface; independent composition review is still
required before calling it accepted.

### CLM-03-02-18 — Guest debug delivery is not closed by retaining generic watchpoint hooks

**Scoped source/specification stop accepted in findings-review Return 8.** The pinned
[`system/bpt_helper.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/bpt_helper.c)
uses DR7 to install guest `BP_CPU` instruction/data/I/O conditions, updates DR6,
and raises #DB. These are architectural facilities, unlike optional plugins.
The generic [watchpoint
path](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/watchpoint.c)
can rewind/reexecute an instruction and request CPU_INTERRUPT_DEBUG. The CPU loop
handles that request separately from normal external interrupt eligibility.
[`breakpoint_handler`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/bpt_helper.c)
raises a matched guest #DB without an inhibit-shadow test and contains an
explicit TODO about delayed #DB under INHIBIT_IRQ. The source therefore does not
supply a completed event-eligibility contract simply by copying this handler.

A precise required case is `mov ss, [selector]; mov esp, new_stack`, with a
successful first instruction's data read matching an enabled guest breakpoint,
valid selector/state and no earlier higher-priority fault. Intel Volume 3A,
**253668-085US, October 2024**, §7.8.3 specifies inhibition of the first
instruction's data breakpoint until the following instruction boundary and
separately specifies TF suppression/instruction-breakpoint behavior. Immediate
#DB after the SS load would expose an unmatched SS:SP pair. [Inspected Intel
primary source, printed page 7-8](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/835754/253668-sdm-vol-3a.pdf).
This is a source/contract discrepancy, not an executed reproduction or claim
that every QEMU debug path fails.

The frontend [`gen_movl_seg`/`gen_eob`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/translate.c)
already distinguishes MOV/POP SS from LSS, suppresses immediate TF and installs
an inhibit flag. The missing interface must carry a pending data-breakpoint
cause across that architectural boundary and release it at the specified next
boundary, preserving DR6, RF/TF and exception ordering. An internal coordinator
pause must neither deliver nor discard it. A host bus service completion must
not reexecute the triggering access merely to reconstruct its debug cause.
Guest-linear debug matching is a separate annotation from physical PTE/backing
accesses; the new checked alias is not itself an x86 debug address.

**Smallest next remedy/stop:** define explicit guest-debug pending/eligibility
state at the common instruction/REP/event boundary, using the existing DR
matching semantics and frontend SS-shadow facts, rather than copying the host
watchpoint rewind/CPU-loop exit machinery into resident repair. Check the SS
case and fault/software-interrupt/ICEBP interactions before claiming closure;
this pass does not equate every INHIBIT_IRQ condition with identical debug rules.
The proposed PTE/cleanup contract above can proceed to independent review, but
complete guest-debug/event composition remains a precise design gap. It cannot
be waived by disabling host plugins, hiding a debug instruction from ordinary
workloads, or demanding an implementation before specifying the rule.

no runtime or implementation. Inspect the inherited breakpoint/event producer
and primary debug rules, including MOV/POP SS, intervening faults, software
