### CLM-03-02-19 — pending debug state needs an event-kind boundary, with one unresolved fusion case

**Reviewed in scope, September 20; source/protocol only.** Independent findings Return 9 accepts the scoped rules, conditional interface and exact stopping case; no source correction remains. The bounded pass stops at
an explicit ICEBP fusion ambiguity below. It closes neither all debug behavior
nor the combined event architecture. The preceding pass began September 19
and crossed local midnight.

**Primary rules inspected:** Intel Volume 3B, **253669-079US, March 2023**,
§18.3.1.2 distinguishes pending data/I/O traps lost on exception delivery from
data breakpoints preserved through INT n/INT3/INTO delivery. The latter trap
occurs at the software handler's entry state. A fault in the instruction after
MOV/POP SS loses its deferred data breakpoint. §18.3.1.4 separately suppresses
the SS-load single-step trap; software interrupts clear TF. Table 18-2 identifies
INT1 as a trap without a DR6 condition flag. These are inspected dated rules,
not a claim about the newest manual. [Intel Volume 3B, printed pages
18-10–12](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/774491/253669-sdm-vol-3b.pdf).

Intel Volume 3A **253668-085US, October 2024**, §7.8.3 makes SS-load instruction-
breakpoint suppression, data-trap delay and TF suppression distinct; executing
the following instruction or delivering an exception ends the inhibition.
Repeated SS loads do not guarantee unbounded extension. LSS is distinct.
[Volume 3A, page 7-8](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/835754/253668-sdm-vol-3a.pdf).
INT1 also differs from software INT's gate-DPL checking and does not itself set
DR6 condition bits. [Intel Volume 2A **253666-082US, December 2023**, INT entry,
printed pages 3-517–519](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf).

**Proposed architectural interface, inference from those rules:** maintain one
per-vCPU debug record with an instruction/iteration identity, captured match
mask, cause class, SS-origin suppression state, and disposition. It is separate
from the native retained frame, external IRQ pending bits, and coordinator
pause state. No queue grows with completed iterations. Capturing a cause is not
permission to inject it, and clearing a pending cause does not mean clearing
all architecturally sticky DR6 bits. DR6 updates remain cause-specific.

| Producer/boundary | Required disposition in this candidate |
| --- | --- |
| Guest data/I/O operation completes | Capture its guest-linear/I/O match once against the operation's debug state. A service-result replay or native retry must not capture a second event. Do not treat physical walker/alias traffic as guest-linear debug traffic. |
| Successful MOV/POP SS with a data match | Retain the cause through the immediately following instruction; do not inject at the first TB exit. Keep TF and instruction-breakpoint suppression as separate controls, not a generic IRQ mask. |
| Following ordinary instruction completes | Resolve eligible debug causes at its architectural completion state before entering another guest instruction; combine applicable causes without replaying either access. |
| Native repair, coordination park, or host service pause | Preserve the record unchanged. These are not guest exception delivery and cannot end the SS shadow or consume a guest instruction. |
| Following instruction faults and guest exception delivery begins | Discard the deferred data/I/O pending cause and end the shadow on the architectural exception path; preserve already committed instruction/REP effects. Merely discovering a recoverable native shadow miss is not this transition. |
| Following INT n, INT3, or taken INTO successfully transfers | Keep the deferred data cause through delivery; present its #DB at the completed handler-entry state before executing the handler's first instruction. Do not trap at the old CPL/stack or synthesize an extra single-step cause from the pre-INT TF. If delivery itself faults, classify that fault rather than pretend the software transfer succeeded. |
| Fault-class instruction breakpoint or DR7.GD | Keep a pre-execution fault path, distinct from completed-access traps; RF suppresses instruction breakpoints, not all data/I/O causes. |
| REP debug point | Carry the existing iteration's architectural progress and completion identity; never require restarting completed MMIO to rediscover a cause. Full REP/SS and simultaneous-event priority are retained obligations, not accepted by this table. |

The event-delivery routine therefore needs explicit entry and successful-transfer
hooks carrying event kind and architectural origin; a plain `raise_exception`
call or TB exit cannot decide the disposition. The post-software-INT hook runs
only after its state transition completes. Delivery faults return through the
normal exception classification path. This does not specify a new guest stack
layout or change inherited gate/descriptor fault priority.

**Pinned source contrast:**
[`breakpoint_handler`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/bpt_helper.c)
contains both the delayed-SS TODO and an instruction to fuse that delayed #DB
with ICEBP. [`helper_icebp`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/excp_helper.c)
ends the instruction, sets vector 1 with `exception_is_int = 0`, records the
next EIP and exits; it does not implement the missing pending record.
The [frontend](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc)
has a distinct INT1 producer. Thus neither classifying all explicit interrupt
instructions as software INT nor discarding every pending cause on any vector-1
request is justified by copying these paths.

**Precise stop/falsifier:** `mov ss,[valid_selector]; icebp`, with the selector
read matching an enabled data breakpoint, no other fault and a valid #DB gate.
The inspected primary text establishes the two component events, but does not
explicitly settle the resulting B0–B3 reporting/fusion; the QEMU TODO is a lead,
not primary proof. This submission deliberately leaves that row unresolved
rather than selecting discard, separate delivery, or fusion from terminology
alone. Smallest next evidence is a primary clarification of this combined case
(or an independently designed x86 oracle study if source evidence remains
insufficient; no execution authorized here). Meanwhile the bounded record and
event-kind hooks are reviewable, and remain compatible with finite coordinator
state. Independent review must check the fault/software-transfer distinction
and this exact stopping condition before the owning summary advances.

### Claim 19 oracle qualification — root addendum, independently accepted

September 20, one bounded 20-minute primary-source clarification of the existing
ICEBP stopping case; no execution and no change to the current matched-MOV work.

**Source-backed:** the upstream
[kvm-unit-tests `x86/debug.c`](https://github.com/kvm-unit-tests/kvm-unit-tests/blob/master/x86/debug.c)
(master inspected September 20) contains the exact MOV-SS/watchpoint/ICEBP case.
It installs `handle_db_save_rip`, whose carry/rotate sequence distinguishes a
single handler entry at the expected location from duplicate/wrong entry.
The test expects `n == 3`; its adjacent `int $1` case expects `n == 7`.
Crucially, this handler does **not** read DR6. The generic `handle_db` elsewhere
does record DR6, but is replaced for this case. This is a primary software test
expectation for event delivery, not observed hardware evidence here and not an
oracle for the unresolved B0–B3 fusion bits. It cannot close claim 19 merely
because its test name matches the pending question.

**Source-backed:** Intel's
[Emerald Rapids specification update](https://edc.intel.com/output/DownloadPdfDocument?id=10616),
document 793902 revision 016US, September 10, 2025, erratum EMR95 (affected
steppings in the summary table), reports missing debug delivery or
incorrect DR6 causes when MOV/POP SS is not immediately followed by an explicit
SP write. This concerns that processor family's documented erratum, not every
x86 processor or a change to the architectural contract.

**Method consequence, inferred:** a future native oracle must identify processor
family/stepping and applicable errata, record event count/RIP and DR6 independently,
and separate the exact ICEBP case from an SP-write control. Inserting an SP write
changes the immediately following instruction and cannot answer the original
sequence. Nor can one affected CPU's missing cause justify choosing discard in
the emulator. The exact DR6 fusion choice remains open; these sources rule out
two insufficient closure methods rather than selecting semantics. No new native
run or research campaign is authorized. Independent machine-agent review accepts
this scoped qualification in the existing findings thread; DR6 fusion remains open.

and EL1-shim design; this corrects prior comparison framing rather than changing direction. TCG/FEX/Box64 are generator adaptations
inside it, not end-to-end emulator alternatives. Debug claim 19's exact ambiguity

### CLM-03-02-20 — codegen suitability separates reusable mechanisms from runtime assumptions

Independent findings Return 10 accepts the scoped source/interface comparison;
no generator ranking, complete extraction or runtime acceptance follows.

**Source facts:** FEX's pinned
[`LoadMemTSO`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MemoryOps.cpp)
selects LDAPUR, LDAPR or LDAR variants from host capabilities and has distinct
vector barrier handling. Some wider paths explicitly require SVE256.
[`VectorOps.cpp`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/VectorOps.cpp)
likewise contains feature-specific AFP/SVE choices. These are reusable lowering
mechanisms, not evidence that a particular M4 captive context enables those
features or that their ordering remains sufficient after runtime replacement.
In particular, `SupportsTSOImm9` refers to the emitter's LRCPC2 capability choice,
not proof that Apple's hardware TSO mode is enabled.
[Host feature assignment](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/Source/Common/HostFeatures.cpp).

FEX's [dispatcher](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp)
uses a CpuStateFrame, returning-stack location, lookup pointers and compilation
paths. Its [relocation code](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/Arm64Relocations.cpp)
resolves named thunks through a context and materializes runtime pointers;
existing relocation records do not make host pointers valid inside the captive
VM. These observations are source facts; the replacement contracts below are
proposed design requirements, not measured porting estimates.

| Required codegen interface | TCG adaptation | FEX-derived adaptation | Common captive-runtime contract |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| State ABI at entry, helper call and exit | Preserve or map selected CPUX86State globals, deferred CC representation and helper annotations. Existing system decode is a semantics source, not a mandate to import CPUState's host runtime. | Map CpuStateFrame/register allocation and flag/vector representation to the selected architectural state. Calling a QEMU semantic helper requires an explicit state conversion or shared representation, not a pointer cast. | Host compiler consumes an immutable context; emitted code addresses resident state, helper targets and private stacks. All state components have defined authoritative locations at a fault/commit boundary. |
| Memory operation lowering | Preserve qemu_ld/st side-effect, spill and recovery obligations while replacing softMMU lookup/slow exits. Claim 08 rules out replacing the IR operation with an ordinary load before allocation. | Existing direct memory lowering is useful machinery, but Linux-user virtual-address validity and signal repair do not establish CPL/segment/page/MMIO/atomic system semantics. Add the declared checked-access and continuation interface around lowering. | Preserve exact effects and live temporaries across retry, completed service and guest fault. RETRY_ACCESS/ACCESS_COMPLETED/GUEST_FAULT are one proposed encoding; no per-access message or call is required. Guest address/mode checks, RAM tracking, MMIO and A/D obligations apply regardless of generator. |
| Helpers and FP | Selected pure helpers/SoftFloat may be compiled resident; system helpers require checked access and exceptional landing. Host siglongjmp, CPU loop and exclusive sections are replacements, not transferred correctness. | Existing fallback calls and FP lowering are candidate components; do not combine FEX state with QEMU helpers without reconciling x87 tags/status, MXCSR, rounding, exceptions and argument/result ABI. | Semantic policy is selected once; generator choice cannot relax NaNs, denormals or exception behavior. Retained helper origins and cleanup must compose with the selected repair protocol; nonnesting is one proposed realization, not a common requirement. |
| Precise faults and interrupts | Existing instruction metadata/global spills aid a derivation but do not recover arbitrary new temporaries. Chained target polling must canonicalize PC as claim 12 requires. | Guest/native PC maps and Linux signal machinery provide evidence to inspect, not a full system fault contract; prior guest-frame recovery limitation still applies. | Full native preservation may resume a private repair; guest faults need the architectural origin and partial effects. Async notifications wait for declared safe boundaries; REP/event and debug obligations are independent of codegen. |
| Dispatch and code lifetime | Replace host CPU execution loop and its TB invalidation/exit authority with the selected resident dispatcher plus host generation requests. Retain useful linking machinery only under code/shadow generation and pin contracts. | Replace compile-on-dispatch host calls, returning host stack, Linux signal/sleep paths and process lookup ownership. Relocate emitted links/thunks to resident targets or marshal requests. | Host owns generation; resident dispatch never calls a host C address. All direct/indirect links obey publication, invalidation, poll and code lifetime rules; no guest-controlled native branch target. |
| Apple ordering/SIMD opportunities | Adapt the AArch64 backend's barrier/vector lowering to the verified captive capability profile; no inference that generic TCG must retain every baseline barrier. | Preserve applicable feature-specific selection as machinery, but replace host process capability assumptions with captive capability input; inspect non-SVE paths rather than treating SVE code as an M4 advantage. | Memory ordering is an explicit proof against the guest contract, including helpers/atomics/device boundaries. A usable hardware TSO mode can motivate removing redundant sequences only after its scope/context contract is established. Feature presence alone is not a throughput result. |

QEMU source anchors for the table remain the pinned
[TCG helper contract](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/docs/devel/tcg-ops.rst),
[AArch64 backend](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/aarch64/tcg-target.c.inc),
[x86 frontend](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/translate.c)
and source traces in claims 08–18. FEX helper/state and recovery observations
retain the pinned JIT/frame sources in claims 05 and 10; no new claim that its
whole codegen pipeline is independently extractable follows from a few files.

**Box64 scope:** it remains a codegen option under exactly the same interface,
not an application-emulator solution to this architecture. The inspected pinned
dynarec helper and signal/configuration sources establish application-oriented
state/recovery and configurable compatibility policies, not the same detailed
extraction inventory just attempted for TCG/FEX. Consequently this pass cannot
rank Box64's adaptation effort below or above them. Its memory, flag/vector
cache, helper and fault boundaries need the same comparison before elimination;
Box32 presence remains insufficient evidence for protected-mode startup.

**Decision consequence, conditional:** do not select a generator by counting
supported system instructions or optimized vector operations. Selected QEMU
system semantics can inform either generator, while a FEX generator does not
carry QEMU's system correctness merely by linking helpers. Conversely, changing
TCG memory/dispatch/helper execution invalidates the assumptions that made those
semantics work in upstream TCG. Required correctness lives in the shared
interfaces and their generator-specific realization.

The decisive unmeasured quantities are adaptation surface for each state/IR
bridge; extra spills or state conversions at faults and helper calls; translation
latency/code size; execution cost after mandatory guards, polls and memory
ordering; and service/invalidations/FP fallback frequency under the same CPU
profile. Source inspection identifies where these costs arise, not their rank.
A generator that cannot express an exact partial-effect/recovery boundary is
excluded on correctness; otherwise the choice requires a matched-interface cost
case. The smallest next architecture artifact is a common codegen input/output
contract with per-generator supported/replacement/unknown entries, rather than
another comparison of whole upstream runtimes or an unbounded helper sample.
No performance ranking, working bridge or end-to-end solution is accepted here.

## Supplement L — common codegen contract and symmetric adapter map

**Completed bounded method, September 20; independently reviewed in scope:** the resumed Track 03 work authorizes
one 45-minute source/design pass. Define the minimum shared host-generator to
captive-runtime interface, then map TCG, FEX and Box64 against identical fields.
Use existing pinned primary sources and targeted reads only; no implementation,
build, generated program, VM or performance experiment. Stop at a reviewable
contract and adapter map or the first decisive incompatibility. Keep semantics
components independent of generator choice and request a separate memory-agent
findings review. The performance purpose is to exploit native paging, faults,
ordering and resident execution where their contracts permit, not to import an
upstream host runtime or infer performance from source feature counts.

### CLM-03-02-21 — Proposed minimum generator–runtime contract

Independent findings Return 12 accepts the proposed behavioral interface and
its explicit limits. It is not acceptance of an adapter or selected generator.

**Normative proposal, not an upstream interface or accepted implementation.**
The generator is a host component that turns admitted guest instructions into
native objects for the captive VM. It need not share an IR or architectural C
structure with other generators. Semantics may come from its existing frontend,
a separately selected frontend/helper library, or a combination whose state and
effect contracts are specified. The common boundary is the request/object and
its behavior, not a universal compiler pipeline.

The target description has independent parameters: exposed x86 profile and
semantics policy; execution privilege/address layout; architectural state/helper
ABI; memory/coordination policy; and native VM capability/ordering profile.
Selecting TCG, FEX or Box64 sets none of these automatically. Host process feature
detection is not sufficient to set the native VM capability profile. A different
state layout may have a different adapter without changing the guest contract.

| Input | Minimum content and constraint |
| --- | --- |
| Work identity | VM lifetime, request generation, guest entry identity and permitted publication context. Cancellation or a stale reply never grants execution eligibility. |
| Admitted instruction input | Immutable fetched bytes/fragments with guest addresses, decode mode/segment context and dependencies. The compiler must not dereference a guest address as a host source pointer or speculatively fetch side-effecting instruction bytes. Additional bytes or constant-folded memory values require their own admitted dependency, not ambient guest-RAM access. |
| Guest semantics/context key | Versioned profile and all state assumptions actually specialized into the object: feature/mode, relevant control/segment/FP conditions. The producer reports those dependencies; dynamic conditions remain runtime checks. A generator's missing implementation is not permission to return guest #UD for an enabled instruction. |
| Target ABI/capabilities | Native ISA/features valid in the captive context, layout/relocation constraints, resident state and stack convention, symbolic helper/service entry points and their effect signatures. These are a selected runtime contract, not arbitrary host addresses. |
| Resource and boundary policy | Object/output limits, allowed linking form and required fault/event/invalidation/coordination boundary guarantees. Limits constrain translation admission; a legal guest operation must have a supported semantic path rather than being rejected because one compiler buffer overflows. |

| Output | Minimum content and consumer rule |
| --- | --- |
| Native object | Code plus literals, entry points, target requirements, sizes and all external relocations. Host addresses are not callable VM targets. Relocations name runtime symbols, state offsets or admitted link identities; publication resolves them under the runtime's authority. |
| Assumption/dependency manifest | Input identity, source/context dependencies and ABI/capability version. Installation verifies current eligibility; an immutable object's later invalidation removes entry eligibility without freeing live continuations. The exact epoch/guard mechanism is a runtime parameter. |
| Boundary/recovery description | Native ranges or equivalent generated landing paths identifying the guest operation and its committed effects at every possible guest-origin fault or required event/coordination boundary. Include live/deferred flags, FP/vector state and access results that cannot be reconstructed from RIP alone. This is a behavior obligation, not a requirement for a per-instruction full register dump. |
| Memory/helper effect interface | The selected lowering states guest access intent, width/span and permitted partial/atomic behavior, plus helper input/output and fault/exit behavior. If a service completes an access, continuation consumes its result without reissuing the effect; if unperformed, a retry preserves earlier effects. A guest fault preserves its required architectural origin/state. Named RETRY_ACCESS/ACCESS_COMPLETED/GUEST_FAULT messages are one encoding, not the contract itself. |
| Transfers and lifetime | Normal exit, guest branch, helper return and direct-link entry obey the selected context/event checks; every retained native PC, return and metadata reference keeps its object live. Report or constrain dynamic link/reference forms sufficiently for the runtime's reclamation bound. A native unknown fault is not guessed into a guest exception. |
| Translation outcome | Publishable object, bounded retry for missing input/resource, or explicit unsupported adapter requirement. No incomplete object is runnable. A missing semantic path is an incompatibility to resolve in the architecture, not a silent profile change or successful execution. |

**Fast path and state freedom.** Ordinary eligible RAM accesses may be direct
native instructions through resident mappings. Checks may be enforced by the
selected mapping/context or proved lowering; the common interface does not add
a host exit, message allocation, shim call or complete canonical spill to each
access or instruction. Register-cached flags/vector state may remain live across
instructions when the chosen recovery and boundary rules can recover it. Pure
helpers may inline or run resident with a private ABI. The runtime must know
which state is authoritative when a fault, helper transition, guest event or
coordination boundary actually occurs; that does not require every such boundary
to use the same representation or mechanism.

**Exceptional paths.** Every admitted service/helper call has declared memory
and state effects, possible suspension/nonreturning exits, and return/cleanup
ownership. The existing nonnesting repair and permanent-landing proposal is one
candidate realization, not an input constraint forcing another generator's
runtime to adopt it. Another realization must establish the same exact effects,
recoverability, private-state exclusion and bounded progress/lifetime properties.
Similarly, explicit per-instruction checkpoints are required only by the selected
invalidation protocol's demanded stop points. Choosing a coarser protocol requires
its own stale-execution argument; an entry poll cannot be relabeled an internal
checkpoint. Direct links may be fast, but may not bypass required eligibility.

**Helper provenance is separate.** A helper signature includes its architectural
read/write set, state format, FP-control interaction, guest-memory interface,
exceptional result and native clobbers. A QEMU helper's CPUX86State pointer is not
a FEX CpuStateFrame or Box64 x64emu pointer. The architecture may choose a common
resident representation, adapters at actual helper boundaries, or different
helpers with equivalent guest behavior. No per-instruction conversion is imposed
by this contract. Walker/PAT/FP/event policies remain independently selected
semantic components; their required guest behavior is not generator-specific.

### CLM-03-02-22 — Symmetric pinned-source adapter map, with no suitability ranking

Independent findings Return 12 accepts the scoped source observations and E/R/U
map. The reviewer re-read the pinned local copies; fresh retrieval failed, so
this review does not claim an independent download or byte match.

**Evidence labels:** `E` means an inspected upstream mechanism exists, not that
the captive adapter works. `R` is a necessary replacement/mapping identified by
a crossed contract. `U` is a gap in this audit, not evidence of source absence or
higher cost. Every cell uses the same proposed contract from claim 21.

| Contract surface | TCG | FEX-derived generator | Box64-derived generator |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Admitted input/context | E: x86 DisasContext/TB specialization. R: supplied source/context and dependency capture instead of ambient host runtime access. U: complete specialization key after extraction. | E: JIT consumes IR and its thread/pass-manager context. R: admit frontend fetches and represent system mode/control assumptions separately from Linux-user context. U: complete system semantic/context adapter. | E: native_pass accepts address/mode and reads the decode window; block tracks x64_addr/readaddr/size. R: separate guest identity from host snapshot address throughout fetch/relative decoding. U: complete system context key. |
| State and helper ABI | E: TCG globals/deferred CC, helper annotations and AArch64 call emission. R: resident state/targets and exits replace host CPU-loop assumptions. U: full enabled-helper closure. | E: fixed/general register classes and CpuStateFrame; fallback result handling. R: resident frame/helper symbols and any selected semantic-library conversion. U: complete fault/partial-effect ABI. | E: x64emu GPR/flags/vector/x87/deferred-flag state, flag/NEON caches; call_c stores selected registers and pushes/pops FP cache. R: resident state/helper targets, clobber/fault semantics for each call. U: complete enabled-helper closure. |
| Guest access/direct memory | E: qemu_ld/st memory IR and backend fast/slow access machinery. R: preserve side-effect/recovery semantics while replacing softMMU access policy. U: full width/atomic/partial-effect lowering. | E: direct and TSO-specific GPR/vector lowering. R: system checks/routing/fault classification and capability input. U: all memory forms under the selected policy. | E: effective-address generation for LDR/STR operands. R: system address/permission/routing and memory-effect boundary around lowering. U: all memory forms and full atomic/partial-effect audit. |
| Recovery/boundaries | E: instruction metadata, allocator spill rules and success continuation labels. R: required internal checkpoint and VM-local exceptional landing mappings. U: complete system fault/event coverage. | E: native/guest PC map and application frame reconstruction; the scoped recovery limitation in claim 05 remains. R: system effect origins, event boundaries and VM fault entry. U: complete live-state recipes. | E: instsize/per-instruction architecture info, flag/NEON cache records and adjustregs signal path. R: captive fault/return state and guest-event boundaries replace application signals/longjmp. U: complete live-state/partial-effect recipes. |
| Dispatch/link/lifetime | E: TB direct/indirect links and host dispatch. R: resident miss/exit/link eligibility and selected lifetime ownership. U: whole-object/resource bounds after changes. | E: lookup/compile dispatcher, runtime frame pointers and exit links. R: host compile request across the boundary, resident dispatch targets and pin/admission discipline. U: full reachable-reference bound. | E: jump-table dispatch, secondary entries and callret records. R: captive entry eligibility and retention for all selected transfer forms; do not assume tail-only TB ancestry. U: bound if callret/secondary-entry optimizations retained. |
| Relocation/publication | E: native helper/branch/literal emission. R: symbol/address-domain manifest and selected publication/reclamation. U: complete relocated-object inventory. | E: named symbol/thunk and guest-RIP relocations. R: resolve to captive runtime symbols, not context-owned host function pointers. U: all emitted pointer forms. | E: table64 address/constant relocation records and dynablock relocs. R: resolve constants/targets into the captive domain and admit all entry forms. U: all embedded pointers and patch-site lifetime. |
| Native opportunities | E: AArch64 memory/barrier/vector selection. R: actual captive capability/policy rather than inherited host assumptions. U: cost after guards/recovery changes. | E: LRCPC2/RCpc, AFP and SVE-specific lowering. R: captive capability/FP/ordering policy. U: benefit on the selected M4 paths after adaptation. | E: native flag/NEON cache and direct-address mechanisms. R: required guest semantics and captive capability input independent of application options. U: matched output/cost and Apple-specific paths. |

**Primary source anchors.** QEMU mappings use the pinned frontend/backend,
TCG allocation/helper contracts and concrete traces already cited in claims
01–03, 08–12 and 14–18. FEX mappings use the pinned JIT,
[dispatcher](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp),
[memory lowering](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MemoryOps.cpp)
and [relocations](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/Arm64Relocations.cpp)
from claim 20. These were re-read; no version changed.

The Box64 map adds targeted reads at the same existing pin:
[`native_pass`](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/dynarec_native_pass.c),
[`x64emu` state](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/emu/x64emu_private.h),
[ARM64 instruction/cache state](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_private.h),
[`geted`, `call_c` and dispatch](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_helper.c),
[block/reference representation](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/dynablock_private.h)
and [emission/relocation macros](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_pass3.h).
The previously read [signal source](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/libtools/signals.c)
contains adjustregs and host siglongjmp paths. The five new files total 49,625
bytes. No application defaults are promoted into our required compatibility policy.

**Concrete mapping consequence:** Box64's block has secondary-entry and callret
records, and emitted callret sites include patchable NOP/UDF behavior. Therefore
INV-03-04's TCG-derived two-link/tail-transfer capacity premise is not a common
codegen property. Retaining these Box64 forms requires accounting for their live
returns/entries; replacing them requires a different generated transfer contract.
This is a specific resource/lifetime adapter decision, not evidence that Box64
is unsuitable or that an unbounded stack is required.

**Performance mechanism and limits.** Captive execution offers resident ordinary
accesses through native mappings, native fault entry and applicable native
ordering/SIMD instructions. Avoided upstream softMMU/signal/host-loop machinery
is potential saved work; the replacement guards, recovery state, invalidation
checks and exceptional service are residual work. Count ordinary-path operations
separately from misses, faults, MMIO and rare helper exits. None of the mandatory
exceptional service protocol should be priced as a host round trip on every RAM
load. Conversely, no fast path is credited unless its guest behavior and relied-on
captive capability are established. The matrix identifies adaptation sites but
neither counts changed lines nor ranks performance from E/R/U totals.

**Disposition and next selection step:** independent findings Return 12 accepts
claims 21–22 and the clarification to claim 20's common column within their
source/proposed-interface scope; no substantive return remains. The earlier column's named
access outcomes/nonnesting regime are candidate realizations, not mandatory
per-access APIs. This pass stops with a finite common contract and symmetric
adapter map, no selected generator and no rejected candidate.

The next unstarted, bounded 45-minute matched source-lowering pass would compare
**ordinary 64-bit MOV loads/stores** for all three generators with the same
stated x86 mode/profile and captive target description: aligned WB RAM hit,
shadow repair and genuine guest fault. Keep unrelated ISA exposure unchanged;
the small comparison kernel is not a reduced guest CPU. Record each emitter's
existing ordinary-path operations, required adapter/checkpoint changes and
exceptional-path work separately, with explicit assumptions for capability-
dependent choices. Use source-derived counts only where a fully resolved emitted
sequence supports them; otherwise mark a count unknown. The output must identify
which adaptation seams differ and which uncertainty can change generator
selection, rather than start three unrelated helper audits. A later actual
emission probe needs its own retained exploratory method; none runs in this pass.

## Supplement M — matched MOV64 source comparison

**Completed method, September 20; independently reviewed in scope:** execute the authorized 45-minute source-only
comparison. No compiler/build, generated executable, VM or timing experiment.
Use the existing QEMU/FEX/Box64 pins. Compare `48 8b 03` (`mov rax,[rbx]`) and
`48 89 03` (`mov [rbx],rax`) in 64-bit mode with flat DS, no address-size override,
no LOCK and no active guest debug/single-step condition. The guest operand is
8-byte aligned ordinary WB RAM; no MMIO, split or atomic-RMW semantics are hidden
inside this sample. Keep the same required full guest profile outside the sample.
Trace (a) permitted mapped hit, (b) internal shadow repair before completion and
(c) genuine guest permission/not-present fault. Preserve other registers, flags
and FP/vector state. Separate guest address checks, native transfer, ordering,
state/recovery and exceptional work. Native feature choices will be stated per
resolved path; no host feature is presumed valid in the captive VM. Stop with
concrete adapter differences and one decision-changing next uncertainty, marking
unresolved emission counts unknown rather than ranking from source coverage.

### CLM-03-02-23 — matched MOV64 fragments expose different ordering and recovery adaptation points

**Source comparison, accepted in scope by independent findings Return 13.**
The review accepts the conditional fragments and adapter implications only, with
no substantive return. Complete the sample context with
CPL3, 64-bit addressing, canonical operand span, flat DS and ordinary user RAM
permissions. The shared fixture state has CR0.PG=CR0.WP=1, CR4.PAE=1,
CR4.LA57=CR4.PCIDE=CR4.SMEP=CR4.SMAP=0, EFER.LME=LMA=NXE=1,
and 4-KiB user pages; the hit page is present/read-write, while the fault
case changes its present or write permission as appropriate to the operation.
XMM state supplies the live-vector obligation without requiring an optional
AVX feature. These are matched fixture inputs, not a selected product profile
or permission to exclude other supported CPU states. Optional profile fields
that do not affect this MOV slice remain identical and unspecified across the
comparison; no emitter is credited with system-mode support from this fixture.
Incoming flags may be deferred from a preceding instruction, and FP/vector
registers may be live even though MOV changes neither. They must survive a
fault. A secondary alias check is `48 8b 00` (`mov rax,[rax]`): original address
and destination identity cannot be conflated during fault/service recovery.

For a reproducible **source branch selection**, use little-endian AArch64 GPR
operations with no LRCPC/LRCPC2-dependent optimization and no hardware-TSO promise.
This baseline is a comparison input, not a claim about M4's full capabilities.
The actual captive capability description remains an independent parameter.
Each upstream ordering policy is stated below; its presence is not a new proof
of complete x86 ordering. For the proposed native-shadow hit, mapping/context
must enforce guest permissions and the selected lowering must exclude private
addresses with correct fault semantics. Exact guard instructions are not selected
here and are not silently charged as zero.

#### Resolved emission fragments, not complete block counts

Counts below cover only the stated native transfer/ordering fragment, with
operands already available in registers. They exclude effective-address guards,
register allocation/spills, state flushes, entry/exit checks, linking, source
publication and exceptional paths. They are neither emitted-object observations
nor comparable whole-access costs. The complete adapted hit count is **unknown
for all three**, since the captive adapters do not yet have resolved emission.

| Generator and resolved source branch | Load fragment | Store fragment | What the count does not settle |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TCG MO64 direct-transfer functions | One 64-bit LDR instruction | One 64-bit STR instruction | `prepare_host_addr` precedes it. Unchanged system mode loads/probes software-TLB metadata and branches to a helper slow path; those instructions are not part of the proposed direct shadow path. |
| TCG requested ordering, isolated nonmerged barrier | Load request intersects the x86 guest mask to LD→LD; `tcg_out_mb` emits one DMB ISHLD | Store request retains LD→ST and ST→ST; emits one DMB ISH | This is the resolved barrier operation, not proof each final block retains one barrier per access; optimization/context can change placement/merging. |
| FEX GPR64 `LoadMemTSO`/`StoreMemTSO`, default TSO enabled, RCpc/LRCPC2 off | LDAR followed by NOP: two instructions | NOP followed by STLR: two instructions | NOP is the source's backpatch slot, not a cost-free annotation. No count is claimed for register mapping, fault handling or later patches. |
| Box64 MOV base+0, no segment override, lock=0, unaligned path off, Unity/volatile exceptions off | `geted` selects the base register; `LDxw` emits one LDR | `STxw` emits one STR | This resolves the transfer macro only. Instruction flags/cache machinery and strong-memory sequence state remain separate. |
| Box64 ordering with level at least STRONGMEM_QEMU, weakbarrier=0 | `SMREADLOCK(0)` adds one DMB ISHLD before that LDR | With start-of-write-sequence state `smwrite=0` and admitted `will_write`, WILLWRITE adds DMB ISH; SMWRITE adds DMB ISH after STR, whether this is the last write or the high-level periodic case: three instructions including STR | This is a conditional sequence-state trace, not every MOV block's output or a reason to rank Box64. Different ordering/sequence policies change it. |

**TCG trace.** The x86 frontend's operand load/store routes use qemu_ld/st IR;
`gen_MOV` itself is empty because decode operand loading/writeback does the work.
`tcg_gen_qemu_ld_i64_int` requests LD→LD/ST→LD ordering and the store requests
LD→ST/ST→ST. The x86 `guest_default_memory_order` excludes ST→LD, while AArch64's
`TCG_TARGET_DEFAULT_MO` is zero. The backend maps those barrier bits as above;
`prepare_host_addr` and the direct transfer remain distinct source stages.
[Memory IR](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/tcg-op-ldst.c),
[x86 memory-order default](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/tcg-cpu.c),
[AArch64 default](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/aarch64/tcg-target-mo.h),
[barrier/address/transfer emitter](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/aarch64/tcg-target.c.inc).
Retaining the IR's side-effect/recovery classification while replacing only the
address policy is a concrete adapter choice. Claim 08's unsafe ordinary-IR
substitution remains rejected. This can retain upstream global synchronization
cost; the count depends on actual incoming liveness, not MOV's unchanged flags.

**FEX trace.** `MOVGPROp` loads its source then stores the result;
`LoadSource`/`StoreResult` use automatic-TSO memory builders for the normal memory
case. `IsTSOEnabled` has explicit force overrides; with default force and no
NonTSO address hint, it selects the context's GPR policy. The pinned
`SetHardwareTSOSupport`/`UpdateAtomicTSOEmulationConfig` supplies a concrete seam:
setting hardware support disables atomic-based TSO emulation. The regular
`LoadMem`/`StoreMem` GPR64 emitter then has one LDR/STR transfer rather than the
ordered pair above. This is **conditional source selection**, not proof that an
Apple captive TSO mode is enabled, sufficient for every access or cheaply switched.
[Frontend MOV and operand routes](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.cpp),
[automatic-TSO selectors](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h),
[context policy](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Context/Context.h),
[memory emitter](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MemoryOps.cpp).

**Box64 trace.** Opcode 0x8B resolves the effective address, emits SMREADLOCK and
LDxw; 0x89 resolves the address, emits STxw and SMWRITELOCK. At ModRM base+0,
`geted` can return the existing native guest-base register without an address
arithmetic instruction. The native pass invokes WILLWRITE before a marked store;
strong-memory macros carry sequence state. Reading only the opcode case would
therefore undercount its selected ordering fragment. The non-unaligned branch
is a stated condition, not an assertion that upstream always chooses it.
[Opcode cases](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_00.c),
[address helper](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_helper.c),
[strong-memory policy](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/dynarec_helper.h),
[pass prelude](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/dynarec_native_pass.c),
[LD/ST/barrier macros](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/arm64_emitter.h).
Disabling software ordering can remove these macro barriers, but is permissible
for the captive adapter only with the same qualified hardware-ordering premise
as the other generators; no application compatibility switch establishes it.

#### Same fault/repair obligation, different known adaptation points

| Case | Required effect/state | TCG adapter evidence | FEX/Box64 adapter evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Aligned permitted WB hit | One guest load commits its result, or one store commits its bytes; flags/FP/vector state unchanged. No required host transaction. | Direct transfer exists, but software address preparation must be replaced while retaining necessary recovery synchronization. | Direct transfers exist; add the selected system address/permission/fault policy without presuming application address validity protects private VM state. |
| Shadow repair before native access completes | Preserve original operand/value and all incoming guest state. Repair may resume the unperformed native operation; a service that performs it must continue after it exactly once. | Existing raddr and load-helper return machinery identify a success continuation, conditional on slow labels being emitted. Every adapted faultable site needs an equivalent contract. | Direct emitter fragments alone do not establish a service-completed result landing. Existing application signal/cache reconstruction is evidence to adapt, not an accepted full guest-fault ABI. Exact recipes remain unknown here. |
| Genuine guest permission/not-present fault | Report the guest MOV origin, address, access/CPL error information and pre-MOV destination; incoming deferred flags/vector state remains authoritative. Do not confuse a native syndrome with guest intent. | Existing globals/CC metadata supply a concrete candidate recipe, but adapting memory access invalidates automatic reuse claims. Actual spill count depends on preceding instructions. | Register/cache representations must identify the same guest state at the fault. No zero-spill precise-system-fault claim follows from the one-instruction transfer. |
| Address aliases destination (`mov rax,[rax]`) | A pre-completion fault uses original RAX as address and unchanged destination. A completed service delivers its new value without retry through that changed address. | Keep address/origin metadata distinct from the helper result destination and success landing. | The same distinction is mandatory even if native register allocation aliases base and destination. Neither PC maps alone nor an application context pointer establishes it. |

The guard/runtime may capture a complete native frame for internal repair without
canonicalizing all guest state first. Delivering a guest fault still needs a
valid architectural-state recipe; that is the distinction likely to change the
normal-path spill cost. Async interruption after a completed store must also
recognize completion rather than repeat the store. This pass adds no proof for
MMIO, split/unaligned accesses, atomics, REP or FP instruction execution; live FP
state preservation here is an obligation, not an audited recipe for all three.

#### Decision consequence and exact stop

All three inspected emitters can express a single native scalar transfer for the
matched operation. Their upstream ordering/preparation fragments differ, and
FEX exposes a direct hardware-TSO policy hook. Those facts do **not** distinguish
whole adapted access cost: removed software-TLB work, new guards, register state
and required boundaries remain unresolved in the comparison. Equal native MOV
counts are not equal generator quality, nor is the larger configured Box64
barrier fragment a performance verdict.

The concrete adapter choice is **retain upstream pre-access synchronization**
versus **retain register-cached state and supply a precise fault recipe**. These
are alternatives within each generator, not TCG versus FEX labels. The decisive
unknown is the ordinary-path state materialization required by a sound recipe
when MOV follows a flag-producing operation and live vector state. Existing
TCG synchronization gives one derivable baseline; the other candidates' maps
have not established an equally precise zero-spill replacement. No generator is
selected or excluded from this slice.

The source pass stopped at that unknown; independent findings Return 13 accepts
the scoped result. The next
45-minute bounded decision action should prepare a **matched emission-only exploration**

adapter remains proposed. Compare the same block across all

### CLM-03-02-24 — A matched emission study needs compile-only entry points and recovery artifacts

**Source result, not a build result:** FEX provides an existing compile-only
entry point. QEMU and Box64 expose compilation functions, but the inspected
sources do not establish a ready equivalent command. A small diagnostic harness
is therefore part of the proposed exploration, not evidence that extraction
already works. This changes the immediate action from “run three disassemblers”
to a finite readiness gate followed by explicitly instrumented compilation.

The inspection used the same three immutable revisions listed above. New
primary anchors are [FEX CodeSizeValidation](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/Source/Tools/CodeSizeValidation/Main.cpp),
[FEX fixture parser](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/Scripts/InstructionCountParser.py),
[its upstream test invocation](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/unittests/InstructionCountCI/CMakeLists.txt),
[QEMU translation entry](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/translate-all.c),
[Box64 initialization](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/core.c),
[Box64 block wrapper](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/dynablock.c)
and [Box64 compilation passes](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/dynarec_native.c).
The following method is a proposal derived from those sources; it is not a
measurement, accepted extraction or performance ranking.

#### Finite fixtures and observations

Use Supplement M’s long-mode CPL3, flat DS, 64-bit address/operand, aligned
canonical WB address, 4 KiB user read/write mappings and stated control-register
profile. Ordinary hits have no MMIO, split, LOCK or debug event. Privilege and
page policy are adapter inputs; FEX/Box64 application decoding does not implement
or validate that system profile. Compare software TSO with RCpc disabled and a
separate explicitly qualified hardware-TSO configuration. Record every effective
capability override; a host model name is insufficient.

| Fixture family | Exact x86 instruction bytes | Count |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Bare load | `48 8b 03` — MOV RAX,[RBX] | 1 |
| Bare store | `48 89 03` — MOV [RBX],RAX | 1 |
| Bare alias load | `48 8b 00` — MOV RAX,[RAX] | 1 |
| Live-state load/store/alias, three fixtures | `66 0f ef c1` PXOR XMM0,XMM1; `48 01 d1` ADD RCX,RDX; one MOV above; `0f 94 c2` SETZ DL; `f3 0f 7f 07` MOVDQU [RDI],XMM0 | 5 each |

The later vector store keeps the vector result live; it is not the fault site
being compared. This is six fixtures × two ordering configurations × three
codegens, at most **36 primary code objects**. Three separately labelled NOP
controls may diagnose tool header treatment, but must not replace raw counts.
Cap block decoding at the stated instruction count and retain the exact mapped
input extent; reject unexpected extra guest instructions rather than silently
comparing different blocks. No fixture is executed in this exploration.

For each marked MOV site derive three symbolic paths: hit completion; resident
repair preserving the native continuation; genuine guest fault before the MOV
commits. A completed serviced access must also deliver its result without
replaying a store. These are behavioral cases, not a requirement to insert
messages or host calls on ordinary accesses. Bare cases carry arbitrary incoming
flags/vector state. Live-state cases require the completed PXOR result, the ADD
result and its flag recipe to survive a MOV fault; load RAX must retain its old
value. The alias case must recover the original address independently of any
destination update. Retain all unaffected architectural state too.

The recovery worksheet must name, at the faulting native instruction, the guest
origin, address, commit phase and location of every live architectural value
(native register, resident slot, constant or reconstruction formula). Include
ADD’s CF/PF/AF/ZF/SF/OF, vector lane contents, register allocation clobbers and
helper/exit state. An absent recipe is **unknown**, not “zero spills.” A full
native frame can support repair; it does not by itself supply canonical guest
state for an architectural exception. Source/disassembly dataflow is the intended
proof method here; runtime fault injection belongs to a later separately bounded
validation.

#### Concrete entry points and required diagnostic changes

**FEX:** the existing path is
`python3 Scripts/InstructionCountParser.py fixture.json fixture.instcountci`,
then `Bin/CodeSizeValidation fixture.instcountci counts.json`.
The JSON `Instructions` entries contain `x86Insts`; `Features` supplies bitness,
host feature enables/disables and environment variables. The parser uses NASM;
compare its output bytes against the table. `CompileAndGetStats` maps a 512 MiB
virtual region, sets up the fixture and calls `CTX->CompileRIPCount` with the
instruction count, without executing it. Initialization still needs the tool’s
dummy signal delegator, syscall handler and thread/core context.

Do not treat its published count as full emitted cost: the tool calculates a
NOP header, removes that header, and trims from `udf #0x420f` through the tail.
It defaults TSO off unless the feature configuration enables it, and forces LSE
atomics and the preserve-all ABI. Capture those choices explicitly; parameterize
forced choices if they contradict the shared fixture configuration. The proposed
diagnostic change tees the raw message before `ParseMessage` and dumps the
compiled bytes, full boundaries and relocation records at the backend return
where `DebugData->HostCodeSize`/`CodeData` are available. Retain allocation/state
mapping as well; sanitized assembly alone cannot establish a recovery recipe.
The precise allocation diagnostic hook is an outstanding harness task, not an
already available public output format.

**QEMU:** `tb_gen_code(CPUState *, TCGTBCPUState)` is the bounded compilation
entry; the cflags count bounds guest instructions. It requires the memory lock,
initialized thread-local TCG context, valid executable guest mappings and CPU
translation state; overflow can exit through CPU-loop machinery. Instrument the
owning initialized TCG worker to compile the fixture and export `tb->tc.ptr`,
`tb->tc.size`, search/recovery data and live-state allocation information before
entering generated code. Preserve the valid exit context even in compile-only
mode. Existing output-assembly logging is useful but not a complete state map.
A paused `qemu-system-x86_64 -machine pc -accel tcg -S -display none -nodefaults`
is only a possible initialization shell: **that command alone does not compile
these fixtures**. The exact worker callback and initialization ordering must be
identified in the harness readiness gate; calling `tb_gen_code` from arbitrary
main-thread code is not the proposed method. Do not select QEMU’s PC machine as
the product architecture because the diagnostic harness uses its initialization.

**Box64:** `FillBlock64(addr, is32bits, inst_max, is_new, noalt)` provides the
compile hook, with `is32bits=0`, fixture count, `is_new=0`, `noalt=1`. Its calling
contract includes initialized `my_context`, memory protection bookkeeping,
dynarec locking and signal handling. The `CreateDBnoAlt` wrapper demonstrates
those dependencies but supplies its own instruction bound; the harness must
preserve the wrapper contract with the fixture count. Dump emitted bytes plus
per-instruction native offsets, flag and vector allocation/cache state after
pass 3 and before transient instruction metadata is freed; retain architecture
metadata with its generation enabled.

Stopping only before `emulate` is insufficient: `initialize` calls
`LoadNeededLibs`, relocates ELF/PLT and calls `RunDeferredElfInit`, which can run
guest initialization before the main `DynaRun`. The proposed harness branches
before those operations, after arranging the minimal context and explicitly
mapped fixture/protection data. Use an audited minimal static input without an
interpreter, dependencies, constructors or IFUNC, and clear preload/config
inputs. Add an execution-entry failure guard and avoid normal guest finalizers.
This requires a diagnostic patch; no inspected upstream CLI guarantees the
requested compile-only behavior. If initialization still requires guest
execution, record that concrete failure and stop this candidate’s run rather
than quietly executing the fixture.

#### Platform readiness, retained artifacts and stopping rule

Read-only inspection found Darwin arm64, Clang and Docker CLI, but no CMake,
Ninja, Meson or these generator binaries on PATH. Both configured Docker
endpoints (`orbstack` and `default`) failed because their Unix sockets were
absent. This establishes no currently accessible Docker Linux worker; it is not
a sandbox denial or proof that Linux provisioning is impossible. No daemon was
started or image downloaded. Retained source files are partial audit copies,
not complete build trees with dependencies.

[FEX’s pinned build configuration](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/CMakeLists.txt)
requires 64-bit Linux or Windows and rejects Darwin; Linux requires Clang and
Python, with further pinned external dependencies. Its CodeSizeValidation target
is not gated on BUILD_TESTING. A supported Linux AArch64 worker is the simplest
common proposed observation host; it need not be M4 because these are emission
observations under declared feature settings, not timing results. A prospective
FEX configure uses Clang, `BUILD_FEXCONFIG=OFF`, `BUILD_TESTING=OFF`,
`ENABLE_VIXL_DISASSEMBLER=ON`, `ENABLE_LTO=OFF`, then builds target
`CodeSizeValidation`. This is a recipe to verify, not an executed successful
command. QEMU’s Meson supports Darwin, but that does not make FEX build there.
[Box64’s CMake](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/CMakeLists.txt)
has ARM64 and an M1/Asahi option; that is not evidence of a native Darwin path.
Use a complete pinned checkout and its declared dependencies, not the audit
folder, for each proposed build.

The follow-on exploration must retain fixture bytes/hashes, all effective options,
compiler/build versions, source/submodule pins, diagnostic patches and build logs;
raw generated bytes and disassembly with full entry/tail boundaries; available
IR/allocation/relocation/recovery metadata; and the symbolic worksheet above.
Keep three columns: **observed upstream output**, **derived recovery recipe**,
and **proposed captive-adapter changes**. Count ordinary-site ordering,
materialization and transfer operations separately from entry/tail overhead;
unknown guards/spills cannot be priced as zero. Do not rank by audited source
lines, tool-normalized counts or build inconvenience.

**Single next bounded decision action, unstarted:** after independent method
review, use a 30-minute readiness gate to establish an accessible supported Linux
AArch64 worker, complete pinned source/dependency availability, and the exact
QEMU worker/Box64 pre-execution diagnostic entry. Readiness must name required
packages, download sizes and instrumentation before provisioning or building;
this record does not authorize an unbounded image download or VM setup. If ready,
propose one at-most-120-minute compile-only exploration with two build jobs,
8 GiB memory ceiling and the 36-object limit above; stop a candidate on an unmet
initialization/no-execution invariant or missing recovery metadata, retaining
the failure and its smallest remedy. No build or execution is part of this
completed method pass. The selection-changing output is whether a precise
live-state recipe needs mandatory ordinary-path materialization under the same
adapter assumptions, not which unadapted tool prints the smallest MOV.

**Review closure:** independent findings Return 14 accepts claim 24’s source

Linux package manifest.

Box64's [CMake configuration](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/CMakeLists.txt)
requires Python >=3.7 and a C/CMake toolchain; its ordinary Linux target links
libc, math, dl, pthread, resolv and util. The shared native Linux worker requires
these development/toolchain inputs and complete source. Optional desktop plugin
or wrapped guest libraries are not automatically build prerequisites of this
compile-only diagnostic target. A concrete distribution/package set remains

**Download sizes:** HEAD requests to the three pinned GitHub codeload tarball
URLs returned HTTP 200 but no Content-Length. No archive body was downloaded.
The repositories' compressed sizes, recursive dependency transfer and Linux
package download totals are therefore **unknown**, not zero and not estimated

files, 28,311 bytes total: QEMU MTTCG worker, accelerator operations,

DNS initially failed; the approved public-source retry succeeded. A later
provisioning/fetch exploration must enforce a transfer cap and retain partial
failures rather than rely on absent server size metadata.

**Proposed exact QEMU diagnostic seam:** use a normal queued callback on the
initialized, paused MTTCG CPU, not an arbitrary main-thread call and not an
already-exclusive callback that would nest an exclusive region. The pinned
[MTTCG worker](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/tcg-accel-ops-mttcg.c)
registers RCU/TCG thread state, assigns `current_cpu`, signals creation, then
processes CPU events before deciding whether execution is allowed.
[`async_run_on_cpu` and `process_queued_cpu_work`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/cpu-common.c)
provide the normal callback route; exclusive queued work has distinct BQL and
exclusive-region handling. Schedule only after machine initialization and mapped
fixture/state setup, while normal vCPU execution remains paused.

Place the diagnostic routine in `cpu-exec.c`, where the private cleanup helper
is available. Its concrete starting skeleton is
[`cpu_exec_step_atomic`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/cpu-exec.c):
local `sigsetjmp(cpu->jmp_env)`, own exclusive region/running marker,
`get_tb_cpu_state`, fixture-selected cflags, lookup/generation and
`cpu_exec_longjmp_cleanup` on exceptional exit. Do **not** copy the atomic
execution helper’s clearing of `CF_PARALLEL`: exclusive host compilation does
not make the matched SMP guest single-threaded. Preserve the fixture’s selected
parallel-memory semantics, replace the entire `CF_COUNT_MASK` field with the
fixture instruction count (do not OR `1` into an existing count), and explicitly
set `CF_NO_GOTO_TB | CF_NO_GOTO_PTR` as this diagnostic’s declared no-chain
configuration. Record these choices with the other effective options. Replace the **entire**
`cpu_exec_enter`/`trace_exec_tb`/`cpu_tb_exec`/`cpu_exec_exit` execution section
with artifact export. The callback must drop BQL before entering this exclusive
skeleton and reacquire it before returning to queued-work processing. Preserve
page-lock cleanup and running/exclusive balance on every exit; stop the batch on
translation faults instead of retrying indefinitely. Copying only the normal
return path is invalid. This narrows the implementation seam but does not prove
an unwritten harness correct. Exact state/page setup and all diagnostic patch
branches must be inspected before its compile-only run.

**Proposed exact Box64 seam:** in `initialize` in the pinned
[`core.c`](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/core.c),
branch immediately after `thread_set_emu(emu)` and before symbol export,
preloads, `LoadNeededLibs`, relocations and `RunDeferredElfInit`. This location
already has `NewX64Emu`, initial stack and `SetupX64Emu`, and avoids the downstream
constructor path. Restrict the diagnostic input as in Supplement N and audit
all earlier initialization branches; a location before the obvious constructors
does not prove every earlier configuration path harmless. Explicitly register
fixture permissions before the locked `FillBlock64` path, export the pass-3
transient allocation state and block data, and terminate without returning to
normal guest initialization/finalization. Execution-entry guards remain required.
FEX retains its existing `CompileRIPCount` tool; raw output/allocation diagnostics
still require a reviewed small patch. None of these patches was written here.

**Readiness conclusion:** not ready to execute the matched emission batch in the
inspected environment. The first practical dependency is access to a supported
Linux worker; the next is a bounded complete-source/toolchain acquisition and
reviewable diagnostic patch, not further generic interface research. This is no
selection or rejection of any code generator. The precise hooks above reduce
what must be invented, but do not convert the method into execution evidence.

**Decision consequence and nonexperimental alternative:** the unavailable worker

A source-derived allocation/recovery construction for the fixed fixtures can
