### CLM-03-02-27 — Matched recovery exposes state contracts, not a scalar instruction-count winner

**Source-backed findings and conditional construction:** same QEMU/FEX/Box64
pins and Supplement N fixtures, with the fault at the scalar MOV and subsequent
SETZ/MOVDQU not executed. No emitted object or runtime result was obtained. This
pass separates a recoverable architectural value from the upstream representation
of that value. In particular, preserving every eager flag computation is not a
necessary consequence of preserving every architectural flag.

#### Common fault-state recipe for this fixture

Let `s` be post-ADD RCX, `b` the still-unmodified RDX, and
`a=(s-b) mod 2^64`. At the marked MOV, the six arithmetic flags can be
reconstructed as follows: CF is unsigned `s < b`; ZF is `s == 0`; SF is bit 63
of `s`; OF is bit 63 of `~(a^b) & (a^s)`; AF is bit 4 of `a^b^s`; PF is even
parity of the low byte of `s`. This is an algebraic inference for **ADD RCX,RDX
with RDX still intact**, not a general deoptimization algorithm. All other flag
bits retain their incoming values. SETZ has not yet overwritten DL at this site.

The fault state also requires the completed PXOR result in XMM0, the unchanged
other vector state (including upper lanes untouched by legacy PXOR), and old RAX
for either load fixture. `MOV RAX,[RAX]` must use that old value as the faulting
address. For stores, RAX is the source and the store remains uncommitted in the
pre-access guest-fault case. The bare fixtures retain arbitrary incoming flags
and vector state through the selected entry ABI. They cannot borrow the ADD
reconstruction formula because they contain no ADD.

Thus a native frame plus locations for `s`, `b`, XMM0, old RAX and unaffected
state can support an exceptional-path reconstruction without six ordinary-path
flag stores. Whether those locations survive actual lowering is a separate
source obligation below. The candidate guard/service lowering must preserve
these values or update the recovery map at every clobber. Saving native state
only after destroying a needed value is not recovery.

#### QEMU TCG: canonical-state baseline is a liveness contract

The pinned [x86 emitter](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc)
`gen_ADD`/`prepare_update2_cc` records the sum and second operand with
`CC_OP_ADDQ`. PXOR uses `BINARY_INT_GVEC` and `tcg_gen_gvec_xor` on CPU-state
offsets; the [gvec expansion](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/tcg-op-gvec.c)
loads source state and writes destination state through vector/scalar expansion
or the corresponding helper fallback. The vector result is not merely an
untracked native register carried across the MOV.

The [memory-op flags](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/include/tcg/tcg-opc.h)
mark qemu_ld/st as side-effecting. In the pinned
[`tcg.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/tcg.c),
`liveness_pass_1` invokes `la_global_sync`; output sync bits then cause canonical
stores through allocation/indirect-global lowering. **At this pin,
`sync_globals` itself asserts memory coherence; it is not the function that
emits all those stores.** Calling that assertion or reusing its name after
replacing qemu_ld/st does not recreate the earlier liveness obligation.

The retained baseline therefore recovers modified RCX and the deferred ADD
inputs from canonical CPU state, XMM0 from its state backing, and unmodified
RAX/RDX/vector/other flags from their canonical or specified entry locations.
[Instruction-start metadata](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/translate.c)
and [`x86_restore_state_to_opc`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/tcg-cpu.c)
restore the guest PC and known CC selector, rather than reconstructing arbitrary
register values from the native PC. With CF_PCREL its incoming PC-page contract
still applies. Scalar load writeback follows successful access; the previous RAX
must remain recoverable before that writeback, including the alias fixture.

**Required retained ordinary-path work:** canonical coherence for dirty globals
at the side-effect boundary and the vector state write required by this gvec
baseline. This establishes required state effects, not a counted number of
AArch64 stores: paired stores, existing coherence, register allocation and helper
choices affect actual instructions. A captive TCG adapter may remove redundant
canonicalization only by replacing this with a sound location/recipe contract;
it cannot call that removal a free consequence of direct RAM.

#### FEX: partial static-register publication exists before each instruction

The newly inspected pinned
[`Core.cpp`, `ContextImpl::GenerateIR`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Core.cpp)
unconditionally calls `FlushRegisterCache(true)` before dispatching each guest
instruction, then emits a GuestOpcode marker for potential side effects. The
comment describes the flush as an allocation heuristic and notes its potential
exception-handling significance. This is stronger evidence than assuming state
remains wholly deferred until block exit, but weaker than a promised precise
system-fault ABI.

[`FlushRegisterCache`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h)
with SRAOnly publishes written GPRs, PF/AF and the first sixteen vector registers
to fixed-register IR operations and processes deferred NZCV. It does not mean a
full memory spill or commit every non-SRA context field. For the live fixture,
this supplies a pre-MOV publication point for the ADD result and preceding PXOR
result. The pinned [flag builder](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher/Flags.cpp)
`CalculateFlags_ADD` creates ADD/NZCV, PF from the result and AF from the operand
XOR; these are representations, not six precomputed flag bytes.

The normal AArch64 (not Arm64EC) layout in
[`Arm64Emitter`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/ArchHelpers/Arm64Emitter.cpp)
supplies fixed GPR mappings, XMM0 in the first fixed vector register (v16), and
PF/AF fixed registers declared in its
[header](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/ArchHelpers/Arm64Emitter.h).
A conditional recipe reads `s`/`b` from the post-ADD/unchanged GPR locations,
XMM0 from its committed fixed vector location and old RAX from its pre-load
location, then uses the common formula. The native NZCV/PF/AF representation is
an alternative only where its pointwise mapping is established. Incoming
unaffected state remains under the entry ABI; SRA-only publication is not proof
of all AVX/x87/context recovery. The v16 location refers to the lower XMM
value in the stated normal-AArch64 layout. Native SVE versus non-SVE and
wider-vector eviction branches were not resolved by this fixture; exposed upper
lanes remain a required independent preservation condition, not inferred from
the low-128-bit mapping.

**Exact unresolved branch, not a blanket FEX rejection:** the
[constrained allocator](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/IR/Passes/RegisterAllocationPass.cpp)
uses preferred/tied registers, inserts spills when a class has no available
register, evicts fixed-register occupants at SRA loads/stores, removes trivial
operations and performs `TryPostRAMerge`. In particular it can remove a previous
StoreRegister/move and rewrite its consumer. The frontend publication point
alone does not fix the physical location at the later faulting load. Closing
this fixture needs the optimized node order, kill bits and physical assignment
through that pre-MOV publication/address/load sequence, including any intervening
GuestOpcode node and merge boundary. `SSAToReg`/spill bookkeeping is cleared at
the end of allocation; it is not automatically retained as a recovery table.

**Required retained ordinary-path work:** the current frontend requests partial
fixed-register publication; coalescing can remove actual moves. No full spill or
extra six flag stores has been shown necessary for this fixture. The adapter
must either make the publication and no-clobber interval an explicit allocation
invariant, or retain accurate per-site value locations/recipes. Claiming all
fixed registers already contain the right fault state without inspecting that
interval would skip the decisive allocator step.

#### Box64: fixed GPRs and cached-vector metadata, with a tail-dependent flag policy

The pinned [scalar emitter](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_00.c)
ADD register form updates its mapped guest destination; MOV uses the mapped
GPR source/destination and effective-address path. In the selected base-only
scalar forms, the earlier RCX sum and RDX source are not the MOV destination;
they provide the common arithmetic recovery inputs. A non-writeback faulting
native load must preserve old RAX in the alias case; any new guard/address
lowering must preserve that original-address contract too.

The [PXOR emitter](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_660f.c)
uses `sse_get_reg(..., forwrite=1)` and VEOR. The
[cache helper](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_helper.c)
marks XMM0 writable/dirty in the NEON cache; the intervening scalar ADD/MOV do
not themselves compute a new vector value. The
[architecture metadata producer and consumer](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_arch.c)
record writable SSE values and deferred/native flag state; `adjust_arch` selects
the previous completed instruction's state and copies mapped vector registers
from the native frame. Its Linux ucontext consumer is a semantic reference to
adapt to a resident native frame, not code that can be called unchanged in EL1.
Enable/retain the architecture metadata; NOARCH settings cannot silently remove
this recovery premise. Bare entry-state recovery remains distinct from the
previous-instruction path.

The [ADD helper](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_emit_math.c)
conditionally writes deferred `op1`, `op2`, `res` and a deferred-operation tag
when `gen_flags` requires X_PEND; native flag use is conditional too. That is
not an unconditional four-store cost for every ADD. In
[`dynarec_native.c`](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/dynarec_native.c),
`updateNeed` derives these bits backwards. At the instruction-count boundary,
[`dynarec_native_pass.c`](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/dynarec_native_pass.c)
calls `interblock_flags_needed`, which can inspect instructions beyond that
boundary according to mapping/protection/configuration. Merely setting inst_max
to five does not completely specify the flag-emission fixture.

**Exact unresolved input:** record the post-block byte/protection window and
interblock-policy inputs, or deliberately force a declared conservative
flag-preservation boundary. The latter changes the diagnostic configuration and
must not be presented as the original unconstrained cost. Independently, retain
the actual per-instruction native-offset/cache record and verify that the MOV's
pre-access prefix preserves the previous completed instruction's vector mapping.
A new captive guard that borrows those registers invalidates the recipe unless
its own map records the clobber. The algebraic ADD recipe avoids depending on
which eager flags `updateNeed` retained, but does not eliminate these value-location
and instruction-origin obligations.

**Required ordinary-path additions:** no new unconditional full vector spill or
six eager flag stores follows from this fixture. Existing conditional deferred
flag stores, cache moves and barriers remain whatever its declared policy emits.
Adapting and retaining the recovery metadata/native-frame decoder is real work
and storage, even if exceptional materialization need not run on the hit path.

#### Bounded result and selection consequence

| Generator | Recoverable-state construction supported here | Remaining decisive seam |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TCG baseline | Canonical GPR/deferred-CC/vector state plus guest-origin/CC-selector restoration | Preserve side-effect liveness when changing memory IR; derive any reduced-store replacement explicitly. Actual instruction counts remain unmeasured. |
| FEX | Pre-instruction SRA publication provides a candidate fixed-register state; ADD flags can use sum/source reconstruction | Prove post-optimization/post-allocation location and no-clobber interval through the marked MOV, including trivial/merge removals; retain a map if it is not invariant. |
| Box64 | Mapped GPR sum/source plus previous-completed-instruction vector/cache metadata and native frame | Fix tail-dependent flag-policy inputs and map the exact pre-access prefix; adapt the metadata consumer to the resident frame. |

This is a narrower and more useful comparison than “TCG spills; the others do
not.” All three can use exceptional-path algebra for this ADD if locations are
preserved. The retained TCG baseline already requires canonical coherence;
FEX already requests ordinary-path fixed-register publication; Box64 already
has conditional deferred-flag stores and architecture records. None is free,
and none of these findings selects a generator or establishes arbitrary
instruction/helper/async recovery. Full native-frame retention supports retry of
an internal repair; guest-fault delivery still needs the architectural recipe
and exact commit boundary. A completed serviced store must never be replayed.

**Single next bounded decision action, unstarted:** after findings review, spend
at most 45 minutes constructing one explicit **fault-state checkpoint invariant**
for the FEX MOV path, because its allocator can erase the apparent frontend
publication. Use the existing source to specify which optimized values must be
kept live and where the native-fault boundary is placed; determine whether a
recovery annotation can survive allocation without forced memory spills or must
force named register moves/stores. Hold TCG's canonical baseline and Box64's
metadata recipe fixed as comparison references. Stop at the complete conditional
checkpoint contract or a concrete destructive-allocation counterexample and its
smallest remedy. This closes a selection-changing uncertainty rather than another
helper-family inventory; it does not authorize a build or renew Linux discovery.

**Review closure:** independent findings Return 17 accepts claim 27’s scoped
source facts and conditional fixture recipes. No generated output, measured
count, runtime recovery proof or generator ranking is accepted. The FEX checkpoint
construction above remains unstarted.


## Supplement R — FEX checkpoint invariant through allocation

**Completed September 20; at most 45 minutes, source/design only; scoped acceptance in memory findings Return 18.** Keep Supplement
N's fixtures and exact FEX pin. Define a concrete fault-state annotation that
survives optimization/allocation, or identify named compulsory materialization
and its trigger. Distinguish a proposed compiler invariant from current upstream
behavior. The selected low-128-bit SSE trace does not prove wider AVX/SVE state;
retain all exposed upper/unaffected state as an explicit input/preservation
obligation. Stop at a complete conditional contract or a concrete destructive
allocation counterexample with its smallest remedy. No build, runtime or worker
discovery; independent review follows.


### CLM-03-02-28 — Select allocation-visible recovery roots with a native-site map

**Conditional design choice, not an upstream implementation claim:** for the
sampled FEX MOV adapter, select a recovery annotation whose required values are
real optimizer/allocation uses, plus a map at the actual native fault site. Do
not rely on the incidental pre-instruction SRA flush or require unconditional
full canonical spills. Values that cannot remain in registers receive ordinary
resident spill slots with explicit recovery lifetime. This supplies a finite
compiler/runtime interface; actual emission and the complete ISA remain unproved.

**Explicit configuration:** normal AArch64 ABI, native SVE/SVE256 disabled for
this construction, RCpc disabled in the software-TSO variant, and the separately
qualified hardware-TSO variant from Supplement N. Guest ISA/profile is unchanged.
Legacy PXOR modifies only the low 128 bits; exposed upper lanes, x87/MXCSR and
other unaffected state must have complete entry bindings and remain recoverable.
Disabling native SVE does not hide guest AVX or authorize dropping upper halves.
The proposed non-SVE adapter keeps upper halves in explicitly identified resident
state when they are not held in native registers. Entry from any path that held
them elsewhere must publish them first. Native SVE/wider-vector allocation needs
its own binding scheme and is not accepted by the v16 low-XMM trace.

#### Source constraint and a concrete failed annotation

At the inspected pin, [`IR.json`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/IR/IR.json)
defines `GuestOpcode` with only a guest-entry offset and side-effect marker; it
has no architectural value roots. `StoreRegister` has a value operand, while
ordinary `LoadMem` does not itself declare the same side-effect property as
`LoadMemPair`/stores. The selected memory operation must retain its guest-fault
effect even if its loaded result would otherwise become dead. This is a required
adapter semantic, not a claim that these particular live-result fixtures were
optimized away upstream.

[`PassManager`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/IR/PassManager.cpp)
runs the x87 and dead-flag optimizations before register allocation in the normal
configuration. The [flag pass](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/IR/Passes/RedundantFlagCalculationElimination.cpp)
uses flag dependencies and SSA uses/side effects; a guest-PC marker is not an
implicit use of every earlier architectural value. The
[allocator](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/IR/Passes/RegisterAllocationPass.cpp)
marks the last backwards-seen RA argument as killed, frees killed source registers
in the forward pass before destination assignment, may spill/remap values, and
removes trivial/merged moves. `SpillReg` records a slot and emits a spill before
freeing its register; those maps are normally cleared at pass end.

**Counterexample to the naive proposal:** placing a standalone
`RecoveryUse(value)` only *before* MOV, with no later use of that value, lets the
current backwards scan mark it killed at that marker. The forward scan then
frees its register before allocating the MOV/address sequence. An external map
that still names that register is no longer protected from reuse. Even when a
particular short fixture happens not to reuse it, that annotation does not prove
the invariant. This is a source-derived failure of the proposed annotation,
not a demonstrated defect in an upstream executed fixture. A guest-PC marker
alone is weaker still. Capturing locations only at a later recovery marker is
also insufficient: allocation can insert a reload between the faulting access
and that marker, so its locations need not exist at the fault.

#### Concrete selected representation and lifetime

A proposed `FaultSite` is attached to the fault-bearing access, with guest origin,
pre-access commit phase, recipe ID, original effective-address value and a bounded
set of architectural value bindings. Each binding is one of:

- a constant or a pure bounded reconstruction expression over named roots;
- an allocation-visible SSA value with a specified width;
- an already authoritative resident field/slot, with owner and lifetime;
- an unchanged architectural register binding from the entry ABI, retained until
  explicitly replaced or published to another listed location.

A binding is not “whatever remains in a fixed register.” Every path that writes
or evicts that register must update the binding or preserve its old value. The
entry table covers the entire exposed state, including upper/unaffected lanes;
the MOV recipe replaces only the values changed by its completed prefix. Helpers
and block links must supply that entry contract, not assume stale resident fields
contain current values.

For this fixture the changed-prefix roots are `s` (RCX sum), `b` (unchanged
RDX), and the low-128-bit PXOR result. Retain old RAX for load/alias recovery and
RBX or old RAX for the original address; store source RAX is retained too. Aliased
identical values may share a root. The six arithmetic flags use claim 27's
inverse-ADD formula; other flags keep their entry bindings. Bare MOV recipes use
the incoming flag bindings, not that formula. No arbitrary guest-memory load,
helper execution or allocation is allowed while evaluating a recovery expression.

**Optimizer lifetime rule:** emit nonexecuting recovery uses immediately *after*
the fault-bearing access, before any architectural result commit/next guest
operation. They keep each needed SSA value live through the access. A simple
realization is one side-effect-retained `RecoveryUse(SiteID, SSAValue)` per root;
these are compiler metadata operations, not native calls. The attached access
itself owns the pre-access fault effect and cannot be removed/moved across the
checkpoint as a dead pure load. Rewriting a root to an equivalent constant or
pure expression is legal only if its recipe changes with it. Reordering,
combining or deleting the access must preserve the same guest fault/commit
contract; the scoped adapter can forbid such transformations at this site.

**Allocator location rule:** ordinary RA uses of those post-access roots prevent
last-use retirement before the access. They need not all occupy native registers:
`SpillReg` may move a value into a resident slot. Capture the site's map from the
actual allocation state at the access after preceding eviction/spill/fill and
destination preparation, *before* any later reload for RecoveryUse. For each
root, choose the currently valid physical register, initialized spill slot or
constant, and freeze that descriptor in the code object. RecoveryUse is a **metadata-use
class** in the adapted allocator: it participates in backwards kill/next-use
analysis but does not require a spilled root to be filled into a register merely
to consume that marker. Its forward handling releases the recovery lifetime from
its current register/slot binding without ordinary argument-fill emission.
Otherwise the unchanged `AnySpilledBeforeThisInstruction` source-fill loop could
insert useless post-access reloads, contradicting the selected no-reload-unless-
ordinary-use rule. This is an explicit allocator change, not a benefit obtained
by adding a generic no-op opcode alone. Track fixed-register
evictions too; `SSAToReg` alone can name an obsolete register after spilling,
so consult current occupancy and slot validity. Export before allocator scratch
maps are cleared. A map consumer must not point into those scratch vectors.

**Merge/clobber rule:** post-RA trivial removal and `TryPostRAMerge` may proceed
only if all affected site bindings remain correct. At this bounded adapter seam,
make fault sites/RecoveryUse operations explicit merge boundaries rather than
assuming the present two-op merge knows about recipes. A changed producer or
pre-access move requires remapping. The backend declares its address-generation,
guard and call scratch registers; none may clobber a mapped root without first
updating the recipe or spilling it to a reserved slot. This requirement covers
implicit NZCV clobbers only if a recipe actually uses NZCV; the sampled inverse
ADD recipe does not.

#### Native fault, repair and success boundaries

The pinned [`MemoryOps.cpp`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MemoryOps.cpp)
scalar i64 LoadMem uses one LDR after address selection; the declared software
TSO branch uses its selected scalar transfer and patch slot. Store lowering has
its corresponding transfer. Bind the map to the native memory instruction's
address, not merely the guest-op start. Any added guard fault/slow gateway needs
its own pre-access site or a proven identical map. Address generation precedes
this site and must be included in the clobber check. The map records the load
result location and success continuation separately from old guest RAX.

On resident repair, preserve the full native continuation and pins; resume an
unperformed access only when the repair result permits it. On an architectural
guest fault, freeze the native register/stack frame, materialize the mapped
pre-access guest state and inject the fault at the MOV origin. The aligned scalar
non-writeback transfer's pre-access exception contract is a platform premise;
this proposal does not prove arbitrary split/vector partial faults. A completed
service consumes its result and enters the success continuation; a store is not
replayed. An asynchronous pause after successful access retains completion phase
and resumes forward, rather than misusing the pre-access recipe. Guest event
injection remains at the existing canonical boundaries.

Resident spill slots and maps stay pinned with the native continuation until
repair/success or completed exceptional materialization no longer refers to them.
The map uses code-object-relative native offsets plus relocation/lifetime identity,
not host compiler pointers. Recovery executes without a host request or another
guest memory access. Unexpected private-runtime faults remain containment events.

#### Ordinary-path work and conditional closure

This selected annotation does **not** mandate a full state spill per instruction,
a host/shim call per load, or materialization of six arithmetic flags. It does
mandate root liveness and a truthful per-site map. Extra moves/stores occur when
preserving those roots conflicts with allocation, scratch use or a helper ABI:

| Concrete conflict | Smallest permitted ordinary-path remedy |
| --- | --- |
| A root register must be overwritten before the access | Copy to an available nonclobbered register or spill that root at its declared width before the overwrite; update the map. |
| No register remains for a retained root | Keep an initialized resident spill slot alive through the site; reload only if later ordinary computation needs it. Recovery can read the slot directly. |
| A gateway/helper ABI clobbers mapped values | Publish only the affected roots to preserved registers/slots before entering it, or provide an equivalent native-frame capture before the clobber. |
| Exposed upper/vector state arrives outside the selected non-SVE resident binding | Publish the missing lanes at that entry transition; never infer their value from low XMM registers. |
| A transform cannot maintain site/root identity | Disable that transform for the bounded fault interval or materialize the affected state before it; do not silently discard the site. |

For the fixture, potential root stores have explicit widths: 64-bit scalar roots
and the 128-bit changed XMM value, plus any missing exposed entry state. Their
number is not inferred without allocation. Metadata has storage and code-lifetime
cost; extending liveness may increase register pressure. These costs remain
separate from ordinary executed instructions and exceptional-path flag arithmetic.

**Cost qualification:** keeping roots live through a post-access RecoveryUse is a
sufficient conservative construction, not minimum fault-edge liveness. For the
aligned non-writeback scalar load, a destination may safely alias old RAX because
a pre-access fault leaves that destination unchanged. Keeping old RAX artificially
live after successful access can prevent this safe coalescing and introduce a
copy or spill that a fault-only lifetime would not need. Such added operations
are costs of this selected conservative construction, not proved unavoidable
costs of precise recovery or FEX. A possible refinement ends that old-value
lifetime on the successful transfer edge while retaining it on the fault edge;
it must preserve the exact native instruction's no-writeback/fault contract and
all pre-access clobbers. No inspected allocator support for that refinement is
claimed, and the selected safe contract is unchanged. Matched cost comparisons
must label this distinction rather than rank generators by an avoidable lifetime
constraint.

**Conditional closure:** select this allocation-visible root/site contract as the
FEX MOV recovery adapter direction. It resolves the identified “frontend flush
versus physical fault state” interface: producer transformations preserve named
values, allocator exports valid locations, backend binds them at the exact fault
instruction, and resident recovery consumes that immutable map. This is a
reviewable design argument, not a requirement to finish the compiler before
choosing the interface. Existing upstream GuestOpcode/SRA flush is insufficient
without the specified adaptation. No source proof here establishes zero added
spills, a complete FEX system translator, or a ranking against TCG/Box64.

**Next bounded decision action, unstarted:** independent findings review of this
checkpoint contract, then one 45-minute matched **adapter decision** against
TCG's retained canonical-state path and Box64's existing metadata path. Compare
which upstream invariants each route must preserve/change to meet the same state,
access and code-lifetime requirements, and identify the smallest reversible
extraction experiment that would decide between them. Carry the explicit native
feature/profile assumptions and unresolved wider-instruction recovery forward;
do not reopen worker discovery or select a generator from this fixture alone.

**Review closure:** independent Return 18 accepts claim 28’s scoped source
constraints and conditional adapter choice, with no substantive return. Return 18’s narrow
recheck also accepts the post-access-lifetime cost qualification above; no new
source pass or runtime evidence accompanies it. The matched adapter
decision remains unstarted.


## Supplement S — matched adapter decision and reversible extraction

**Completed September 20; at most 45 minutes, existing evidence first; scoped acceptance in machine findings Return 19.** Compare
TCG canonical-state, FEX recovery-root and Box64 instruction-metadata adapters
against the same fixed host-codegen/captive-VM contract. Separate semantics
provenance, executed operations, metadata and runtime adaptation. Choose a
reversible extraction direction only where supported; otherwise name the decisive
uncertainty and smallest exploration. No worker discovery, build or runtime.
Independent machine findings review follows.


### CLM-03-02-29 — Choose a reversible FEX checkpoint extraction pilot, not a generator winner

**Inferred bounded decision from claims 21–28:** keep all three generators under
consideration. The evidence supports selecting a **throwaway FEX checkpoint
extraction pilot as the next experiment preparation**, because it addresses the
new producer/allocation/recovery interface specified in claim 28. This is an
information-gathering choice, not an inference that FEX has the cheapest port,
fastest generated code or best system semantics. TCG's canonical-state route
and Box64's existing instruction metadata remain explicit alternatives. No new
upstream source or execution finding is claimed by this comparison.

#### Same contract, different adaptation work

All rows assume host compilation, captive execution with an EL1 shim, checked
guest access authority, immutable code with explicit relocation/lifetime, precise
architectural faults, native continuation retention for repair, and guest event
injection at the established boundaries. Generated-code privilege, guest profile,
semantics/helper provenance and native capabilities remain separate parameters.
Native SVE is off in this sampled comparison; guest upper/unaffected state is
still mandatory. Qualified hardware TSO can remove only redundant ordering work,
not guest checks, recovery or authority. No row assumes a host exit on ordinary
RAM access.

| Contract dimension | TCG canonical path | FEX root-map path | Box64 metadata path |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| State at scalar MOV fault | Retain side-effect liveness and canonical GPR/deferred-CC/vector backing, plus PC/CC restoration | Preserve named architectural roots through transformations and allocation; export exact native-site locations | Retain mapped GPR state and prior-completed-instruction vector/flag records; adapt native-frame consumer |
| Ordinary-path work already evidenced | Required canonical coherence and gvec state effects; actual native store count unresolved | Existing partial SRA publication, plus any root-preservation moves/spills actually forced by the adapter | Conditional deferred-flag stores/cache moves and configured ordering; tail policy changes emitted flag work |
| Specific compiler change | Replace backend access lowering while preserving the fault-bearing IR contract; reducing synchronization needs a new proof | Add fault-effect/site identity and allocation-visible metadata uses; support spill-slot maps and merge/clobber rules | Bind existing instruction records to the precise pre-access prefix and added guards; ensure metadata includes any newly clobbered values |
| Resident recovery change | Consume canonical state plus origin selector; retain native repair continuation separately | Consume immutable location/recipe map from resident native frame/slots; evaluate bounded pure recipes | Replace Linux ucontext dependence with resident-frame decoding; maintain exact instruction/native-offset and cache-state identity |
| Lifetime/relocation | Keep TB/native continuation and its metadata valid across repair and linking | Keep maps, spill slots, code object and relocated site identities valid through completion | Keep block architecture records and secondary/call-return references valid; do not borrow TCG-only reference bounds |
| Unestablished extension | Cost/correctness after aggressive canonical-store removal or broader helper replacement | Precise maps across remaining instructions, helpers, split effects and native vector configurations | Pointwise state during multi-access instructions and completeness after system/guard adaptations |

These distinctions follow the pinned [TCG liveness pipeline](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/tcg.c),
[FEX frontend publication](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Core.cpp),
[FEX allocation](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/IR/Passes/RegisterAllocationPass.cpp),
and [Box64 metadata construction/consumption](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_arch.c)
already inspected in claims 27–28. They are differences in required interfaces,
not estimates from source-file size or number of audited findings.

**Semantics remain separate:** adopting any row does not choose its whole
upstream CPU/runtime. System instruction semantics, x86 walking, privilege,
startup32, FP/x87 behavior and helpers still require their selected component
contracts. In particular, reusing QEMU system helpers does not select TCG;
FEX's application decoder does not supply a system profile by implication;
Box64's metadata does not make its Linux signal runtime resident EL1 code.
The sampled inverse ADD recipe says nothing about FP compatibility. Existing
SSE NaN-policy remedies, x87/vector-state coverage, helper cleanup and partial
REP/vector operations remain material selection inputs. License implications
remain warnings attached to actually reused components, not an excuse to rank
unexamined implementations.

#### Why this pilot can change the decision

The unresolved question is whether a register-cached path can supply a truthful
fault map with localized compiler changes and bounded value retention, rather
than reverting to canonical state publication at each faulting access. Claim 28
selects a conditional interface but does not establish its actual extraction
burden or generated ordinary-path work. A pilot on the actual pinned FEX
frontend/allocator can falsify the assumption that the proposed site/root seam
is local, reveal required extra preserved values, or demonstrate a concrete
map for the fixture. Those outcomes change which adaptation deserves the next
comparative effort. They do not alone select or reject a generator family.

TCG's retained canonical path is the recovery baseline that a failed map
construction must still match semantically. Box64's existing metadata is the
alternative reference for what source-described state records already provide.
Neither is an emitted numerical control until actually produced under the same
contract. A within-FEX diagnostic cannot establish that FEX beats either one;
its unmodified-versus-annotated delta isolates the proposed new recovery mechanism.
A failure caused only by the conservative post-access lifetime is not evidence
that precise recovery intrinsically costs that much: safe fault-only coalescing
remains a qualified alternative under claim 28's caveat.

**Opportunity cost:** this pilot postpones work on broader system/FP/helper
adaptation, and a scalar success cannot settle those gaps. Cap it tightly. Do not
spend an open-ended campaign porting FEX's entire runtime to macOS merely to run
the diagnostic. Conversely, do not select TCG or Box64 because their inspected
source is more familiar or because the local Linux worker is unavailable.
The pilot is worth preparing only while it remains a small, reviewable change to
the named compiler seam; the stop conditions below prevent it becoming a product
implementation.

#### Achievable next deliverable and smallest subsequent experiment

**Next bounded action, unstarted: 45 minutes to prepare a disposable patch and
fixtures, with no build or service work.** Proposed ownership is a new research
artifact directory `experiments/track03/codegen-checkpoint/`, containing:
`README.md`, `fex-checkpoint.patch`, `fixtures.json`,
`expected-recovery.json`, and `source-manifest.json`. This names future files;
none is created by the present source/design pass. The patch targets copied,
pinned sources, not the product runtime or shared source-audit originals.

The existing partial audit tree already contains the principal patch targets:
`Core/Core.cpp`, `Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h`, `IR/IR.json`,
`IR/Passes/RegisterAllocationPass.cpp`, `Core/JIT/JIT.cpp`,
`Core/JIT/MemoryOps.cpp` and `Source/Tools/CodeSizeValidation/Main.cpp`.
Prepare the diagnostic behind an opt-in compile-only setting. Capture fixture
roots at the frontend seam, retain them through the metadata-use handling,
export the actual site/slot map before allocator scratch state is cleared, and
dump raw code/site boundaries from the emitter. If this needs an additional
shared diagnostic header, include it in the patch and manifest. IR-generated
interfaces must be regenerated using the pinned `FEXCore/Scripts/json_ir_generator.py`
in a later complete build; do not hand-edit generated headers or claim that the
partial source directory builds. Any missing small source dependency needed to
write a valid patch must be named explicitly and obtained at the same pin;
stop rather than replacing uninspected dependencies with invented APIs.

The expected map file is an **oracle specification**, not fabricated output. It
names the required values/widths and the inverse ADD recipe, old-RAX/address and
upper/unaffected-state obligations, plus allowed location kinds. It must not
invent expected physical register assignments. The reviewable static checks are:
no root dies before the site; a mapped spill slot is initialized before the
faulting native instruction; mapped registers are not clobbered by its prefix;
old RAX remains recoverable on the load fault; and all exposed unchanged state
has a valid binding. Exact hit/repair/fault and completed-store continuation
semantics remain required; emitted metadata alone cannot prove their runtime.

If a complete patch cannot be expressed within the budget, retain the partial
patch and the exact unresolved compiler dependency. That is useful extraction
evidence, not a conforming adapter. Do not respond by starting another generic
interface inventory or worker search. Review the producing patch before any
later decision-evidence run; an unbuilt patch cannot be accepted as emission,
recovery conformance or performance evidence.

The smallest later compile-only exploration would use the same six fixtures and
two declared ordering configurations, with **unmodified and annotated FEX**
controls: at most 24 primary objects, not the original 36-object three-generator
comparison. It must preserve raw bytes, effective settings, pre/post allocation
IR and site maps, and evaluate the symbolic oracle at the actual transfer. It
runs no guest block. Existing 120-minute/two-build-job/8-GiB bounds are ceilings,
not permission to proceed without a supported complete build environment and
its own retained run record. That environment remains unavailable in the recorded
session; patch preparation is nevertheless achievable from the available source.
No service discovery/provisioning is part of the next action. If exploratory
results later justify comparing execution costs, obtain matched TCG and Box64
artifacts instead of extrapolating their counts from this FEX-only control.

**Decision limit:** choose this reversible preparation direction; do not yet
choose the production code generator. The pilot targets one decision-changing
unknown—whether the proposed register-state recovery interface extracts cleanly
and what it forces—not a new claim that scalar emission settles system semantics,
FP, SMP or end-to-end performance.

### CLM-03-02-31 — One-fixture joint checkpoint diagnostic patch, unbuilt

**Exploratory preparation, not decision evidence.** The retained
[six-target standalone patch](../../../experiments/track03/codegen-checkpoint/fex-root-preparation.patch)
now connects frontend architectural roots, a metadata-use IR operation, an
allocator consumer and an exact native transfer-site diagnostic for the existing
`live_load` fixture. This is an actual unbuilt patch, not a successful extraction
or observed map. It is an alternative against pristine FEX at the existing pin,
not stacked on claim 30’s unchanged observation patch. Its
[target manifest](../../../experiments/track03/codegen-checkpoint/root-source-manifest.json)
and [README](../../../experiments/track03/codegen-checkpoint/README.md) identify
scope and remaining work. No production source changed. The retained `root-fixture.json` is the exact
one-test subset; the artifact README records the future parser/tool command shape,
which has not run.

The compile-only tool guard accepts only the exact 17-byte/five-instruction
fixture or its existing NOP control. Under `NATEMU_CHECKPOINT_ROOT_PREP`, the MOV
frontend captures post-ADD RCX, unchanged RDX, old RAX, address RBX and low-128-bit
post-PXOR XMM0. It appends `NatemuRecoveryUse` after the scalar LoadMemTSO and
before the RAX commit, carrying those roots plus the access result. Mode, width,
offset and native-feature assertions reject other lowering branches. These are
actual source changes, not executed IR. They do not establish all the fixture’s
memory assumptions or full exposed-state entry bindings. Independent review
identified that the initial draft’s `LOGMAN_THROW_A_FMT` guards compile out when
`ASSERTIONS_ENABLED` is off, as verified in the pinned
[logging header](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/include/FEXCore/Utils/LogManager.h).
All new diagnostic rejection gates were replaced with unconditional stderr plus
`abort` before the final static snapshot; existing upstream assertions are not
claimed as unconditional diagnostic guards.

The schema marks the operation side-effecting and supplies six SSA uses to the
backwards allocation analysis. The new forward consumer snapshots all five roots
before consuming kills, accepting an occupied register with matching SSA owner
or a unique spill slot whose spill was inserted before the access. It consumes
the normal next-use stream without metadata-only fills, guards invalid assignments
before checking occupancy, and replaces SSA arguments only after writing separate
location fields. The post-access uses conservatively retain old values through
the load; their cost is not minimum fault-edge liveness. Constants and unsupported
location forms reject rather than become guessed maps.

The consumer requires the immediately previous iterated and nontrivial node to be
the referenced LoadMemTSO; the selected load and checkpoint cannot merge across
that boundary. This is a narrow **reject-on-rewrite rule**, not a general optimizer-
stable fault-label scheme. The backend uses actual `GetReg`/`GetVReg` mappings for
fixed or general register classes and the existing spill-slot stride for SP-based
locations. It emits `NATEMU_SITE` immediately before the selected 64-bit LDAR,
with access node ID and code-object-relative cursor offset, and emits the five
root descriptors at the metadata marker. The following NOP is not the fault PC.
BEGIN/END records bracket emission attempts; a future consumer must reject
abandoned/restarted attempts and require a complete unique matching site/map.
No trace consumer or collected trace exists yet.

**Scope limit:** this connects the selected changed-state roots to a diagnostic
site; it does not yet export the full unchanged/upper state, all preserved flag
bits or the inverse-ADD recipe as a validated complete map. It has no resident
map consumer, repair path, fault injection, guest exception, immutable code-object
metadata or lifetime integration. Raw code export, qualified hardware-TSO mode,
other fixtures and annotated/unmodified comparisons remain unimplemented. The
new standalone patch does not implicitly inherit claim 30’s overlapping raw-INFO
tee. No count or timing follows from the number of patch lines or metadata fields.

Source interfaces at the pinned FEX revision are
[the MOV frontend](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.cpp),
[IR schema](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/IR/IR.json),
[schema generator](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Scripts/json_ir_generator.py),
[allocator](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/IR/Passes/RegisterAllocationPass.cpp),
[memory emitter](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MemoryOps.cpp),

and [compile-only tool](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/Source/Tools/CodeSizeValidation/Main.cpp).

### CLM-03-02-32 — Non-SVE AVX uses split state; unchanged backing is conditional

**Scoped source finding and proposed binding, not all-AVX correctness or runtime
acceptance.** FEX at `48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825` already has a
non-SVE split-vector frontend. The isolated i256 memory-backend branch is not
evidence that using FEX on the native non-SVE target requires inventing all AVX
lowering. A separate recovery gap is concrete: an upper half modified earlier in
the same block can remain in the frontend cache, so its resident backing cannot
be assumed current at the scalar fault site.

#### Actual component path and representation

| Inspected boundary | Source result | Consequence inside the captive design |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Decoder selection | `Decoder` selects the ordinary VEX tables when SupportsAVX and SupportsSVE256 are both true; SupportsAVX without SVE256 selects `VEXTableOps_AVX128` and its group table. | Native SVE absence selects an existing alternative frontend; it does not itself turn guest AVX off. |
| State layout | `CPUState` has `xmm.sse.data[16][2]` for low halves and separate `avx_high[16][2]` for non-converged high halves. The converged alternative uses `xmm.avx.data[16][4]`. | A guest YMM register is not necessarily one native register or one contiguous 32-byte slot. Component input must name which layout applies. |
| Register reads/writes | `AVX128_LoadXMMRegister/StoreXMMRegister` route low halves through the XMM register cache and high halves through context-cache indexes 48–63, mapped to `CPUState.avx_high`. | High halves can be SSA values awaiting publication, not only memory bindings. Each high half is 128 bits. |
| Sample 256-bit arithmetic | The selected table binds VPADDD to `AVX128_VectorALU(OP_VADD, i32Bit)`. A 256-bit source produces separate low/high i128 VAdd operations; the backend i128 path uses Q-register vector arithmetic. | This sampled AVX2 arithmetic needs no newly invented i256/non-SVE backend operation. It does not establish complete AVX2/FP coverage or total adaptation cost. |
| Sample vector memory | AVX128 source/result helpers use a 128-bit pair. Auto-TSO memory helpers select a pair load/store where allowed, otherwise two 128-bit accesses at offsets 0 and 16. | The previously inspected i256 LoadMemTSO/SVE256 branch is bypassed for this split route. Its multi-access effects still need the captive memory/recovery contract; the scalar pilot does not prove them. |
| State import/export | `SetXMMRegistersFromState` and `ReconstructXMMRegisters` explicitly split/join low/high arrays in the non-SVE256 branch. AVX128 save/restore/default helpers address high state separately. | There is concrete representation machinery to adapt; these host utilities and guest XSAVE-like helpers are not automatically a captive exception ABI or complete system XSAVE implementation. |

Primary sources: [decoder selection, lines 82–87](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Frontend.cpp#L82),
[state layout](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/include/FEXCore/Core/CoreState.h#L74),
[split helpers and arithmetic](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher/AVX_128.cpp#L24),
[VPADDD dispatch](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/X86Tables/VEXTables.cpp#L243),
[backend vector operations](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/VectorOps.cpp#L58),
[pair memory lowering](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h#L2549),
[host state conversion](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Core.cpp#L264),
and [AVX128 save/restore](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher/AVX_128.cpp#L1693).

The AArch64 host-feature initializer sets SupportsAVX independently of its SVE2
feature/width checks. FEX’s own CPUID setup copies that policy into its XCR0_AVX
bit, and `SupportsAVX()` then controls exposed AVX/AVX2 and related bits. These are
upstream user-runtime exposure choices, not the proposed captive guest’s CPUID,
CR4.OSXSAVE, XCR0 or #UD policy. Reusing the split generator does not require
importing that policy; turning off native SVE does not justify silently hiding
previously selected guest AVX state. AVX2 remains the charter’s ceiling, and the
actual exposed profile is independently selected. [Host-feature initialization](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/Source/Common/HostFeatures.cpp#L531),
[CPUID setup](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/CPUID.cpp#L421),
[SupportsAVX accessor](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/CPUID.h#L154),
[AVX2 exposure](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/CPUID.cpp#L679).

#### Binding the existing fixture without hiding upper state

For claim 31’s exact 64-bit `PXOR XMM0,XMM1; ADD RCX,RDX; MOV RAX,[RBX]` prefix,
legacy PXOR updates only the low XMM0 path under the non-SVE layout. The other
instructions do not update vector state. Thus all sixteen YMM high halves can
use **unchanged-entry backing** at `CPUState.avx_high[i]`, provided that backing
was initialized to the admitted guest’s current high halves and remains resident
and unchanged for this fixture. This is an explicit entry/boundary premise, not
inference from zero initialization, `GetGuestVectorLength()==128`, or the absence
of high roots in the five-root patch. An arbitrary high-half bit pattern must
survive; the proposed binding does not make it zero. No extra vector instruction
is required merely to name that already-valid backing. Establishing the backing
on entry has its own adaptation/cost obligation.

The low XMM0 value still comes from the post-PXOR root descriptor. Other unchanged
low vector lanes, GPRs, non-ADD flags, x87/MMX/MXCSR and control state must retain
explicit valid entry bindings; the guest PC and ADD recipe come from the existing
fixture contract. **Do not infer that all `CPUState` memory fields are current**:
normal low GPR/XMM publication uses fixed native registers, and direct block
chaining need not spill those fields to memory. An entry binding may be an
appropriately preserved native value or initialized private backing; if it can
be clobbered, it must itself participate in lifetime/recovery handling. This pass
supplies the concrete high-half backing option, not a complete export of all those
entry bindings by the current diagnostic. [Legacy vector XOR/write path](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher/Vector.cpp#L281),
[legacy write routing](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h#L1098),
[fixed versus context publication](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h#L1177),
[static-register memory spill as a distinct operation](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/ArchHelpers/Arm64Emitter.cpp#L681).

#### Counterexample to unconditional resident-upper recovery

Consider a permitted source-level block `VPADDD YMM0,YMM1,YMM2; MOV RAX,[RBX]`,
with a nonzero initial high YMM0 and high inputs whose sum differs from it.
The selected split handler computes the high sum and calls `StoreContext(48,
high_sum)`. `StoreContext` updates `RegCache.Value/Cached/Written`; it does not
immediately emit a store to `CPUState.avx_high[0]`. Before the MOV, the normal
`FlushRegisterCache(true)` mask includes GPR/PF/AF and FPR indexes 32–47, **not**
AVX-high indexes 48–63. The backing alone is therefore not established as the
current high YMM0 at that source boundary. A fault map that unconditionally uses
its entry bytes would describe the wrong completed-instruction state if those
bytes remain old.

This is a counterexample to the proposed *always-current backing premise*, not
an observed FEX native fault bug. A later optimizer may move stores, and a native
proof must follow its actual effects; the frontend’s partial flush gives no such
proof. Full flushes at inspected Jump/ExitFunction boundaries publish context
state, but that does not make every interior instruction boundary a full flush.
[Context cache indexes and loads](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h#L1920),
[StoreContext](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h#L2073),
[per-instruction partial flush](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Core.cpp#L626),
[mask and publication](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h#L1199),
[control-flow full flushes](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h#L170).

**Smallest conditional remedy:** classify each exposed high half at the fault
boundary as (a) unchanged with a proven valid entry backing, (b) a known constant
such as a VEX128-cleared upper half, or (c) a dirty SSA value requiring its own
128-bit recovery root and allocated location/recipe. A dirty root must survive
optimization/allocation to the access, just like claim 28’s low roots. A selective
pre-access publication of dirty high halves is a valid alternative realization,
with real stores to account for; a global per-instruction canonical spill is not
implied. This remedy concerns state binding and recovery. It does not require a
new vector-add lowering for the sampled path. The current patch exports neither
this dirty-high classification nor constants/full entry bindings, so it cannot
be called a complete AVX recovery adapter.

#### Decision consequence, stopping condition and next action

Continue the reversible checkpoint pilot **conditionally**: the concrete concern
that non-SVE necessarily lacks an AVX lowering path is resolved for the inspected
split operations, and the upper-state gap has a finite existing-IR root remedy.
Do not select FEX on that basis. Whole AVX2 coverage, system XSAVE/XRSTOR and control
checks, vector memory fault/partial-effect rules, gathers, native helper behavior,
FP compatibility and cost remain unaccepted; no complete opcode inventory or new
performance estimate was attempted. The inherited FP boundary and independently
selected CPU profile remain decision-critical. Startup32 is not proved by this
64-bit fixture or by the save helper’s separate eight-register branch.

The pass stops at the compatible conditional unchanged-high binding and the
concrete dirty-high counterexample/remedy. No build, generator invocation, schema
run, native emission, guest execution or diagnostic patch was performed. Seven
new pinned source files (including a small dispatch header that did not itself
supply the vector binding) are retained in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-fex-nonsve` with a hash/length manifest; other source
anchors were read from the existing pinned partial tree. Source presence and the
proposed remedy are not implementation/conformance acceptance.

**Review closure:** independent machine Return 22 accepts the inspected decoder,

binding/remedy. It found no substantive return. It does not accept emitted fault

**Next bounded action, unstarted (45 minutes):** make the full entry/dirty-state

### CLM-03-02-33 — Complete conditional field-map contract for the two fixtures

**Proposed component contract, with a scoped RF correction; no emitted-map or
runtime acceptance.** The map describes architectural state at the selected
scalar MOV data access after the preceding instructions completed, before the MOV
commits RAX. It is not a dump of FEX `CPUState`, an instruction-entry snapshot, or
an exception-handler frame. Guest semantics determine the logical fields and
instruction phases; the generator supplies value locations/recipes. Neither
interface imports FEX’s user-mode system-state policy by implication.

The two source fixtures are:

- **L:** the existing `PXOR XMM0,XMM1; ADD RCX,RDX; MOV RAX,[RBX]` prefix. The
  selected MOV is at entry+7 and has length 3; later SETZ/MOVDQU have not executed.
- **H:** claim 32’s `VPADDD YMM0,YMM1,YMM2; MOV RAX,[RBX]` counterexample, with
  AVX/AVX2 admitted and native SVE disabled. Its MOV address/length come from the
  decoded origin, not an assumed encoding or a reused L offset. This pass adds
  no assembled H fixture or changed diagnostic gate.

Both retain the existing CPL3/long-mode/flat-DS/aligned-WB scalar-access context.
An admission record must certify that the stated prefix actually completed and
no intervening guest event transferred control; a different trace cannot use this
map. All required instructions/features remain available outside the sample.
The map does not bypass the prefix’s feature/#NM checks, instruction-breakpoint
priority, or applicable event-boundary processing. It names one admitted data-
access phase, not every possible fault phase of those instructions.

#### Coverage and field identity

The input is a versioned **architectural field catalog** from the selected guest
profile/semantic components, together with the context-layout ABI. Every exposed
field belongs to exactly one of the three dispositions below. Missing fields,
overlapping incompatible aliases or an unknown profile/layout version prevent
publication of a runnable code object; there is no `all other fields unchanged`
wildcard that manufactures a valid location.

| Disposition | Concrete fields/families for these fixtures | Map responsibility |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Reconstruct working values | RAX–R15 including RSP/RBP, RIP, all architecturally exposed RFLAGS bits, XMM0–15 low 128 and, when exposed, YMM0–15 high 128 | Explicit root/constant/recipe/backing rows below; complete width and alias coverage are mandatory. |
| Retain authoritative semantic state | MXCSR; physical x87/MMX payloads and their TOP/tag/control/status/opcode/IP/DP fields; segment selectors and hidden state including FS/GS bases; GDTR/IDTR/LDTR/TR; CR0/2/3/4/8 and exposed extended controls such as EFER/XCR0; DR0–3/6/7 and other exposed debug controls; every admitted per-vCPU/system MSR and extended-state component owned by the semantic profile | Keep the current resident semantic owner’s values; these instructions do not write those fields. The profile supplies actual field IDs/widths/owners, not just these family labels. An unavailable/dirty nonresident representation must be imported into a valid retained binding or added as roots before admission. FEX private layout is not proof of ownership or completeness. |
| Keep live subsystem authority | Pending event/debug records, interruptibility progress, APIC/interrupt routing state, time/TSC policy and evolving time, shared device/memory/coherence state | Reference the current owner and current operation/event cursor. Never copy an old entry image over later asynchronous activity or previously committed effects. This is not a value rollback set. |

Aliases have one canonical owner: MMX payloads alias the corresponding x87
physical storage; ST(i) uses TOP rather than creating a second independent bank;
FS_BASE and GS_BASE MSRs alias the selected segment-base fields; CR8/TPR must not
be restored through contradictory owners. Optional MSR/XSAVE fields are enumerated
by the profile actually chosen, even if the generator never touches them here.
This contract requires that catalog and rejects an incomplete one; it does not
claim that the still-provisional product CPU profile has already supplied every
MSR bitmask. Padding, host pointers, FEX call-return bookkeeping and compiler
containers are not guest architectural fields.

The retained owner is a live per-vCPU/shared context under the established
execution/maintenance authority, not an entry-time copy to replay. If a field
can be changed by another actor, its owner’s current synchronization semantics
remain in force. The map neither holds a topology lock through repair nor rolls
back code/PTE generations, page-walk A/D changes or other completed effects.
Actual #PF delivery later updates CR2 and exception state through the semantic
owner. There is no speculative copy-back of entry CR2 before doing so.

#### Value forms and validity

`ENTRY(field)` is a **provenance role**, not a location. Before publication it
must resolve to one of these finite forms:

| Form | Required descriptor and validity |
| --- | --- |
| Root | Logical value ID, bit width, and a native register or initialized private spill slot valid at the exact access PC/phase. The native frame is captured before handlers clobber it; register class/index must be mapped through the backend ABI. A spill names frame/slot/offset and initialization before the transfer, not merely a reserved slot number. |
| Backing | Canonical field ID, resident bank/offset/width, layout version, authority and validity interval. The bytes must equal the required logical value at the fault site. An entry version alone does not prove that: dirty cached writes invalidate the backing claim until published. Bank residency/lifetime must extend through consumption. |
| Constant | Width and bit pattern justified by the completed instruction semantics or fixed profile field. It is not a substitute for an unknown entry value. |
| Pure recipe | A bounded expression over valid roots/backings/constants; all intermediate widths and truncations are explicit. No guest memory reread, helper execution, host callback or reexecution of the guest prefix is allowed while reconstructing it. |

For a register-backed ENTRY value, either its location remains valid through the
access or it participates in metadata liveness and gets an updated descriptor.
Calling it “unchanged” does not prevent the allocator or a guard from clobbering
its original native register. Likewise `CPUState.gregs`/low-XMM bytes cannot be
used just because those fields exist: FEX can keep their current values in static
native registers. Control/helper transitions invalidate or reestablish bindings
under the same rule. A valid bank need not be copied on every instruction, and a
map need not be interpreted on the ordinary fast path.

The descriptor identity is `(profile, layout, code-object generation, exact
native site, guest origin, operation phase, vCPU continuation)`. Code and map are
retained together; root spill storage and resident banks stay live until repair
returns to that operation or canonical reconstruction has consumed them. A stale
code-site association, overwritten native frame or expired spill is a rejected
internal map, never permission to fabricate guest #PF/#UD or execute an enabled
instruction incorrectly. This is the existing lifetime/continuation contract’s
application to fields, not a new per-access host round trip.

#### Concrete working-field bindings

For this pilot, choose a conservative complete working-register instantiation:
name all 16 current GPR values and all 16 current low-XMM values as recovery roots
at the MOV. This avoids assuming uninspected allocator preservation for an
unreferenced incoming native register. Shared value IDs may be deduplicated.
It is a diagnostic construction, not a minimum root/copy/spill bound or a mandate
for the eventual codegen ABI. Proven stable backing may replace a root later only
with the same validity proof. Claim 31’s current five-root patch does not yet
implement this 32-role set.

| Logical field | L binding at the access | H binding at the access |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RAX | Root `old_RAX`; MOV destination has not committed | Root `old_RAX` |
| RBX | Root `address_RBX`; also the selected effective-address input | Root `address_RBX` |
| RCX | Root `s`, the completed 64-bit ADD sum | Root `ENTRY(RCX)` |
| RDX | Root `b`, unchanged by ADD and needed for the flag recipe | Root `ENTRY(RDX)` |
| RSI, RDI, RBP, RSP, R8–R15 | Individually named current roots, each equal to its valid entry value | Same |
| RIP | Constant decoded MOV address, entry+7 for L | Constant decoded H MOV address |
| XMM0 low128 | Root `PXOR(entry.XMM0, entry.XMM1)` result already computed | Root low four dword sums from the completed VPADDD |
| XMM1–15 low128 | Individually named current roots with entry provenance | Same |
| YMM0 high128, if exposed | Backing `avx_high[0]`, only with the initialized/current-entry and no-write proof in claim 32 | Root of the completed high four dword sums; old `avx_high[0]` is explicitly not accepted if still dirty |
| YMM1–15 high128, if exposed | Individually named valid unchanged `avx_high[i]` backing | Same; reading YMM1/2 did not change them |
| CF/PF/AF/ZF/SF/OF | Width-bounded inverse-ADD recipe below | Valid incoming arithmetic-flag recipe below; VPADDD/MOV do not change them |
| RF | Constant 0 at this data-access phase | Constant 0 at this data-access phase |
| Every remaining exposed RFLAGS bit | Explicit unchanged semantic flag binding, normalized under the selected profile | Same |

Every backing row can be used only if that actual entry has current initialized
bytes. A chained predecessor with unpublished dirty high state must publish it
or pass corresponding roots; it cannot claim the row by address alone. The H
variant adds one dirty high-half role to the 32 working-register roles. Its
arithmetic-flag roots below are additional. These are logical roles, not measured
native register pressure or emitted instruction counts. Neither variant exposes
SVE registers or hides guest upper state to make the map smaller.

For L, let `s` and `b` be 64-bit roots and `a=(s-b) mod 2^64`. Then CF is unsigned
`s<b`; ZF is `s==0`; SF is bit63(s); OF is bit63(`~(a^b)&(a^s)`); AF is
bit4(`a^b^s`); PF is even parity of low8(s). All other flag bits are outside this
arithmetic recipe. This needs no replay of ADD and does not require materializing
six separate flag values on the ordinary path.

For H, an incoming canonical arithmetic-flag backing may be used if proven current.
Otherwise the FEX component can export **three explicit roots** for its current
NZCV representation, `pf_raw` and `af_raw`, plus the current carry-inversion tag.
The corresponding pure recipe uses N/Z/C/V bits 31/30/29/28, XORs C with the
recorded inversion bit, takes PF as `popcount((pf_raw & 255)^1) & 1`, and AF as
bit4(`af_raw ^ (pf_raw & 255)`). These roots must be obtained after pending flag
calculation and protected before any inserted guard clobbers flags. The inversion
tag cannot be guessed from PSTATE or silently assumed constant at an arbitrary
internal point. A valid entry recipe may be reused only if its inputs remain live.
This avoids requiring an eager packed-RFLAGS store while still naming every
input; it does not assert that the existing five-root patch exports them.
[Existing FEX flag reconstruction](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Core.cpp#L188),
[NZCV indexing and carry metadata](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h#L1140),
[flag calculation/extraction](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher/Flags.cpp#L70).

#### RF correction and event/commit phases

The previous shorthand “all non-arithmetic incoming flags” needs an RF exception.
Intel Volume 3B **253669-079US, March 2023, §18.3.1.1** specifies that RF is cleared
at instruction start after the instruction-breakpoint, CS-limit and FP-exception
checks. At the selected MOV **data-access** phase it is therefore 0, even if it
was 1 at block entry. The same section separately says that a fault-class
exception other than an instruction-breakpoint #DB saves RF as 1 in the exception
frame. Thus an entry-RF=1 snapshot is a concrete counterexample to blindly copying
all non-arithmetic flags at this phase; equally, RF=0 in the checkpoint does not
mean #PF should push RF=0. [Primary Intel volume](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/774491/253669-sdm-vol-3b.pdf).
The inspected text is retained as
`/private/tmp/natemu-memory-review02-debug-vol3b.txt`, lines 8711–8739; no newer
revision or native observation is implied. This scoped correction supersedes the
RF part of the older shorthand and requirements-only JSON; those earlier records
were not complete-map acceptance. No artifact patch or JSON was changed here.

The semantic owner supplies the phase/progress record and processes RF and any
interruptibility/debug transitions exactly once. The generator map cannot restore
an entry SS/STI shadow, pending-debug record or instruction-start phase after it
has advanced. A map for an earlier instruction-breakpoint fault has different
phase/flag requirements and is not this map. Repair of an internal shadow fault
must not reexecute the completed prefix or rerun guest event checks as if MOV had
not yet started. RF/TF/DR behavior outside this admitted fixture remains mandatory
under claim 19 and its unresolved debug-fusion obligation.

| Continuation result | Required use of this map |
| --- | --- |
| Native success | Commit the loaded value to RAX and advance to the decoded next instruction; preserve all other architectural fields and retain current event progress. No reconstruction or map interpretation is inherently required on the ordinary hit. |
| Internal repair / RETRY_ACCESS | Keep full native continuation, map/code/bank pins and current phase; retry only the unperformed native transfer. Do not reconstruct from stale entry state, replay the prefix or undo PTE/device effects. |
| ACCESS_COMPLETED by service | Consume the saved 64-bit result exactly once, commit RAX and the success continuation; do not retry the load. The old-RAX map remains a precommit map and cannot be reused as the postcommit state. |
| GUEST_FAULT | Reconstruct all working fields at the faulting MOV, retain authoritative semantic/live state, then ask the selected exception semantics to update CR2/error/frame/handler state, including the saved RF rule. No completed prefix effect is undone. |

Publication of the canonical result and semantic phase is one owner-controlled
landing transition. Partially reconstructed GPRs must not become observable to
guest execution while flags/vector state still describe another phase. The
repair path instead restores the retained native continuation. These are two
separate uses of the same location evidence, not permission to mix canonical
state and stale native registers after a guest fault.

#### Rejection cases and bounded conclusion

Reject a publishable map if a profile field lacks a disposition, a dirty high
half is labelled unchanged backing, an ENTRY register has no surviving location,
a spill has no dominating initialization, an incoming flag recipe has missing
roots/tag, or the exact code/site/phase lifetime is not established. Reject the
L map for H, a postcommit state for precommit recovery, and the old RF shorthand.
Missing component capability is an internal adaptation failure to remedy, not a
new guest exception or an optional feature silently removed from the profile.

This is a reviewable **conditional** complete field-map interface: the profile
catalog, owner-state validity and actual allocated descriptors remain explicit
inputs/proofs. It is not a claim that the product profile or current diagnostic
has produced them. It permits resident fast paths and selective root retention;
it mandates neither per-instruction full canonical spills nor host/shim calls for
normal RAM accesses. The 32-role pilot is deliberately conservative and its cost
must not be reported as unavoidable. Broader ISA/FP and split-access semantics
remain outside this finite field-binding result.

The pass stops at this contract and the RF counterexample/remedy. Only existing
pinned source/specification files were read; there was no build, emission, schema
execution, guest runtime, new source download or diagnostic patch.

**Review closure:** independent machine Return 23 accepts the scoped conditional
field/owner contract and RF correction after checking the Intel rule and FEX
flags/carry representation. No substantive return remains. The complete profile
catalog, valid entry-state authority and actual allocated root lifetimes remain
inputs to establish; the current patch, complete product profile and runtime
behavior are not accepted by this review.

**Next bounded action, unstarted (45 minutes):** implement the complete
descriptor/entry-validity exporter and checker for these two admitted fixtures,
including explicit stale-backing, dirty-high and RF phase rejection cases. That
would test this contract rather than extend its prose. It is not an emission,
build or guest-runtime authorization, and no successive author pass begins in
this checkpoint.


**Requirements-artifact reconciliation after Return 23:** root updated
`expected-recovery.json` and its README to carry the accepted data-phase RF=0 /
saved-#PF-image RF=1 distinction. The historical source pass changed no artifact;
this subsequent correction prevents its superseded all-incoming-flags shorthand
from directing new work. It adds no descriptor exporter, schema execution,
allocated map or runtime result, and leaves retained historical inputs unchanged.
