### CLM-03-02-34 — Partial descriptor consumer/checker exists; complete producer export remains missing

**Corrected exploratory preparation accepted in scope by machine Return 24.** Added
`experiments/track03/codegen-checkpoint/recovery-map.py` and README instructions.
The stdlib-only program serializes/checks supplied L/H field witnesses and imports
the existing unbuilt patch's exact `NATEMU_BEGIN/SITE/ROOT/END` interface. No FEX
patch, guest fixture bytes or historical evidence was changed. This is a partial
increment, **not completion of the requested actual full-state exporter**.

The model maps all diagnostic-catalog fields to roots, backing, constants, named
flag recipes, retained semantic owners or live owners. It verifies declared
entry identity/current versions, root provenance, exact phase/site and code/frame
pins, root occupancy or initialized resident spills, nonoverlapping distinct
storage, explicit carry inversion, and RF's checkpoint/exception-frame distinction.
Dirty H YMM0.high requires a completed-VPADDD root and cannot be relabelled incoming
backing. Modified serialized descriptors are compared with freshly checked
field dispositions. The opaque owner banks remain conditional on their owner's
complete profile catalog and correctness; this is not that complete CPU catalog.
Witness consistency is checked, not the truth of native execution declarations.
The named inverse-ADD/FEX flag recipes are serialized contracts, not a new arithmetic
execution oracle. Model locations and owner tokens are explicitly synthetic.

The trace consumer is connected to the **actual current producer format**: entry
is hexadecimal; access/guest offset/site/size and SP spill offsets are decimal;
registers are backend-native x/v indices. A new BEGIN discards an abandoned
attempt. Each nonempty END requires exactly one matching access/site, five unique
roles, admitted guest offset 7 and a site inside that emitted object. Invalid
register classes, destination/live-root aliasing, unbracketed or unfinished
attempts and producer rejection markers reject. An empty completed NOP-control
attempt is ignored; at least one complete five-root attempt must remain. Even a
well-formed current trace yields **`publishable: false`, exit 2**, never a complete
map. No observed producer trace was available or fabricated.

**Concrete missing producer delta.** `Data.cpp::MOVGPROp` currently captures only
RCX, RDX, old RAX, RBX and low XMM0. The schema has five metadata-root operands plus

location arrays/role lists. L requires the other **27 working-register roles**
under claim 33's conservative construction, plus upper-half entry currency and

current dirty YMM0.high and NZCV/PF/AF roots/carry tag. The source currently emits
none of the entry-validity records. Increasing array sizes alone cannot establish

refuses to manufacture complete witnesses from the five available locations.
This gap is concrete missing producer/owner-interface work, not a tooling or
external-worker blocker and not a reason to reject the FEX component.

## Entry backing and owner binding after the working-role extension

Root source/interface pass, September 20, bounded to 30 minutes and fixture L's
entry prerequisites after claim 35. Inspect actual FEX full versus SRA-only flush,
upper-half backing and block exit; choose the smallest resident entry obligation

and its remaining producer/runtime seam; no complete profile proof is implied.

### CLM-03-02-36 — Use completed upper-state publication as an entry prerequisite, not a whole-frame snapshot assumption

**Source-backed conditional interface choice, independently accepted in machine Return 26.**
Pinned FEX `48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825` provides a concrete entry
construction for unchanged upper halves that the five/32-root diagnostics alone
cannot supply.

In [`OpcodeDispatcher.h::FlushRegisterCache`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.h#L1177),
full mode starts with every dirty cache bit, whereas SRA-only mode masks to GPR,
PF/AF and low FPR slots. Upper slots 48–63 map explicitly to `CPUState.avx_high`
with 128-bit FPR width. Full flush emits StoreContext/StoreContextPair (or the
zero-value store specialization), then clears cache bookkeeping. The ordinary
`ExitFunction` wrappers invoke full flush before emitting the exit. This gives
an actual predecessor-side publication seam for the non-SVE upper bank; the
per-instruction SRA flush from claim 32 does not establish it.

[`IR.json::StoreContext`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/IR/IR.json#L421)
marks the store as side-effecting and carries its explicit offset/class/width.
[`MemoryOps.cpp::StoreContext/StoreContextPair`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MemoryOps.cpp#L77)
lower 128-bit FPR stores to Q-register STR/STP relative to STATE. These are source
semantics and a concrete emitted-store construction, not an observed successful
native store or a proof covering every optimizer, helper or entry path.

**Do not generalize full flush to a memory-resident copy of every field.** The
same routine sends GPR and low-FPR values through `StoreRegister` and fixed
register classes; PF/AF have their own operations. Those are not the upper-bank
StoreContext path. Thus the diagnostic's allocated 32 working roots remain
necessary for its conservative reconstruction. A blanket claim that CPUState
contains every latest guest register after `FlushRegisterCache()` would be wrong.
Full mode also normalizes carry convention, so a mid-block flag recipe cannot
silently adopt a block-boundary carry tag.

### Concrete resident-entry choice for L

Select an admitted entry record produced by the resident execution owner, with
these distinct responsibilities. It is not supplied by the compiler's JSON model
or an unchecked host callback.

| Input or transition | Required concrete binding |
| --- | --- |
| Backing initialization | Resident startup/reset initializes each exposed upper half and semantic field according to the CPU contract before first dispatch. Uninitialized bytes cannot become valid through a new version number. The full product field catalog remains question 1's obligation. |
| Predecessor to new entry | Before entering L, dirty upper halves must have completed publication to the same resident STATE backing. An ordinary adapted full-flush exit supplies the source construction; an exceptional landing, helper or alternate entry must explicitly satisfy the same obligation. Unsupported entry routes cannot present a valid upper-bank record merely because their target is L. |
| Working values | Bind the active native register/spill ABI to the actual code object and L site. Do not replace its 32 roots with CPUState GPR/low-XMM offsets. The values are those current at the precommit MOV, including completed ADD/PXOR, not an entry snapshot. |
| Entry record | Identify VM/vCPU lifetime, entry serial, admitted guest PC and mode/profile/ABI identity, resident STATE backing and initialized upper-bank version, and the executing code/metadata identity. The resident owner creates this binding only after the predecessor or startup obligation finishes. Code-supplied expected offsets/roles are static metadata, not proof of backing currency. |
| At L's MOV | The admitted prefix does not modify upper halves. The single vCPU owner retains their current backing through this phase. Working values come from the actual site descriptors; unchanged uppers can use the explicitly bound backing. A concurrent owner mutation must end/reconcile this admission before reuse. |
| Semantic banks and live state | Reference the current resident semantic owner, not a copied entry image. Mode/control/segment/x87/MXCSR and event-progress validity require their actual owner catalog and mutation rules. Pending events, APIC, time and committed memory effects retain live ownership; they are never rolled back to make the entry record match. |
| Lifetime | Pin the code/metadata, native spill frame and backing through fault handling, service continuation or canonical landing. Reusing a frame, record slot, code object or vCPU lifetime invalidates old identities; serial wrap cannot recycle a live identity. Numeric versions do not pin storage. |

For L, current upper backing plus the fact that this prefix leaves it unchanged
provides the required unchanged-half argument. H's completed VPADDD dirties its
upper result after entry: this entry record does **not** legalize old backing for
H. H still needs its actual dirty-high/flag roots or a separately proved completed
publication before its MOV, and remains rejected by the current producer.

### Entry, continuation and landing are different boundaries

A new direct-linked block entry may use this contract when its predecessor has
the required full upper publication and both sides agree on the working-register
ABI; the resident entry binding must follow that transfer. This does not require
routing every block through a host dispatcher or spilling all 32 working values.
The actual chain-entry record update/guard is a missing native adapter change,
not something the existing diagnostic BEGIN log implements. Any unqualified
entry routes through resident normalization until that route is bound.

Internal native repair keeps the same pinned entry, precommit access identity
and already completed prefix. It must not re-execute the block to obtain a fresh
entry certificate. If a semantic service completes the load, its postcommit
result needs the corresponding continuation disposition; old-RAX fault recovery
cannot be reused after completion. Canonical exception/event landing reconstructs
working guest state once, consumes the old phase, and establishes the next
resident entry only after current-owner state is coherent. A helper that changes
mode, FP controls or backing ends or explicitly reconciles affected validity;
it cannot silently continue using a stale entry stamp. These transitions retain
claim 33's data-phase RF and saved-fault-frame distinction.

The resident entry record and static map therefore meet at an exact code/site/
phase/ABI binding. A compiler output can declare expected backing fields and
recipes; only the resident predecessor/entry path can establish their current
values and lifetime. This separates the missing producer inputs rather than
encoding an invented valid marker in `NATEMU_AUTHORITY`. That marker remains
`missing` in the current patch, correctly refusing publication.

**Decision consequence and limit:** select this backing-publication/entry-owner
construction for the L adapter instead of asserting whole-frame canonicality or
forcing all working registers to memory per access. Upstream full upper flush
provides a concrete source seam; captive entry/chain/landing code must implement
and bind it. No complete CPU catalog, H producer, native recovery consumer,
all-instruction fault proof or cost acceptance follows. The next bounded adapter
work is to connect the selected entry record to the actual chain/dispatch and
exception-landing seam with a complete L disposition, preserving explicit
missing status for unimplemented owner facts. Stop for independent review now.

**Claim 36 review closure:** machine Return 26 independently checked the full
upper-bank publication versus fixed-register paths and accepted the scoped
resident-entry construction. No substantive return remains. Actual native
entry/chain/landing record production and complete recovery remain open.

## L entry veneer and typed native dispositions

**Method:** machine-authored source/interface pass, 2026-09-20, bounded to 30
minutes following claim 36. Inspect the current unbuilt working32 producer,
pinned FEX dispatcher/linker/branch lowering and exact scalar load. Select L's
resident transfer contract without changing code, building C++, running native
code or ranking generators. Newly fetched `BranchOps.cpp` and copies of six
inspected local inputs are retained with SHA-256 and source paths at
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-fex-entry-01/sources.json`. FEX remains pinned to
`48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825`.

### CLM-03-02-37 — Bind all external L entries at a resident target veneer; repair and completion use separate interior continuations

**Source-backed conditional interface choice; independently accepted in root
Return27 after lifetime and immutable-link corrections.**
The important source finding is that a linker callback is not an every-entry
hook. [`JIT.cpp::ExitFunctionLink`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/JIT.cpp#L567)
looks up/compiles the target and patches an in-range branch directly, or fills
an indirect thunk target. Its invalidation/link bookkeeping does not manufacture
a resident entry serial on each later execution. Adding admission solely to
that callback would leave warmed direct chains unbound.

[`Dispatcher.cpp::EmitDispatcher`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Dispatcher/Dispatcher.cpp#L54)
fills static registers on its fill entry, branches directly to an L2 lookup hit,
and spills/refills around a compile miss or exit-linker call before branching
to the returned address. The ordinary loop-top entry does not perform the same
fill as the fill entry. Thus dispatch is also not a universal canonical-state
boundary. These source paths support preservation of working native values,
not a claim that every register has been stored in CPUState.

[`BranchOps.cpp::ExitFunction`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/BranchOps.cpp#L46)
resets the spill stack and can use linked immediate branches, L1 indirect
lookups, or a call/return cache. Its internal `Jump` lowering is a separate
intra-compilation-unit branch. Together with claim 36's frontend full-flush
wrappers, this locates both the predecessor publication and target admission
seams. It does not justify putting the entry hook on every internal IR jump,
nor assuming cached return addresses always mean a new block entry.

**Selected L construction.** Give each published L code object an external
resident entry veneer as its sole dispatch/link target. Both lookup-cache
addresses and the adapted runtime-owned data-link cells name that veneer.
Select INV-03-04’s existing immutable transfer sequence: it reads a guarded
data-link cell whose target is updated atomically under coordinator/lifetime
rules. Warmed code bytes are not rewritten. Immediate relocation before code
publication remains possible; upstream live branch backpatching is source
evidence for callback bypass, not the selected mutation policy. Host compilation
only supplies immutable code/metadata and cannot execute its binding. The
veneer first relies on the existing native-reference lifetime rule: its entry
storage remains allocated and unreused until incoming data-link references, cached
lookup addresses and in-flight native transfers that can reach it have been
retired under the all-participant parking/reclamation protocol. Invalidating
source eligibility alone does not reclaim those bytes. The executing
predecessor/transfer pin and retired incoming-reference accounting cover the
interval before the target's first instruction and before its own pin is
acquired. If that retirement cannot be established, retain the allocation;
do not branch speculatively into reclaimed/reused storage and expect a guard
to rescue it. This selects the existing retained-object lifetime rule, not a
new indirection mechanism. Only on that protected entry does the veneer verify
source/mode/profile/ABI eligibility and admit the target under
the resident coordinator, pin target code/metadata/backing, and establish
claim 36's entry identity *before* entering the block body. Transfer of the old
pin to the new pin follows the accepted bounded handover rules; the old code
cannot be reclaimed while still executing its transfer. Source invalidation
and admission have one coordinator linearization, so a transfer using a stale cached/data-link target
cannot bypass the target guard merely because link retirement is delayed.

The veneer preserves the agreed fixed-register, flags and stack ABI. Its own
scratch use must be reserved or saved/restored without overwriting incoming
working values; it is not an implicit full guest-register spill. Native register
values supplied by a qualified predecessor stay live. Startup or canonical
landing instead installs the agreed working ABI from canonical state before
this admission. The predecessor must have completed required upper publication
before transfer; a target guard cannot repair unknown dirty upper values by
reading old CPUState bytes. Unknown predecessors normalize through a resident
exit capable of recovering their actual current state, or remain inadmissible.
No unsupported predecessor receives a fabricated valid entry record.

This is one target hook per external entry and preserves direct resident
chaining without a host dispatcher visit. For L, no guest call/return occurs in
the admitted five-instruction body. Any future cached return targeting an
interior continuation requires its own pinned continuation identity; it is
neither silently redirected through new-entry initialization nor accepted as
an unguarded L entry. ARM64EC/native thunk entry, generic callbacks and arbitrary
external jumps into the body are outside this L contract. Internal IR jumps
keep the same admitted entry unless an explicit semantic boundary ends it.

### Complete disposition for L's selected MOV access

The current patch fixes the bytes `PXOR; ADD; MOV RAX,[RBX]; SETZ; MOVDQU`,
MOV at guest offset 7, next instruction offset 10. It rejects SVE and RCpc/
TSO-immediate variants. The inspected
[`MemoryOps.cpp::LoadMemTSO`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/MemoryOps.cpp#L818)
therefore supplies a specific 64-bit `LDAR Dst,[MemReg]` followed by the reserved
half-barrier NOP. `NATEMU_SITE` records this instruction's actual offset,
destination and address register; it is not a trap handler or success record.
The selected adapter must bind both access PC and the following emitted label
in immutable metadata, and validate the expected instruction sequence. The
following label is concrete for this source case, not an arbitrary host `PC+4`
rule for other accesses, patched sequences or other generators.

| Disposition | Resident action and next state |
| --- | --- |
| New startup/dispatch entry | Initialize or normalize exposed state, install working ABI, finish upper-bank publication and enter the external veneer. Create a new serial only on admission; do not derive currency from compiler logs. |
| Qualified direct chain | Execute predecessor publication and transfer through the same target veneer, preserving working registers. Pin handover and guard/admission replace any assumption that the link callback runs every time. |
| `RETRY_ACCESS` after internal repair | Retain the same entry, code/frame pins, precommit MOV identity and native context. Repair may change mapping but has performed no guest operand access. Restore context and resume at the exact load PC; do not replay PXOR/ADD or create a new entry. |
| `ACCESS_COMPLETED`, ordinary continuation | The service result is retained exactly once. Apply required load ordering, restore the captured native context with only the actual allocated load-result destination replaced by the returned 64 bits, and resume at the bound post-load label. Preserve the reserved barrier slot and emitted suffix, including later architectural RAX assignment. The old-RAX recovery root remains a precommit descriptor, not the completed state. |
| `ACCESS_COMPLETED`, boundary exit required | Without replaying the access, materialize current working roots with architectural RAX replaced by the completed result, retain ADD flags/PXOR result and unchanged bound uppers, and commit MOV at guest PC `entry+10`. Consume the native continuation once and use canonical resident landing; source invalidation/event handling may then choose a fresh entry. Do not execute the stale suffix merely to obtain canonicality. |
| `GUEST_FAULT` | Materialize the precommit roots, including old RAX, and current bound unchanged state; consume the access/native continuation and land at fault PC `entry+7`. Resident x86 exception logic, not Linux signal-frame emulation, owns exception state and the saved RF rule. No upper/pending-event/memory rollback occurs. |
| Host failure or missing identity/metadata | Keep the VM contained; never convert unknown native effects or an unclassified native PC into a guessed guest fault, repaired retry or a fresh entry. |

Both completion dispositions require the actual service to establish the
selected x86 load semantics, including ordering, before consuming its result.
A context restore must preserve all live native values/flags needed by the
suffix, not only the 32 *guest reconstruction* roots. The native exception
frame supplies that continuation context; the roots serve canonical materialization.
L's data-phase RF and exception saved-image RF remain distinct as in claim 33.
A true guest fault abandons this native access; subsequent guest return/retry
is a new architectural execution, not a transport replay of its completed
prefix. Pending asynchronous events are delivered only at the selected canonical
boundary, without rolling back their live owners.

The pinned Linux
[`GuestFramesManagement.cpp`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/Source/Tools/LinuxEmulation/LinuxSyscalls/SignalDelegator/GuestFramesManagement.cpp#L125)
restores guest signal-context RIP/segments and has Linux frame responsibilities.
It is source context for why ordinary FEX signal return is not our EL1 exception
landing. The captive resident exception vector, context restore, x86 delivery
and veneer are **unwritten adapter code**, not silently inherited Linux runtime.

**Decision consequence:** L now has a selected place to bind entry authority and
a disposition for new entry, warmed direct chain, interior repair, completed
load and guest fault. Source construction plus the existing immutable-code/data-link adaptation
supports this interface without
requiring a native implementation to choose it. The current producer correctly
continues to emit `NATEMU_AUTHORITY status=missing`: neither its site map nor
this source choice establishes runtime entry/owner facts. Complete CPU-field
catalogs, H's dirty-upper mapping, native conformance and adaptation cost remain
outside this scoped closure. Next action is independent review of claim 37;
any later implementation must attach the selected veneer/typed continuation
contract to actual native code, not add another claimed-valid diagnostic marker.

**Claim37 closure:** root Return27 accepts L’s source-based entry and typed
continuation construction after the stated lifetime/link-policy corrections.
No additional diagnostic marker or runtime mock is needed to select that
interface; actual producer/adapter conformance remains unestablished.

## Full-precision x87 arithmetic is separate from exception commit

**Method:** machine-authored bounded 30-minute source/decision pass on September
20, 2026. Match `FSQRT` of a valid, nonempty extended-precision ST0 containing
exactly −1, with invalid unmasked, followed by `FWAIT`. Trace arithmetic,
destination/status commit and waiting delivery separately in the existing QEMU,
FEX and Box64 pins. No code changes, native run, precision reduction or generator
ranking. New pinned source files plus inspected cached inputs and the Intel
manual are retained in `/private/tmp/natemu-track03-x87-01/sources.json`.
An attempted FEX `Primary.inl` URL was absent; the actual BaseTables and
X87Tables/stack pass below supply the dispatch evidence.

### CLM-03-02-38 — Select staged floatx80 arithmetic and resident x87 commit/pending ownership for the matched invalid case

**Source-backed conditional semantic-component choice; accepted in root Return28.**
Use the selected QEMU pin's `floatx80` SoftFloat operation as a reusable pure
arithmetic component, with an invocation-local status object and staged result.
The resident x87 owner decides architectural destination/stack/status commit
and waiting exception delivery. Do not transplant the complete QEMU x87 helper,
FEX fallback destructor/frontend commit, or Box64 native FP/status path as that
owner. This choice is independent of the eventual code generator and does not
rank whole runtimes. Full 80-bit guest state and workload coverage remain
required; neither binary64 substitution nor masking all exceptions is selected.

**Architectural matched case.** Intel Volume 1, **253665-078US, December 2022**,
§§8.3.12, 8.5.1.2/Table 8-10 and 8.7.1 identify negative nonzero FSQRT as invalid,
require IE, preserve TOP and source operands when invalid is unmasked, and
specify later native-mode delivery before a waiting instruction. ST0 is both
source and destination here, so replacing it with an indefinite NaN before
handler delivery is not permitted. With CR0.NE=1, initially clear pending state,
valid nonempty ST0, CR0.EM=TS=0 and FCW.IM=0, FSQRT leaves −1 in ST0, records
invalid/pending status, and the following FWAIT delivers #MF before advancing
past that waiting instruction. A non-waiting status read can observe pending
status without delivering it. The faulting arithmetic's x87 instruction metadata
and the waiting instruction's architectural exception PC are distinct. This
fixture avoids stack underflow, denormal and memory-access priority ambiguities.
[Primary Intel manual](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/671436/253665-sdm-vol-1.pdf).

### Matched inspected paths

| Component and pin | Actual arithmetic/status/waiting path | Consequence for reuse |
| --- | --- | --- |
| QEMU `c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294` | `helper_fsqrt` saves local arithmetic flags, assigns `ST0 = floatx80_sqrt(...)`, then merges new flags. `fpu_set_exception` sets status and SE/B from unmasked bits. `helper_fwait` checks SE; `fpu_raise_exception` selects #MF for NE or the system FERR route. | Existing pending/#MF structure is useful source guidance, but the sampled helper's unconditional ST0 assignment lacks the required unmasked-invalid suppression. Its status/delivery coverage does not make wholesale reuse correct. |
| FEX `48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825`, normal full-precision mode | X87Tables D9/FA binds `OP_F80SQRTSTACK`; `X87OpHelper` emits it; `x87StackOptimizationPass::HandleUnop` selects F80SQRT when reduced mode is false and calls `StoreStackValue(Value)`. F80 fallback calls SoftFloat with FCW precision/rounding; its scoped destructor records IE. BaseTables maps opcode 9B to the empty `NOPOp`. | FEX demonstrably has extended precision; it is not a binary64-only candidate. This sampled full-precision stack path still needs commit suppression, fuller status ownership and a waiting boundary. The direct FWAIT no-op binding is stronger evidence than inferring global behavior solely from the local IE function. |
| Box64 `454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647`, inspected ARM64 dynarec path | D9/FA uses native FSQRTS or FSQRTD into its cached ST0 register, with optional rounding save/restore and `X87_CHECK_PRECISION`. The inspected precision macro can narrow double to single; it is not an 80-bit arithmetic operation. Opcode 9B, when `dynarec_div0` is enabled, tests `(SW & ~CW) & 0x3f` and calls `const_native_div0`/exits if pending; otherwise it does not emit that check. | This specific emitted arithmetic path is not the selected full80 component. A configurable pending check exists: do not claim Box64 never checks FWAIT. Its call is not a captive #MF delivery interface, and the inspected FSQRT path does not itself establish invalid-status translation or result suppression. Other modes/interpreter paths are not disproved by this sample. |

Primary code: QEMU [x87 helper](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c#L2318),
[WAIT frontend](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc#L4693),
[system FERR helper](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/fpu_helper.c);
FEX [decode](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/X86Tables/X87Tables.cpp#L372),
[stack result](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/IR/Passes/x87StackOptimizationPass.cpp#L525),
[80-bit fallback](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Interpreter/Fallbacks/F80Fallbacks.h#L209),
[WAIT binding](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher/BaseTables.h#L53);
Box64 [FSQRT](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_d9.c#L447)
and [FWAIT](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/arm64/dynarec_arm64_00.c#L1837).

### Selected resident transaction

The component takes the exact 80-bit operand and a private initialized rounding/
precision/NaN-policy context, and returns an 80-bit proposed value plus newly
raised arithmetic flags. QEMU
[`floatx80_sqrt`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/fpu/softfloat.c#L4461)
unpacks, computes and repacks through its precision-controlled floatx80 path;
its caller's architectural ST0 assignment is outside this function. Adapt this
small interface rather than sharing a mutable host FP environment or presenting
a foreign CPUState pointer as a complete resident x87 state object. Its numerical
conformance for the full input space is not established by this one case.

Before starting a waiting arithmetic operation, the resident owner checks the
applicable availability and pending-exception conditions. For this initially
clear fixture, it snapshots ST0 and FCW at the admitted guest instruction and
performs the pure calculation into scratch. It merges newly raised flags into
sticky architectural status once, derives the unmasked/pending state, and
records the arithmetic instruction's x87 metadata. If invalid is unmasked,
it discards the proposed result and preserves ST0/TOP/tags for this valid-input
case; if invalid is masked, it may commit the specified indefinite result.
Defined condition-code/status behavior remains owned by the instruction policy,
not copied blindly from native FPSR. No memory or guest exception is delivered
by the pure arithmetic function itself.

The next FWAIT checks pending state using current architectural control/status
and applicable #NM rules, then produces the resident typed #MF outcome for
CR0.NE=1. It does not recompute FSQRT or commit its discarded result. Exception
delivery retains current live event owners; x87 pending state is not erased as
a side effect of entering the handler. Non-waiting state/control operations use
their separate policy, including architecturally specified clears or mask changes.
Internal helper repair/reentry retains the same instruction transaction; it
cannot merge status twice or reapply a completed stack update. Arithmetic helpers
are resident pure computations and do not require a host round trip.

For a generator that caches x87 values, admission must materialize or explicitly
bind the current operand/TOP/tag/FCW to this owner; a suppressed result must not
leave a speculative cached NaN masquerading as committed ST0. Successful commit
publishes or invalidates the corresponding cached representation. This is the
same authority distinction as the GPR/vector recovery contract, not a second
independent x87 state machine. A waiting landing needs the current instruction
identity and canonical status even if no ordinary load/store fault site exists.

**Remaining exact scope.** This closes the semantic boundary and the matched
unmasked-invalid/next-FWAIT disposition; it does not certify all x87 operations.
Full80 transcendental accuracy, all exception priorities and stack faults,
state-image operations and every waiting/non-waiting form retain their complete
profile obligations. CR0.NE=0 is not silently forbidden by choosing this fixture:
QEMU's inspected system helper uses global FERR state/BQL and first-CPU IGNNE
handling, so the captive machine still needs an explicit per-machine FERR/IGNNE
and IRQ13 contract through its reviewed owner bridge. #MF uses the resident
exception owner, not QEMU `raise_exception_ra`/host CPU-loop unwinding or Box64's
native call. Those are named adaptations, not reasons to rank whole codegens.
Root independent review is next; no implementation or experiment is required
merely to accept this scoped source construction.


**Claim38 closure:** independent root Return28 accepts the matched arithmetic/
commit/waiting boundary. Full x87 coverage and NE=0 machine integration remain
explicit obligations; this does not select a code generator.


## Resident FP state images — successful FXSAVE/FXRSTOR boundary

**Method:** root, September20; bounded30-minute primary-source pass following
claim38. Inspect Intel253666-082US FXSAVE/FXRSTOR and the pinned QEMU/FEX
state-image paths. No native execution, numerical rewrite or generator ranking.

### CLM-03-02-39 — Select one resident state-image owner with adapted encoders

**Source-backed findings and conditional interface choice; independently accepted
in machine Return29.**
The selected full80 arithmetic component does not supply a complete architectural
state-image interface. Bind FXSAVE/FXRSTOR to the same resident x87/SSE owner
used by claim38, with reusable upstream layout/rotation routines adapted to its
authoritative fields and checked guest-memory service. This is metadata/state
adaptation, not hand-written replacement floating-point arithmetic.

[Intel Vol2A FXSAVE/FXRSTOR, Tables3-43–47](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf)
defines FCW/FSW, physical-order abridged tags, logical ST slots, opcode and
instruction/data pointers, MXCSR/mask and mode-dependent XMM count. FXSAVE
retains current registers and does not deliver pending x87 exceptions; FXRSTOR
also does not deliver them immediately. A subsequent FWAIT performs the waiting
check. Its formats distinguish compatibility/legacy, 64-bit REX.W=0 and
FXSAVE64/FXRSTOR64. Software bytes464–511 are not state to overwrite/restore.

The inspected QEMU
[`do_xsave_fpu`/`do_xrstor_fpu`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c)
provides TOP rotation, full80 data and abridged-tag conversion. Its save explicitly
writes zero pointer fields and does not emit FOP; the restore shown reads
FCW/FSW/tags/data, without corresponding pointer/opcode restoration. It is not a
complete image of the required metadata. The matched FEX
[`SaveX87State`/`RestoreX87State`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher/Vector.cpp)
synchronizes cached stack state, rotates slots and supports full80 when reduced
mode is false. Comments describe FIP/FDP/FOP layouts, but the inspected emitted
operations save/restore control/status/tags/data without those metadata fields.
Its MXCSR export/import masks to0xFFC0, which cannot preserve architectural
sticky exception-status bits. These are specific inspected paths, not claims
that another mode, surrounding runtime or Box64 was exhaustively audited.

**Selected successful-access contract.** At a guest instruction boundary, bind
current full80 physical registers, TOP/tags, FCW/FSW, FOP/FIP/FDP and applicable
selectors, plus current MXCSR and XMM roots. Materialize live generator caches
or supply valid working-root bindings; never serialize stale backing. Preserve
all defined metadata according to the exposed CPU's pointer/deprecation policy.
Opcode and pointer values come from the resident semantic owner, not the host
helper PC or zeros. FTW bits retain physical-register order while ST slots use
TOP-relative order. Preserve mode/REX-dependent formats and8 versus16 XMM slots.
The MXCSR mask is the selected CPU capability mask, independent of native FPSR;
its status bits cannot disappear because a host generator does not track them.

For successful aligned access to admitted ordinary RAM, capture the save image
from those roots, then publish only the instruction's defined writable fields
through the memory owner. Keep software bytes464–511 unchanged. Saving does not
reset x87 state, clear pending status or raise #MF. Restore binds input fields
to the same state owner, validates the applicable control/MXCSR constraints,
and installs the resulting register/control/metadata state before further guest
execution. Invalidate or update stale generator caches at this boundary. Loaded
pending x87 status remains available to the next waiting operation; restored
MXCSR sticky flags do not themselves inject a SIMD arithmetic exception.
FXRSTOR does not import MXCSR_MASK as a writable CPU capability register.
[Intel Vol1 §§10.5.1.2–10.5.3](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/671436/253665-sdm-vol-1.pdf)
explicitly says it ignores that field, and that outside64-bit mode the XMM8–15
image bytes are neither written by FXSAVE nor read by FXRSTOR. These are actual
field-access exclusions, not merely a smaller register set after a whole-image
transfer.

With OSFXSR=0 select the documented permitted policy of leaving XMM/MXCSR
untouched; with it set include those fields. This choice must be consistent
across save and restore. Keep the existing candidate's unadvertised AMD FFXSR
behavior out of this Intel-vendor path rather than inheriting that helper branch
by accident. Retain guest instruction availability/privilege/address/prefix
checks, including FXSR, CR0.EM/TS and16-byte alignment; no native memory cast
bypasses the memory owner.

**Exact limits and next action.** This closes a proposed successful-access state
ownership/encoding boundary, not all state-image instructions or fault ordering.
The helper's512-byte preparation does not itself prove which reserved/software
bytes may be touched or which effects survive a cross-page/MMIO fault. Resolve
that instruction-specific access/partial-effect contract before accepting full
FXSAVE/FXRSTOR semantics; do not silently assume an atomic512-byte transfer or
all-or-nothing device effects. XSAVE/XRSTOR additionally require XCR0/request
mask, header validation, initial-state and upper-vector rules. FSAVE/FRSTOR are
different formats and reset semantics. These are retained obligations, not an
ISA exclusion. Review this boundary independently, then address the exact
state-image access/fault contract. No runtime mock is needed to select the owner.


**Claim39 closure:** machine Return29 accepts the successful-access state-image
interface independently. Full instruction fault semantics and other extended
state operations remain explicit obligations.


## State-image access exclusions and invalid restore — source refinement

**Method:** root, September20; continue the bounded state-image source pass with
Intel Vol1 §§10.5.1–10.5.3, Vol2A FXRSTOR and Vol3A §§7.5–7.6. Inspect the
pinned QEMU512-byte access preparation against its actual field accesses. No
native fixture was executed and no full fault-priority claim is made.

### CLM-03-02-40 — Prepare architectural field spans, and reject invalid MXCSR before live restore

**Source-backed facts and conditional construction; independently accepted in
machine Return30.**
Use the state-image owner's mode-specific field list as the input to checked
memory admission. The image's nominal512-byte allocation size is not itself an
instruction to read, write or probe every byte. Intel Vol1 §10.5.1 expressly
states that FXSAVE/FXRSTOR do not use bytes416–511; §10.5.1.2 additionally excludes
XMM8–15 image accesses outside64-bit mode. FXRSTOR ignores the mask field.
[Primary Intel Vol1](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/671436/253665-sdm-vol-1.pdf).

The QEMU pin's `helper_fxsave` and `helper_fxrstor` call `access_prepare` with
`sizeof(X86LegacyXSaveArea)`, whose definition and build assertion set it to512.
The accessed-field routines from claim39 then perform selected scalar accesses.
This whole-range preparation is not selected as the captive admission interface:
its conservative page set cannot be treated as evidence that unused image bytes
are architecturally required accesses. Retain its data-layout/rotation reference,
replace the admission extent with the actual field spans.
[QEMU helpers](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c),
[QEMU image definition](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/cpu.h).

**Discriminating source-derived case:** an aligned64-bit-mode image begins at
linear0xE60 in a4KiB page. Defined state through offset415 ends at0xFFF. Offsets
416–511 lie on the next page. With the first page valid for the operation and
the second unmapped, admission must not introduce a #PF solely by probing that
unused suffix. All other instruction, address and value checks still apply.
This is a proposed conformance fixture derived from the field-access rule, not
an observed hardware result. Likewise a compatibility-mode transfer must not
acquire access rights just to transfer untouched XMM8–15 fields.

**Invalid-MXCSR restore case:** for aligned, fully available ordinary RAM and
OSFXSR=1, validate the fetched MXCSR against the selected CPU's legal-bit mask
before committing any restored architectural state. Intel Vol1 §10.5.3 describes
#GP for an illegal MXCSR value and restoration otherwise; Vol2A lists that fault.
Stage the image's x87/control/vector data privately rather than incrementally
mutating live state before this validation. On the rejected value, return the
guest #GP(0) at FXRSTOR's instruction identity with old architectural FP state
retained. Internal helper repair cannot merge a partially restored cache with
old resident state. Page-walk effects remain separately owned; this CPU-state
rule does not erase them. The general restart rule in Intel Vol3A §§7.5–7.6 is
consistent with this construction, but its stated exceptions mean it is not
being used as a universal proof for every extended-state instruction.
[Primary Intel Vol3A092](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf).

For successful RAM admission, all selected field routes and their mapping
identities are held by the existing memory transaction until consumption. An
ordinary-RAM save may use the existing coordinator to publish the bounded field
writes; the instruction need not expose an invented new512-byte atomicity
promise. Source/PTE aliases and A/D effects still use the all-writer owner.
The immutable source image prevents interrupted internal service from rereading
mutated CPU registers. These interface choices add no host round trip requirement.

**Limits:** this settles the ignored-suffix access and valid-memory invalid-value
cases. Competing address/alignment/value faults, cross-page faults inside used
fields, MMIO access widths/order and surviving partial external effects still
need their own exact construction. No blanket all-or-nothing device transaction
or mandatory restore order is inferred. The next action is independent review
of these two cases, then the remaining used-field access/fault composition.


**Claim40 closure:** machine Return30 independently accepts the ignored-suffix
and valid-memory invalid-MXCSR cases. Remaining fault/partial-effect semantics
are not closed by these two cases.


## State-image alignment and competing page faults — bounded source choice

**Method:** root, September20, one20-minute source/design pass within the current
state-image fault action. Inspect Intel253666-082US FXSAVE/FXRSTOR exception
lists and implementation note, plus the accepted field-layout/admission contract.
Stop at a supported exception-order choice or the precise remaining uncertainty.
No code, experiment or additional instruction-family audit.

### CLM-03-02-41 — Select the permitted alignment exception; do not overgeneralize FXSAVE priority to FXRSTOR

**Source-backed facts and narrow choice accepted in machine Return31.**
Intel253666-082US FXSAVE pp.3-468–469 and FXRSTOR pp.3-460–461 explicitly permit
#GP in place of alignment-check #AC for an operand not aligned to16 bytes, even
when alignment checking is enabled at CPL3. Select that permitted variant for
both instructions: operand misalignment produces #GP(0), subject to their other
applicable decode/feature/privilege and exception-priority rules. This does not
change ordinary instructions’ #AC behavior.
[Primary Intel instruction reference](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf).

FXSAVE’s implementation note separately says simultaneous #GP/#PF priority can
vary by processor implementation. For the case where preceding checks pass but
the FXSAVE destination is both misaligned and on an inaccessible page, select
**alignment #GP before destination translation/access**. This is an explicit
virtual-CPU choice within the documented implementation variation, not a claim
that every Intel CPU prioritizes it that way. No payload write or operand page
walk is needed to discover that misalignment; instruction-fetch effects are
separate. The CPU contract must retain this choice consistently across adapters.

The analogous FXRSTOR exception list permits the alignment #GP variant, but the
inspected FXRSTOR entry does **not** contain the FXSAVE implementation-priority
note. Do not use proximity or shared image layout to transfer that priority
permission to competing FXRSTOR page/MXCSR faults. Claim40’s valid-RAM invalid-
MXCSR case remains accepted; the competing restore-fault order remains open.

**Geometry consequence, inferred from the accepted layouts:** on the selected
4KiB guest-page regime, a correctly16-byte-aligned image cannot split an individual
naturally placed16-byte XMM field or10-byte x87 payload across a page. Their
slots begin at multiples of16; header fields also remain within their slots.
An image may still span two pages, with a later *used field* on the inaccessible
page. Thus alignment does not remove the used-field page-fault or partial-save
problem; it identifies its actual boundary. The ignored-suffix fixture in40
still must not fault merely because bytes416–511 lack a mapping.

**Limits and next resolution:** this settles the candidate’s permitted #AC/#GP
variant and the stated FXSAVE competing alignment/page case only. Complete
FXRSTOR competing-fault priority, earlier used-field writes before a later-page
fault, and MMIO width/order/irreversible effects remain required. Neither an
all-or-nothing512-byte transaction nor blanket sequential device accesses follow
from this choice. Independent review must check that the implementation-variation
permission is not extended beyond the cited FXSAVE case.

## State-image used-field faults and external effects — bounded source construction

**Method:** memory, September20, source-only follow-on to39–41. Inspect retained
Intel253666-082US FXSAVE/FXRSTOR, Intel253668-092US §§7.5–7.6, page-fault
Program State Change, §§11.1.1,11.2.3.1,14.3, and Intel Vol1 §10.5.3; compare the
pinned QEMU field helpers and `access_prepare_mmu`. No code, mock, native run or
claim about physical Haswell's particular micro-operation sequence.

### CLM-03-02-42 — Separate pre-admitted RAM faults from already completed external effects

**Source-backed proposed construction; pending independent root review.**
The architectural register state, explicit image-memory effects, and implicit
page-walk effects are three different recovery domains. Restarting at FXSAVE or
FXRSTOR does not authorize deleting a completed device transaction or undoing
an A/D update. Conversely, a permitted partial-store outcome is not an obligation
to deliberately perform an early store before a discoverable later-page fault.

**Primary bounds.** Intel092 §11.1.1 pp.11-2–3 explicitly permits x87/SSE accesses
larger than a quadword to be implemented by multiple accesses, with completed
stores visible even if another access faults. This supplements the general
restart description in §§7.5–7.6 and the page-fault Program State Change section:
those general descriptions must not be read as a rollback guarantee for every
memory side effect. The text says such accesses *may* complete; it does not
require a particular prefix to be written on every fault. The separately stated
AVX aligned16-byte atomicity guarantees name MOV-family instructions, not a
512-byte FXSAVE transaction. The WB-scoped §11.2.3.1 also leaves the order of
constituent accesses unconstrained; that section is not a proof about arbitrary
MMIO. [Intel Vol3A092](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf).

For UC, §14.3's note specifically makes x87/SIMD memory-access behavior
implementation dependent, including possible repeated accesses, and recommends
GPR loads/stores for predictable side-effecting accesses. This prevents claiming
that FXSAVE/FXRSTOR have the same externally promised access shape as a sequence
of guest MOV instructions. It also prevents treating one inspected helper's
scalar order as an Intel bus contract. This pass does not infer an unlimited
license to read unused bytes or issue arbitrary device requests from that note.

**Selected ordinary-RAM case.** After the applicable instruction/address/alignment
checks pass, and with no competing invalid restore value or other exception,
use the39–40 mode-specific *used-field* list. Admit its translations and required
permissions before issuing any explicit payload store or consuming an image
into live CPU state. Retain those routes under the existing memory transaction.
This selects a permitted zero-explicit-write outcome when a later used page
fails admission. It neither advertises image-wide hardware atomicity nor requires
all other instructions to prevalidate their operands. Only a bounded state-image
service uses this conservative transaction; native ordinary hits remain native.

Concrete geometry: an aligned64-bit image at0xF00 has XMM5 at offset240 on the
first page and XMM6 at offset256 on the second. If the first page is valid and
the second is not present, and all earlier checks pass, return #PF for the first
required field on that second page, with the actual load/store and privilege
error-code inputs. FXSAVE has issued no explicit image store; FXRSTOR retains
its old FP/vector/control state. This construction does not infer alignment
fault priority for FXRSTOR and does not apply if an invalid MXCSR competes with
the page fault. It is source-derived, not a hardware observation.

Admission is not effect-free. Previously completed walker A/D writes, including
writes aliasing image data, code or paging structures, survive a later #PF and
must complete generation/shadow invalidation before fault delivery or guest
reentry. Restore reads its staged input after the selected walk effects, under
the retained routes; save uses the captured architectural state image. Route
retention and own-PTE writes obey INV-03-04's walk/access contract rather than
silently rewalking against partly written save bytes. An internal repair retains
this operation identity and progress. It is not a second guest execution.

For successful ordinary RAM, finish the staged restore's control/MXCSR validation
before publishing live state, as in40. Publishing save fields through the bounded
coordinator can exclude observers during that service; this is an implementation
choice stronger than required image atomicity, with a cost, not a new guest
promise to be relied upon by other access paths.

**Implementation seam, not ISA evidence.** At pin
`c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294`, `access_prepare_mmu` probes its first
and second pages before returning. The FX helpers pass512 bytes and then perform
individual field operations. `do_xsave_fpu` emits header stores and eight80-bit
values; `do_xsave_sse` emits two64-bit stores per XMM. `do_xrstor_fpu` modifies
live control/status/tags before reading all register payloads, and SSE restore
modifies each half as read. These are concrete adaptation seams: replace the
whole-range preparation with used spans, and target staged restore state rather
than partially mutating authoritative state. The helper's missing metadata from39
still needs correction. [QEMU access.c](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/access.c),
[QEMU fpu_helper.c](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c).

**External-effect recovery interface.** A mixed RAM/device state-image access
cannot be implemented by copying512 bytes or rolling back completed beats.
Each actually issued endpoint operation retains its admitted route, width,
direction, value/result, completion disposition and serial access identity.
`RETRY_ACCESS` means the access was not performed; `ACCESS_COMPLETED` advances
past that beat and never retries it merely because the coordinator parked.
Pending operations retain their identity and route until resolved. Completed
RAM writes use all-writer invalidation; completed device effects remain device
state. Staged restore register values can be abandoned on a guest fault without
undoing device reads. A genuine delivered guest fault ends this invocation;
a later architectural restart is a new invocation, not an internal replay whose
ledger suppresses legitimate guest accesses. Asynchronous events wait for the
appropriate guest boundary, rather than treating an internal beat boundary as
instruction completion. No endpoint callback may require guest progress while
holding RAM exclusion.

This ledger defines how a *legal selected access sequence* recovers; it does not
by itself establish that sequence. In particular, a device error/status is not
automatically an x86 #PF, and the endpoint must specify its access response.
Already completed effects cannot be relabeled unperformed to simplify exception
recovery. No defined partial effect is discarded by the interface.

**Exact remaining issue and falsifiers.** The inspected FXRSTOR entry and Vol1
§10.5.3 define invalid-MXCSR #GP and memory #PF, but do not explicitly order an
accessible invalid MXCSR against a later inaccessible used field. Likewise41
has not established FXRSTOR alignment/page priority. Table7-2 describes priorities
at instruction boundaries and is not used here to order operand micro-accesses.
A source-supported priority construction is still required for those competing
cases. For device operands, choose and justify the actual used-field beat widths,
order and stop rule under the UC implementation-variation permission and the
selected endpoints; do not demand that they reproduce the QEMU sequence or
physical Haswell's undocumented bus trace. Full FX semantics remain open at
these precise boundaries, not at the already selected RAM staging mechanism.

The RAM choice would reopen if an applicable instruction rule required explicit
payload progress before its later-page fault. The external interface fails if
repair can repeat a completed beat, if a fault erases prior A/D/source effects,
or if unused suffix/ignored state fields acquire new accesses. No such behavior
is accepted by this proposal.

**Next bounded action:** independent review of42, then one30-minute source/design
choice for the competing FXRSTOR checks and a concrete UC used-field access
sequence. Resolve whether existing instruction plus UC contracts permit the
candidate order; do not add a mock or require a physical micro-operation match.


**Claim42 disposition:** root Return32 independently accepts the scoped RAM
staging and external-effect recovery construction. The next action is the
competing-check/UC access choice above; do not repeat the accepted RAM case.

## State-image execution-check and UC sequence choice

**Method:** memory, September20, bounded source/design follow-on to accepted42.
Inspect the retained Intel253669-079US §23.25.2, the FX entries in253666-082US,
Vol1 §10.5.3, and accepted Intel092 §§11.1.1/14.3. No runtime, mock or physical
micro-operation matching is needed for this virtual implementation choice.

### CLM-03-02-43 — Select a consistent FX execution-check order and finite UC field sequence

**Scoped semantic contract independently accepted by root Return33.**
Intel **253669-079US, March2023, §23.25.2 p.23-22** distinguishes previous-instruction
traps, external interrupts, fetch faults, decode faults and execution faults.
It explicitly permits implementation variation *within* those major categories.
That is an applicable general rule about instruction execution, unlike Vol3A
Table7-2. It is not a license to override a particular instruction's specified
priority or transfer execution faults ahead of decoding/fetching the instruction.
[Primary Intel Vol3B](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/774491/253669-sdm-vol-3b.pdf).
The retained text is `/private/tmp/natemu-memory-review02-debug-vol3b.txt`,
lines24620–24629. Vol2A §2.5's legacy/VEX exception-class tables do not assign
FXSAVE/FXRSTOR to a generic vector class; their own entries govern their exceptions.

**Selected order.** Preserve already accepted event/fetch/decode handling, including
unsupported FXSR and illegal LOCK #UD. Within FX execution, check CR0.EM/TS #NM,
then mode-specific operand address/segment validity, then16-byte alignment using
the selected #GP(0) variant. Only after those pass admit all actual used-field
translations and permissions, in increasing linear field order. A translation
failure delivers its actual #PF; earlier walker effects survive as in42. An
otherwise valid misaligned FXRSTOR therefore faults for alignment without operand
page accesses, even if its purported image page is inaccessible. This choice
now rests on the execution-category variation rule, not on copying FXSAVE's
separate implementation note into FXRSTOR.

For FXRSTOR with OSFXSR enabled, all used-field admission precedes reading and
validating MXCSR. Thus an aligned image with accessible invalid MXCSR but an
inaccessible later used field yields #PF, not value #GP. Once admission succeeds,
read the defined fields in the sequence below, validate MXCSR immediately after
its read, and stop on illegal bits without installing any staged FP/vector state.
If OSFXSR is disabled, the previously selected policy omits MXCSR/XMM entirely:
neither their image values nor their mappings create new restore faults. When
multiple address checks themselves fail, use the increasing used-span order and
the memory owner's mode-specific #SS/#GP rules; never invent a page walk for a
noncanonical address. These are deterministic virtual-CPU decisions within the
cited bounds, not inferred behavior of a particular Haswell stepping.

Vol1 §10.5.3's illegal-MXCSR condition still applies whenever that value is reached;
the rule is not ignored because another state component was successfully staged.
No illegal MXCSR becomes live. This completes the competing-case design gap in42
without requiring that all physical implementations share the chosen precedence.
[FX instruction rules](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf),
[MXCSR restore rule](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/671436/253665-sdm-vol-1.pdf).

**Selected UC/mixed-route access sequence.** Generate a finite list from the
accepted mode/REX/OSFXSR field schema: increasing image offset, covering only
defined accessed bytes. Include save's MXCSR_MASK, omit it on restore; preserve
all reserved/software gaps and excluded XMM slots. Within each contiguous field,
choose the largest naturally aligned beat among8,4,2,1 bytes that fits entirely
inside that field and the current admitted physical route extent. Never merge
across a field's unused padding. Thus an80-bit slot is8+2 bytes, an XMM slot is8+8,
a two-byte FCW is one2-byte beat, and a one-byte abridged FTW is one1-byte beat.
The schema supplies the selected pointer/selector/FOP fields; it must not inherit
the helper's zero-pointer or omitted-FOP defect. These widths describe virtual
bus requests, not a requirement to emit native GPR access instructions to MMIO.

Perform each beat once in that order and await its defined completion before
issuing the next. All image-route admissions precede the first explicit beat,
so a later not-present used page produces no image/device beat. Once beats have
started, completed read or write side effects remain recorded; they are never
rolled back. For restore the sequence reaches MXCSR after lower-offset fields;
invalid MXCSR stops before higher-offset fields, discards staged CPU values and
retains any already completed device-read effects. For save, all captured values
come from one architectural state identity; an internal pause does not recapture
a different image. A mixed RAM/device image uses this same sequencer rather than
performing the RAM subset first and changing the promised device order.

**Why this is a permitted construction.** Intel092 §11.1.1 permits multi-access
x87/SSE state traffic rather than promising image-wide atomicity. Its separate
named MOV-family16-byte guarantees do not apply to FX state images. Section14.3
explicitly makes x87/SIMD UC behavior implementation dependent and allows even
repeated accesses. Choose the simpler nonrepeating field-contained realization;
software receives the virtual machine's declared sequence, not a guarantee of
portable physical-device behavior from arbitrary FX instructions. This choice
preserves UC's nonspeculative traffic and instruction order, all defined state
bytes, ignored-field exclusions and prior completed effects. It does not derive
UC order from the WB-only §11.2.3.1, nor extend the variation permission to
unrelated guest MOV instructions or endpoints' own register semantics.
[Primary Intel memory rules](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf).

**Endpoint outcomes and stop rule.** Dispatch the declared beat to the selected
endpoint width/address handler. If the endpoint does not implement that width,
use its defined unsupported-access response; do not silently invent a different
width, bulk operation or x86 #PF. A normal response, including a defined unmapped
read value or discarded write, completes the beat and advances. A pending
response retains the request and route; internal repair cannot reissue a completed
beat. A legitimately specified guest fault stops with the retained effects and
original instruction identity; an internal backend failure requiring contained
VM termination stops without a fabricated guest exception or successful retirement.
An ordinary bus error/status alone is not that guest-fault permission. This
reuses the existing typed device/access contract rather than adding a new device
error model. No wait for host I/O or guest progress holds the RAM grant.

Page/PTE/code aliases use the held routes and all-writer protocol. Each committed
RAM beat and walker update contributes to required invalidation before guest
reentry; the operation's captured decode remains pinned while internal service
continues. Genuine delivered faults release this invocation according to42, and
architectural reexecution is a new invocation. Successful completion publishes
the staged restore state and invalidates stale generator bindings before the next
guest instruction. Defined pending x87 status is retained without an invented
#MF during FXRSTOR.

**Disposition proposed for39–43.** Together these contracts select the FX state
owner, actual accessed fields, successful encoding, ordinary-RAM fault staging,
execution-check precedence and UC effect/recovery sequence. Actual adapters must
conform to them; no current generator/helper has thereby been shown conformant.
This is scoped FXSAVE/FXRSTOR design closure, not XSAVE/XRSTOR, FSAVE/FRSTOR,
all floating-point arithmetic or a code-generator selection. Costs of coordinated
images and scalar device beats remain unmeasured. A contrary instruction-specific
priority/width rule would reopen only the affected choice; proof that an adapter
reads ignored bytes, drops completed effects or loses metadata is an implementation
failure against this contract rather than justification to change guest scope.

**Next bounded action:** independent review of43 and the scoped FX closure. If
accepted, address the distinct XSAVE/XRSTOR request-mask/header/initial-state
contract with the exposed XCR0=bits0–2 profile; do not repeat the same FX fault
fixtures or request a physical bus-trace oracle.


**Claim43 disposition:** root Return33 accepts43 and scoped FX design closure
from39–43. The next bounded semantic action is XSAVE/XRSTOR under the declared
XCR0 profile; the FX precedence and UC choices no longer await a new oracle.

## Standard XSAVE/XRSTOR — selected three-component contract

**Method:** memory, September20, source/design pass after accepted FX39–43.
Read Intel253665-078US §§13.4–13.8/13.13 and Intel334569-092US XSAVE/XRSTOR;
inspect the pinned QEMU standard restore helper only as implementation evidence.
No execution, mock or optimized/compacted/supervisor save variant is introduced.

### CLM-03-02-44 — Bind standard masks, headers and initialization to the resident state owner

**Scoped design independently accepted by root Return34.** The exposed profile
has supported XCR0 bits0–2, legal current XCR0 values1,3,7, standard AVX component
at offset576 with256 bytes, and no XSAVEOPT/XSAVEC/XSAVES/XGETBV1/XFD. Guest
software can request any subset through the low32 bits of EDX and EAX:
`R = XCR0 & ((uint64(EDX) << 32) | EAX)`. Bits outside current XCR0 are ignored
in this instruction mask, not a reason to #GP. They are different from forbidden
bits in a restore header. Current mode, REX.W, XCR0 and the request mask are
captured once with the operation identity.

**Instruction checks.** Keep fetch/decode precedence; unsupported XSAVE,
CR4.OSXSAVE=0 or an illegal LOCK prefix produces #UD. CR0.TS produces #NM.
Apply mode-specific address checks and select #GP(0) for a base not64-byte
aligned, within the expressly permitted #AC/#GP variation. Do not inherit
FXSAVE's CR0.EM check or its CR4.OSFXSR-dependent omission: the XSAVE entries
and XCR0 select this instruction's behavior. Then follow the header and selected
field stages below. Instruction-specific sequencing takes precedence over43's
FX-only ordering. [Intel Vol2D092, XRSTOR pp6-49–53 and XSAVE entry](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922485/334569-092-sdm-vol-2d.pdf).

| Component | Save when requested | Restore when requested and header bit1 | Restore when requested and header bit0 |
|---|---|---|---|
|0, x87|Defined x87 header/pointers/opcode and eight80-bit payloads|Load the same mode/REX-defined state|FCW037F, FSW0, all tags empty, pointers/selectors/opcode0 and all eight80-bit data values0|
|1, SSE|Mode-visible XMM slots|Load mode-visible XMM slots|Zero mode-visible XMM slots|
|2, AVX|Mode-visible upper128-bit YMM slots at576+16i|Load those upper halves|Zero those upper halves|

Unrequested components retain their old state. Outside64-bit mode only registers
0–7 change; registers8–15 retain their prior state. Lower and upper vector halves
are separate components: initializing AVX does not zero the low XMM half.
**MXCSR is the exception to this table:** standard XSAVE saves MXCSR and its
capability mask whenever `R & 6 != 0`; standard XRSTOR reads and validates MXCSR
whenever `R & 6 != 0`, regardless of either component's XSTATE_BV bit. Initializing
SSE/AVX does not replace this memory value with1F80. XRSTOR ignores MXCSR_MASK.
The x87 initialization uses the documented XSAVE initial configuration plus the
usual initialized opcode; it is stronger than executing FNINIT alone, which does
not zero the eight data registers. No pending #MF or SIMD exception is invented
by restoring status. [Intel Vol1 §§13.5–13.8](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/671436/253665-sdm-vol-1.pdf),
[FNINIT operation](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf).

**XSAVE header and field transaction.** Standard save does not use the init or
modified optimizations. Select the permitted conservative in-use model: every
supported component is considered in use, including one currently holding its
initial values. Always save requested components. Read the existing64-bit
XSTATE_BV at offset512 and compute `new_BV = old_BV | R`; bits not requested
remain exactly as read, including bits unsupported by this CPU. Do not validate
or sanitize that old bitmap, clear XCOMP_BV, or zero reserved header bytes.
Standard XSAVE writes no header bytes other than512–519. Even R=0 retains this
header read/write operation; it is not an instruction-wide no-op. The old header
value is a guest input, not a compiler-supplied zero. The conservative in-use
choice is permitted by §13.6 and does not expose an unsupported optimization.

Build the write list from R and the mode-visible defined payload fields, including
MXCSR/MASK ifR&6. Admit the header read/write and those payload writes before
explicit payload effects. Capture current architectural state, read old_BV once,
write the selected payload in increasing field order, and write new_BV last.
Header-last is the selected sequence, not an atomic-publication promise to a
concurrent unsynchronized guest reader. Each committed write remains owned by
the all-writer protocol; a later internal failure cannot pretend it never happened.
The operation is not an indivisible832-byte native store. R=0 performs only the
header transaction, without touching x87/vector payloads or their mappings.

**XRSTOR header before data.** Admit/read offsets512–535 as three64-bit words,
in increasing order: XSTATE_BV, XCOMP_BV and the next reserved word. Require
`XSTATE_BV & ~XCR0 == 0`, and both remaining words zero. These checks apply even
forR=0 and even if an offending header bit is not requested. Any nonzero XCOMP_BV
is rejected: bit63 cannot enable compacted restore when XSAVEC is unadvertised,
and other bits are forbidden in the standard form. The standard rule explicitly
checks header bytes8–23; bytes24–63 are reserved with a software-zero recommendation,
not an additional selected #GP test. This realization neither reads them nor
borrows the compacted-form all-reserved-header check. Thus no blanket read of576
bytes is needed just to validate a24-byte header.

After a valid captured header, derive `load = R & XSTATE_BV` and
`initialize = R & ~XSTATE_BV`. Build payload spans only for components actually
loaded, plus the independent MXCSR read ifR&6. Stage initialized components
without a payload read. Admit all those spans, then consume them in increasing
field order; validate MXCSR when reached. On success publish the combined staged
state at one guest boundary, retaining every unrequested component and unaffected
upper/mode-inaccessible register. A bad header precedes payload translation;
a valid header followed by a bad requested payload page yields #PF; after payload
admission illegal MXCSR yields #GP. These choices retain the primary header-first
validation rule and the permitted execution-check ordering from43. No live state
is partially overwritten before these checks finish.

**Access permissions are not copied blindly from FX.** Intel Vol1 §13.13 expressly
permits XSAVE-family accesses to any byte in an operated-on component, including
mode-inaccessible register regions that must not be modified outside64-bit mode.
Consequently, FX's stronger field-access exclusions cannot be stated as an XSAVE
architectural prohibition. This implementation selects the smaller defined-field
access realization, with no incidental reads of padding or inaccessible slots;
it still preserves the distinction between permitted access and prohibited
modification. It does not require another CPU to make the same choice. Header
accesses remain required even when no payload component is requested; MXCSR
access remains required even when SSE/AVX are initialized rather than loaded.
No whole832-byte read/write/probe is substituted for the derived list.

**Repair, partial effects and aliases.** Reuse42–43's captured operation, finite
8/4/2/1-byte field-contained device beats and completed-effect ledger. XRSTOR's
header read may itself have device effects before the derived payload admission
faults; those effects survive, while live FP/vector state remains old. Internal
pause resumes from the captured header and serial beat, not by reading a changed
header again. XSAVE's header read and later write use that captured old bitmap;
there is no LOCK promise against concurrent guest modification. Admitted aliases
use the memory owner's retained translations and all-writer invalidation for
header, payload and walk A/D writes. No native/private pointer leaks through
staged state. Invalid source-pointer metadata remains guest architectural metadata,
never a native continuation target. Guest-delivered faults end this invocation;
a subsequent guest restart legitimately reads its inputs again. Endpoint status
is not converted into an invented #PF, and host/device waits do not hold RAM
exclusion. Publication updates all generator caches/working-root ownership before
subsequent execution, including unrequested vector halves that must survive.

**Pinned-helper adaptation evidence.** QEMU pin
`c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294` `valid_xrstor_header` reads the same
three header words and checks their standard-form predicates. However,
`helper_xrstor` first prepares the entire576-byte legacy/header range and then an
extended range; that is not the selected sparse admission interface.
`do_xrstor` calls `do_xrstor_mxcsr` only under the SSE-request condition, while its
AVX branch restores/clears upper halves independently. ForR=4 the shown helper
path would therefore omit the standard-form required MXCSR load. Adapt that call
condition toR&6 and stage all state before commit; do not accept the upstream
helper unchanged merely because the header check matches. Its component
initializers/data layouts remain reusable references, subject to the metadata
corrections already found in39. [Pinned helper source](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c).

**Discriminating cases, not a requested mock.** R=4 with XSTATE_BV2=0 initializes
only upper vector halves but still loads MXCSR; R=0 with XSTATE_BV containing a
bit outsideXCR0 faults for the header; XSAVE R=0 still updates the header with
its preserved value; XRSTOR R=1 with XSTATE_BV0=0 initializes x87 without fetching
its data slots. A valid header with no loaded vector components must not create
a fault merely by probing the unneeded AVX area. Each follows directly from this
contract and can later test adapter conformance, without being a new acceptance
experiment or a narrower product instruction set.

**Proposed closure and next action.** This selects standard XSAVE/XRSTOR masks,
header validation, initial states, state ownership, accessed spans and effect
recovery for the accepted three-component profile. It adds no optimized,
compacted or supervisor forms; those unsupported encodings remain #UD under
that profile. Actual implementation conformance and costs remain unmeasured.
Independent root review should check the MXCSR exception, reserved-header extent
and mode-dependent access choice. After review, the next distinct state-image
obligation is legacy FSAVE/FNSAVE/FRSTOR environment/reset/waiting semantics;
do not repeat standard-save fault permutations as a separate architecture gate.


**Claim44 disposition:** root Return34 accepts the standard three-component
XSAVE/XRSTOR contract. Preserve the explicit helper correction and sparse-access
choice; actual adapter conformance remains planning/implementation work.

## Component selection proposal — root

**Bounded decision pass:** September20, source/interface synthesis of accepted
claims21–23/27–29 and the now-selected CPU/runtime/memory/lifetime contracts.
No emitter run, instruction-count claim or new helper implementation. The decision
is which coherent component combination to take forward, rather than whether
an upstream emulator already supplies our machine. Stop at a reviewable selection
and its actual disqualifiers; keep unresolved semantics with their owners.

### CLM-03-02-46 — Propose TCG with an adapted QEMU semantic frontend as the first planning combination

**Author:** root; scoped component policy independently accepted by translator
Return35 after the rationale correction below. Select the pinned
TCG IR/AArch64 generator with an adapted QEMU x86 semantic frontend and inherited
QEMU/SoftFloat arithmetic helpers as the first component combination to carry
into planning, subject to the remaining semantic-contract resolution. This is
not acceptance of unchanged system TCG, its execution loop, softMMU, device
runtime, process boundary or helper exception machinery. The architecture remains
host generation plus guarded captive EL1 execution with resident shims.

**Decision policy and tie-break:** choose QEMU-derived semantic/frontend/helper
provenance as the initial integration baseline, and keep that selection in its
existing TCG producer chain. This is a reversible engineering policy under
uncertainty, not a comparative advantage proved over every coherent FEX or
Box64 combination. Their own frontend/generator chains are also coherent.
The existing field/helper descriptions make the chosen combination concrete;
they do not establish a unique winner. We accept TCG’s canonical synchronization
and potentially weaker register-cache optimization headroom in exchange for
not introducing a mixed-provenance state/IR mapping in this first combination.
Avoiding a new cross-IR bridge is an advantage over a QEMU-frontend/other-backend
hybrid, not over FEX’s own frontend. No measured speed ranking, globally minimum
adaptation effort or engineering-hour estimate is inferred from this policy.

| Combination under the same runtime contract | Integration consequence |
| --- | --- |
| Adapted QEMU frontend + TCG + selected QEMU/SoftFloat helpers | Frontend globals, deferred-condition descriptors, helper annotations and memory operations already belong to one semantic/IR pipeline. Retarget that pipeline to one resident field/effect contract. This avoids a new cross-IR bridge for this particular semantic selection; it still requires every captive boundary transformation below. Proposed first combination. |
| FEX-derived frontend/generator + selected resident semantics | Retains its optimizing IR and register-cache opportunities. Either adapt its own semantic/state model to the complete system contract, or bind separately reused helpers to its authoritative fields at actual calls. No per-instruction state conversion is inherently required. The accepted allocation-root/entry work establishes a concrete route; lack of a local Linux build is not a rejection. Retain as an alternative, not an accepted kill. |
| Box64-derived frontend/generator + selected resident semantics | Retains its direct lowering and flag/NEON caches.
