Adapt the same semantic/access/recovery boundaries, and replace or account for secondary entries/callret references under the accepted bounded lifetime contract. Those transfers are mutable design choices, not evidence that Box64 is intrinsically unbounded. Retain as an alternative, not an accepted kill. |

Claims21–23 and27 supply the symmetric interface/source comparison. The declared
tie-break chooses that semantic provenance and keeps its frontend and IR in
their existing producer relationship; source inspection makes this policy
feasible but does not prove that another coherent chain is more expensive. It is **not** that the other two lack
system-emulator products, that their unaudited paths are defective, or that more
QEMU source has accumulated in this investigation. Using a QEMU frontend with
another backend remains possible, but would require a specified frontend/IR
bridge; the proposal deliberately does not add that separate component now.
Choosing FEX's own frontend instead would be a different coherent combination,
not a requirement to preserve QEMU's representations inside it.

**Mandatory transformations define the selected component.**

- Host translation consumes admitted immutable source and explicit guest/native
  contexts. Neither guest fetch nor an optimization may acquire ambient guest
  RAM or host pointers. All object references/relocations use runtime symbols and
  the accepted publication/lifetime manifest.
- Ordinary eligible RAM accesses lower through the guarded native mappings and
  selected TSO/atomicity policy. Replace upstream softMMU lookup/slow-return
  assumptions. Address-domain guards, x86 permission/type routing and exact
  effect/recovery identity remain mandatory. A QEMU memory helper's existence
  is not proof that it can be called unchanged at EL1.
- Keep one resident architectural field contract. Bind frontend global offsets,
  deferred flags and selected helper read/write sets to it together. Do not copy
  host-only fields or treat the full upstream CPU object as a transferable ABI.
  The exact C layout is planning work; field authority and relocation rules are
  architectural requirements. Register caching remains permitted where recovery
  and helper effects preserve authority.
- Replace host CPU-loop exits, longjmp, host fault handlers and helper return-PC
  assumptions with the accepted permanent landing, captured effects and resident
  service dispositions. Legal guest boundaries/checkpoints must occur inside
  objects where the code-lifetime contract requires them; a TB-entry poll is
  insufficient. Do not impose a full canonical spill on every ordinary instruction
  merely because a diagnostic fixture used one.
- Apply the reviewed semantic corrections, including hidden PAE roots, state-image
  metadata/sticky-state ownership and the standard XRSTOR MXCSR request rule.
  Continue to resolve known FP and precise-delivery discrepancies against primary
  rules. An enabled opcode cannot silently become #UD because extraction is
  unfinished. Inherit numeric algorithms; no new handwritten floating-point
  implementation is selected.

The relevant pinned producer sites include
[frontend globals/instruction origins](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/translate.c),
[helper effect contracts](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/docs/devel/tcg-ops.rst),
[AArch64 memory/call lowering](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/tcg/aarch64/tcg-target.c.inc),
and [FP/state helpers](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c).
The same pinned FEX/Box64 sources and limitations remain in22; this proposal
neither promotes their unknown cells into costs nor erases their viable adapters.

**Expected advantage and retained risks.** Native paging, fault entry and TSO can
remove work from upstream software memory/ordering paths while keeping ordinary
execution inside the VM. That mechanism comes from the architecture, not a
unique TCG capability. All three generators may exploit it. Guards, checkpoints,
recovery state, compilation, code transport and exceptional services consume
part of the savings. No current observation establishes material end-to-end
superiority or economic break-even. Tracks04/05 must estimate/measure those
costs; synthesis must apply its stop/continue/planning decision. TCG's allocator
and state synchronization may leave optimization headroom that another generator
uses better. A measured unacceptable common-path cost or a concrete unresolvable
semantic/relocation dependency reopens this choice.

Maintaining altered memory lowering, helper contracts and frontend state fields
creates an upstream merge burden even though this combination avoids a new IR
bridge. Licensing remains the project's required warning, not a selection veto.
The FEX preparation stays retained as diagnostic work; it is neither thrown away
as a failed generator nor required to finish merely to justify this alternative.

**Review boundary:** accept or return the component choice on architectural fit,
explicit tradeoff and the existing matched evidence. Do not interpret it as an
accepted quantitative ranking, actual exporter correctness, permission to begin
implementation, or complete question2 closure while its remaining semantic
contracts are open. A reviewer may reject this rationale if it merely restates
upstream system coverage; the listed captive transformations and alternative
paths are the substance to evaluate. The next action is independent decision
review, not another isolated MOV sample or a build-host search.

## Legacy x87 environment family — common ownership and completion contract

**Method:** memory, September20, bounded source/design pass after accepted44.
Read Intel253666-082US FLDENV/FRSTOR/FSAVE/FNSAVE/FSTENV/FNSTENV, Intel
253665-078US §§8.1.7–8.1.10/Figures8-9–12, and Intel253669-079US
§§23.18.4/23.18.6.9–12. Inspect pinned helpers and their immediate frontend
call sites. No native execution, mock or per-opcode experimental gate.

### CLM-03-02-45 — Keep legacy environment formats and post-save effects in the same full80 owner

**Scoped family interface independently accepted by root Return36.** These instructions use
the same authoritative x87 full80 registers, physical empty/nonempty tags, TOP,
control/status/pending state and guest pointer/opcode metadata already selected
for FX and XSAVE. They do not require another floating-point engine or a host
FPU state image. Their layout and completion effects are nevertheless different.

| Form | Memory/state operation | Effect only after successful operation |
|---|---|---|
|FNSTENV|Save environment before modification|Mask all six x87 exceptions in the live control word; retain data and TOP|
|FSTENV|FWAIT followed by FNSTENV|Same mask effect, only if the FN instruction completes|
|FNSAVE|Save environment plus eight full80 stack payloads|FINIT-style environment reset; tags empty, not zeroing raw register payloads|
|FSAVE|FWAIT followed by FNSAVE|Same reset effect, only if the FN instruction completes|
|FLDENV|Restore environment; leave raw full80 data unchanged|Install loaded control/status/TOP/empty tags and represented metadata|
|FRSTOR|Restore environment plus eight full80 stack payloads|Install complete restored x87 state|

The stored control word for FNSTENV precedes the new mask bits. FNSAVE's reset
sets FCW037F, FSW0, tagsFFFF, pointers/selectors/opcode0; it does not perform
XSAVE initialization's extra zeroing of all data payloads. Neither family changes
MXCSR or XMM/YMM. FSTENV's condition-code results are undefined; select preservation
of the prior C0–C3 as a permitted result, rather than adding a fake arithmetic
operation. Data tags becoming empty do not authorize losing raw full80 payloads
that an environment-only restore could subsequently expose.
[Intel Vol2A instruction entries, pp3-409–410,3-424–427,3-441–442](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf).

**Waiting is a real boundary.** The instruction reference explicitly describes
FSAVE and FSTENV assembler forms as separately executed FWAIT and FN instructions.
Do not fuse away the first instruction's exception identity, event boundary or
second instruction fetch. A pending exception caught by FWAIT prevents the save;
a fault in the following FN instruction is attributed to that instruction.
FN forms do not themselves check an old pending arithmetic exception. The selected
modern Intel virtual profile does not inherit the old486/Pentium MS-DOS no-wait
interrupt window or the Quark exception. FLDENV/FRSTOR's newly loaded unmasked
status is not delivered during that load; it becomes available to the next
waiting operation. Existing old pending-state checks, CR0.EM/TS, LOCK rejection,
segment/canonicality and mode-specific exception rules remain at the semantic
entry, not delegated to a native cast. FWAIT's own CR0.MP/TS behavior remains its
existing instruction contract rather than being replaced by the FN check.

**Four format schemas, not an FX image.** Operand size selects14-byte or28-byte
environment; full save/restore appends80 bytes with eight contiguous10-byte
logical ST slots. TOP maps those slots to physical register ownership. The full
16-bit tag image is in physical-register order. Protected/compatibility modes
use the protected layout; real/v8086 use the real-address packing. The legacy
forms retain these operand-size layouts in64-bit mode; REX.W does not turn them
into FXSAVE64. No padding to16-byte ST slots, abridged FTW, MXCSR field or512-byte
extent belongs here.

The authoritative schema is Intel Vol1 Figures8-9–12: protected32 has CW/SW/TW
at0/4/8, FIP at12, FCS plus11-bit FOP at16, FDP at20 and FDS at24; protected16
has seven16-bit words at0,2,4,6,8,10,12, with no separately represented opcode.
Real-address formats pack pointer high parts and opcode differently from selector
fields and must not use the protected struct. Preserve explicit zero bits and
ignore reserved input bits according to that schema. Restored pointer state is
guest metadata; upper32 pointer bits clear as specified in§8.1.8, never become
native PCs. The selected CPU does not advertise selector deprecation. A final
field-binding detail remains below for metadata not represented by the14-byte
protected layout; it is not concealed by calling the formats interchangeable.
[Intel Vol1 §§8.1.7–8.1.10](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/671436/253665-sdm-vol-1.pdf).

For tags, the modern x87 rule explicitly interprets an incoming00/01/10 tag as
nonempty and11 as empty. Subsequent use examines the full80 value; save classifies
a nonempty value to construct the actual2-bit tag. Thus an inconsistent nonempty
tag supplied by software is not an independent persistent numeric classification
to preserve forever. Empty/nonempty plus untouched full80 data is a sufficient
representation; arithmetic, TOP rotation and image stores must all use it
consistently. This is primary architectural support for the representation, not
merely a QEMU convenience. [Intel Vol3B079 §23.18.4](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/774491/253669-sdm-vol-3b.pdf).

**Fault and effect composition.** Select the established bounded RAM admission,
immutable save snapshot and staged restore approach. An unsuccessful payload
admission does not reset the live FPU or install FNSTENV's new masks. Only after
all required save effects complete does the resident owner commit its reset/mask
transition. If device beats have completed, retain them on a subsequent fault;
there is no rollback of external state. Header/data writes and walker A/D aliases
join all-writer invalidation before reentry. A captured operation survives internal
repair without recapturing registers or replaying completed beats. Host/device
waits do not retain RAM exclusion. Genuine fault delivery ends that invocation.

Unlike aligned FX slots, contiguous10-byte register data can cross a page or
route boundary. Use checked spans and the bounded field/route-contained beat
sequencer; never infer a safe native10-byte access. Nor impose a new16/64-byte
base alignment requirement. Ordinary #AC rules apply where listed; Intel
Vol3B§23.18.6.9 explicitly excepts the register-stack portion from alignment
checking. Do not generate #AC merely because a10-byte payload's native sub-beat
is unaligned. The guest environment check and native safe-access splitting are
separate. FLDENV explicitly allows nonuniform intermediate register state after
page/limit faults; retaining the old complete state until staged publication is
a permitted conservative realization, not a claim that every physical fault
handler observes that state.

**Actual reuse limits.** At QEMU pin
`c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294`, `do_fsave`/`do_frstor` use contiguous
10-byte full80 slots and TOP-relative accesses; save calls `do_fninit` afterward.
The tag classifier has the required empty/nonempty/value structure. However,
`helper_fldenv` prepares its operand with `MMU_DATA_STORE` despite loading it;
that would incorrectly reject read-only readable RAM. `do_fldenv` restores only
control/status/tags, omitting the pointer/opcode state required here. The inspected
`helper_fstenv` and its direct frontend call do not implement the required post-save
mask operation. `do_fstenv`'s shared data32 split is not evidence that the distinct
real-address pointer/opcode layout is implemented. These are concrete adapter
corrections; retain read-only input permissions and the selected schema instead
of adopting the helper unchanged. [Helper source](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c),
[immediate frontend calls](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/translate.c).

**Scoped closure and remaining field detail.** This selects one sufficient owner,
format-dispatch and completion/recovery interface for the entire family. The
protected14-byte image has no opcode slot, while generic instruction prose says
restore loads the opcode. This pass does not silently decide preserve versus
clear for that unrepresented FOP, or invent extra input bytes. The exact legacy
metadata rule requires a bounded primary-source resolution before claiming full
bit-level legacy-image conformance. The owner already holds both old metadata
and staged image, so either supported rule fits this interface without another
engine, recovery mechanism or experiment. The grouped contract is therefore a
proposed architecture choice, not a claim that all bit-level adapters are finished.

**Remaining semantic categories that can change architecture.** Further work
should be grouped by shared mechanism, rather than repeating this exercise for
every opcode:

- Floating-point result/status compatibility across the required x87/SSE/AVX
  families: rounding, masked/unmasked exception suppression, NaN/subnormal and
  transcendental behavior. A reusable engine's unsupported semantics could change
  component choice; another image offset or missing mask assignment does not.
- Restartable multi-access execution and implicit accesses: REP progress,
  descriptor/task/event transitions and device effects. They must fit the retained
  operation/commit boundary and bounded callback rules; an instruction requiring
  a missing kind of continuation would change that interface.
- Debug/event ordering and state ownership across asynchronous stops: deferred
  faults, pending debug and suppression boundaries must compose with the semantic
  owner, rather than being lost in codegen helper conventions.
- Actual full-state recovery and helper ABI extraction for a selected generator:
  the current native-root prototypes do not yet prove all live state can be bound
  through fault/repair/commit with acceptable cost. That can change generator
  choice even when an instruction's semantic contract is settled.

Scalar field encodings, supported-mask checks, helper load/store permission fixes
and post-success reset/mask assignments are implementation-conformance work
against the selected interfaces. The remaining14-byte metadata rule is a precise
source gap, not a reason to demand a new runtime architecture or a mock per opcode.

**Next bounded action:** independent review of45, then settle the remaining
legacy unrepresented-metadata rule within one short source pass and reconcile
this family. Subsequent architecture research should select one of the above
shared categories with an explicit component/interface decision at stake.

### CLM-03-02-47 — Correct the inherited binary SSE/AVX NaN policy through SoftFloat's existing selector

**Author:** root; source-backed adaptation independently accepted by memory
Return37.
Resolve claim11's known ADDSD discrepancy by selecting `float_2nan_prop_ab` for
the inherited SSE arithmetic status, rather than accepting the x87 payload rule
as an implicit compatibility concession. Keep the x87 status's `float_2nan_prop_x87`
rule. This uses an existing library mechanism; no handwritten FP algorithm or
new reduced-precision mode is introduced. The proposal is independent of46's
pending generator decision: any component inheriting these helpers needs the
same semantic correction.

[Intel Vol1-078 §4.8.3.5 Table4-7](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/671436/253665-sdm-vol-1.pdf)
requires binary SSE/AVX arithmetic with two NaNs to select the first source's
payload, quieting it when signaling. With only one NaN that NaN supplies the
result. x87 instead has its specified signaling/quiet and significand preference.
The table's footnote distinguishes the legacy destructive source from the first
source in VEX nondestructive syntax. Preserve that semantic operand order through
IR optimization and helper argument placement; algebraic commutativity of finite
addition is not permission to swap observable NaN operands.

At the pinned QEMU revision, [partsN(pick_nan)](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/fpu/softfloat-parts.c.inc)
first raises invalid/signaling status if either input is signaling. Its existing
`float_2nan_prop_ab` case chooses `a` if it is a NaN, otherwise `b`, and quiets
a selected signaling NaN. This supplies the required payload choice while still
recording invalid when an unselected second input is signaling. Default-NaN mode
must remain off for this payload-preserving policy. The source-derived ADDSD
trace in11 already establishes that its host fast path declines NaNs and reaches
this selector; a native arithmetic fast path added later must preserve the same
behavior or dispatch the exceptional operands to the inherited path.

The discriminating cases are first-QNaN/second-larger-QNaN (return first),
first-QNaN/second-SNaN (return first and record invalid), and first-SNaN/second-QNaN
(quiet the first and record invalid). These are source deductions, not new
hardware observations or a request for another mock. Guest mask/sticky-state and
result suppression use the resident FP effect owner; selecting a payload does
not authorize committing a result when an unmasked exception suppresses it.

Do not apply this table blindly to every instruction involving a float. MIN/MAX,
comparisons, conversions and instructions with explicit special rules retain
those rules. FMA retains its separate three-input NaN/invalid policy in
[cpu_init_fp_statuses](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c);
x87 retains its own precision/rounding and NaN policy. The unexposed 3DNow path
is not a justification for using its status configuration as SSE behavior.

**Decision consequence:** if accepted, the known binary-arithmetic mismatch has
an explicit inherited-library correction and no longer needs a choice between
silent payload incompatibility and new FP implementation. This is not acceptance
of all numerical helpers, unmasked SIMD delivery or actual emitter conformance.
Those remaining categories must be resolved once at their owner/interface level;
this correction does not justify an open-ended opcode-by-opcode campaign. Next:
independent source review of the selector and its scoped applicability.


**Claim46 review correction:** independent translator review identified that
coherence alone does not distinguish TCG from the other generators’ own frontend
chains. The decision policy above now says so explicitly. This is a reversible
choice of QEMU semantic provenance plus its existing producer relationship, with
the canonical-state/optimization tradeoff retained, not an evidence-derived
least-cost ranking or an advantage over a fictitious mandatory hybrid.

## Shared FP result/status contract and inherited transcendental limitation

**Method:** memory, September20, bounded source/design pass after accepted45/47.
Read retained Intel Vol1 numerical/exception rules and actual pinned QEMU
arithmetic/trigonometric paths; identify an existing full80 replacement candidate.
No native arithmetic, mock, implementation or measured accuracy experiment.

### CLM-03-02-48 — Keep one staged FP effect owner, but replace the binary64 x87 trigonometric path

**Conditional interface and concrete incompatibility accepted in root Return38.**
Accepted47 supplies the inherited binary SSE/AVX NaN correction. Keep separate
x87 and SSE status configurations, and define each numerical invocation by its
architectural operands, precision/destination format, rounding control, masks,
DAZ/FTZ where applicable, and instruction-specific NaN/exception policy. Snapshot
those inputs together. Compute through existing numerical engines into scratch
results and operation-local status, then let the resident owner commit the
architecturally permitted effects. A helper directly writing its result into
`env` is not an exception/commit contract.

**Shared result/status interface.** Return candidate result(s), newly generated
exception classes, required condition/flag changes, and an explicit disposition:
normal result; suppressed destination; or the x87 exceptional scaled register
result described below. Distinguish new exceptions from already sticky bits:
a prior sticky flag alone must not make every later arithmetic operation fault.
The owner combines new status with old sticky state under the selected instruction's
priority rules, applies masks and decides data/flag/TOP/pop/store publication.
Preserve input identity and all unaffected register bits, including legacy upper
vector state; SIMD lane calculations contribute to one instruction-level commit,
not partial live destination writes before a later lane's exception.

- **x87:** FCW selects rounding and the precision-control modes only for the
  instructions affected by that field. The underlying full80 storage remains
  intact; transcendental operations do not become binary32/64 merely because
  FCW precision selects narrower basic arithmetic. Masked exceptions use their
  defined default/result and status effects. Unmasked invalid, denormal and
  divide cases use their specified suppression and deferred pending-exception
  rules. Do not extrapolate claim38's invalid-SQRT suppression to all exceptions.
- **x87 overflow/underflow:** Intel Vol1 §§8.5.4–8.5.5 distinguishes memory stores
  from register results. Unmasked memory-result overflow/underflow preserves
  destination and TOP; unmasked register-result overflow/underflow writes the
  appropriately rounded exponent-biased result (bias24576), together with
  specified status/C1. FSCALE's massive overflow/underflow has further defined
  infinity/zero outcomes. An engine API returning only already saturated infinity
  or zero plus a flag is insufficient to reconstruct that scaled value. Bind an
  existing numerical routine's appropriate extended/intermediate result or its
  existing exceptional-result support; do not invent a generic rollback or
  handwritten replacement FP arithmetic. Precision exceptions likewise retain
  their own result/rounding effects rather than inheriting invalid suppression.
- **SSE/AVX:** MXCSR supplies its own rounding, masks, DAZ and FTZ controls.
  Masked outcomes commit with the defined sticky flags. Unmasked SIMD exceptions
  use the defined pre/post-computation priority and destination suppression,
  with #XM/#UD delivery determined by the OS exception-support control. Source
  operands, memory input effects and destination are separate: suppression of a
  destination does not erase a completed input-device read. Native host FPSR
  and host exception masks are not the authoritative guest status.

[Intel Vol1 §§8.5,8.7,11.5](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/671436/253665-sdm-vol-1.pdf)
provides these different effect classes. The pinned QEMU `update_fp_status`
maps FCW rounding/precision and `update_mxcsr_status` maps rounding, DAZ, FTZ and
sticky flags into SoftFloat status. These are reusable configuration seams; they
do not prove that an unchanged helper's direct assignments already implement
the staged owner. The rounding/denormal policy remains operation-specific where
the instruction overrides it. Accepted47 does not override MIN/MAX, comparisons,
conversions or the distinct FMA policy.
[QEMU FP helper source](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c).

**Concrete architectural incompatibility in inherited trigonometry.** At that
pin, `helper_fsin`, `helper_fcos`, `helper_fsincos` and `helper_fptan` route through
`floatx80_to_double`, host binary64 libm, and `double_to_floatx80`. This is a real
precision bottleneck, not an unmeasured performance concern. Intel Vol1
§§8.3.8/8.3.10 specifies full80 transcendental accuracy bounds for reduced
arguments, including less than1 ulp in round-to-nearest and the stated directed
rounding bounds. Its66-bit internal pi warning does not excuse a binary64 output
for small already-reduced inputs.

A source/mathematical counterexample requires no execution: choose exact
`x = 2^-29` for FSIN, with full80 state and round-to-nearest. For positive smallx,
`x^3/6 - x^5/120 < x - sin(x) < x^3/6`. The upper term is
`2^-87/6`, and the correction is negligible relative to `2^-92`.
In particular the difference exceeds five units of `2^-92`, whereas the actual
full80 ulp just belowx is the still smaller `2^-93`. The adjacent binary64 value
belowx is `x - 2^-82`. Thus sin(x) lies much closer tox than to that neighbor,
yet farther than five conservative full80 ulps fromx; every binary64 value is
outside the required full80 error bound. Converting any binary64 libm result
back to full80 cannot repair this. The input itself is exactly representable in
binary64, so the argument does not depend on input-conversion error or a measured
libm result. The tiny66-bit-pi argument-reduction issue is irrelevant here.
This disproves accepting the unchanged path, not the use of TCG as generator.

**Existing inherited replacement candidate.** Bochs upstream at commit
`c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f` has
[`bochs/cpu/fpu/fsincos.cc`](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/fpu/fsincos.cc)
and [`fpu_trans.cc`](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/fpu/fpu_trans.cc).
The former identifies its provenance as a SoftFloat extension written for Bochs;
its interface accepts full80 and a software status object. Inspected reduction
and polynomial paths use extended/integer/float128 operations and existing
67-bit intermediate helpers, then round back to extended format, without the
binary64 libm bottleneck. The wrapper stages a local full80 result, handles its
out-of-range return separately, and consults `FPU_exception` before publishing.
These are useful existing component and effect seams, not a proposal to write
new transcendental mathematics.

Retained files and direct URLs/hashes are in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-fp48/sources.json`; initial sandbox DNS failure and
successful approved read-only fetch are recorded there. No downloaded code ran.
This bounded read identifies a credible replacement candidate, **not** proof of
its full error bound or compatibility. Its comments about physical hardware
matching are author assertions, not our observations. The dependent constants,
67-bit helpers, status semantics and numerical error argument still need a single
bounded component review. Preserve its source/provenance notices if selected.
Do not silently import its entire emulator or let two runtimes own the guest
FPU. Select the numerical core behind the same resident effect owner; TCG remains
a separate code-generation choice.

**Legacy14-byte metadata disposition.** The short primary follow-up reread Intel
Vol1 §8.1.8/Figure8-11 and FLDENV/FRSTOR. It still provides no represented FOP
field or explicit preserve-versus-clear rule for that protected16-bit image.
AMD's official instruction descriptions likewise refer to mode-dependent images;
they do not establish the selected Intel-vendor rule. An attempted AMD Vol2
content fetch returned404; no claim is based on an unavailable body. This pass
does not transform missing wording into permission to invent a value. The
specific metadata binding remains unresolved, with the existing owner able to
express either supported outcome. Do not schedule another generic search loop
or make it a generator-choice blocker. Resolve it when binding the exact legacy
adapter, separately from the proved numerical incompatibility above.

**Decision boundary.** One staged owner plus inherited numerical engines remains
a coherent architecture; unchanged QEMU helpers are not sufficient for the full
required FP behavior. The binary64 trig path is a demonstrated component blocker
that requires replacement, not a compatibility waiver. Proper scaled unmasked
x87 results are a required numerical API capability; mere flags after saturation
are not sufficient evidence of it. Helper metadata/mask fixes, cache publication,
per-lane staging and supported instruction-specific flag tables are implementation
conformance against this owner, rather than reasons to demand a probe per opcode.

**Next bounded action:** independent review of48, then one component-level source
pass on the inherited Bochs full80 transcendental dependencies and exception-result
interface, deciding whether that existing core can replace the incompatible
QEMU path under the owner. No implementation, runtime or full-opcode audit is
automatically authorized by this finding.

## Inherited full80 trigonometry — component and resident ABI choice

**Method:** memory, September20, bounded source-only follow-on to48. Inspect
Bochs pin`c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f` numerical core, constants,
67-bit helpers, software-status declarations, extended rounding/packing and CPU
exception wrapper. No build, numeric experiment or new arithmetic implementation.

### CLM-03-02-49 — Select the inherited Bochs full80 core behind a field-mapped status bridge

**Proposed component choice with explicit numerical-conformance premise; root
review pending.** Replace the incompatible binary64 FSIN/FCOS/FSINCOS/FPTAN
numerical paths identified in48 with the existing pinned Bochs numerical core and
its required modified SoftFloat dependencies. Keep TCG generation and the resident
architectural owner. Do not import Bochs CPU execution, memory access, global
register ownership or event delivery. This is a reversible inherited-component
choice under the documented compatibility premise below, not a mathematical
proof of all outputs or a claim about physical Haswell's last bit.

**Concrete dependency boundary.** `fsincos.cc` exposes full80 inputs/results plus
a caller-owned `softfloat_status_t`. It uses the immutable coefficient tables in
`fsincos_constants.h`, pi definitions in `fpu_constant.h`, `f128_67.cc`, integer
multiword helpers and Bochs's modified SoftFloat3e extended/float128 operations.
The67-bit functions call the explicit precision/rounding overloads of `f128_add`,
`f128_mul`, `f128_div` and `f128_round`; these are not an ABI-compatible drop-in
for arbitrary stock SoftFloat or QEMU's `float_status`. `fpu_trans.h` and
`softfloat-specialize.h` provide the existing declarations/specialization.
Preserve the pinned dependency set and notices, give it an isolated symbol/type
namespace, and expose only the narrow numerical call to the resident owner.
This avoids accidentally selecting QEMU's binary64 conversion or mixing two
libraries' identically named but differently configured functions.

[Core](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/fpu/fsincos.cc),
[67-bit helpers](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/fpu/f128_67.cc),
[status/API](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/softfloat3e/include/softfloat.h).
Nine additional source files, their exact URLs/lengths/hashes, are retained in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-fp49/sources.json`; the two core/wrapper files remain
in the48 retention. Downloaded code was read, never executed.

**Field-mapped bridge, not a struct cast.** Copy the raw64-bit significand and16-bit
sign/exponent between the existing owner and the numerical type explicitly;
ignore host struct padding. Initialize a fresh status for each invocation:
guest FCW RC maps to nearest-even/down/up/toward-zero, exception masks map
by their six semantic classes, suppression starts clear, DAZ and FTZ are false
for these x87 operations, and extended destination precision is80. Newly raised
flags start clear, independent of prior guest sticky status. The current Bochs
status has explicit rounding mode, masks, suppression, DAZ, FTZ, precision and
exception fields; it is not global host fenv state. Map returned invalid,
denormal, divide-by-zero, overflow, underflow, inexact and the distinct C1
round-up indication by name/meaning, not by assuming all libraries share bit
encodings. The returned numeric result and flags are candidates for the48 owner,
not permission for the numerical core to mutate architectural state.

For these transcendental instructions,80-bit destination precision is deliberate:
Intel's trigonometric accuracy contract is full80 and is not the FCW basic-
arithmetic24/53-bit precision choice. Guest rounding direction still applies.
The Bochs wrapper explicitly ORs its80-bit precision selection before invoking
the trig core. The core uses fixed intermediate67/64-bit rules where its algorithm
requires them and guest rounding for final reconstruction. Do not replace those
internal modes with guest FCW precision at every intermediate step, or reduce
intermediate operations to native double for speed.

**Argument range and selected reduction.** Retain the default66-significant-bit
pi constant: `BETTER_THAN_PENTIUM` is off and no alternate `SC_PI2_HI/LO` override
is supplied. The alternate wider constant is not automatically more compatible
with the selected Intel reduction behavior. `fsincos` classifies unsupported
extended encodings, NaNs/infinities, zero and denormals before finite reduction.
Finite `expDiff >= 63` returns the out-of-range indicator without publishing a
new result. The resident wrapper sets the defined C2 outcome and preserves
register/TOP state. For the accepted finite range it reduces using integer
multiword arithmetic and a fixed192-step remainder loop, folds to the reduced
interval, and evaluates the selected existing reconstruction. Thus no guest
input can request an unbounded host math loop or a guest-memory callback.

The core distinguishes standalone FSIN/FCOS from the two-result FSINCOS path;
FPTAN uses its own quotient path. Keep these entry identities. Source comments
assert microcode-like behavior, but those assertions do not establish a physical
Haswell trace. Full80 operands and full80-capable intermediates remove the
*proven representation bottleneck* in48; they alone do not prove an error bound.
[Constants](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/fpu/fpu_constant.h),
[Intel Vol1 §§8.3.8/8.3.10](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/671436/253665-sdm-vol-1.pdf).

**Exception-result support actually exists.** The retained
`s_roundPackToExtF80.cc` inspects the caller's exception masks. Its unmasked
underflow/overflow paths respectively add/subtract0x6000 from the exponent
before packing; masked paths use the normal gradual/overflow behavior. The
Bochs `FPU_exception` wrapper distinguishes suppressing a memory result from
publishing the adjusted register result, and does not suppress a result merely
because inexact is unmasked. These are concrete inherited capabilities answering
48's objection to an API that only returns saturated data and a flag.
Reuse the numerical capability; the captive owner applies its own accepted
pending-state, instruction-pointer, C1, register/pop and delivery contract.
Do not call Bochs's CPU-bound wrapper with fabricated CPU state.
[Extended round/pack](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/softfloat3e/s_roundPackToExtF80.cc),
[exception wrapper](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/fpu/ferr.cc).

**Resident call shape and lifetime.** Before entering the numerical core, the
owner completes pending-exception and x87 stack checks and snapshots inputs.
The call consumes private full80/status values and immutable resident constants,
returns at most two full80 candidates, new status and a range disposition, and
performs no guest access or host RPC. Push/pop and result publication happen
only in the owner's success/exception disposition. A native pause pins the
helper continuation and its scratch/constant backing under the existing helper
ABI; it is not a newly committed x87 instruction. There is no device completion
ledger inside this pure numerical core. The compiler's actual frame/dependency
footprint still must obey the existing resident-capacity contract, as ordinary
implementation conformance. No new thread, CPU state authority or runtime service
is required to make this source interface fit the architecture.

**Compatibility premise and limits.** Select this inherited component on the
explicit premise that its reduced-argument errors and monotonicity meet Intel's
stated bounds, its selected66-bit reduction/range behavior is compatible, and
its status/default-result behavior satisfies the48 owner inputs. The inspected
sources provide a concrete precision-preserving algorithm and required mask/
rounding/result interfaces, not a machine-checkable error certificate. We have
not found a counterexample in this bounded pass, but have not proved those
numerical properties. This premise must remain visible in the component decision;
source comments, source size and a handful of passing bit examples cannot turn
it into a measured or universal result. If a counterexample violates the required
bounds, replace/correct the inherited numerical component under its provenance;
do not reduce guest precision, silently waive the bound, or handwrite a new FP
algorithm. No such counterexample requires abandoning TCG generation itself.

The decision deliberately accepts an inherited numerical-conformance premise
instead of demanding a new mathematical proof for every opcode before selecting
a component. A component-level numerical validation/error analysis is later
required before claiming implementation correctness; it is not a new mandatory
native-oracle gate in this source pass. Broader F2XM1/FPATAN/FYL2X behavior and
other QEMU numerical helpers remain separate from this four-entry replacement;
no unexplored helper inherits acceptance from its directory name.

**Next bounded action:** independent review of49's component/ABI selection and
explicit premise. After review, reconcile the shared FP owner and numerical
component choices into the semantic selection inventory; name any remaining
architecture-changing gap before authorizing more source work. Do not repeat
trigonometric point fixtures or extend to every opcode by default.

## Resident event-delivery and return continuations

**Method:** machine-authored bounded30-minute primary/source/interface pass,
September20, after root's grouped precise-event request; root reviews independently.
Read accepted CPU/profile, recovery/effect-owner and question8 contracts, Intel
Vol3A092 §§7.5–7.7,7.10–7.14, event8 and10/§10.3, Intel Vol2A082 INT/IRET, and
pinned QEMU `seg_helper.c`/`excp_helper.c`. No downloads, native execution, mock or
implementation. Exact four input hashes are in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-event-delivery-01/sources.json`; original Intel source
URLs/provenance remain in the retained split-cmpxchg source manifest.

### CLM-03-02-50 — Add delivery/return phase ownership and correct NMI, nested-fault and local-shutdown adaptation

**Scoped source corrections and conditional grouped interface accepted by root
Return40, including the continuation below.** Claim46's QEMU semantic provenance does not select upstream
`cpu_loop_exit_restore`, its host exception loop or its whole-machine reset policy.
Reuse the descriptor/gate/stack algorithms behind the resident gateway, with the
specific corrections below. This covers the selected non-CET/non-FRED/non-VMX/SVM
profile; unsupported extension branches are not accidentally activated by copying
a larger upstream helper.

**One resident delivery record.** Once accepted8's event selector admits an event,
materialize the interrupted guest roots and give the resident CPU owner a bounded
record containing event kind (not merely vector), original/following RIP where
applicable, error-code presence/value, external provenance, old semantic context,
current delivery phase, captured descriptor/TSS values, stack cursor and completed
access/result identities. Fault/trap/software interrupt/INT1/NMI/external IRQ are
separate kinds. INT1 cannot acquire software-INT gate-DPL checks merely because
its vector is1. RF in the saved image follows the fault/event rule; it is not
copied from a native fault frame or universally cleared. Descriptor-related error
codes preserve EXT/IDT/TI, while #PF keeps its own error layout and CR2 update.
[Intel INT operation and EXT definition](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf)
pp3-519–531 and [Vol3A §§7.5,7.12–7.13](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf)
are independent semantic sources.

The IRQ/PIC acknowledgement or LAPIC IRR→ISR transition already admitted by8
occurs **once**. A malformed IDT leading to a delivery fault does not replay IACK,
put the same request back into IRR or undo ISR/TMR. NMI blocking is set before
its IDT delivery, including when that delivery fails. Other arrivals continue in
the accepted bounded pending state while the delivery record is active; they do
not recursively enter a C helper or cause old owner state to be restored.

| Phase | Owned work and recovery boundary |
| --- | --- |
| Gate | Read IDT fields through typed guest system accesses, checking IDTR bounds/type/presence and event-kind gate DPL; do not form unchecked native pointers from IDTR |
| Target | Read GDT/LDT code descriptor; check selector, type, present, privilege, L/D constraints and target offset; capture the permitted hidden-state candidate |
| Stack | For privilege/IST switching read only the required TSS fields, with TSS limit/canonical checks; select the correct access privilege, width, alignment and old-stack values |
| Frame | Apply the architecturally ordered stack-limit/canonical/target checks, then frame memory operations with explicit cursor and completed-effects state; no repeat of a completed write/read on internal retry |
| Enter | Publish the permitted CS/SS/RIP/RSP/flags/CPL tuple, choose matching shadow/code context and admit the handler through the normal external-entry lifetime contract |
| Task switch, when applicable | A distinct precommit/new-task phase, not an ordinary gate's all-register rollback; preserve descriptor busy/backlink/TSS writes and the task-context commit rule below |

These are explicit native **semantic continuations**, not a saved host stack or
longjmp target. A gateway access returns `RetryAccess`, `AccessCompleted(value)`
or `GuestFault(details)` to the same delivery phase. Retry restarts only its
unperformed access; completed results advance its cursor. A guest fault invokes
the pairing rule below using the phase's defined architectural context. UC/device
reads and writes, A/D updates and descriptor accessed/busy writes use the existing
memory/effect owner; its service may borrow the coordinator's drain authority
without acquiring a second held epoch. Completed effects and pending events do
not roll back because a later descriptor check fails. Code/PTE aliases remain
tracked writes, so the next actual instruction is admitted against current code
identity. Descriptor values already consumed by this single delivery have their
phase lifetime; no reusable shadow is published from a stale dependency merely
because it was usable for one access.

**Specific source corrections.** At
[pinned `seg_helper.c`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/seg_helper.c),
`do_interrupt64` derives target `offset`, pushes the frame, then assigns it to EIP;
the inspected function has no canonical-target check before those pushes.
Intel's intra/inter-privilege INT paths require that check before frame writes.
Add it to the adapted Target/Stack ordering, with #GP(EXT); a later instruction
fetch fault at the invalid target is not equivalent. The protected helper's
stack-room check is disabled under `#if 0`; select the actual Intel stack-limit
checks instead of importing this omission. Its direct IDT/selector errors also
lack an EXT parameter: compute those errors from the retained event provenance,
not only `intno*8+2` or `selector&~3`. This is a scoped inspected-path discrepancy,
not a claim that every upstream call path or every QEMU version behaves alike.

For long-mode successful delivery retain mandatory SS:RSP saving,16-byte stack
alignment and64-bit frame slots, including same-privilege delivery. Interrupt
gates clear IF; trap gates retain it. TF/RF/NT and the saved versus live flags
remain separate. Do not copy virtual-8086/CET bookkeeping into the selected
ordinary long-mode case. Protected startup-mode delivery retains its own gate
width/segment checks rather than treating protected32 as long-mode operand-size
variation.

**Pairing is delivery-local, not vector-only.** Use the current delivery kind and
[Intel Vol3A Tables7-4/7-5](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf):

| First delivery | Second benign | Second contributory | Second #PF |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Benign/interrupt | Serial handling | Serial handling | Serial handling |
| Contributory (#DE/#TS/#NP/#SS/#GP in this profile) | Serial handling | #DF(0) | Serial handling |
| #PF | Serial handling | #DF(0) | #DF(0) |
| #DF | Serial handling | Local shutdown | Local shutdown |

“Serial” means dispatch the new exception under the specified delivery context;
it is not a queue that later blindly retries the discarded original event.
The active pairing ancestry ends when delivery transfers control to a handler;
a fault in that handler's subsequent instruction is not still nested delivery
of the original exception. In particular, distinguish fetching its first
instruction from the gate/stack transfer itself: Intel event8 explicitly places
instruction-prefetch segment/page faults outside Table7-5, though a later fault
while delivering that new exception can again pair. A software `INT 8` is a benign software interrupt,
not an abort-class #DF solely because vector8 was named. CR2 records a new
#PF during delivery even when the pairing escalates to #DF; #DF error code is0
and its saved CS:RIP/program state is architecturally undefined, not a promise
that retrying the original instruction succeeds.

The pinned
[`check_exception`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/excp_helper.c)
contains useful contributory/#PF classification but treats any new exception
while `old_exception==#DF` as a system-reset request. Replace that branch with the
table's benign-versus-shutdown distinction and a resident **CpuShutdown**
disposition. Do not copy the QEMU policy as mandatory architectural reset.
No unbounded C recursion is required: replace the active delivery record while
retaining the finite pairing class, surviving effects and current CPU context.
A repeated benign-fault pattern is guest execution/progress failure; it still
passes native management/coordination safe points rather than consuming an
unbounded retained history.

**Local shutdown and machine lifecycle.** Intel event8 distinguishes shutdown
from HLT and lists NMI, SMI, hardware reset and INIT wake events. In ordinary
shutdown, preserve the shutdown reason and native management responsiveness;
only an allowed wake class proceeds through the accepted event/reset rules.
Maskable IRQ is not sufficient. If shutdown occurred while executing an NMI
handler or in SMM, Intel requires hardware reset; do not let INIT or NMI escape
that variant. The immutable66 SMM service does not intentionally fault, but a
local state cannot silently become a successful whole-VM reboot. Other vCPUs and
device time keep running under the declared active-time policy. The supervisor
may explicitly terminate or perform69's whole-machine cold reconstruction; that
is a platform action with new lifetime and drain rules, not a forged instruction
completion. An AP in shutdown does not return to cold mailbox-wait or regain its
version0 wake allowance; only the appropriate reset/lifecycle transition changes
that state.

**Return is another operation, not a restore-all command.** IRET/IRETD/IRETQ
has its own phase record for frame reads, target/stack descriptor checks, privilege
and flag masks, and successful mode/shadow publication. It does not pop an
exception error code automatically. Preserve consumed device-read results on
native retry, faults at the IRET instruction's defined context, and the semantic
banks that IRET does not restore. Successful long-mode return handles its
SS:RSP fields even at the same privilege; canonicality/segment checks happen
before admitting the target, not as an accidental native fault later. The
[Intel IRET definition](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf)
pp3-539–547 supplies the mode-specific checks and privilege-dependent RFLAGS mask.

One effect is intentionally **not rollbackable with a failed IRET**: NMI becomes
unblocked when executing IRET, before any resulting guest exception is delivered
(Vol3A §7.7.2 and Vol2A p3-540). In the inspected QEMU
`helper_iret_protected`, `HF2_NMI_MASK` is cleared only after
`helper_ret_protected`/task return succeeds. Move this effect into the resident
IRET operation's once-only entry phase so faulting checks retain it. This is not
a requirement to unblock on an instruction-fetch/decode failure before IRET
executes, and it does not permit an asynchronous event halfway through an
unfinished noninterruptible semantic return. Clear pairing ancestry appropriately;
an IRET fault is a new instruction fault, not a retry of its handler's original
external IRQ. Normal event selection observes the resulting pending/block state.

**Protected task-gate boundary.** Reuse `switch_tss_ra`'s explicit old/new-task
split only after binding it to Intel §10.3/exception10. TSS/descriptor writes
are tracked guest writes, and paging/access checks use the relevant old/new CR3
phase. Precommit failures preserve the old execution context; postcommit failures
must finish the specified new-task state load without additional segment checks
and deliver there. Intel separately permits corrupt state on an unrecoverable
step9 save error; do not claim that all such memory failures have ordinary
instruction rollback. QEMU's `X86Access` preprobe/raw-access machinery is not
portable native pointer authority: adapt its admitted ranges to the same typed
memory/effect owner and preserve the task commit marker. Task gates are not
silently converted into #UD to avoid this startup-mode obligation. General real
mode/virtual8086 remain outside the previously accepted profile boundary.

**Exact remaining seam, not blanket helper uncertainty.** The protocol above
represents the necessary continuation kinds and resolves the four concrete
upstream corrections. It does **not yet select a permitted partial-frame fault
construction** for a privilege-changing IDT delivery whose earlier stack stores
complete but a later stack page faults (including stack/descriptor aliases and
UC/device effects). QEMU stages SS:RSP/CS changes until after its pushes, whereas
Intel's INT pseudocode assigns some target/stack state before the pushes; treating
that pseudocode as literal externally visible commit order without the fault
rules is not justified. Likewise a preprobe-all-pages/no-prefix construction
cannot be assumed valid for every effectful route merely because it simplifies
recovery. The missing result is the architectural context used to deliver that
second fault and the permitted surviving frame prefix—not the absence of an
implementation or a need to mock the helper. Task-switch commit has an explicit
primary rule above and should not be reopened by this separate ordinary-gate gap.

**Next bounded action:** independent review of these scoped source corrections
and interface, then one targeted primary-source selection for that partial-frame
case (ordinary RAM first, explicitly separating device effects) with one concrete
legal construction or a precise remaining ambiguity. No opcode survey or native
execution is implied. Question5 remains open on that named seam and existing
instruction-specific recovery owners; this finding does not claim whole
architecture acceptance.

**Claim49 scope clarification — ordinary x87 rebias already exists in QEMU.**
At root review's request, a bounded existing-API check resolves whether the
four-entry Bochs selection leaves48's ordinary register overflow/underflow
requirement with no numerical implementation. It does not: the pinned QEMU
`include/fpu/softfloat-types.h` declares `rebias_overflow` and `rebias_underflow`
in `float_status`. In `fpu/softfloat-parts.c.inc`, `partsN(uncanon_normal)`
subtracts/adds `fmt->exp_re_bias` when those controls are set, while preserving
the corresponding exception flags. `FLOAT_PARAMS_(E)` in `softfloat.c` defines
the bias as2^(E-1)+2^(E-2); the15-bit exponent format therefore supplies24576.
`floatx80_round_pack_canonical` selects the x80 format for full or reduced FCW
precision and routes through this machinery. The inspected `floatx80_add`,
`floatx80_sub`, `floatx80_mul` and `floatx80_div` reach that packer after their
existing extended arithmetic. This is actual source support for an inherited
adjusted result, not a proposal to recover a value after it saturated.
[Status controls](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/include/fpu/softfloat-types.h),
[rounding/rebias](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/fpu/softfloat-parts.c.inc),
[format and ordinary arithmetic paths](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/fpu/softfloat.c).

Select a per-operation QEMU status copy for these ordinary x87 register-result
operations. Bind rebias_overflow from an unmasked FCW overflow exception and
rebias_underflow from an unmasked FCW underflow exception; otherwise clear them.
Retain the separately selected RC/PC and x87 NaN settings. The current i386 helper
configuration does not set those controls, so adopting its current status object
unchanged would miss this requirement. The48 owner still handles exception
priority, pending state and whether this candidate register result commits.
For conversions destined to memory, keep ordinary numerical conversion behavior
and suppress the memory store/pop when the architectural exception requires it;
do not enable register-style rebias merely because the source was full80.

Accordingly, the selected boundary remains **QEMU/SoftFloat ordinary arithmetic
and conversions plus the Bochs four-entry trigonometric core**, with one semantic
effect owner. The demonstrated ordinary rebias capability does not require
migrating all arithmetic to Bochs. It also does not certify every status bit,
conversion, special operation or extreme FSCALE exponent: the latter's second
out-of-range disposition and other instruction-specific effects remain required
bindings, not inferred from a single ordinary rebias. Broad numerical/status
conformance and the49 Bochs error premise are not proved by this API check.
Question2's whole closure therefore still cannot be inferred solely from the
presence of suitable APIs; neither48's arithmetic requirements nor49's component
scope have been silently weakened.

### Claim50 continuation — choose staged transfer state with surviving ordered frame effects

**Method and status:** machine, one targeted source-only continuation requested by
root after50, bounded30minutes; scoped refinement accepted by root Return40.
Read the already retained Intel3C081 §28.1 pp28-1–2 and §27.6.1, alongside the
ordinary Intel3A092 #PF state rule and Intel2A082 INT ordered pushes. Search found
no separate ordinary-stack rollback mandate; secondary search results are not
used as evidence. No source/runtime implementation or native execution.

**The missing explicit hardware boundary.**
[Intel3C081 §28.1](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/789585/326019-sdm-vol-3c.pdf)
defines an event causing a VM exit *indirectly* to include delivery encountering a
nested exception. Before the exit commences, the event's debug/CR2 updates, NMI
blocking and interrupt acknowledgement have occurred, but the delivery's
CS/RIP/SS/RSP/RFLAGS have not changed; incomplete delivery may nevertheless have
written the stack. These are architectural state **before** VM-exit processing,
not the host registers subsequently loaded by an exit. Directly intercepted
exceptions have different rules, including no CR2 update; do not import those
into ordinary guest #PF handling.

This supplies a hardware-observable existence argument for a **staged transfer
tuple plus surviving frame writes**, not a blanket equation of all VMX and bare
processor behavior. The selected virtual CPU need not expose VMX to model that
ordinary delivery transaction boundary. Independently, the ordinary
[Intel3A #PF Program State Change](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf)
p7-50 says normal page faults preserve program state and separately identifies
task-switch commit exceptions; the
[INT operation](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf)
pp3-523–528 orders the frame pushes. **Inference/choice:** use that stated
pre-transfer CPU tuple, the architecturally ordered completed push prefix, and the
normal #PF/pairing effects as one permitted implementation of the selected
ordinary gate path. Do not treat intermediate assignments in explanatory INT
pseudocode as a requirement to expose a half-switched register tuple. This
resolves50's identified ordinary-gate seam without asserting that all physical
processors must expose an identical write prefix or importing guest VMX machinery.

**Concrete selected execution.** Keep the old CS/RIP/SS/RSP/RFLAGS/CPL and hidden
segment state authoritative while ordinary gate delivery runs; compute the new
transfer tuple separately. Retain existing gate/TSS/stack-limit/canonicality
check order. Then, in frame order, admit and perform each push using the candidate
stack address/access privilege, not the still-old CPL's ordinary data-access
rights. Do not preprobe all future frame pages. After a push completes, its guest
memory effects and A/D/descriptor effects are committed through the existing
all-writer protocol and its continuation advances. A later guest #PF:

1. Keeps that completed memory prefix; it performs no compensating writes and
   does not restore an old RAM snapshot.
2. Discards the uncommitted target tuple and abandons this delivery attempt.
   Start the new exception/pairing operation from the original execution tuple,
   with the correct original-instruction boundary. In particular a fault during
   software INT delivery reports the INT instruction, not its prospective
   successful next-RIP frame value. Intel3C §27.6.1 also explicitly distinguishes
   that nested-fault RIP from the successful software-injection return RIP.
3. Updates CR2 to the actual failing stack linear address and derives #PF error
   bits from that failing system/stack access. Pair it with the prior delivery
   **kind**, possibly producing #DF(0), with50's latest-CR2 rule retained.
4. Preserves separately owned debug/event effects: acknowledged IRQ/ISR state,
   NMI blocking and unrelated arrivals/time are not restored from the tuple.
   A second delivery performs its own descriptor/stack reads against current
   memory; it cannot reuse an abandoned delivery's stale descriptor snapshot.

Once all required frame operations and checks succeed, publish the candidate
transfer tuple in one semantic commit and end the delivery ancestry before
admitting/fetching the handler's first actual instruction. Internal native repair
is different: it retains the phase and resumes only the unperformed access,
without abandoning or replaying the completed prefix. An actual guest #PF handler
return does **not** resume an invisible half-completed old delivery. Reexecution
of a faulting software INT is a new architectural instruction attempt; a consumed
external interrupt is not automatically reissued. This agrees with Intel3A's
IDT warning that interrupt delivery occurs once and a bad IDT may leave handling
incomplete.

**Worked RAM boundary.** Let a valid long-mode external IRQ select a CPL0 stack
whose aligned initial RSP is0x2010. The page at0x2000 is writable; the preceding
page is not present; target descriptors/canonicality are otherwise valid. The
selected ordered pushes store old SS at0x2008 and old RSP at0x2000. The RFLAGS
push at0x1ff8 faults before storing. Preserve those two writes, keep the original
CPU tuple, set CR2=0x1ff8 and deliver #PF (external IRQ is benign for pairing).
A valid independent #PF IST can receive its frame from that original tuple.
If #PF's own delivery instead faults in a contributory/#PF way, select #DF; if
#DF delivery then hits that class, select local shutdown. No old IRQ ack, first
two writes or NMI state is undone by any of those transitions. An already
permitted #DF undefined saved-state outcome is not used to justify incorrect
ordinary IRQ→#PF state before it.

**RAM versus effectful routes.** The above ordinary RAM trace chooses one
allowed prefix; it does not promise that every fault leaves exactly two stores.
For the virtual machine's supported UC/device routes, use the **same ordered
access interface** already selected for guest stores: each accepted frame beat
can return one retained completion or a guest access fault; completed device
transactions cannot be undone or repeated on native retry. No future-page
permission probe is moved ahead of an earlier effectful beat. A later guest #PF
therefore preserves earlier completed device effects just as it preserves the
RAM prefix. A backend failure with uncertain external effects remains contained
failure under the existing backend contract, not a fabricated #PF or an atomic
rollback. This is composition with this virtual machine's specified MMIO width/
side-effect contract, not a claim that arbitrary physical devices make interrupt
stacks safe. Invalid/unimplemented device register accesses retain the selected
device behavior; do not create an extra CPU rejection of all non-RAM stacks.

Frame writes that alias descriptors, page tables or executable bytes likewise
retain their real effects. Fields already legally consumed by this attempt can
remain its private values; later unperformed accesses resolve under the admitted
route/walk contract. A new fault-delivery attempt rereads its own inputs. Reusable
shadows/blocks must still pass generation validation, and current code is admitted
after the eventual transfer; no global memory rollback or stale-code exception is
introduced to simplify delivery.

Only a bounded cursor, current access/result and candidate tuple are needed
(maximum six ordinary64-bit frame slots for this profile). Completed writes live
in guest memory, not an ever-growing undo/history log. Multi-beat physical access
and UC splitting retain the existing typed access phase; this count does not
misrepresent one logical push as necessarily one native/store transaction.
Task gates keep50's distinct old/new-task commit rule and are expressly not
covered by this ordinary-gate rollback construction.

**Updated disposition:** root Return40 accepts the named ordinary-gate
later-stack-#PF context/prefix choice and its scoped primary-support/refinement
argument. No runtime equivalence or whole question5 acceptance is claimed. The
next debug retirement increment is51 below; no generic all-helper audit or
preprobe-all-frame gate is added.

## MOV-SS / ICEBP retirement refinement

**Method:** machine, bounded30-minute source/interface pass requested after50;
root reviews independently. Re-read accepted18/19 and oracle qualification, Intel
Vol3A092 §7.8.3/event1, retained Vol3B079 §§18.2.3/18.3.1, Vol2A082 INT1 and
pinned TCG producer/debug helpers. No implementation or native debug test. A
narrow primary-author mailing-list search was supplementary, not hardware evidence.

### CLM-03-02-51 — Select a single retirement-owned ICEBP/debug request, with explicit fusion inference

**Root Return41 accepts the bounded interface only; the combined DR6 rule
remains provisional.** This reconciles the exact19 stopping sequence with claim46's selected TCG/resident
boundary. It does not turn the inherited QEMU TODO or an unexecuted test into
proof of the combined DR6 rule. Whole question5 still depends on its other
instruction/effect-recovery owners.

**Exact scope:** a successful `mov ss,[valid_selector]` whose operand read hits
an enabled data breakpoint, immediately followed by valid one-byte `INT1`
(`ICEBP`,F1), with TF=0, no intervening higher-priority event, no earlier fault
and a valid #DB delivery path. Keep SS-load instruction-breakpoint suppression
and single-step suppression distinct. Do not insert an SP write to make the
case easier: it would replace the immediately following instruction. TF=1 and
multiple independent simultaneous debug causes still use the existing debug
cause rules; this specific inference is not a new universal BS-bit theorem.

The selected rule is **one #DB trap request at the completed ICEBP boundary**,
carrying both the privileged-software origin and the retained data-match cause.
Its successful saved RIP is after ICEBP, with no error code, no software-INT
DPL check and no fault-class RF synthesis. Keep the ICEBP instruction-start RIP
separately for faults during its delivery, as50 requires. Update the enabled
B0–B3 causes from the retained data match; INT1 itself contributes no DR6 status
bit. Do not add a synthetic BS cause when TF=0. INT1 without a pending data cause
continues to leave DR6 unchanged. Other sticky/control/reserved bits follow the
already selected cause-specific DR6 policy, not a whole-register zero operation.

**Why this is the proposed composition, and what is inferred.**
[Intel3A092 §7.8.3 and event1](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf)
delays an SS-load data breakpoint until completion of the next instruction and
defines #DB as reporting one or more debug conditions. INT1 is itself a trap;
[Intel2A082 INT](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf)
says that it generates #DB without contributing DR6 bits. The
[debug-status/cause rules](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/774491/253669-sdm-vol-3b.pdf)
associate enabled B bits with matched data conditions at #DB generation, while
an exception delivery discards still-pending data causes. The refinement groups
the two eligible trap causes **before** delivering their single #DB: the data
cause contributes its status, the INT1 cause contributes no status, and delivery
consumes the pending record. Thus “INT1 adds no bits” is not interpreted as
“INT1 erases a simultaneously reported data cause,” and “pending causes are lost
on exception delivery” is not used to erase a cause already included in that
very event. No later second #DB is left queued at the handler entry.

This is a **compositional inference**, not an explicit SDM sentence naming this
exact two-instruction combination. Claim19's original uncertainty concerned
that missing combined statement; this submission proposes a concrete refinement
and argument rather than mislabeling it as a newly discovered normative quote.
It must be rejected if the independent review finds the separate INT1 retirement
must precede data-cause eligibility or requires different DR6 behavior. The
alternative “always discard before constructing INT1's #DB” is not justified
merely by calling INT1 an exception; equally, primary rules do not justify
preserving a second queued trap after it. A plain software `INT 1` remains a
different operation: claim19's successful software-transfer hook can produce
its deferred #DB at that handler-entry state.

**Resident state transition, independent of native block boundaries.**

1. On the SS load's successful architectural completion, capture the match mask,
   operation identity and one-following-instruction shadow. Do not manufacture a
   completed memory access or a second match on native repair. Pending cause
   storage is distinct from committed DR6 and from external IRQ masking.
2. Native exits, translation waits, helper access service and coordinator parks
   retain that record unchanged. No host watchpoint rewind is needed to learn the
   same guest data address again, and no TB boundary counts as the next guest
   instruction's retirement.
3. ICEBP's producer submits `RetireWithEvent(INT1,startRIP,nextRIP)` to the
   resident boundary. That boundary consumes this instruction's SS shadow,
   combines the eligible delayed data cause into the one event payload and
   updates DR6 through its semantic owner before gate delivery. The event keeps
   its instruction-origin distinction even though both causes use vector1.
4. Clear the pending record once that payload is owned by delivery. Delivery
   faults use50; they do not restore the pending mask or roll back a committed
   DR6 update. A new fault can prevent the #DB handler from being reached without
   entitling the runtime to replay ICEBP or the SS operand read internally.
5. If the following instruction instead faults before successful retirement,
   retain19's loss rule for the deferred data cause. If it is INT n/INT3/taken
   INTO, retain19's distinct successful-transfer hook. The fusion selection does
   not flatten those paths into a vector-number switch.

The record is fixed-size (mask, identity, shadow and disposition), with at most
one handoff to the existing delivery record. It needs no dynamically growing
queue, retained ordinary native stack or global memory transaction merely to
combine debug causes. A native pause after payload ownership resumes delivery;
a pause before ownership resumes retirement resolution, never both.

**Actual selected-source seam and cost.** Pinned
[`gen_INT1`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc)
materializes next-EIP before `helper_icebp`; that helper currently calls
`do_end_instruction`, creates a vector1 host-loop request and exits. Replace
that request boundary with the resident retirement call above, retaining both
start/next RIP rather than copying the host loop fields as the whole contract.
`gen_movl_seg`/`gen_eob` already encode SS-origin ending, while
[`breakpoint_handler`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/bpt_helper.c)
explicitly leaves delayed/fused debug handling unfinished. Reuse matching
semantics and producer facts; do not import immediate host watchpoint dispatch.
The source's single inhibit flag is not enough to merge SS-debug and STI-IRQ
semantics, so the resident event record supplies the distinction.

This is an adaptation of the selected candidate, not a demonstrated intrinsic
cost of all generators or a reason to disable guest debug. Matching/pending work
can follow debug-enabled paths/guards and the existing instruction-boundary
mechanism; this finding does not require unconditional full register spilling,
one-instruction translation blocks or host exits for every guest instruction.
Its real costs are the bounded record, cause checks on applicable paths and
recovery integration; their measured frequency/cost remains unestablished.

**Evidence limits retained.** The existing kvm-unit-tests case checks one
handler-entry/RIP pattern but not the combined DR6 bits. The previously inspected
EMR95 erratum still qualifies native-oracle interpretation. A newly located
[January7,2026 author patch](https://www.spinics.net/lists/kernel/msg5991439.html)
proposes merging pending VMX debug causes and reports combined hardware status;
that is a primary author's implementation proposal, not independently reproduced
hardware evidence or an accepted upstream correction here. It does not establish
our exact TF=0 case by itself. Retained Intel3C081 §27.7.3 mentions the INT1-after-
MOVSS scenario but its earlier vectoring exclusions and later subcase do not
supply an unambiguous standalone DR6 fusion proof; this proposal does not use
that text to overrule the ordinary debug contract. No physical erratum behavior
is selected as the ideal virtual-CPU rule.

**Disposition:** root Return41 accepts the bounded ownership/interface but
not the exact combined DR6 rule. The unresolved premise is inclusion of the
deferred data cause in INT1’s reported #DB before delivery discards pending
conditions. Intel3C §26.5.2’s MTF boundary after INT1 delivery and §27.7.3’s
ambiguous privileged-software clauses prevent assuming ordinary retirement
equivalence; they do not themselves refute fusion. Keep the candidate visible
without calling its semantics established. No new experiment or generic source
search is authorized. Whole question5 remains open; the inventory below
separates this semantic gap from implementation binding.

## Question2 decision synthesis — selected boundary and finite remaining gaps

**Method:** memory, September20; reconciliation of existing accepted evidence
only. No additional source survey, numeric evaluation, code or runtime.

### CLM-03-02-52 — Retain the selected combination without confusing inherited premises with adapter proof

**Synthesis accepted as a decision inventory in root Return42; no numerical proof or whole question2 closure.** The current planning
choice is TCG plus adapted QEMU semantic/frontend provenance (46), with QEMU
SoftFloat ordinary arithmetic/conversions, the47 binary SSE/AVX NaN correction,
per-operation ordinary x87 rebias, and the49 inherited Bochs four-entry full80
trigonometric replacement. The resident state/effect owner remains singular.
This is an actual component policy, not an undecided comparison of three whole
emulators; it is also not a claim that the chosen unmodified sources implement
the captive runtime correctly.

| Kind of remaining issue | Current evidence and consequence |
|---|---|
|Architecture-changing incompatibility already found|48 proves that the old QEMU binary64 trig result cannot meet full80 accuracy.49 binds a precision-capable inherited replacement; the incompatible old path is excluded from the chosen implementation.47 similarly binds the known binary-NaN correction. These findings no longer require leaving the generator choice open.|
|Ordinary inherited numerical-correctness premise|The selected Bochs core's actual error/monotonicity/status bounds and remaining inherited numerical algorithms are not universally proved here. This is a visible correctness premise of reuse, not an observation or a reason to require a new arithmetic proof for every opcode before choosing components. A real violation reopens the responsible numerical component, not automatically TCG.|
|Concrete capability bindings still unresolved|48–49 do not bind extreme FSCALE's second out-of-range disposition or establish the ordinary x87 C1/round-up result source across affected paths. The owner can express those outcomes, but a type capable of holding them is not evidence that every selected primitive supplies them. These are finite numerical-adapter capability gaps, not demonstrated failures of every inherited operation.|
|Implementation conformance|Apply already identified metadata, admission-permission, NaN/configuration, mask/reset and event-helper corrections; preserve operands, vector halves and live roots; implement the accepted checked-access, continuation and state-publication contracts. Unwritten code and the limited disposable producer do not by themselves negate the architecture choice.|
|Performance uncertainty|Canonical synchronization, exceptional coordinator service, pure numerical-call costs and dependency/state bridging are unmeasured. There is no evidence-derived speed or least-adaptation-cost ranking. A first implementation may reveal a material performance reason to revisit producer or numerical-component choice.|

**What the accepted contracts now cover.**43–45 bind FX, standard three-component
XSAVE and the grouped legacy state-image owner.48 binds normal, suppressed and
scaled FP result dispositions;49 identifies real rebias APIs rather than a promise
to reconstruct a value after saturation.36–37 bind resident entry and distinct
repair/completion/fault continuations.50, accepted in root Return40, adds grouped
event-delivery/return phases, including surviving frame effects and staged CPU
transfer state. Claim51's debug retirement composition is separately under root
review and is not self-accepted by this synthesis. These records replace vague
“preserve all semantics” instructions with actual owners, sequencing and recovery
contracts; they do not yet prove every helper/emitter follows them.

**Known unbound versus merely unproved.** The concrete FSCALE/C1 capability gaps
must not disappear into “test FP later.” Conversely, this synthesis has no source
evidence proving those paths impossible with the selected inherited primitives.
Their bounded resolution is to identify an existing numerical result/status API
and the owner's disposition for those two shared capabilities, or identify a
counterexample requiring another inherited primitive. It is not permission to
write new FP arithmetic. Protected14-byte FOP remains a narrowly identified
metadata rule with no current evidence that it needs a different component or
owner. Its unresolved preserve/clear value must remain in implementation planning;
claiming full bit-level conformance before resolving it would be false.

The Bochs accuracy premise is different: representation suitability and a
full80 interface remove the demonstrated binary64 impossibility, but do not prove
its numerical bounds. Keep that premise in the component acceptance and later
validation/error-analysis plan. Passing a few specially chosen points would not
