close it. The source pass should not repeatedly rediscover that any reused math
library has a correctness premise and use it to prolong architecture selection.

**Adaptations and alternatives.** The selected combination needs a resident
semantic entry/exit adapter, checked helper-memory access, operation-local FP
status/result staging, the named helper corrections and isolated Bochs numerical
dependencies. Direct normal guest accesses need no universal host call or complete
state spill. FEX/Box64 remain coherent alternatives with their own producer/state
adaptations;46 selected TCG by a reversible provenance/planning policy, not by
proving those alternatives worse. If FSCALE/C1 cannot be supplied by the current
numerical slice, an existing Bochs or other inherited primitive can replace that
slice without importing another CPU owner. Such a replacement requires its own
specific interface evidence; it is not presumed accepted now.

**Proposed decision.** Retain the scoped generator/component planning choice and
the accepted owner contracts. Do not grant whole question2 semantic closure just
because every required effect has a name, and do not condition that choice on a
full implementation or universal numerical proof. The remaining explicit
architecture-level check is whether the finite unresolved numerical capabilities
and helper/recovery compositions have a compatible inherited binding; actual
adapter verification and the disclosed numerical premise then remain correctness
work with distinct scope. No known incompatible path is intentionally retained
as a compatibility concession.

**Single next bounded action:** independent review of52, then one bounded existing-
API binding check for extreme FSCALE and ordinary x87 C1/round-up capability.
Stop at a concrete inherited binding or a counterexample requiring a component
change. Do not extend into all opcodes, new numeric code or runtime by default.

## Question5 recovery inventory after claims50–51

**Method/status:** machine-authored bounded editorial/design synthesis of existing
accepted evidence and current summaries only, at root's request. No new primary
source audit, code, execution or claim acceptance. Root independently reviews
this inventory. Its purpose is to identify finite architectural work, not turn
unwritten adapters into an indefinite semantic gate.

| Finite owner | Established interface/evidence | Remaining decision versus conformance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Guest execution state and recovery roots | INV03-01 accepted guarded-EL1/owner contracts;02-21/22/33/36–37 separate working roots, authoritative banks and live subsystem owners;46 selects TCG provenance | Actual allocated register/spill maps, canonical global synchronization and helper ABI bindings must conform. The partial FEX diagnostic is neither that proof nor a current architectural blocker for selected TCG. A discovered unrepresentable live value would reopen this owner; none is established by an unbuilt producer alone. |
| Native access fault, guest access result and operation lifetime |01 continuation integration plus02-14–17/37 and04 coordination distinguish RetryAccess, AccessCompleted and GuestFault; full native pause retains temporaries while guest faults use semantic roots | Binding every selected access site to its existing continuation is implementation work. No new success/retry kind is currently needed. A concrete path that can produce an external effect without its retained result would invalidate this interface and must be fixed at its producer. |
| Walk checks and fault side effects |04-13/14/41/42 bind staged walk results, A/D mutation, dependency mismatch/service-only use, width/type and guest fault information | Current accepted memory choice owns permissions, CR2/error data and prior walk effects. Re-inspecting each helper load does not establish a new Q5 decision. Actual access privilege or route-identity omissions are conformance defects against that choice. |
| Interrupt/gate/return and nested fault |02-50 plus accepted03-63/64/66/69 bind admitted event ownership, entry/return phases, ordinary frame prefix/staged tuple, pairing, local shutdown and lifecycle | The prior ordinary-stack-fault seam is accepted in root Return40. Task gates retain their explicit old/new-task commit boundary, not generic rollback. Implementing descriptor-field checks, the named QEMU corrections or the reset handoff is conformance, not a fresh architectural choice per descriptor. |
| Restartable compound guest operations |04-10/11 and02 REP findings establish iteration commit, internal retained decode versus actual interruption, restart RIP and no replay of completed effects;02-43–45 establish multi-beat state-image commit/effect rules | A **finite remaining composition gap** is the non-REP, non-state-image partial-destination family, notably AVX2 gather/masked multi-access and implicit multi-access stack operations. Existing records name these but do not yet bind one selected helper's actual partial register/mask/stack commits to the retained operation/root model. This could require an additional commit phase or temporary lifetime. It is not evidence that all such instructions fail or require new codegen, and does not justify an opcode catalogue. |
| FP result/status, deferred exception and image state |02-38/43–45/48–49 separate numerical result/status from destination/TOP/mask publication, pending x87 delivery and staged image restore | Extreme FSCALE and C1/result-source capabilities remain question2's concrete numerical binding gaps. If a primitive cannot provide the required result/status, that can change the semantic component, but the recovery owner already represents normal/suppressed/scaled effects. Protected14-byte FOP preserve/clear is a field rule within the same owner. Do not duplicate these as unexplained Q5 gaps. |

Other unresolved simultaneous-debug details are not automatically closed by that record. Resolve one coherent debug-boundary policy, with explicit cause timing, rather than claim a pending-bit container proves it. |

Requiring a native implementation or repeated mock for each use would not strengthen the architecture argument. A new wait cycle or reference class must be handled if a real compound-operation mapping exposes one. |

**What can still change the architecture.** The immediate Q5 questions are
therefore (1) the exact debug semantic boundary, and (2) whether the remaining
compound-operation family fits the current retained-progress/root/lifetime
construction without a missing phase. The numerical capability gaps remain
visible at their separate question2 owner. There is no current evidence that an
additional process, host exception loop or new code-generator family is required
for recovery. Conversely, this inventory does not treat the ability to invent a
phase name as proof that actual inherited partial effects fit it.

The compound-family pass should take one existing selected partial-destination
path and one implicit multi-access path, identify source-established committed
fields and unperformed phases, then either map both to the shared operation owner
or show the precise missing primitive. That tests the shared mechanism; it must
not expand into individual audits of every arithmetic opcode. Masked-off accesses
must remain absent, and existing memory atomicity contracts do not erase the
instruction's permitted partial progress. Its resulting conclusion can cover a
family only to the extent the shared lowering/owner argument actually applies.

**Conformance carried into planning.** Native recovery metadata, actual host/shim
calling conventions, complete selected-state field mapping, exception-code/flag
assignments and per-site checked-memory lowering remain real work. Their absence
is not by itself a correctness uncertainty requiring another architectural
selection. End-to-end validation must exercise them, and a discovered semantic
counterexample can reopen the affected choice. No statement here asserts that
the current disposable code implements those bindings or has passed that
validation.

**Next bounded action:** independent root review of this inventory; then, if
assigned, one grouped existing-source compound-progress mapping as described
above. Keep the DR6 fusion premise as a separately unresolved semantic item,
without another generic web search or any unreviewed native oracle. Whole
question5 is not marked complete by this synthesis.

## FSCALE and C1 — finite inherited-API binding

**Method:** memory, September20, the single bounded capability pass requested by52.
Inspect existing pinned QEMU FSCALE/status and Bochs scale/rounding/conversion
routines. No runtime, arithmetic implementation or broader opcode audit.

### CLM-03-02-53 — Bind FSCALE and rounded x87 results to existing Bochs status-capable primitives

**Scoped component-boundary refinement accepted in root Return43.** Extend49's
inherited numerical slice to `extF80_scale` and the existing Bochs rounded x87
arithmetic/conversion primitives that provide the required C1 result. Retain
QEMU semantic/frontend provenance, TCG generation, QEMU SSE/AVX numerical paths
with47, and the one resident effect owner. This deliberately changes the earlier
“QEMU ordinary x87 plus four Bochs trig entries” boundary; it does not pretend
that boundary already supplied all rounding status. Existing QEMU rebias remains
a valid capability, but need not force a duplicate numerical evaluation just to
recover status that another inherited primitive already returns.

**FSCALE binding.** The pinned QEMU `helper_fscale` converts the finite ST1 scale
to signed32-bit integer and passes it to `floatx80_scalbn`, suppressing flags from
that integer conversion. That source path is not a demonstrated implementation
of arbitrary finite scale magnitude and massive post-bias overflow/underflow.
Select Bochs `extF80_scale(a,b,status)` instead. It accepts both full80 inputs,
classifies unsupported values/NaNs/infinities/zeros and normalizes denormals before
finite scaling. Small scale magnitude truncates to zero; intermediate finite
magnitudes are truncated through the significand/exponent calculation, without
an out-of-range host integer conversion.

For finite scale with exponent field above0x400E, its magnitude is at least65536.
The routine uses a bounded signed scale of±0x10000. Every finite nonzero extended
input still overflows/underflows after the one24576 unmasked exponent adjustment
at that magnitude, so this replacement preserves the required terminal outcome
rather than the irrelevant enormous exact exponent. The already reviewed Bochs
round/pack applies the mask-dependent bias and then explicitly produces signed
infinity or signed zero if still out of range. Other finite scales go through
the same full80 round/pack with their actual truncated scale. This supplies the
massive FSCALE outcome required by48 without new FP mathematics or a guess made
after saturation. Architectural stack checks, exception priority, pending state
and publication remain the owner's responsibility; the numerical primitive does
not perform a pop or directly alter ST0.
[QEMU path](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/fpu_helper.c),
[Bochs scale](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/softfloat3e/extF80_scale.cc),
[Bochs extended packer](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/softfloat3e/s_roundPackToExtF80.cc).

**C1 capability actually returned.** QEMU's inspected `float_status` exposes
exception flags and rebias controls but no corresponding generic round-up result
bit. Bochs's status explicitly carries `RAISE_SW_C1`, with set/clear helpers.
Its extended round/pack sets the magnitude-round-up indication from the final
rounding decision and clears an earlier wider-stage hint where final narrowing
has its own fractional decision. The same capability exists in the binary32/64
conversion packers, signed32/64 integer-rounding routines and `extF80_roundToInt`.
The inspected extended addition/multiplication paths reach the extended packer;
`extF80_to_f32` reaches its binary32 packer. These are concrete available result/
status routes, not an inference that inexact alone determines C1.

Select those existing Bochs rounded x87 numerical primitives as the result/status
provider for basic rounded arithmetic and conversions: one candidate value and
its status from one inherited engine. Map guest FCW precision to the primitive's
24/53/64-significand selection where the instruction uses PC, and preserve the
instruction's own fixed-precision or truncation override elsewhere. FSCALE and
the selected trig paths use their existing full80 rule. Initialize fresh local
flags and C1 indication per operation; map the indication back only where the
instruction defines C1 as rounding information. Stack overflow/underflow, compare,
remainder and other meanings of C1 remain semantic-owner rules; an arbitrary
numerical round-up flag must not overwrite them. For memory conversions retain
48's suppression and no-pop rules before committing a store. This selects an
existing numerical capability, not all current wrappers or an unreviewed Bochs
CPU execution path.
[Status definition](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/softfloat3e/include/softfloat.h),
[binary32 rounding](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/softfloat3e/s_roundPackToF32.cc),
[integer rounding](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/softfloat3e/s_roundToI64.cc).
Nine bounded source files and exact hashes/URLs are retained in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-fp53/sources.json`; no source was compiled or run.

**Why not infer C1 by blanket comparison?** Comparing a normal rounded result
against a second toward-zero result can reveal magnitude rounding for a restricted
ordinary finite case. It is not a complete x87 C1 rule: a rounded overflow may
rebias one result while the toward-zero result stays in the original exponent
range, so raw magnitude comparison reverses the intended meaning. Exceptional
results and instruction-specific C1 definitions add further conditions. This
pass selects the already exposed rounding indication instead of inventing a
second evaluator plus an unproved normalization/exception reconstruction. The
cost is a somewhat wider inherited Bochs numerical dependency slice, rather
than two numerical evaluations or handwritten arithmetic; neither alternative's
performance is measured here.

**Disposition relative to52.** FSCALE's massive-outcome and ordinary rounding-
status *capabilities* now have concrete inherited bindings fitting49's per-call
bridge and48's owner. The exact adapter call/flag mapping still needs implementation
conformance. The Bochs numerical accuracy/monotonicity premise is unchanged and
unproved; suitable APIs remain distinct from proof that every result satisfies
Intel's bounds. Unaffected inherited x87 operations do not acquire blanket
acceptance from these sampled routes. Protected14-byte FOP remains its recorded
metadata-binding issue. None of these facts authorizes a precision reduction,
compatibility waiver, whole question2 closure or new numeric implementation.

**Next bounded action:** independently review53's explicit boundary refinement,
then update the component inventory and report the stable decision state. Do not
start another numerical source pass or per-opcode runtime campaign automatically.

**Current reconciliation after53:** root Return43 accepts the numerical capability
refinement; Return44 accepts the bounded question5 recovery inventory, not whole
question closure. Earlier FSCALE/C1 entries describe the gap before53 and no
longer constitute unbound APIs. Exact debug fusion and compound-operation progress
composition remain current; the disclosed numerical premise and implementation
conformance remain distinct. The next bounded action is the grouped compound
mapping described above, with no automatic numerical audit or runtime campaign.

## Compound progress: AVX2 gather and ENTER

**Claim CLM-03-02-54 — source-backed progress mapping; independently accepted
by memory Return45 within the scoped limits below.** This is the bounded compound-operation pass authorized by the current
summary and Return44: inspect one AVX2 partial-destination path and one implicit
multi-access stack path, map their live state/effects to the selected resident
owner, and stop at an actual missing semantic rule. No opcode catalogue, native
execution, adapter implementation or whole question5 acceptance is claimed.
The inspection used the existing QEMU pin and Intel primary manuals; the newly
needed combined Intel089 PDF was downloaded read-only (60-second/40MB limit;
sandbox DNS failed once, then the authorized ordinary-network fetch succeeded).
Ten exact input identities, lengths and SHA256 values are in
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-compound-01/sources.json`; its Intel089 text extraction
is retained beside the PDF. The pass stops at the two ENTER refinements below,
not at missing emitter code.

### Sources and actual inherited paths

- QEMU `c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294`,
  [`ops_sse.h`, `helper_vpgatherdd`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/ops_sse.h#L2360):
  increasing lane index; test mask sign bit; load a dword through
  `cpu_ldl_le_data_ra`; assign the destination lane on return; clear that mask
  lane. Its VEX dispatch is
  [`gen_vsib_avx`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc#L1099),
  passing vector pointers plus base/scale/address mask. The helper's `GETPC()`
  host-unwind convention does not itself preserve a captive resident continuation.
- The same pin's
  [`gen_enter`](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/translate.c#L1885)
  stores old BP, copies `level-1` prior frame pointers through temporary `fp`,
  stores `FrameTemp` for nonzero nesting, then changes BP/SP. It emits **no
  final-SP write-permission probe** before those register assignments.
- Intel [combined SDM089](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/868137/325462-089-sdm-vol-1-2abcd-3abcd-4.pdf),
  Vol2C `VPGATHERDD/VPGATHERQD`, printed pp5-551–554 (local text124438–124611),
  supplies the lane-fault, mask, interruption and MMIO rules. This claim selects
  the VEX.256 **VPGATHERDD** eight-dword case; EVEX and unequal index/data widths
  are not imported into this example.
- Intel [Vol2A082](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf),
  `ENTER`, pp3-354–356 (local text23515–23685), supplies nesting/push order and
  the final-stack-pointer fault requirement. Intel
  [Vol3A092](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf),
  §§7.5–7.6 and #PF program-state discussion pp7-5/7-50, distinguishes fault
  restart from partial-state exceptions. These rules are independent of QEMU's
  implementation choices.

### Gather: commit a lane, not the whole instruction

Before any lane service or mask normalization, apply gather's encoding/entry
checks: any equality among index, mask and destination vector registers, or a
16-bit address-size attribute, raises #UD. The memory encoding must include the
required SIB byte. Gather performs no alignment checking and must not acquire
#AC from the generic typed RAM access policy. These instruction-specific
qualifications accompany the accepted ordinary permission/type checks; the
shared access mechanism does not override them or allow aliased vector banks.

Select increasing lanes0–7, preserving input index/base/scale and the instruction's
start/next RIP. This is an allowed deterministic ordering: Intel permits arbitrary
load order but requires all enabled lower-index lanes to have completed before
reporting a higher-index lane's fault. No higher lane is speculatively serviced in
this selected path. Masked-off lanes perform no operand memory access and leave
the destination unchanged.

Before the first lane access, normalize each mask dword to all ones or zero from
its sign bit, as the VEX.256 pseudocode specifies. This exposes a concrete reuse
correction: the inspected helper tests sign bits but leaves an unvisited lane's
other bits unchanged when an earlier load faults. For example, input lane1 mask
`0x80000001` must be normalized under the selected pseudocode binding even if
lane0 faults; unchanged helper execution is not that binding. This is a resident
mask-state operation, not another memory access or a reason to change generator.

For an enabled lane, the retained phase is `load pending` or `read done(value)`;
only after the latter does the owner commit **both** destination dword and zero
mask dword before any guest event can observe the state. A native stop between
those host stores retains the uncommitted phase and cannot publish a half-pair.
The bounded roots are the live destination/mask banks, stable addressing inputs,
lane index, and at most one loaded dword awaiting commit. They use claim33's
working/bank owner and claims36–37's continuation distinction. They are not
restored from instruction-entry vector snapshots on a later lane fault.

`RetryAccess` repeats only an unperformed lane access; `AccessCompleted(value)`
commits it without issuing another read. On actual `GuestFault`, publish completed
lane pairs, retain the faulting/unvisited mask bits, and invoke50's delivery owner
with restart RIP at this instruction. The internal operation record then ends;
a later guest restart consumes the **architecturally modified** mask/destination,
so cleared lanes are not read again. On ordinary success all mask lanes are zero
and next RIP retires once. These are different from an internal repair, which
keeps the original decoded operation and its lifetime pins.

Intel additionally says pending traps/interrupts from already gathered elements
replace a later element exception and set RF for continuation. Bind that case to
the existing pending-event owner: retain the selected element exception as an
unreported candidate, publish prior lane progress, deliver the eligible prior
cause with gather's restart RIP/RF semantics, and discard the unperformed fault
candidate when the guest takes control. Re-execution resolves it afresh; do not
queue both it and the replacing event. This is a gather-specific restart rule,
not the provisional MOV-SS/INT1 DR6 fusion rule of51. The inspected C loop alone
does not implement that event arbitration. Event eligibility/priority continues
to use accepted event contracts; a blocked external interrupt is not made
eligible merely because a lane failed.

UC RAM uses the accepted typed access policy. For MMIO, Intel explicitly warns
that gather's ordering, widths and repetition are implementation-specific and
that software should not use it there. The selected virtual behavior is a finite
increasing-lane sequence of dword endpoint accesses, using the existing width/
beat contract; no duplicate access is introduced by internal repair. This selects
one device behavior, not a universal hardware exactly-once guarantee. A device
side effect already completed before a reported fault remains completed. The
source/code allocation stays pinned through internal repair; a real guest event
abandons that continuation, as for REP. Source invalidation can prevent later
entry without re-decoding the middle of an already admitted gather.

### ENTER: retain internal temporaries without publishing intermediate BP/SP

For a 64-bit `ENTER alloc,2`, the inspected lowering performs:
old-RBP store at old-RSP−8; prior-frame read at old-RBP−8; store that value at
old-RSP−16; store `FrameTemp=old-RSP−8` at old-RSP−24; then BP/SP publication.
The existing owner can represent every internal interruption with old BP/SP,
FrameTemp, current phase/address, and one read-result temporary. A completed
prior-frame read followed by an internal stop before its store must keep that
value even if the source memory changes; restarting the read is not repair.
Nesting is capped by `imm8 mod32`, so one phase index plus one value suffices,
without an unbounded log of earlier writes. Successful publication sets BP to
FrameTemp and SP to the final allocated position, with the selected operand/
stack-size wrapping rules; flags are unaffected.

All frame reads/stores use the same typed UC/device service, AD-walk handling,
source invalidation and all-writer admission as other accesses. Completed writes
are recorded as completed phases and cannot be repeated by *internal* repair.
A code/PTE alias does not silently discard this progress. The retained decoded
operation/pins survive the internal stop, and invalidation/reclamation waits on
the admitted operation in the existing way. This introduces neither a second
coordinator epoch held while waiting nor a host `longjmp` continuation.

Two **architectural refinements remain unresolved by these inspected sources**:

1. Intel requires a page fault whenever writing at the final stack-pointer value
   would fault. QEMU's shown lowering never performs that check. The adapter
   must include a `final stack check` before publishing BP/SP, not invent a real
   store or change stack contents. An allocation that moves final SP onto an
   unmapped page while all frame accesses succeed is an immediate counterexample
   to unchanged helper reuse; implementing the omitted phase is not optional.
   The independent inherited recipe below makes operand-size checking concrete;
   its architectural effect/exception qualification remains part of this seam.
2. QEMU delays BP/SP assignments until all accesses finish, but that establishes
   only its register strategy. A later **actual guest fault** can follow earlier
   externally visible frame writes, including aliasing a later frame source.
   ENTER's sequential pseudocode and generic restart rule do not, by themselves,
   prove every such surviving-prefix memory outcome. Claim50's explicit Intel
   IDT-delivery allowance cannot be generalized to ENTER without justification.
   Accordingly this pass does **not** accept a blanket ENTER prefix-survival
   rule, undo device effects, or preprobe all future operands (which can change
   aliasing/UC semantics). Old BP/SP restart, surviving memory effects and final
   check ordering must be reconciled as one narrow ENTER fault rule. Internal
   repair preservation above remains valid independently of that choice.

**Finite independent implementation check.** At the already used Bochs pin
`c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f`,
[`stack64.cc:96–131`](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/stack64.cc#L96)
also performs ordered frame stores with temporary BP/SP, then calls
`tickle_write_linear(SS, finalSP, 8)` before committing those registers.
`stack32.cc` supplies width4 and `stack16.cc` width2, including the 16-bit operand
in 64-bit mode. This supplies a concrete **operand-size check recipe**, supported
by ENTER's specified frame-pointer operand size, rather than an invented byte
probe. It does not make Bochs a hardware oracle or establish prefix legality.

The actual pinned
[`access2.cc:338`](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/access2.cc#L338)
checks the extent's endpoints and write translations (both pages when crossing)
but issues no operand-memory store or device write. It also contains an alignment
check branch and normal write-translation effects; those are explicit inherited
behavior, **not silently accepted as Intel ENTER requirements** merely because
this helper exists. The proposed adapter seam is therefore `check stack write
extent(finalSP, operandBytes)` with no target data transaction, serviced by the
existing retained walk owner, after frame phases and before BP/SP commit. The
remaining review question is its fault/AD/exception behavior together with the
surviving-prefix rule, not availability of a check implementation. Four exact
Bochs files are retained beside the Intel089 extraction. No additional source
family or implementation work was started.

**Decision consequence.** Gather needs partial vector/mask publication plus a
pending-event handoff; ENTER needs finite temporary/cursor retention. Both fit
existing owners without a new execution architecture or mandatory full-state
spill at every access. This source construction does not certify the unchanged
helpers: mask normalization/event arbitration and ENTER's omitted check are
concrete adaptation work. The remaining semantic question is now the explicit
ENTER fault contract above, rather than a generic unknown compound-family owner.
No conclusion is extended automatically to POPAD, far returns, other gather
widths or arbitrary multi-access instructions.

**Next bounded action:** independent review of54, then one primary-source
resolution of the coupled ENTER final-check/fault-effects rule if still needed.
Keep51's DR6 fusion separate. No native experiment or broader opcode audit is
authorized by this finding.

## Source-only method preparation — native MOV SS watchpoint followed by ICEBP

**Author:** memory; source-level feasibility accepted in root Return46. No code, build, native execution
or execution budget. This prepares decision evidence for claim51's exact deferred
B-bit fusion, not a numerical or generator comparison. **Inference:** an ordinary
unprivileged Linux parent tracing its own child can observe the needed distinction,
subject to the actual kernel/source and host conditions below. No kernel module
or deliberate kernel-mode debug trap is required.

**Observer contract (source-backed).** Linux v6.12
[`traps.c`, `debug_read_clear_dr6` and `exc_debug_user`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/traps.c)
reads hardware DR6 before clearing it and normalizes its reserved-bit polarity.
The user handler initializes `virtual_dr6` with BS only. Therefore ptrace DR6 is
**not an untouched hardware register snapshot**. However,
[`hw_breakpoint_handler`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/hw_breakpoint.c)
processes each asserted hardware B bit for the installed breakpoint and calls its
perf callback; [`ptrace_triggered` and `ptrace_get_debugreg`](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/x86/kernel/ptrace.c)
reconstruct the corresponding logical ptrace slot's B bit and return it through
PEEKUSER. Physical slot allocation need not equal logical DR0. With exactly one
installed ptrace data watchpoint, this path exposes whether that watchpoint
contributed a B cause. It does not manufacture B0 just because the saved RIP
follows ICEBP. When the original normalized DR6 is zero, the user handler still
requests SIGTRAP as the ICEBP case; with a watch cause, reconstructed B bits also
reach `send_sigtrap`. No second user instruction or user signal handler is needed
before the parent reads the first signal-delivery stop.

**Proposed minimal sequence and controls.** A fresh own-child TRACEME/initial
stop per case avoids previous debug causes. Before enabling the watchpoint,
initialize a naturally aligned two-byte operand with the child's current valid
SS selector, prepare/warm code, operand and stack, and communicate their addresses.
Use fixed adjacent machine instructions for `MOV SS,[operand]; F1`, with named
start/after-F1 labels and inspected bytes. Parent installs only logical DR0,
read/write data match of length2 (x86 has no read-only data-watch encoding),
checks every ptrace result and DR0/DR7 readback, clears the exposed DR6 causes,
sets a start context with TF=0, and uses CONT. No SINGLESTEP, syscall tracing,
software breakpoint, intervening stop or instrumentation inside the two
instructions. Operand setup and other accesses to that address precede arming.
At the first stop collect wait status, siginfo, full saved registers/RFLAGS,
RIP relative to the retained labels, and PEEKUSER DR6; do not substitute si_code
for the B-bit oracle. Do not resume into a handler to obtain the observation.

- Plain ICEBP with the watch disabled must produce the expected post-F1 SIGTRAP
  without logical B0/BS. This validates the no-watch event/observer path.
- Watched MOV SS followed by NOP must reach the post-NOP debug stop with B0 and
  BS=0. This validates both delayed-watch delivery and Linux's reconstruction.
- Watched MOV SS followed immediately by ICEBP is the discriminating case:
  post-F1 RIP plus B0=1 supports fused reporting on that host/kernel; post-F1 RIP
  plus B0=0 supports non-inclusion there. Neither result may be predeclared PASS
  for the architecture choice. Other RIPs/signals, extra causes, API failure or
  timeout are retained inconclusive/control failures, not repaired by stepping.

The upstream Linux
[`mov_ss_trap.c` selftest](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/tools/testing/selftests/x86/mov_ss_trap.c)
already programs a two-byte read/write SS watch and runs `MOV SS; .byte 0xf1`.
Its commentary and survival-oriented signal output are useful feasibility
precedent, **not the missing combined-DR6 oracle or retained observation**. This
method does not adopt its child-attaches-parent/PR_SET_PTRACER_ANY arrangement;
parent/own-child tracing avoids needing that relaxation. Normal ptrace security
policy or disabled hardware-breakpoint support may still reject the setup.

**Limits and stopping condition.** Identify actual Intel family/model/stepping,
microcode, kernel build/configuration and virtualization before selecting a run.
The v6.12 source chain must match the running observer or be rechecked at its
actual source; another debug notifier, perf consumer or tracing modification can
invalidate the inference. Do not assume a VM or non-Intel result is native Intel
behavior. Check the named processor's published debug/MOV-SS errata when that
processor is known; no absence-of-erratum finding is made here. Fault avoidance
is substantive: a fault can discard the deferred condition, so prefaulted valid
selector/data/code/stack and exact stop classification are required, and an
unexpected fault ends that sample. Ordinary scheduling does not authorize an
inserted instruction; exceptional NMI/platform interference remains a finite-run
limit, not a universal hardware proof. First-stop inspection establishes only
that event's RIP and reported cause, not the absence of every later event.

This is a feasible **source-level preparation**, conditional on those observer
and control checks. The next step, only if a suitable host is available, is a
small retained producer/method with finite sample/time limits and independent
pre-execution review. No run, remote access, host changes or retry campaign is
authorized by this section. Local source copies for review are
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-debug-method/{traps.c,ptrace.c,hw_breakpoint.c,mov_ss_trap.c}`;
all are the linked v6.12 files. The initial sandbox DNS fetch failure is not a
native sample; the subsequent authorized read-only source fetch succeeded.

## ENTER: sequential effects and a non-writing final check

**Claim CLM-03-02-55 — narrowed components independently accepted by memory
Return47; ordinary-RAM fault-prefix premise remains unresolved.** Bounded source-only continuation of54/Return45: compare newer retained
Intel ENTER wording, general restart and MMIO effects with the existing independent
Bochs recipe. Stop with a concrete permitted-outcome construction or exact
unsupported premise. No new download, opcode survey, execution or implementation
was needed. Sources are the exact ten inputs indexed by54's
`/private/tmp/natemu-track03-compound-01/sources.json`; source interpretation and
selected virtual behavior are distinguished below.

### Primary rule that narrows the fault question

Intel [combined SDM089](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/868137/325462-089-sdm-vol-1-2abcd-3abcd-4.pdf),
Vol1 §20.3.1, printed p20-2 (local text24261–24279), explicitly permits an
instruction to take an exception or VM exit after a completed MMIO read **or
write**. Its examples include multiple-access operations such as PUSH from
memory, POP to memory and PUSHAD; later guest re-execution can repeat the side
effect. This is not an ENTER-specific observation, nor does it permit replay of
an access merely because the emulator internally repaired its mapping. It does
establish that generic fault restart is **not a global memory/device rollback
promise** for a multi-access instruction.

The same manual's newer ENTER entry, Vol2A pp3-295–297 (local text51040–51202),
retains the earlier082 rules: sequential frame pushes/copies, bounded nesting,
unchanged flags, and #PF if a write at final SP would fault. It does not specify
an all-frame transactional store or a preliminary check of every eventual
operand. Intel [Vol3A092](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/922487/253668-092-sdm-vol-3a.pdf)
§§7.5–7.6 supplies restart register/stack-pointer restoration, while §11.1.1's
memory atomicity guarantees concern individual qualifying accesses, not ENTER's
whole frame. Lack of whole-frame atomicity alone would not prove fault effects;
the positive sequential operation and explicit multi-access/MMIO exception rule
are the additional grounds for the construction below.

### MMIO effects established; ordinary-RAM prefix remains unresolved

The existing owner can preserve completed frame effects while staging BP/SP,
and can distinguish internal repair from actual guest restart. For MMIO,
§20.3.1 positively supports completed effects surviving a later exception and
possibly recurring on actual guest re-execution. Internal repair still resumes
only an unperformed phase; it is not guest re-execution and cannot manufacture
another device transaction. Unrelated event/controller/time state is never
rolled back.

For **ordinary WB RAM**, the proposed ordered-prefix rule remains an unresolved
architectural premise. §7.5 describes restoration of machine state; §7.6's
register/stack-pointer explanation does not establish that memory is excluded.
Successful sequential pseudocode and the absence of a separate ENTER rollback
sentence do not distinguish a fault-visible prefix from an implementation that
stages ordinary RAM effects while maintaining the successful access sequence.
MMIO's explicit permission and individual-access atomicity rules cannot be
extended to settle that distinction. Independent review R47-1 correctly rejects
this pass's initial attempted closure on those grounds.

Bochs's staged64-bit tuple and ordered stores provide a concrete candidate
implementation but not the missing architectural permission. In particular,
an earlier frame destination can alias a later frame source; that success-path
ordering must be preserved regardless of the fault rule ultimately selected.
Neither undoing MMIO nor preprobing/snapshotting every future operand is an
accepted remedy. The exact remaining question is whether a completed ordinary
RAM frame-store prefix may remain visible when a later ENTER access or its final
check produces an actual guest fault. No observation of a specific processor's
entire microsequence is required to answer it, but an applicable positive rule
or discriminating native result is needed. The retained-operation/root interface
already represents either policy; no new owner is justified by this uncertainty.

### Final-SP check is validation, not a dummy data transaction

After all frame phases, compute final SP using the instruction's operand/stack
size rules and validate the hypothetical stack write before committing BP/SP.
Select an extent of `operandBytes` (2,4,8) starting at final SP. This binds the
instruction's frame-pointer operand size and the independent Bochs
[`ENTER16/32/64` recipe](https://github.com/bochs-emu/Bochs/blob/c38d6a4ab10e084b7d52d6606e90b7395c6e688f/bochs/cpu/stack64.cc#L96);
it is an explicit interpretation of the unspecified-width word “write”, not
Intel hardware measurement. Do the normal stack range/canonical and **write
permission** checks for the extent, including both pages if it crosses a page
boundary. Report the relevant stack/address fault or #PF with write intent and
its faulting linear address. The record retains which validation subphase is
complete, so internal repair need not replay a completed non-RAM page-table read.

The validation does **not** read or write target data, call an MMIO register,
perform a read/modify/write of its old contents, or publish a data-access debug
match. ENTER's exception entry names the final check's #PF and stack condition;
it does not require a target data transfer or an alignment fault for this
validation. Do not inherit the Bochs generic tickle helper's #AC/debug branches
just because its extent computation is reused. Actual frame operand accesses
retain their separately applicable instruction/access rules.

Use the accepted walk owner with write-permission intent but **no target-store
commit**. Translation may set A according to §5.8 and the accepted failed-walk
rules. The validation itself does not require D: §5.8 associates D setting with
a write to the linear address, and this instruction phase transfers no data.
Select leaving D unchanged for this phase, without claiming every processor's
hypothetical-write check does the same. Earlier real frame writes retain their
own D updates; never clear an existing D bit. A probe result alone must not
publish a shadow that assumes the later real-store dirty obligation was already
satisfied. This separates permission validation from store side effects rather
than importing Bochs's generic `BX_WRITE` translation wholesale.

For example, if all frame stores succeed but final SP lies on an absent page,
the check faults with old BP/SP. Earlier MMIO effects remain completed;
the visibility of earlier ordinary-RAM frame writes is the unresolved premise
above. If
final SP is writable MMIO, validation succeeds without touching that register.
If final SP aliases the stack's own page table, ordinary admitted walk semantics
and already completed frame writes determine the translation; the check does
not manufacture an additional operand store into the table. On successful
validation, publish BP/SP and next RIP once. On internal suspension, retain the
same decoded operation, cursor and lifetime pins; on a real fault, abandon it
and enter the accepted delivery owner. No second held coordinator epoch or
unbounded prefix log is needed.

**Decision consequence and remaining premise.** The pass binds a finite
permission-only final-SP check to54's existing owner and establishes the explicit
MMIO/re-execution distinction. It does **not** close ENTER's ordinary-RAM
actual-fault effects. The operand-sized extent and no-target-data validation are
proposed source-based interface interpretations, not hardware observations;
review assesses those separately from R47-1's rejected prefix argument. A
contrary applicable requirement reopens that exact rule. No full question5
acceptance or broader compound-instruction conclusion follows. MOV-SS/INT1
fusion and numerical premises retain their separate statuses.

**Next action:** finish independent review of55's narrowed components. The next
research proposal should seek a direct architectural clarification or prepare
an applicable native oracle for the **exact ordinary-RAM ENTER fault-prefix
question**, rather than repeat a general source survey. No native method or
execution is authorized by this finding.
