## Comparison preparation — actual AArch64 emission routes, September 20

**Author:** `/root/comparison_sources`, independent source-preparation context;
not the findings reviewer. This appendix consumes the owning INV-03-01 method
preparation allowance (subtask cap 20 minutes), not a new investigation budget.
Read the current workflow/charters, common contract and Supplements N–Q, then
rechecked retained pinned primary source. No builds, emitted objects, generator
execution, service changes or architecture acceptance were produced. Attempts to
open two additional upstream files through web retrieval returned cache misses;
no claim depends on their unavailable contents. Review remains pending.

**Decision consequence:** actual emitted blocks can expose optimization, ordering,
state materialization and helper costs that source-level MOV counts miss. They
can define a bounded execution comparison; they cannot select a generator from
instruction count or tool availability. Reuse N/O's identified compilation hooks
and six matched fixtures; do not start another full recovery-field survey or
implement all three captive adapters to obtain the first discriminating output.

| Candidate | Minimum compile-only route and raw export | Concrete prerequisites/limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| FEX | Existing `CodeSizeValidation` calls `CompileRIPCount`; its existing parser uses NASM. Tee raw INFO before normalization and export `CodeData.BlockBegin`, `CodeData.Size`, entry offsets and relocation records at `Arm64JITCore::CompileCode` return. | Linux, Clang/CMake, Python/NASM and the pinned dependencies already enumerated in Supplement O. Enable VIXL disassembly. The normalized NOP-subtracted count is not the complete block. Existing checkpoint preparation is optional diagnostic material, not a prerequisite to plain byte export. |
| TCG | Supplement O's paused initialized MTTCG-worker callback compiles through `tb_gen_code`, exports `tb->tc.ptr`/size and search data, and never calls `cpu_tb_exec`. | Native AArch64 QEMU build with x86 target, compiler/Python/Meson/Ninja/GLib/zlib and selected subprojects. Preserve initialized CPU/mapping state, fault cleanup, cflags count and parallel-memory semantics. A paused QEMU command alone produces no fixture output. |
| Box64 | Supplement O's branch after `thread_set_emu` but before ELF initialization, with mapped fixture permissions and locked `FillBlock64`; export final native block and instruction offsets before transient pass data is freed. | Linux AArch64 C/CMake/Python toolchain and libc development inputs. Audit the earlier initialization and guard guest execution entries. Its normal loader is not a compile-only CLI. Export native code separately from jump-next data, literal tables and state metadata. |

Direct sources: [FEX tool](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/Source/Tools/CodeSizeValidation/Main.cpp),
[FEX compiler output](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/JIT/JIT.cpp),
[QEMU translation](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/translate-all.c),
[QEMU execution/cleanup skeleton](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/accel/tcg/cpu-exec.c),
[Box64 initialization](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/core.c),
[Box64 emission/allocation](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/src/dynarec/dynarec_native.c).

**Does AMD/Linux prevent AArch64 emission? Source-backed answer: no for FEX's
explicit diagnostic route; ordinary native QEMU/Box64 builds are different.**
FEX's [CMake](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/CMakeLists.txt)
permits x86-64 CI/debug builds with `ENABLE_X86_HOST_DEBUG=ON`; its
[`InitializeCompiler`](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Core.cpp)
still creates `CreateArm64JITCore`. This supports a source-level compile-only
route, not an observed successful build. The optional VIXL simulator is not
required by the fact that fixture execution is excluded; its defaults must not
be mistaken for M4 features. [HostFeatures.cpp](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/Source/Common/HostFeatures.cpp)
uses `CPUFeaturesAll` on x86 and simulator-specific SVE defaults, and applies
later overrides. The code-size tool also forces atomics/preserve-all and disables
simulator indirect calls. Therefore capture the final feature object, disable
unsupported SVE and reconcile ABI/order options before comparing output from
any host. A host name or a JSON feature list alone does not prove equivalent
lowering. x86 debug build helper ABI differences also preclude treating those
host helper pointers as executable AArch64 helpers.

[QEMU Meson](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/meson.build)
sets `tcg_arch = host_arch` (unless TCI is selected). Choosing an AArch64
*guest* target on an x86 host does not select the AArch64 output backend.
[Box64 CMake](https://github.com/ptitSeb/box64/blob/454d5387571bb5223fe8b43a4624debde8b74647/CMakeLists.txt)
includes AArch64 prolog/epilog/next/lock assembly for `ARM_DYNAREC`; changing the
option on an x86 compiler is not an established compile-only route. An AArch64
cross toolchain plus an AArch64 execution environment for the generator, or an
additional diagnostic extraction, would need its own validation. Neither is
necessary if the newly reported local Linux AArch64 worker is usable. This
appendix relies on root's fresh worker observation for that availability; it
does not reinterpret the earlier failed OrbStack launch as a permanent blocker.

**Smallest proposed observation:** build/export one marked load per candidate
under one identical effective feature/order profile, retain raw bytes and full
entry/tail context, and reject accidental guest execution or incorrect decoded
extent. If export works, expand only to the already fixed six fixtures and
second ordering profile within the owning method's budget. This is preparation
screening, not performance evidence or the final workload suite. A compiler
failure records an unresolved acquisition/diagnostic dependency, not a loser.

**Boundary before captive timing:** raw upstream bytes may embed dispatcher,
state-frame, literal-pool and helper addresses. FEX's compiler output explicitly
relocates code storage and exports relocations; Box64 builds jump-next references;
TCG assumes its CPU/runtime context. Merely copying bytes into the VM is not a
valid benchmark. For a helper-free bounded region, preserve the emitted
instruction sequence, establish its entry register/state contract and all
address dependencies, and use a common declared return wrapper. Count excluded
entry/exit/state/helper work separately; a truncated leaf cannot substantiate
whole-generator performance. Memory leaves additionally need identical guards,
TSO state and address/mapping assumptions. If a candidate cannot supply such a
region within the budget, report that comparison cell unavailable and its exact
remedy; do not substitute hand-written code while calling it compiler output.
No change to the semantic/recovery acceptance contract follows from this limited
execution scope.

**Remaining uncertainty and next action:** source identifies concrete seams but
cannot establish their buildability, emitted output, address independence or
performance. The owning method should prepare and inspect the three minimal
export patches, then run the bounded screening with retained producing inputs.
Stop at the declared budget or a usable matched export; do not open a new
source-only recovery campaign as a substitute for missing output. The next
selection evidence must measure comparable useful work and disclose residual
adaptation cost, rather than rank successful tool setup or instruction counts.

## Queued-recovery FEX construction and bounded hot-path envelope

### CLM-03-02-56 — Bounded FEX native objects with checked permanent semantic exits

**Source construction independently accepted by root in the findings thread;
not implementation conformance or measured adaptation cost.** `/root/comparison_sources`, September21, source-author task
within the integrated closure package. Reuses accepted claim28's allocation-
visible recovery uses and INV03-01 claim19's queued faults. No new source,
experiment or full-state catalog audit. This is not an amendment of the guest
ISA or a revival of universal instruction-commit polling.

#### Native eligibility and semantic coverage

Use FEX for an explicitly admitted ordinary native lowering domain. An initial
object contains at most32 guest instructions. Each admitted guest instruction's native lowering has at
most one scalar, non-atomic guest access of1/2/4/8 bytes, at most one GPR
architectural destination, and no other potentially faulting semantic effect.
The initial FEX native domain uses the admitted long-mode context; protected/
compatibility/startup32 contexts use the concrete TCG service provider in claim58.
Native guest accesses require the resident memory owner's checked ordinary-WB
route and the single-transfer precise-effect premise; unsupported alignment,
page/route splits, devices and protected effects enter checked service **before**
their unperformed access. Permit register-only integer ALU/logic, comparisons,
branches and register-only128-bit vector logic with one vector destination.
Native flag production must fit the explicit current PF/AF/NZCV recipe and carry
tag below. Implicit multi-access stack/string operations, RMW/atomic operations,
DIV/trapping arithmetic, unsupported flag shapes and system/control operations
use the permanent semantic exit initially. Root's separate FP status proposal
must establish its own eligibility/commit predicate before FP joins this domain.

This selects implementation paths, not unsupported guest instructions. A cold
service consumes the admitted instruction/decode and current operands, performs
its checked semantic work, and returns via current-source admission. Required
startup32 may run through the system semantic owner until its native context
binding is implemented. No real-mode requirement is introduced. This is
architecturally possible without translating a complete QEMU IR into FEX IR:
the service interface is architectural state plus typed effects, not a backend
IR interchange. However, a complete checked resident semantic service is an
**implementation obligation**, not a library already produced by this study.
Existing QEMU/selected helper semantics supply reusable operations, but Linux
FEX fallbacks or a host TCG compiler are not automatically that service.

#### Current values and staged effects

At each guest commit the adapter maintains one current binding for every exposed
architectural field. The native domain changes only the16 GPRs, low16 vector
registers, and arithmetic flags; all other state keeps claim36's authoritative
entry/backing binding. This does not assume those low registers are in memory.
For this domain, finish pending flag construction into at most three scalar-
sized bindings: PF raw value, AF raw value, and NZCV with a carry-convention tag.
The tag is metadata, not a fourth changing guest value. Non-arithmetic flags
remain in their unchanged owner binding. Unsupported mixed flag recipes leave
through service rather than exceeding this declared bound.

Relevant existing seams are pinned FEX `Core.cpp:643` (SRA flush),
`OpcodeDispatcher.h:1177–1240` (fixed GPR, PF/AF, low-vector and separate context
publication), and `GetNZCV` at1698. These supply operations/classes to implement
the representation; they do not prove a complete recovery consumer. The pinned
sources live in `fex-consolidated-01/upstream/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core`.

For a fault-bearing instruction, preserve the **current pre-instruction** state,
its original effective address and any distinct operand/result needed by cold
completion. Stage a register/flag result separately until the instruction's
required effect succeeds. A scalar store has no RAM before-image: the pending
store is unperformed or its successful effect is recorded, never rolled back.
A successful scalar load may commit its staged result; there is no later native
semantic trap in this admitted instruction shape. A queued load completed by
service instead finishes the remaining ALU/writeback in permanent service and
leaves to current-source dispatch, unless a typed interior continuation proves
it cannot enter a stale successor. The old register value must survive until its
last actual recovery obligation; keeping every historical instruction version
forever is unnecessary.

Represent FaultSite and CommitState as real ordering/effect boundaries in the
adapted optimizer. A guard, guest access or architectural publication cannot
cross its commit boundary, and a fault-capable read cannot disappear because
its returned value is dead. Pure transformations remain eligible only if they
preserve the site's phase/recipe. At the initial seam, prohibit cross-boundary
merges/reordering rather than assuming GuestOpcode supplies these rules.
`IR.json:193` only declares its guest-offset marker; `JIT.cpp:1005/1117` exports
PC correspondence, not architectural before-images.

#### Allocation and concrete space/work limits

Apply claim28's mechanism exactly: post-access recovery uses participate in
backward liveness through the access/commit obligation, but are a metadata-use
class that does not reload spilled values simply to consume the marker. Export
locations at the actual native fault/guard instruction after pre-access
allocation, eviction and scratch preparation. Keep current register occupancy,
initialized spill slots and constants synchronized with the map. A later reload's
location or a stale SSAToReg entry is not the fault map. Prevent post-RA merges
across these sites unless the descriptors are updated. Reserve/check scratch
clobbers, including NZCV, at guards, branches and veneers.

The following is a conservative **admission envelope chosen by this design**,
not a claim about unmodified FEX or its typical costs:

| Bound | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
|19 current scalar-sized roots|16 GPR values plus PF/AF/NZCV; NZCV may remain native flags, not occupy a GPR.|
|16 current vector roots|Low128-bit XMM values; unchanged upper/state fields remain authoritative under entry bindings.|
|At most3 additional scalar and1 additional vector value|Staged destination/before-image, effective address and distinct completion operand allowances; many alias existing roots. A single instruction cannot exceed this shape.|
|39 simultaneously named roots, conservatively|35 current plus4 staging allowances. This is an upper bound on named recoverable values, not39 extra registers or39 mandatory stores.|
|At most64 actual emitted spill slots per object|With the pinned32-byte slot stride, at most2048 bytes of generated spill area, retained with the frame. Extra spill slots due specifically to recovery cannot exceed this total cap.|
|At most4096 emitted AArch64 instructions /16KiB code, at most96 sites and128KiB metadata|Inspect the actual post-RA output and declared descriptors before publication; shorten the object or use semantic service when any bound is exceeded.|

The ordinary-domain before-image addition is therefore at most3 scalar-sized
values plus1 vector value at an instruction, over a complete current-state map.
Preserving current values previously considered dead by upstream optimization
may additionally extend **all35 current** root lifetimes; the four staging
values are not a claim that recovery adds only four live values relative to
unmodified FEX. Spills can keep the roots recoverable without register residency.

The slot cap is essential: pinned
`RegisterAllocationPass.cpp:280–349` chooses spill victims, allocates a fresh
slot for a newly spilled SSA value, and explicitly leaves slot coloring as a
TODO. The39 simultaneous-value bound does **not** imply39 cumulative slots in
an object. `CPUBackend.h:144` defines MaxSpillSlotSize=32;
`JIT/MemoryOps.cpp:371/460` uses that stride for spill/fill, and
`JIT/JIT.cpp:842/1243` allocates/releases the resulting stack area. Use that
actual convention and cap the emitted count; do not assume free slot reuse or
introduce another allocation framework. If normal IR temporaries or newly
preserved values exceed the total spill/code cap, the native object is not
admitted in that shape. Splitting at a legal guest boundary is permitted.

Thus storage and generated work have explicit finite upper bounds even before
an implementation exists. They are not a nanosecond bound, an estimate of usual
register pressure, or a proof that every desired instruction fits those caps.
Metadata markers can emit no native instructions while their extended lifetimes
still add moves/spills or inhibit optimization. Only actual adapted output can
measure that difference. The coarse4096-instruction ceiling bounds generated
work between the stop opportunities below; it does not bound memory stalls,
mutex contention, native scheduling or permanent service cost.

#### Branch, helper and REP policy under claim19

All external links, including warmed links, name resident admission veneers;
no unchecked upstream linker callback or mutable branch patch supplies the
lifetime contract. Pinned `JIT/BranchOps.cpp:46` and `JIT.cpp:570` identify those
replacement seams. Native helper calls are excluded from an ordinary object's
interior initially: leave through a typed service boundary with current roots,
declared clobbers, bounded stack and no active guest-RAM grant while waiting.
A future admitted helper must supply those same properties explicitly.

Every internal cycle contains an actual stop check. One simple construction
orders the object's guest blocks and checks every edge to an equal-or-earlier
block; edges without checks then form a DAG. Validate native internal loops too:
no lowering-introduced unbounded loop is admitted. With code-size limits, a peer
reaches a check/exit after finite generated work under the unchanged old view.
Checks/entry veneers preserve current native roots; they do not publish a dense
canonical frame on normal execution. Synchronous exceptions, debug/single-step
and control changes retain their instruction-specific semantic exits. No load/
branch is required at every ordinary instruction solely to support mutation.

REP is deliberately outside this initial native domain. FEX
`OpcodeDispatcher.cpp:3250` states that the MemSet REP STOS lowering does not
support partial faults; MOVS at3277 likewise updates architectural progress after
MemCpy. A permanent checked REP service instead owns legal iteration/subaccess
progress, bounded stop opportunities and admitted-route obligations. Reusable
TCG `target/i386/tcg/translate.c:1304` do_gen_rep shows count/CC progress and
REP_MAX-bounded exits, but adapting its system memory effects remains work.
This avoids claiming that a captured native frame can reconstruct progress its
lowering never preserved. REP performance is a separate measured frequency/cost
term, not silently charged as a service on every scalar access.

#### Constructibility and decision boundary

This is a finite construction using existing FEX IR, fixed/general register
classes, spill emitter and branch seams plus claim28's explicit allocator/map
changes. It can support an architecture with FEX ordinary code and checked
permanent compound/system/startup32 service without universal spills or relaxing
required guest semantics. It does **not** assert that those changes, semantic
service, exact field consumers or scratch/phase verification already exist.
The concrete remaining hot uncertainty is adapted allocation/commit/guard cost
inside the admitted envelope; cold uncertainty is semantic-service cost and
frequency. The author's source construction can justify a provisional component
direction only with those residuals retained. It cannot turn unmodified finite
body timings into general recovery conformance or a quantified total-speed claim.


### CLM-03-02-57 — A conservative inherited native-SSE path with complete status ownership

**Source/design contract independently accepted by `/root/direction_review`,
September21, in the existing findings thread.** This closes
the specific policy gap exposed by FEX's discarded sticky flags without relaxing
SSE semantics. It is a component construction, not proof of the unwritten adapter
or acceptance of every floating-point instruction. Preserve the selected guest
instruction set through checked inherited service outside native eligibility.

Own the complete MXCSR in the resident architectural state. Replace FEX's
GetMXCSR/RestoreMXCSRState/STMXCSR/LDMXCSR path with the accepted owner, including
reserved-bit rejection, state-image coupling and CPU feature/exception controls.
Never apply its0xFFC0 mask to stored/read architectural status. CPU/control changes
reselect the admitted native context; native FPCR/FPSR are temporary execution
state, not authoritative guest status.

For binary32/binary64 ADD/SUB/MUL/DIV and positive SQRT, a conservative native
specialization inherits the corresponding QEMU SoftFloat hardfloat arithmetic.
The initial construction is scalar; packed and other instructions remain checked
service until their full instruction-level status/commit binding is admitted.
Native eligibility requires all six SIMD exceptions masked, nearest-even rounding,
DAZ=0 and FTZ=0, and guest inexact already sticky. Native FPCR uses that rounding,
no flush/default-NaN controls, and the verified AFP configuration where the emitted
scalar instruction relies on lane preservation. Expose no dependency on native
host exception traps. The profile is checked at admitted entry and cannot change
inside an object without a semantic exit; all actual data checks remain per
arithmetic instruction.

The bit-pattern precheck admits only normal inputs (binary64 exponent1..2046,
binary32 exponent1..254), and SQRT additionally rejects a negative source.
Retain the original architectural destination and captured operands while computing
a candidate through the inherited native instruction. The postcheck admits only
normal results safely above the minimum-normal boundary (binary64 exponent2..2046,
binary32 exponent2..254). Commit the candidate only after that check. Scalar upper
bits retain the ISA's legacy/VEX rules. If either check fails, the checked service
receives original operands and the instruction's preserved effect record; a failed
trial is never mistaken for a committed destination or replayed guest memory read.
The next guest instruction does not execute before this disposition.

**Source argument:** pinned QEMU `fpu/softfloat.c::can_use_fpu` requires inexact
already set and nearest-even. `float64_gen2`/`float32_gen2` then apply input flush
and normal/zero predicates, execute the native operation, record overflow on
infinity, and fall back around minimum-normal results. The stricter normal-only
precheck makes input flush irrelevant. The stricter postcheck excludes infinity,
zero, subnormal and the entire minimum-normal exponent bin; it therefore excludes
all its overflow/underflow corrections. Division's denominator-normal check is
included. `float64_sqrt`/`float32_sqrt` use the same eligibility plus a nonnegative
normal/zero source. No NaN/signaling, divide-by-zero, denormal, overflow or underflow
class can arise on this admitted path. Inexact may arise, but is already sticky
and masked, so complete architectural MXCSR remains unchanged. This is a strict
specialization of inherited machinery, not a new floating-point algorithm.

For all other operands/controls, use the existing status-capable inherited library
under claims47/48, including its instruction-specific NaN policy and staged effect
owner. Unmasked service starts with operation-local exception state so a prior
sticky bit cannot suppress or invent a new exception. Source-memory/MMIO effects
are retained independently of destination suppression. Discard speculative native
FPSR from a rejected trial; it must not contaminate the service's status or later
host helpers. Each arithmetic instruction in a multi-instruction object owns its
own commit: fallback on a later instruction never replays earlier instructions.

**Cost contract:** price input/result classification, staging/before-image lifetime,
entry profile admission and service frequency explicitly. The measured naked FEX
FP leaf does not price these additions. Give competing QEMU helpers the same
sticky-inexact/control state so their existing native path is eligible too. The
clear-inexact service case remains a distinct cost regime. A measured preference
inside one admitted regime is not a workload-wide winner or numerical conformance
outside that regime. FMA, MIN/MAX, conversions, packed aggregation and x87 retain
their own inherited bindings; this construction does not silently broaden them.

**x87 remains separate:** the lower-cost numerical profile may use inherited
binary64 approximations under the owner's explicit accuracy concession. Existing
claims48/53 also identify numerical-status issues (including C1 round direction)
that can require a status-capable provider. The owner has been asked whether the
accuracy concession includes documented numerical-status differences; no response
has been inferred. Stack/state images, memory effects and exception delivery stay
required. This question does not block the independent SSE construction above.


### CLM-03-02-58 — Proposed FEX ordinary objects with a concrete TCG service provider

**Composition independently accepted by direction_review in INV03-01 findings;
component preference is assessed separately in INV03-01 claim20.** Select FEX for claim56's admitted ordinary objects and claim57's
admitted numerical paths. Use the already investigated QEMU x86 frontend and
AArch64 TCG backend to produce the checked service objects for instructions
outside that native domain. These are two host code-production components inside
the same captive architecture, not two independent virtual machines or a new
IR-to-IR translator. This paragraph proposes the mechanism; the final preference
must still be supported by the reviewed matched comparison and its cost assumptions.

**Concrete execution provider:** the pinned QEMU system frontend supplies CPL,
segmentation, mode/control and instruction lowering, including startup32. The
existing AArch64 TCG backend emits immutable service objects that execute inside
the captive VM with resident state/helpers. An object admits one architectural
instruction, or a bounded REP step retaining the same instruction/decode identity.
Use the state, helper, fault/commit and typed access adaptations already defined
in claims21–24/36–55, and the resident paging/event/lifetime owners; do not import
QEMU's ambient host CPU loop, host pointers, longjmp or application signal path.
Frontend-generated sequences therefore have an execution engine even when no
standalone numerical/system helper exists. Particular integer/FP helper bodies
remain inherited components, not a claim that a generic library supplies every
instruction.

At a service entry, materialize the actual current-state view from FEX's registered
locations into the resident architectural/TCG bindings. Unchanged state remains
with the existing authoritative owner. After service, reload the fields the
operation actually modified, including flags, vector/extended state, control,
segment and event/context effects as applicable; the set is not restricted to
claim56's35 ordinary roots. Context-changing operations reselect current-source
admission and cannot return into an incompatible FEX object. State/image and
exception effects retain their accepted staged owner. A complete field adapter
is implementation work under that concrete contract, not an existing unmodified
FEX/QEMU ABI.

**No compilation dependency during drain:** obtain or compile a TCG service
object while holding no active guest-RAM grant and outside EXCLUSIVE. A compile
miss may leave the vCPU PAUSED while the existing host worker produces code;
clock/event receipt and old-epoch drain remain live. Publication and stale-reply
rejection use claim14's identity/generation rules. Before native FEX admission,
reserve every executable suffix-completion handler and storage item that its
possible queued fault can require. Before a TCG service instruction begins an
admitted effect, pin the service code, helpers and phase continuations needed to
complete that effect without another host compile. A drain-time fault must not
discover a missing completion routine. Unavailable capacity causes a pre-effect
wait, different admission or contained termination under the existing resource
contract, not a false quiescence acknowledgement.

Both generators share claim14's finite compilation/job slots and the same
three-reference per-vCPU union; a second producer receives no extra uncounted
capacity. At a pre-effect semantic handoff, finish materializing the current
FEX view, discard its native return path and release that object's execution
reference before entering the TCG service object. A queued FEX frame instead
retains its old object but uses permanently reserved suffix handlers, not a new
retirable service object during drain. TCG service helpers/phase continuations
are permanent or part of the pinned admitted service allocation. No nested
service-object call stack is introduced. Any outstanding reply/next-target pin
still occupies the existing union and shared P-slot accounting; the admission
census rejects an object or waits before effects if those limits would be
exceeded. Both compiler kinds share the bounded host work queue rather than
independently multiplying per-vCPU jobs. Their private memory/worker limits stay
inside the accepted per-role resource contract.

A queued FEX scalar access or rejected FP trial completes only its saved remaining
effects through those reserved handlers. It never replays a guest memory input
or an already committed instruction. TCG service lowerings likewise expose their
admitted subaccesses/partial effects to the same owner. REP preserves decoded
instruction identity, count/pointers/flags and completed subaccesses across bounded
steps. Admitted-route completion precedes conflicting remap/ack/reuse; code and
state pins survive inactive waiting. Service ownership is nonnesting: nested
semantic/A-D work is completed inside the admitted transaction using its reserved
permanent machinery, not by waiting for a transaction queued behind itself.

**Selection rationale to assess:** ordinary cached execution can avoid TCG's
repeated canonical state traffic while the concrete service provider supplies the
system/compound semantics FEX does not supply unchanged. The guarded numerical
comparison must price classification/staging and use the same initial status for
TCG. This is a conditional performance-led implementation direction, not a claim
that every FEX instruction is faster or that a whole workload has been measured.

**Costs and falsifiers:** retain both host compiler dependencies, their maintenance
and compiled footprint; FEX recovery-root/commit adaptations; full service state
conversion; resident helper/service execution; initial and recurring compilation;
and service frequency. There is no zero-cost fallback assumption. If common
required instructions leave the native domain so often that conversion/service
cost erases the ordinary advantage, or adapted liveness/code costs erase it even
without service, use the TCG ordinary path or reopen the component choice. A
translation-miss/low-reuse workload can prefer the cheaper compile path; unmatched
Linux diagnostic compile timings do not establish that crossover. Track04/05
implementation measurements must test those stated falsifiers, not discover them
after an unconditional speed claim. The finite architecture decision does not
require universal workload dominance, and future conformance tests do not replace
an unresolved source-level composition requirement.

Claim59 and INV03-01 claim20 now supply the separate x87 numerical-provider/
accuracy choice. No status waiver was inferred from the unanswered optional
clarification; the lazy C1 construction preserves the selected provider contract.
The withdrawn universal TCG/Bochs preference is not reinstated.

#### Low-added-cost admission refinement for claim56

**Source-author refinement, September21; no new experiment.** The envelope above
is a ceiling, not the default emission policy. Prefer native instruction shapes
whose actual lowered state is already commit-safe: register ALU/logic completes
before the next faulting access; a scalar load writes its destination only on
success; a store has one precisely classified effect; no later unmasked semantic
trap invalidates that instruction's register commit. Admit that form only when
the post-RA site map proves every current root still exists. If a required root
was lost, repair its lifetime locally or decline that native lowering in favor
of the checked semantic path. Declining a lowering changes its performance, not
the guest behavior; no speed is credited to instructions executed by the service.

Use each retained fixture's actual prefix. In the original live fixture, ADD
and PXOR precede the scalar LDR, so their completed results/current flags are
required, not their earlier before-images. In the consolidated branch fixtures,
ADD or XOR precedes LDR but PXOR follows it: a fault at that LDR requires the
incoming XMM0, since the vector XOR has not committed. The same current-state
rule handles both orders; the guest opcode list and native-site map decide which
version is current. The destination's old value remains necessary only until the load's success
commit; a non-writeback scalar LDR does not destroy it on its pre-access fault.
An aliasing destination/address must retain the same old value at that site.
Those actual native forms therefore do not require35 compulsory root stores or
a historical snapshot at every instruction. This is a conditional local binding,
not a guarantee that every allocation of the same guest opcode preserves it.

The concrete counterexamples remain the pinned allocator's last-use/free-before-
destination assignment (`RegisterAllocationPass.cpp:738–765`), fixed-register
remapping and spill/fill, and GuestOpcode's absence of value operands. A compiler
can otherwise overwrite a current value that is dead for later ordinary code
but still required at an earlier fault; a PC marker and saved native frame do
not prevent that. Retain claim28's metadata-only recovery uses and actual-site
validation. Where all current roots already survive, those metadata changes
need emit no additional store/load; where they do not, report the actual extra
moves/spills or service decision. Do not assume unchanged code solely from the
opcode's architectural commit safety.

The scalar guard already exercised in `boundary.py:21` is six instructions:
SBFX/EOR/non-S ADD/EOR/ORR/CBNZ, using x1/x2. They are scratch for the retained
FEX bindings, not guest x8/x9; the guard does not modify NZCV. Thus that guard
adds no compulsory NZCV save/restore and no guest-register spill for those
finite sites. Its six instructions and any relocated branch remain real hot
work. A generalized adapter must reserve/check those scratch registers against
its actual allocation map rather than infer they are always free. The separate
FP guard uses a saved-NZCV binding and a destination before-image because status
classification can reject a computed FP result; that cost must not be charged
to nonflag-clobbering scalar guards or omitted from FP. Root owns that FP method.

The proposed architecture default is therefore current-root preservation with
local repair, commit-safe native admission and bounded semantic exits. It is
constructible through the named IR/allocation/backend seams, without a mandatory
canonical publication pass. Its aggregate performance still depends on how
frequently actual lowered instructions meet the predicate, the measured local
repair costs, and checked-service frequency. This refinement supplies no full-
ISA coverage claim or unmeasured low-cost promise.

### CLM-03-02-59 — Lazy C1 with a bounded cheaper x87 numerical domain

**Source construction independently accepted by decision_audit in INV03-01
findings, September21; no runtime or performance claim.** The owner's
numerical-accuracy concession permits an approximate committed value. Preserve
C1 against the inherited instruction-specific status provider by evaluating it
only when observed. This is C1 for the operation on its original ext80 inputs,
not a claim that C1 describes the approximate binary64 value's rounding.
The existing provider's transcendental-status uncertainty relative to Intel
silicon remains; this construction does not establish new silicon fidelity.

C1 is overwritten or preserved according to the instruction, not accumulated
like exception flags (Intel SDM089 Vol1 §8.1.3.2/Table8-1). Keep at most one
64-byte copied-value descriptor per vCPU: operation identity, original one/two
ext80 operands, captured FCW and required format/disposition. An instruction
that writes C1 replaces it, one that preserves C1 retains it, and a known
constant setter materializes that bit directly. Undefined C1 uses the explicit
instruction disposition. The adapter must implement that instruction-specific
table; absence of an entry selects the inherited checked path.

Resolve before any architectural or canonical consumer reads the pending bit:
FNSTSW/FSTSW, FNSTENV/FSTENV, FNSAVE/FSAVE, FXSAVE/XSAVE and semantic consumers
of the full status word. Environment/state restores and FINIT/FNINIT replace or
invalidate it transactionally with their architectural commit. A faulting save
may have materialized C1, but cannot discard/reset x87 state merely because the
save was attempted. Later FLDCW cannot alter replay of captured operands/control.

Replay uses the inherited instruction-specific Bochs status-capable primitive,
a fresh local status initialized with captured FCW, and merges only C1. Discard
the strict numeric result and every replay exception flag; do not repeat guest
stack, memory, pop/push or other effects. FSINCOS uses its own two-result status
rule, not a generic sine/cosine function. Retained Bochs fsincos.cc and its
rounding helpers explicitly make final-operation rounding determine C1.

The initial cheap arithmetic domain is ADD/SUB/MUL/DIV with normal canonical
ext80 inputs converting to finite normal binary64, all exceptions masked,
nearest/extended FCW and PE already sticky. Retain the existing pending-exception
and stack-admission checks. Stage inherited binary64 arithmetic and accept only
a finite normal result away from the minimum-normal exponent bin; otherwise
use the full-status path with original values before commit. Input exponents
near binary64's range and 64-bit significands bound true ext80 results far
inside ext80 overflow/underflow limits, including cancellation. Nonzero normal
divisors exclude zero-divide and invalid; only possible new numeric exception
is PE, already sticky. Host trial flags are discarded. Numeric exception bits
therefore remain unchanged; instruction-defined TOP, stack and other condition
bits still receive their required updates.

A similarly bounded optional cheap transcendental domain is FSIN/FCOS/FPTAN/
FSINCOS for original ext80 absolute input in [2^-100,1], with the same masks,
PE and stack admission. Use the existing QEMU binary64/libm path; C2 is cleared
for this in-range domain and results stay far from ext80 range exceptions.
Keep instruction-specific stack/result commit and lazy C1. No new arithmetic
algorithm is proposed. Other controls, inputs and operations retain the inherited
full-status service. State images, tags, TOP, pointers, memory, pending exceptions
and delivery retain their existing owners and are not accuracy concessions.

The descriptor owns values and a fixed permanent helper identity, no guest-RAM
or old-code references. Its bounded pure completion needs no host compilation,
new queue entry or guest-memory reread, so claim19's drain and lifetime rules
apply unchanged. Helpers/storage must be reserved before admission as in claim58.

**Cost and falsifier:** input conversion, guards, descriptor writes and cold
resolution are real costs. Frequent C1 observation can erase the cheaper-value
benefit; known immediate observation can use the strict provider directly.
Existing primitive timings motivate the choice but do not measure this adapter
or a workload. This is a bounded source construction, not a new experiment or
permission to waive status semantics. No answer to the optional status question
has been inferred.


## Owner-requested extension versus hybrid comparison — September21

**Bounded source-only comparison; no new experiment or selection acceptance.**
Claims56–59 establish possible contracts, not the relative engineering or workload
cost of extending FEX versus retaining two generators. Both alternatives reuse
FEX's ordinary emitter, so the measured FEX-versus-TCG leaf advantage does not
distinguish them. The hybrid must earn its added integration cost through useful
semantic reuse; extending FEX must earn its added frontend work through simpler
execution and maintenance. No numeric effort estimate follows from this evidence.

### What extending FEX actually requires

Retain FEX's decoder, IR, optimizer and AArch64 backend; add or replace the missing
system-context semantics and bind its generated memory, exception, state and
helper effects to the resident owners. This does not mean writing another backend
or implementing every numerical algorithm. FEX already has typed numerical
fallback machinery and SoftFloat-based operations; selected inherited helpers can
remain resident calls. The owner's cheaper-x87 policy applies independently of
the generator choice. [FEX fallback interface](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Interpreter/Fallbacks/FallbackOpHandler.h),
[F80 fallbacks](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/Interpreter/Fallbacks/F80Fallbacks.h).

Instruction recognition is not full-system support: FEX's `IRETOp` explicitly
excludes CPL switching in its 32-bit path, and its `HLT` handling constructs a
userspace-style protection fault. Those paths require actual privilege, event,
stack and fault-order semantics. Ordinary instructions also need correct memory
protection and precise recovery; adding privileged opcodes alone cannot supply
claim56's commit/liveness adaptation. [FEX OpcodeDispatcher.cpp, IRETOp and HLT](https://github.com/FEX-Emu/FEX/blob/48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825/FEXCore/Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.cpp#L137).

QEMU reuse is possible without TCG, but must include the semantics outside helper
bodies. Its frontend performs `check_cpl0` while generating code; its emitted
control-register write calls `helper_write_crN` and ends the block. The helper
itself reaches CR updates, exception paths, APIC state, CPUState and BQL locking.
A borrowed helper therefore needs a transitive dependency and effect audit, with
resident replacements for ambient QEMU machinery. Neither copying one C function
nor calling it through FEX preserves the whole instruction automatically.
[QEMU privilege check](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/translate.c#L1527),
[control-write emission](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/emit.c.inc#L340),
[control helper dependencies](https://github.com/qemu/qemu/blob/c1c18d1e640b64292859ce9f30f3c344edfb0294/target/i386/tcg/system/misc_helper.c#L77).

### Alternatives and the costs they move

| Alternative | Reuses | Additional work and recurring cost | Evidence boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Extend FEX; borrow selected resident helpers** | Existing FEX frontend/backend and inherited semantic/numerical routines. | System contexts, missing instruction lowering/checks, precise effects and helper adaptation. One generated-code state convention, but helper calls still need materialization and a maintained ABI. | Avoiding a second generator is structural; lower total effort or faster complete execution is unmeasured. |
| **FEX plus concrete QEMU/TCG service** | Both frontends/backends, including QEMU-generated system/compound sequences. | Both captive adapters plus complete state/flag/context handoff, resident helper dependencies, two compiler integrations and service code-cache lifecycle. One-instruction service objects can make transitions frequent. | Claim58 supplies a construction, not a validated bridge. Native-body timings do not measure handoff frequency/cost or prove less work than extending FEX. |
| **Adapt QEMU/TCG alone; optimize measured hot paths** | One frontend, backend and state convention with full-system semantics already expressed. | Captive memory/helper/exception/lifetime adaptation remains. Improving register residency or hot lowering may require allocator/state synchronization changes. | Existing finite results expose particular TCG disadvantages and a SQRT advantage. They do not quantify the cost or outcome of targeted improvements. |
| **QEMU frontend into FEX IR/backend** | QEMU's semantic coverage and potentially FEX's optimized backend. | A compile-time semantic bridge for TCG operations, globals, memory ordering, helper effects and fault/commit boundaries; backend benefits depend on the resulting IR. | Replaces the runtime cross-generator boundary with compiler integration work. No implemented bridge or comparable output exists in this study; it cannot inherit the FEX timing result. |

All four retain host translation and in-VM generated execution/helpers. None
inherently requires a host VM exit for every system instruction. Compilation
misses, host I/O and policy-defined exits remain separate costs. Kernel versus
userspace is not the partition: eligibility is determined by instruction shape,
CPU context and required effects. Claims56/58 define the current candidate's
particularly narrow native eligibility, not an intrinsic FEX limit.

Box64 is another possible frontend/backend, but does not remove the shared
full-system, precise-recovery and resident-helper work; this study has no
source-backed reason to treat it as an escape from those obligations. Existing
finite Box64/FEX/TCG comparisons remain scoped to the emitted fixtures.

**Decision implication:** the serious near-term comparison is extended FEX,
FEX/TCG hybrid, and one adapted TCG engine. Architecture-interface count alone
cannot rank implementation effort, and leaf performance alone cannot rank the
first two. Evidence still missing is the actual semantic delta for the required
CPU envelope, handoff/service frequency and cost, and adapted recovery overhead.
This section records those limits; it does not authorize another campaign or
reinstate TCG as the default winner.

### Extension cost breakdown — owner follow-up, September21

**Source-informed effort assessment, not a measured schedule or selection.**
The bounded follow-up uses retained source only (one3-minute independent source
inspection; root checked the cited paths); no build or experiment. FEX already
contains PUSH/POP/CALL/RET, MOVS/STOS, DIV and CMPXCHG lowerings. Claim56's narrow
admission envelope does not mean these must all be implemented from nothing.

| Work | Reuse and incremental burden |
| --- | --- |
| Decoder/IR/optimizer/ARM64 backend and ordinary arithmetic/vector code | Retain existing machinery; extend affected operations and context handling, not a backend rewrite. |
| Captive load/store, helper, dispatch and publication interfaces | Required for either FEX-based design. The hybrid does not remove this cost. |
| Exact native state recovery and partial progress | High-risk FEX adaptation for either design's native instructions; extension must also handle newly admitted complex forms. Backend allocation/commit metadata changes may be needed. |
| Privilege/mode/flags and control instructions | Incremental system frontend/context work; reuse selected QEMU semantics with resident dependencies. Adding opcode recognition alone is insufficient. |
| Event entry/return, segment/stack validation and startup32 | System semantic integration and adversarial validation; the runtime owner exists architecturally, not as finished code. Hybrid reuses more generated semantics but must still adapt their execution. |
| SMP, fault and performance validation; upstream maintenance | Continuing integration cost. One backend removes a cross-generator interface, not the need for a maintained helper/state ABI or a system-support fork. |

Concrete difficult path: pinned FEX OpcodeDispatcher.cpp STOSOp lowers REP to
MemSet and updates architectural progress after completion; its source explicitly
identifies partial-fault REP as unsupported by that implementation. MOVS likewise
commits count/pointers after MemCpy. These require a restartable progress binding,
bounded steps or adapted resident semantic execution. They are not evidence that
all string instruction lowering is absent. Root also checked POPF's forced IF
and the existing typed helper ABI in FallbackOpHandler.h. See the pinned source
links in the preceding comparison; STOSOp begins at3222 and POPFOp at3689.

The critical cost uncertainty is retaining performance while making native state,
partial effects and system transitions precise. A helper-first implementation
can reduce new ARM64 emission but still needs complete instruction semantics and
can pay substantial boundary cost. Pure numerical algorithms remain inherited;
full80 fidelity and new Intel hardware are not added cost prerequisites.
There is no defensible person-week quote or completion date in the current
records: no complete semantic-delta inventory, implemented vertical slice or
measured engineering throughput exists. Relative-risk descriptions above must
not become numeric estimates by relabeling. This follow-up ends at that limit;
it assigns no new campaign or implementation work.

### Where userspace assumptions occur — owner follow-up, September21

Root and extension_comparison inspected the pinned source in a bounded read-only
pass. Explicit policy spans frontend, CPU state, selected IR operations and their
backend/runtime bindings; it is not confined to decoder tables. OpcodeDispatcher
contains the previously cited IRET/POPF/HLT policies. CoreState.h147–157 models
process-global segment arrays and228 initializes IF with an always-set contract.
IR.json122/324 encodes signal-oriented Break data, and330 defines Syscall through
the SyscallHandler. JIT/BranchOps.cpp285 lowers that operation to a runtime handler
call with state spilling; JIT/MiscOps.cpp59 lowers Break to signal landing pointers.
These paths require replacement/binding to guest system-state and resident events.

Separate precision gaps must not be mislabeled privilege policy. IR GuestOpcode
at193 records an offset, not recoverable values; MemSet/MemCpy at696/704 describe
final results rather than partial-progress outputs. Their backend loops use native
temporaries. RegisterAllocationPass.cpp738–765 frees last-use operands before
assigning destinations. This is ordinary allocation behavior: recovery dependencies
must be explicit so optimization and allocation preserve them. It does not mean
all optimization or native arithmetic emission is intrinsically userspace-only.
Native memory instructions likewise remain reusable when address, mapping,
permission, scratch and precise-fault contracts are supplied by the adapter.

Sources: pinned FEX CoreState.h, Source/Interface/IR/IR.json,
Source/Interface/Core/OpcodeDispatcher.cpp, JIT/BranchOps.cpp, JIT/MiscOps.cpp,
JIT/MemoryOps.cpp and IR/Passes/RegisterAllocationPass.cpp at the existing
48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825 pin. No new experiment, effort estimate or
replacement selection follows. The conclusion is frontend/state redesign plus
selected IR/backend/runtime adaptations, not a frontend-only patch or full rewrite.

### Count of demonstrated fault-handling gaps — owner follow-up, September21

**Scoped source audit, not an exhaustive ISA conformance count.** Count affected
families/forms once rather than multiplying operand/address sizes. Pinned FEX
48d71752e2a363d5535181f803b0b0e9666c1825; root inspected frontend/backend and source
comments, extension_comparison independently inspected string/gather and reviewed
the specific8-bit divide paths. One4-minute source task, no new build or experiment.

| Category | Counted item | Evidence and scope |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Partial progress | REP STOS | OpcodeDispatcher.cpp3250 explicitly disclaims partial-fault support; MemSet lowering publishes progress afterward. |
| Partial progress | REP MOVS | OpcodeDispatcher.cpp3277–3308 lowers bulk MemCpy then commits RCX/RSI/RDI; source-derived missing architectural intermediate-progress binding, not a new runtime observation. |
| Partial progress | AVX2 gather | OpcodeDispatcher/Vector.cpp5748 and AVX_128.cpp2258 explicitly assume nonfaulting behavior and clear the mask after the gather. JIT/MemoryOps.cpp1057–1168 scalar lane sequence does not clear architectural mask bits per completed lane. Eight mnemonic variants share the inspected family machinery; no separate count for vector widths. |
| Fault generation, separate from recovery | DIV r/m8 | DIVOp3740 emits UDiv with invalid upper operand; ALUOps978–983 executes native UDIV/MSUB and returns without zero or8-bit quotient overflow checks, then frontend commits AX. |
| Fault generation, separate from recovery | IDIV r/m8 | IDIVOp3782–3790 extends operands then emits Div with invalid upper operand; ALUOps877–897 executes native SDIV/MSUB and returns without zero or8-bit quotient overflow checks, then frontend commits AX. |

The defensible result is **three partial-progress families, plus two specifically
inspected arithmetic forms with fault-generation gaps**. This is a lower bound,
not five individually counted x86 opcodes or a complete list. The gather family
covers VPGATHERDD/DQ/QD/QQ and VGATHERDPS/DPD/QPS/QPD. REP STOS/MOVS size suffixes
are deliberately not multiplied into the family count.

REP LODS and SCAS expose per-iteration progress; they are not declared unsupported
from the generic REP comment. CMPS flag recovery merits a question but is not a
confirmed defect here. Stack/RMW, remaining division widths, FP exceptions and
state-save/restore forms do not gain a pass or fail label from this bounded audit.
Unsupported privileged semantics and optional CPUID faulting are not counted as
ordinary instruction partial-recovery gaps. No estimate of the total number of
faultable encodings follows from comments or the absence of a test filename.

Root independently checked both gather nonfaulting-policy sites and the8-bit
frontend/backend divide chains; extension_comparison separately confirmed the
8-bit paths. Native A64 divide-by-zero behavior is specified in ARM DDI0487C.a
C3.4.7 (Divide): no divide exception is generated. Primary manual copy:
https://www.cs.princeton.edu/courses/archive/spr22/cos217/reading/ArmArchitectureReferenceManual.pdf
The pinned FEX files are linked in the preceding source comparison. Source proof
of these paths does not promote old normal-input DIV timings into exceptional
conformance. The audit stops here with the scoped count and unknown total.
